# VL — Changelog

Shipped work, in rough area order. Each entry is a terse one-liner; detail lives in git
history, linked PRs, and `docs/`. For the forward plan see **`ROADMAP.md`**; for rationale
see **`DECISIONS.md`**.

---

## Type system (Track A)

- **`substTyDeep` now FOLDS a degenerate union, so the two type paths stop disagreeing about what `T | string` at `T := string` is** — the annotation path answers `string` (`annUnionInnerTy` returns `members[0]` for a one-member list, after a dedup); the substitution walk rebuilt a TWO-MEMBER union of identical members and stopped. One type, two representations depending on which door it came through. #1483 worked around it by excluding unions from the monomorphizer's return hand-over; this fixes the root, and **the exclusion is removed** — verified over the same 20-cell union-return grid at 0 moved. **The dedup is by ARENA INDEX, not `sameVariantTy`, and the narrowness is deliberate**: two members that are literally the same row are the same type by construction, which is exactly what substitution creates, while a structural merge would flatten mixed unions whose arena SHAPE is load-bearing (`annUnionInnerTy`'s own header records that flattening a litunion run buys zero grid cells and costs `==` two). **The checker is now more accurate, and one fixture records it**: `const a: string = f("a")` over `function f<T>(v: T): T | string` gains a *redundant type annotation* hint, because the call really does resolve to `string`. Before the fold the checker saw a union and said nothing — the annotation looked load-bearing because the type was wrong. The hint is DECLARED in the fixture rather than removed, so a regression in the fold surfaces as a MISSING hint. **This is a CHECKER change and was gated as one**: corpus A/B 0 of 2,022 including diagnostic text, suite 2,171/0, cases_wasm 1,951/0, native 2,188/0, fixpoint byte-exact, rep-fuzz exact, `mono-tyaram-grid` 0 BAD, plus all three behavioural grids this programme has accumulated (20 union-return cells vs pre-#1481, 20 hand-over audit cells vs pre-#1478) at 0 moved. (`tests/cases/generics/generic-return-union-collapses-under-binding.vl`)
- **A0 Type-algebra inventory** — `Alias`, `Function`, `Object` (structural + index sigs), literal types, `Union`, `Nullable`, `Intersection`, `Negation`, `Unknown`/`Infer`, `Never`, `Type`, `Custom`.
- **A3 Intersection surface syntax** — `A & B` parses, binds tighter than `|`, folds through `intersectType` at parse time; narrowing algebra (`intersectType`) done. Object-type structural intersection: `{a} & {b}` now merges field-wise to `{a, b}` (shared fields refine to their per-field meet; an impossible field → `Never`).
- **A3 fix: `never`-typed value rejected cleanly (no codegen crash)** — forming a VALUE for a binding/annotation whose type folds to `never` (`type C = A & B` with `A` an object and `B` a disjoint string literal) is now a clear, source-located type error — `Type \`C\` is \`never\` (an empty type — \`A & B\`, an object and a string literal, have no common values), so it has no values` — instead of slipping through to codegen and dying as the opaque `Unhandled AST -> WASM "Never" type`. The diagnostic RANGE points at the `A & B` intersection EXPRESSION (a source span the parser captures when it folds the `&` and threads alongside the empty-intersection tag), not the alias name or the value site. `ensureType`'s never branch reports when the TARGET is a (tagged) empty intersection, a real value flows into it, and a real `ctx` is present; legitimate `never` (a narrowed unreachable branch, a membership/narrowing query) is untouched. A defensive backstop in `toWasm.ts`'s `toWasmType` throws a clear compiler diagnostic if a `Never` ever reaches codegen (unreachable in practice). (`tests/cases/types/never-value-*.vl`, `never-narrowing-legit.vl`)
- **A14 fix: honest messages for degenerate self-referential aliases** — a pure self-cycle `type D = D` no longer claims `opaque/recursive type aliases are not yet supported` (which implied a future feature); it reports `Type \`D\` is defined in terms of itself with no base type, so it can never have a value` — it has no structural base, so nothing can inhabit it. A self-referential intersection `type E = A & E` (same class: a definition in terms of itself that collapses to `never`) reports `Type \`E\` refers to itself inside an intersection (\`A & E\`), so it reduces to \`never\``, ranged on the `A & E` expression. LEGITIMATE recursive aliases (`type List = { rest: List[] }`, mutual recursion) are unaffected — they resolve to a real structural body and never hit the degenerate-cycle path. (`tests/cases/types/self-alias-still-clean.vl`, `never-value-self-intersection.vl`, `recursive-array-element.vl`, `mutual-recursive-type.vl`)
- **B17 lint: unconditional-recursion** — a function that calls ITSELF on every path through its body can never return; flagged at its NAME token with a `warning` (`code: unconditional-recursion`) — `This function always recurses: every path through \`loop\` calls it again, so the call never returns`. The rule is ANNOTATION-INDEPENDENT, which is the whole point: `function loop(n: i32): i32 { return loop(n + 1) }` produced no diagnostic at all, while dropping the annotation produced `cannot infer a return type for 'loop'` — so the fix people reach for silences the message and leaves the infinite recursion. The analysis is a clean-PATH existence question, not "does the body contain a self-call": each statement reports whether some path through it reaches a function EXIT or the NEXT statement without a self-call, and the rule fires only when the body has neither. That keeps real recursion quiet — a base case is a clean exit (an `if` with no `else` is itself a clean fall-through past the guard), a self-call in a `while`/`for` body is a path that skips it (a loop may run zero times), and a self-call on the RIGHT of `&&`/`||`/`??` is a path that short-circuits before it. FIRES on: both `if`/`else` arms recursing, the self-call as the LEFT operand of `&&`, as a call ARGUMENT, as the block's implicit-return tail, in a `const` initializer, in every `match` arm, followed by a `return`, and a nested function that recurses on itself. SCOPE is DIRECT self-recursion keyed by the declaration's own name over ONE body — the walk never descends into a nested `FuncDecl` (whose body runs only when IT is called; the shared walker analyses it separately against its own name), and mutual recursion (`f` → `g` → `f`) needs a must-call graph over the module and stays out. A base case unreachable only by VALUE (`if n != n { return 0 }`) is a clean exit path syntactically and stays quiet — the lint does no constant folding beyond the literal booleans `constant-condition` already handles. Severity is `warning`, not `error`: an error would make `vl check` reject programs it accepts today. Zero findings across `compiler/*.vl` + `std/` (`scripts/lint-self.sh`). (`compiler/lint.vl`, `tests/cases/lint/unconditional-recursion.vl`, `unconditional-recursion-nested-fn.vl`, `unconditional-recursion-base-case-no-warn.vl`, `unconditional-recursion-conditional-no-warn.vl`, `unconditional-recursion-mutual-no-warn.vl`)
- **B17 lint: empty-intersection warning** — a `type X = A & B` (or a binding so annotated) whose two CONCRETE operands share no values folds to `never`; the declaration is flagged with a `warning` (`code: empty-intersection`, `source: vital`) — `Intersection \`A & B\` is empty (\`never\`) — an object and a string literal, have no common values` — ranged on the `A & B` intersection expression (not the alias name), matching the never-value error. A self-referential `type E = A & E` instead warns `Type \`E\` refers to itself inside an intersection (\`A & E\`), so it reduces to \`never\``. Scoped to concrete operands: a generic `T & U` (empty only for some instantiation) is NOT flagged. (`tests/cases/lint/empty-intersection.vl`, `generic-intersection-no-warn.vl`)
- **A14 Zero-cost nominal newtypes** — `type EntityId = new i32` / `type F32View = new { base: i32, length: i32 }` mints an identity a structural checker cannot express. `new` is a CONTEXTUAL keyword (only after a `type` declaration's `=`), so it remains a legal identifier everywhere else. Distinct in every position (let, global, param, return, struct field, array element, map value, nullable, union member): a newtype does not flow to its base, from its base, or to a sibling. A syntactic LITERAL is brand-polymorphic and adopts the destination's brand (`const e: EntityId = 0`, `e + 1`, `e == 0`); `as` is both the construction and the unwrap form; same-brand arithmetic keeps the brand and a mixed pair rejects. ZERO-COST is inherited rather than implemented — canon already rewrites a one-member alias annotation to its base, so NO emitter file changed and the emitted module is byte-identical to the same program with `new` deleted. `std:buffer`'s `F32View`/`I32View` drop the `f32base`/`i32base` field-name workaround and get 12 bytes SMALLER (two shapes collapse to one wasm heap type while staying two types). (`docs/internals/newtype-design.md`, `tests/cases/types/newtype-*.vl`, `tests/cases/memory/newtype-struct-views-distinct.vl`)
- **A14 fix: a one-member alias over a UNION is transparent (D-ALIASLU / D-ALIASNUL)** — the zero-cost claim above rests on canon rewriting a one-member alias annotation to its base, and `singleAliasMemberTyIx` had no arm for a UNION or NULLABLE member — so `type NK = new K` over `type K = "a" | "b"` (and the un-branded `type A = K`, the brand being incidental) stayed OPAQUE: `collectU` minted it a one-variant union ROW, `isUName` claimed every annotated slot for the `{tag, anyref}` BOX, and every READ classified from the arena and lowered the interned i32 ATOM — `call __print_i32__` on a `(ref $box)`, i.e. INVALID WASM with `vl check` rc 0, at every position and for every use. Measured on a 9-base × 9-position × {`new`, `alias`, `direct`} grid (243 cells) graded on the RUN VALUE: correct **117 → 175**, check-clean invalid wasm **65 → 0**; a 19-use grid at module scope went from 18 of 19 uses disagreeing with their alias-free control to **19 of 19 agreeing**. All three spellings now build **BYTE-IDENTICAL** modules (875 bytes for the one-liner, 2795 for a seven-position program). `new` is not the variable — the `new` and `alias` legs agree in all 81 base-position cells before and after. The two gates are `transparentMemberEmitName`'s two legs read backwards: a DECLARED union renders as its own name (a NUMERIC litunion declines on purpose so both spellings get `numLitUnionAliasName`'s base scalar), and an INLINE union is claimed only when its MEMBERS are name-faithful (`unionAliasMembersFaithful`) — ungated, a `(Cat | Dog)` alias renders `{n:i32}|{m:i32}` and `x is Cat` becomes ``emitProgram: `is` receiver is not a union``. Nullable members are gated on the INNER (`nulAliasMemberFaithful`), the nullable analogue of `arrSpineIsScalar`. Separately, a `null` OPERAND against nullable storage is no longer a brand MIX, so `v != null` works on a newtype over a nullable — gated on `tyAdmitsNull` because that predicate is read a second time as the equality arm's `nomEqLit` escape, and an ungated version suppressed the general `cannot compare … with null` reject too. (`tests/cases/types/newtype-over-litunion-alias.vl`, `alias-over-nullable-null-test.vl`, `union-alias-transparent-and-its-gate.vl`, `newtype-over-litunion-non-member-rejected.vl`, `null-test-on-non-nullable-newtype-rejected.vl`)
- **A14 fix: a declaration referenced BEFORE it is declared resolved against the EMPTY placeholder** — `cUserTypes` gets a declaration's PLACEHOLDER in the checker's pass 0a and its SHAPE in pass 0b, and pass 0b walked SOURCE ORDER, so a name resolved between the two answered from an empty arena entry. That is a WRONG TYPE, not a missing one: `declaredTyOfName`'s transparency collapse (`singleAliasMemberTyIx`) needs the alias's ONE member to be present, so an unfilled `type Flag = boolean` handed back the one-member `TyUnion` WRAPPER and the struct field that named it kept it. The SPELLING channel never had the bug — `canonEmitTypeNames` runs AFTER pass 0b and rewrote `Flag` to `boolean` either way — so field STORAGE was already right and only the arena-reading consumers diverged, which is what made the headline case SILENT: `type Wrap = { f: Flag }` above `type Flag = boolean` checked rc 0, emitted VALID wasm and printed `1`, while the same program with the two declarations swapped printed `true` — the two modules differing in nothing but `call __print_i32__` vs `call __print_bool__`. Pass 0b is now DEMAND-ORDERED: `fillTypeDeclAt` has two callers, the source-order driver and `declaredTyOfName` on demand (`forceDeclFill`) the moment a body names a declaration the driver has not reached. Cycles are unchanged because the done-marking still precedes the body — `type List = { rest: List[] }` and mutual recursion keep landing on their own empty placeholder, which is the cyclic arena shape they MEAN. Measured on a 744-cell grid (24 payload types × 10 use positions × 10 consumers, every program run at BOTH runtime inputs and graded on the RUN VALUE against the analytically-known stdout, with the same program's alias-BEFORE spelling as the control): ORACLE PARITY — the alias-after cell landing on its control's exact verdict AND exact stdout — **254/360 → 360/360**, with **114 cells newly correct, 0 backward**. The nine check-clean SILENTLY-WRONG cells go to **0**: a `boolean` payload printed `1`, a nullable LITUNION payload printed its interned atom id `0` instead of `la`. The other 99 were LOUD but UNACTIONABLE — **90 diagnostics across 49 cells rendered BOTH SIDES IDENTICALLY** (`argument 1: expected i32?, got i32?`, `cannot compare f64? with f64?`, `cannot assign i32?[] to 'bx1' of type i32?[]`, `operator '+' is not defined for i32 and i32`) and are now **0**: both sides really were the same type, so the fix ACCEPTS them rather than teaching the renderer to split them — verified by probe that a genuine nominal difference still renders distinctly (`expected i32, got NB` for a newtype). Two further shapes need the referenced declaration's BODY and not merely its slot, so neither is fixed by early placeholders: a field-wise struct INTERSECTION merged two empty placeholders to `{}` (`no field 'a' on {}`), and a two-hop chain `{f: I}` / `I = K & !"lb"` / `K = …` subtracted from nothing and invented ``Type `I` is `never` `` at the declaration. Alias-after vs alias-before BYTE-IDENTITY 232/254 → 343/348 buildable pairs; the 5 residual pairs are identical on master and are two independently-declared structs interning in source order, i.e. a permutation of type indices 0 and 1 with the same run value. The soundness boundary is pinned in the other direction and did not move: an unknown name, a pure self-cycle and a disjoint intersection, all FORWARD-referenced, still report EXACTLY ONCE and at their DECLARATION. (`tests/cases/types/alias-declared-after-use.vl`, `-litunion-niche.vl`, `-litunion-chain.vl`, `-struct-intersection.vl`, `-still-rejects.vl`)
- **A15 Flat record layouts (webcraft P1.2, declaration half)** — `flat type TValue = { value: i64, tt: i32, pad: i32 }` declares a record whose BYTE LAYOUT is declared rather than chosen: the C-struct tier WasmGC structurally cannot provide, for a Lua 5.3 VM needing bit-exact `Table`/`Node`/`TValue` layouts. `flat` is a CONTEXTUAL keyword before `type`, and the guard is exact — `type` is a hard lexer keyword, so an IDENT before it is not a legal statement under any parse and the widening is TOTAL. **NO IMPLICIT PADDING**: offsets are the running sum of declared field widths, the compiler never inserts a byte, and field order IS layout (the spec's own explicit `pad` field is the argument — under C rules it would be redundant — and unaligned access is legal in wasm, so there is no correctness case for the alternative). Fields must be `i32`/`i64`/`f32`/`f64`, a newtype over one of those (free — a brand is a second index over the same payload), or another `flat` type, which INLINES; `boolean`, heap references, generics and cycles reject, each naming the representation fact. The layout reads back as `TValue.size` and `TValue.tt` (a field's byte offset), folded to `NumLit` in the arena by the checker — so **no emitter file changed** and a `flat` declaration emits byte-identically to the same declaration with `flat` deleted. `flat` ADDS validation and constants and SUBTRACTS nothing: the type is still an ordinary record everywhere. REMAINING and filed with measurements: `buf.rows<T>` and the `stack[i].tt` sugar. (`docs/internals/flat-records-design.md`, `tests/cases/types/flat-*.vl`, `tests/cases/memory/flat-*.vl`)
- **A15 Flat record layouts (webcraft P1.2, FUSION half) — a library idiom, ZERO compiler lines** — `stack[i].tt` was filed as the hard half, on the reading that `"[]"` must return a ROW and the row must then be fused away. It need not: `"[]"` returns a row ADDRESS (`type RowAddr = new i32`), so no row is ever materialized and the field accessor is an offset load off an integer. The three pieces already shipped — `flat`'s folded `TValue.size`/`TValue.tt` constants, B14's free index operator, and P1.5 newtypes giving the bracket a branded RETURN type — compose with nothing added. `st[i].tt()` is `cmp`-identical, byte for byte, to the hand-written `tt(slotAt(st, i))` at BOTH `-O0` and `-O3 --closed-world`, where both accessors inline away entirely and the loop body is `(i << 4) + base + 8; i32.load` with no call remaining. The brand is load-bearing twice: it is what the bracket dispatches its result into, and it is what stops a bare `i32` from being read as a row. The spec's paren-less `stack[i].tt` is a LOUD checker reject (`member access '.tt' on non-object RowAddr`) and that is deliberate — branding over a STRUCT instead would make the wrong spelling compile and be slower. The design doc's "the bracket deletes two accessors PER FIELD" was corrected by building it: it deletes NONE (a 2-field record needs the same 5 functions), it deletes the CONTAINER axis — one accessor set serves N containers, N×M → N+M. (`docs/internals/flat-records-design.md` §9, `tests/cases/memory/flat-fused-row-*.vl`)
- **renderEmit P1 — the mixed-union litunion soften (owner ruling 2026-07-28)** — `tyToEmitNameGo`'s union arm loses both litunion-ALIAS preserving legs, so a string-litunion alias standing as a MEMBER of a union is spelled by its base `string` (`K | f64` → `string|f64`) — the spelling `canonEmitName` has produced for that annotation all along (its union-member loop expands a union-alias member through `unionAliasMembers`, whose render of a pure litunion IS `string`), so the two producers now agree instead of minting two names for one type. Census class **Lsoft goes 55 → 0 on B1's axis (24 files)** and B1's canon-only residue with it; canon's own column is byte-identical record for record, and all 60 moved renders are `K`/`K0`/`K1` → `string` inside a union. The rep does not move — `repCanonKey`'s union arm already mix-widens, `emitUnionCoerce` classifies the incoming value by EXPRESSION (`exprString`), and `isArmTagOfTy` already states in the arena that a litunion arm's box tag IS `string`'s; a 13-site coercion census (6 arena-keyed, 7 name-keyed) finds none keying on the preserved spelling. Ship-condition evidence: the nine cited fuzz-seed runs are byte-identical to master (0 unsound), and the canon-reverted control reproduces the historical breakage (13 MISMATCHes on 7 of 9 runs, every shape `K0 | <non-lit>`), proving the seeds pinned the DISAGREEMENT rather than the alias spelling. The same soften on `tyToNominalName` is measured, **reverted and filed**: deleting the twin variant row it was minting exposes that `IsExpr.isVariant` is not in the emitter's vocabulary (fuzz seed 1000003 d6 case_00039 → ``is` names a type that is not a union variant``), and canon'ing the `is` spelling costs a wrong answer in `soundness/literal-is-runtime-value.vl` — that is the next slice. Corpus 1,700 files × 6 channels: 0 moved; fuzz A/B 2 × 6,000 cases/side byte-identical; sabotage witnesses 26 files; byte delta −1,139. (`docs/internals/destringify-types-program.md`, `tests/cases/literal-unions/mixed-union-alias-member-softens.vl`)
- **renderEmit P1's REVERSAL — the mixed-union litunion PRESERVE (owner ruling 2026-07-29, reversing 2026-07-28)** — *"I'm reverting the softening. `K | f64` should be preserved as `K | f64` (or flattened to the constituent parts of `K | f64`). Softening to a string is just wrong as I see it."* All FOUR sites that speak this vocabulary move in one slice, not a `git revert` of #1296 (which would restore the 55-row disagreement): `tyToEmitNameGo`'s union arm regains its regroup and per-member alias legs (with its dual-written predictor `tyEmitNameIsString`), `canonEmitNameAt`'s union arm gains their string-side MIRROR (`mixedUnionLitAliasRegroup`), the emitter's stored-name normalizer `emit_classify.canonLitUnionArms` stops re-widening, and — the one nobody had enumerated — the ARM SCANNER `unionRefArrayArmSlotForElemAtom` learns R3's boxed-member rule. Census: **the Lsoft class reaches ZERO on all three axes** — B1 (never present), B2 (57 → 0, total 96 → 39, 46 → 21 files), B3 (57 → 0, total 193 → 136, 88 → 63 files; the 2 rows the classifier still scores `Lsoft` are the generic type-param SHADOW family and are byte-identical on master). All 60 moved records are `string` → `K`/`K0`/`K1` inside a union, on canon's column (57) and B1's (60) and on **ZERO B2/B3 cells** — the nominal renderer had preserved every one all along, which is why this reaches the numbers #1296's BLOCKED nominal half was aiming at without touching it. ALIAS beat FLATTEN by measurement (4 vs **13** corpus cells rc 0 → 1): a single quoted literal is not a union ATOM in the emitter's grammar. The REP key does not move — `repCanonKey`'s union arm is arena-keyed and mix-widens structurally. **The fourth site is the finding.** It is a CONSUMER, not a producer, and its atom vocabulary is CLOSED (`"string"`/`"boolean"`/`"f64"`/`"i64"`/`"i32"`/a variant name), so a preserved `K` matched nothing, a list literal flowing into a union lost its ref-array arm, and `(K | i32 | null)[] | {w: i32} = ["a", 5]` wrote a STRING list where the union box list belonged — invalid wasm, on a cell that was a clean reject one commit earlier. It was invisible to two successive 13-site coercion censuses because while `canonLitUnionArms` re-widened, no preserved alias ever reached it. One line fixes it, and only the `"string"` probe atom needs a leg: a VL literal union is string-literal-only by construction (`tyIsLitUnion` rejects `litKind != "str"`). Corpus 1,700 × 6 channels vs master: **6 files moved, all BYTES, zero rc / message / run** — and each of the six is a UNIFORM union-box TAG renumbering (identical byte size, only `i32.const` operands differ, one delta per module), which the published band formulas (`scalarTagOfKind`/`refArrSlotTag`/`mapSlotTag`, all rooted at `uVariants.length`) resolve to the duplicate union ROW disappearing — the ruling's whole point, made visible. Falsifiability, one config per site: reverting canon moves 6, restoring the emitter's soften moves 4 (all rc 0 → 1), deleting the arm-scanner leg moves 1 (the survivor), and the structural renderer's half is a reported ZERO on all six channels and all nine cited-seed runs, carried on the contract like #1296's own dual-write. Poison: SAB1 (canon's alias suffixed `ZZ`) moves **17** named files, SAB2 (the renderer's) 0. The seven cited seeds (9 runs × 200) and the 10-seed × 3-depth × 200 fuzz A/B (**6,000 cases per side**) are **BYTE-IDENTICAL to master**, 0 unsound on both sides — including the one shape the parked checker-only branch turned into a reject. (`docs/internals/destringify-types-program.md`, `tests/cases/literal-unions/mixed-union-alias-member-preserves.vl`)
- **Post-preserve P1 — a GROUPED inline literal union stops softening under `| null`** — `canonEmitName` preserved `("a"|"b")[]|null` as `K[]|null` but softened its sibling `(("a"|"b")|null)[]` to `(string|null)[]`: a top-level `|` split hands out the element part `("a"|"b")` with its GROUPER still on, and `nameIsLitUnionType`'s inline leg opens with an O(1) reject that requires the name to begin with a quote. `nulLitUnionPreserve` gains the rung its sibling classifier `litRunPartTexts` already states in a comment (*"a GROUPER is not a member"*), with the identical two-conjunct guard, and hands the interior to `litUnionPreserve` so the declared-ALIAS preference keeps one home — 9 lines of code. Both renderers already spelled it `(K|null)[]`, so the census moves on canon's column ALONE: **4 records, one shape, B1 1,933 → 1,929 · B2 39 → 35 · B3 136 → 132**, and 0 cells on B1/B2/B3 themselves. It is not a spelling tidy — the position sweep (9 positions, 7 byte-identical) says master compiled `(("a"|"b")|null)[][]` to a 530-byte module that DOES NOT VALIDATE at `vl check` rc 0, and rejected the same list as a map VALUE (`emitProgram: field access but no struct type declared`); both now run, and their `(K|null)` alias controls are byte-identical on both compilers. The mechanism reads off the module: the fixture's emitted binary loses TWO heap types (`(array (mut (ref null $aType)))` + its wrapper — the STRING-LIST family the softened name pulled in) and 13 bytes, with identical stdout; the two columns' REPS were always the same, only their NAMES forked. Corpus 1,703 files × 6 channels: **1 moved** (BYTES, and the byte count inside BUILDMSG), 0 on CHECKRC/CHECKMSG/BUILDRC/RUN; the `ZZ` poison takes that same cell rc 0 → 1. Fuzz A/B 6,000 cases/side byte-identical **and vacuous, proved by grepping the generator's 2.38 MB of output: 0 grouped inline litunions in 12,000 records**. Byte delta +89. Also re-derives the post-preserve B2/B3 frontier — B2 is a strict SUBSET of B3, the G class is **ZERO** (#1274's nominal sidecar closed all 114 and the direction inverted), 80 of B3's extra 97 rows are a generic type PARAMETER against its own instantiation rather than a disagreement, and the `is`-spelling seam #1296 filed is **RETIRED** (`uVariants` for its witness reads `1 :: [0]{f:K0|i64}`, was 2). (`docs/internals/destringify-types-program.md`, `tests/cases/lists/litunion-inline-nullable-element.vl`)
- **LINSOFT — both renderers learn the UN-ALIASED inline literal union** — canon has preserved a pure inline `"a" | "b"` at a keep position since #1302, but `tyToEmitName` and `tyToNominalName` had the ALIAS half of that rule at six positions each (`litUnionAliasNameOfTy`) and no leg at all for a run the program never named: every `TyLit` member rendered its base and the arm's dedup collapsed the run to one `string`. `litUnionInlineNameOfTy` is the ARENA twin of canon's `litUnionPreserve` / `nulLitUnionPreserve` pair — same two gates (`| null` preserves at EVERY position, the bare form only under `ctxKeepsLitUnion`), same `kept.length < 2` decline, same declared-alias preference — plus one call in each renderer's `TyUnion` and `TyNullable` arm. Census: **the LINSOFT class reaches ZERO on B2 and B3** (B2 39 → 28 in 21 → 16 files, B3 137 → 126, B1 1,935 → 1,926; 11 rows removed, 0 added, canon's own column byte-identical record for record). The 11 partition EXACTLY between the two legs — deleting the `ctxKeepsLitUnion` branch re-opens 7, deleting the `| null` legs re-opens the other 4, disjoint and exhaustive. **The registration key that moved is the STRUCT LAYOUT row and it MERGED**: master interns 42 rec-group heap types for the grown fixture where this head interns 41, and the one that vanishes is `(struct (mut (ref $str)) (mut i32))` — the string-field twin of the atom-field struct the annotation already registered. Union rows and all three tag bands do NOT move (a pure litunion reps as an interned i32 atom, never a box — 0 BYTES cells on 1,703 files), and the rep key cannot (arena-keyed). Two `vl check` rc-0 **silent invalid wasm** shapes close: a LAMBDA whose inferred return is an inline-litunion-field shape, and the same shape as a closure-VALUED map's result — both with green `K`-alias and `string` controls, and both green on master when written with a NAMED function, which is how the search space narrowed to the lambda's inferred pin. **The slice's own first form created a SPLIT and the position sweep found it before the corpus could**: `genAppNameOfTy` rendered a generic ARGUMENT at `RC_FIELD` where canon canons it at `RC_ROOT` (a new census row on a pair that AGREED on master — fixed by taking canon's constant), and the STRUCTURAL renderer, which has no application route at all, reached the argument through the EXPANSION at a field and wrote an invalid module for `Box<"aa"|"zz">` at the lambda position — fixed by `appKidCtx`, after building the alternative (teach CANON the argument's real position) and measuring it worse. Corpus 1,703 × 6 channels, run twice: **0 moved over master's OWN corpus** — this compiler change alters no existing program — and **1 over the shipped one**, the grown fixture going BUILDRC 1 → 0. Seven falsifying configs, one per rung: `nokeep` 1 · `nonul` 0 · `noapp` 1 · `garoot` 0 · `nogate` **4, all invalid wasm** (the gate's own justification, at the PARAM positions `ctxKeepsLitUnion`'s header predicted in prose) · poison `SAB-STRUCT` 1 · poison `SAB-NOM` **0, reported as 0** — the third slice to measure the nominal renderer inert. Fuzz A/B 10 seeds × 3 depths × 200 = 6,000 cases/side byte-identical **and vacuous, proved by grep: all 1,710 quoted-literal type spellings in 2.38 MB of generated source are `type K0 = …` alias DECLARATIONS, 0 inline**. Byte delta +1,086. (`docs/internals/destringify-types-program.md`, `tests/cases/literal-unions/inline-atom-shape-field.vl`)
- **A16 litunion COMPACT REP — DESIGNED AND FILED, and its own rationale REFUTED by measurement** — the preserve ruling (#1300) was justified partly by a door it kept open: a mixed union box could store its litunion member as the compact interned ATOM id instead of a string ref, *"cheaper allocation, faster equality"*, and that is only spellable once the vocabulary distinguishes `K` from `string`. Walked through and measured, the allocation half is **wrong**: `collectStrPool` interns every distinct literal into an immutable global, so `const x: K | f64 = "aa"` emits `i32.const 2 / global.get 0 / struct.new 0` — **exactly ONE `struct.new`, the box itself, and a payload that is a `global.get` rather than an allocation**. No rep allocates less; the obvious compact encoding (the existing `$vbI32` scalar box round the atom id) allocates **twice**, and only `ref.i31` — of which the emitter has **zero** today — matches one. The brief's ship bar was *"show a witness program's WAT losing the string-ref path"*; there is no such witness because that path does not allocate, so the verdict is FILE. The equality half survives intact (a `ref.cast` + an O(len) `__str_eq__` CALL becomes one `i32.eq`). **What the measurement DID find is 81 broken cells of 244**, all `vl check` rc 0 — **42 SILENT wrong answers**, 34 invalid wasm, 5 loud — with ONE root cause: **`valueAtomKind` has no code for a literal-union member** (it returns `-1`; `emit_base.unionHasValueAtom` carries the workaround with the reason written out), and **35 of its 42 call sites have no compensating litunion leg**. Four families: storing an atom-typed VALUE into `K | f64` emits `f64.convert_i32_s` **on the atom ID** and tags it f64 (24 cells); reading a narrowed arm back into a `K`-typed position is invalid wasm in **9 of 9** spellings (16 cells); `K | string` puts two members on one tag, so `const x: K | string = "zz"; x is K` prints **K** in six lines — refuting the invariant `tests/cases/unions/litunion-value-union-is.vl` has asserted in a comment since #845; and `K | K2` reps as a bare string with `is K` **const-folded to `i32.const 0`**. **A loud reject is not available for the collision shapes** — counted, not assumed: it would move **12** and **4** `RUN-OK` cells DOWN, so they are BLOCKED on the rep. Three owner rulings gate the build (the tag scheme — a 14th value-atom kind bumps both slot bands by a constant where a per-set slot band re-bases them wholesale, renumbering every union box tag in every program; whether `ref.i31` enters the emitted vocabulary — measured available in wasm-tools' shipped set, V8, and wasmtime 47, the last proved by a module that reaches IMPORT resolution; and what `K | K2` should mean), plus the `repCanonKeyGo` mix-widen inversion that fragments struct-twin rows. **The `K | K2` answer turns out to need no ruling at all and is the slice to take first**: a union all of whose members are literal unions IS a literal union, and the flattened target already runs on master with ZERO compiler changes (`type KA = "aa"|"bb"|"cc"|"dd"`, `x is K` → `K` for `"aa"` and `O` for `"cc"`), because `emitIs`'s membership ladder tests the tested type's own members over the interned atom — so slice C's runtime half is free and only the checker's render (which today spells `K | K2` as `string`) has to move; a CHECKER-level reject is not an alternative, since §4's never-move-down arithmetic does not care which phase a reject comes from. **Two method corrections shipped with it**: this slice's own first grid greened falsely on #1304's control rule — an inline `("aa"|"bb")` spelled in a file declaring `type K = "aa"|"bb"` let the alias answer the inline lookups and hid **10 cells**, including a narrowed read that is invalid wasm in 5 of 6 spellings; and the fuzzer's litunion-in-mixed-union reach is **real** (26 and 14 of 800 cases on two seeds, correcting the standing "zero reach" note) but **vacuous on every defect family** — it only ever stores a member LITERAL, which is the cell that already passes. No compiler file changed; the working cells are pinned. (`docs/internals/litunion-compact-rep-design.md`, `docs/internals/destringify-types-program.md`, `tests/cases/literal-unions/mixed-union-litunion-arm-floor.vl`)
- **Contextual f32 literals (webcraft P2)** — `let x: f32 = 0`, `f(1)`, `return 2`, `{v: 1}`, `[1]`, `xs[0] = 1`, `xs.push(1)`, `fv + 1`, `fv > 1`, `f32 | null = 1` and the `"[]="` operator all take a bare literal. Measuring the ask first SPLIT it: the `.`-literal half was ALREADY shipped in all 32 positions grid-tested, while the INTEGER half was missing in **18 of 18**, every one of which the f64 twin already compiled. The two halves take deliberately DIFFERENT rules, forced rather than chosen for symmetry — a `.` literal is **context-typed** (f32 from birth, rounding ONCE at 24 bits) because an exactness gate would reject `0.1`/`0.2`/`3.14` and leave nothing to admit; an INTEGER literal is **exactness-gated** (`intLexemeIsExactF32`, a closed-form bit test: `bitlen <= 128 && bitlen - trailingZeros <= 24`, where the 128 bound is EXACT because the largest finite f32 and the largest 128-bit integer with ≤24 significant bits are the same number) because it denotes an exact integer and silently turning `16777217` into `16777216` is the lossy implicit conversion the lossless-only rule forbids. So `16777216` and `33554432` are admitted while `16777217` and `9000000000` reject, with both one-token escape hatches (`16777217.0`, `as f32`) intact. **`as f32` is NOT the oracle for what the sugar lowers to**: for `1.00000017881393432617187` the bare literal encodes `0x3F800001` (one rounding) and the cast `0x3F800002` (decimal→f64, then ties-to-even on the demote) — *different numbers* — and the cast is a runtime `f64.const` + `f32.demote_f64` that cannot sit in a `const` global initializer at all. Both halves now funnel through ONE `isF32AdoptingLitExpr` so they cannot drift position by position; the mixed-arithmetic lattice (`mixesNumeric`/`widerNumeric`/`binMixHardF64`) is untouched, since the literal retypes before it runs. Corpus: 1 of 1,656 files moved, +2 bytes, all values identical (`i32.const 7; f32.convert_i32_s` → the shorter, constexpr-valid `f32.const 7.0`). (`docs/internals/contextual-f32-literals-design.md`, `tests/cases/numerics/f32-contextual-integer-literal.vl`, `f32-integer-literal-exactness.vl`, `f32-integer-literal-inexact-reject.vl`, `f32-literal-single-round.vl`)
- **fix: a radix literal in a FLOAT context was a silent wrong value** — `const x: f64 = 0x10` printed **7210** and `const x: f64 = 0b101` printed **50101**, rc 0 with `vl check` clean. `ieeeBytes` — the shared decimal→IEEE-754 encoder — folded mantissa digits with a hard-coded `* 10` and `c - '0'`, walking straight over the `0x` prefix (0, then `'x' - '0'` = 72, then 721, then 7210). Only the CONSTEXPR path was wrong: a float context lowers an integer literal directly as `f64.const`/`f32.const` so a `const` global initializer stays a valid wasm constant expression, whereas the call-ARGUMENT spelling `f(0x10)` converts from i32 and was always right — which is why it hid. Fixed in the encoder, so both widths and every position take it at once. (`tests/cases/numerics/float-literal-radix-constexpr.vl`)
- **fix: an integer literal boxed into a float-only union was invalid wasm** — `const x: f64 | null = 1` emitted `type mismatch: expected anyref, found i32`, `vl check` clean, in the GLOBAL, LOCAL and ARGUMENT positions alike. `emitUnionCoerce`'s value-atom ladder promotes its i32 default to `i64` when the union has no i32 atom — but the LIST arm of the SAME ladder promotes an i32-element list to `i64[]`/`f64[]`/`f32[]`, three targets, on identical reasoning. The scalar arm had been taught only the first, so an integer flowing into a float-only union kept the i32 code and boxed an i32 into the float value box. The two spellings that WORKED localize it exactly: `i64 | null = 1` took the taught half and `f64 | null = 1.0` classifies as a float up the ladder and never reaches the default — both are controls in the pin. (`tests/cases/unions/nullable-float-integer-literal.vl`)
- **`m.set(k, v)` takes the assignment path's literal narrowing (webcraft P2 polish)** — the map `.set` VALUE argument was the last store position checked with the bare structural `assignable`, so `m[k] = 0.5` over a `{[string]: f32}` compiled while `m.set(k, 0.5)` was `set: expected f32, got f64` — and the same for an exact integer literal, a doubly-signed one, a member literal into a literal union (string OR integer members), an object literal adapting field-wise, an array literal adapting element-wise, and each of those again through a `V | null` / `f32 | string` value slot or a receiver reached from an array element or a struct field. It now runs the SAME two steps `xs.push(v)` runs — `seedExpected`, then `assignableExpr` — which is not a second copy of the rules but the very function the assignment path calls, so the two cannot drift; that also subsumes the `nomSlotAccepts` newtype retry the arm carried for its value (`m.set("k", 1)` over `{[string]: EntityId}` still adopts). `seedExpected` is the half `.push` already had: `m.set("k", (x) => x + 1)` over a `{[string]: (i32) => i32}` now infers `x` instead of "cannot infer a type for parameter". A 146-cell twin grid against the `m[k] = v` control went **16/38 → 38/38** direct and **0/12 → 12/12** nested map-store pairs agreeing on verdict AND stdout, 34 cells moving CHKERR → OK, every one of them printing the value its index-assignment twin prints. The REJECTED side is unchanged and is what makes it a rule: an inexact integer (`16777217`), a non-member literal, and a non-literal `f64` VALUE all still reject. MEASURED AS NEEDING NOTHING: `Set`'s `.add` (7/7 twin pairs already agreed — its argument is a KEY, and keys are `string`/`i32` with no literal to narrow) and the `.set` KEY itself (`checkIndexNode` checks `m[k]`'s key with the bare `assignable` too, so widening it would diverge in the other direction). (`tests/cases/maps/set-value-literal-narrowing.vl`, `error-set-value-still-rejected.vl`)
- **fix: a doubly-signed literal in an f32 context was invalid wasm** — `const x: f32 = -(-1.5)` (and `-(-(-1.5))`) emitted a WebAssembly translation error, `vl check` clean, while the f64 twins ran. The defect shape this family keeps taking: THE CHECKER GRANTED MORE SHAPES THAN THE EMITTER COULD LOWER. `isFloatLitExpr` recurses through unary minus when deciding an expression is a literal eligible for the f32 context; `emitExprAsF32` matched exactly ONE minus, so a doubly-signed literal was admitted by the checker, not recognized as a literal by the emitter, and lowered through the f64 path into an f32 slot. Found by an edge sweep run AFTER the three position grids were green — it sat one syntactic step outside all of them. Both sides now share one peel (`signedNumLitText`), folding any number of signs into the literal's text; `f32Bytes` parses a leading `-`, so the fold costs no `f32.neg`. (`tests/cases/numerics/f32-nested-sign-literal.vl`)
- **A4 Negation surface syntax** — `!A` and `x !is T` (Kotlin-style negated guard); `subtractType` narrowing algebra. → `DECISIONS.md`
- **A5 Flow narrowing (broad)** — nullness, union-member, post-guard clauses, `&&`/`||` chains, De Morgan, else-of-else-if, literal discrimination, `?.`/`??`. (→ `docs/narrowing.md`)
- **A6 `is` operator + tagged unions** — `x is T` discriminates a value union at runtime (niche/value-kind/boxed encodings, global tag registry, `coerceUnion`). (→ `docs/unions.md`)
- **A6b Proof-carrying narrowing (degenerate)** — a body that is exactly `return <predicate-on-a-param>` is an inferred guard; guard-clause `if absent(v) { return }` supported.
- **A7 Real `string` type (core)** — proper Object (`{[i32]: i32}` index sig → i32-array of char codes), `.length`/`s[i]`/`+`/`==`/`slice`/`indexOf`/`includes`/`charCodeAt`.
- **A10 Parametric types / generics** — Stage 1 (function type params), Stage 2 (array element inference), Stage 3 (generic `type` aliases incl. nested/array); `map`/`filter` build-side generics over growable `T[]`. (`tests/cases/generics/`)
- **A-infer-null `let x = null` as a nullable hole** — an un-annotated `let x = null` is no longer pinned to the exact `null` type (which made `let x = null; x = 5` error `expected null, got 5`); `null` is now hole-bearing like `[]`. The binding is `T | null` where `T` is a fresh shared inference hole (`Nullable<Infer<Unknown>>`), and a later constraint — a reassignment `x = <expr>`, a `T | null` param, an annotated assignment — FILLS the hole's `T` in place via the same `constrainElementHole` machinery the empty array uses (`x = 5` ⇒ `x: i32 | null`). This is NOT a pin violation: VL still pins COMPLETE types (`let x = 7; x = "foo"` errors — `7` is complete, `null` is not), and once the hole is filled the cell behaves as that complete type (`let x = null; x = 5; x = "foo"` errors). The initializer contributes the `| null`, which is sticky (it reflects that `x` really was null) EXCEPT flow-narrowing strips it on a path where `x` is provably non-null: a straight-line `x = <non-null>` narrows `x` to its non-null base for the rest of the block (no null tax — `let x = null; x = 5; x + 1` runs), threaded through the shared `postGuardNarrowings` entry point so both the type checker's scope overlay and codegen's narrowed overlay get it; an assignment inside an `if` branch stays in that branch, so the conditional-assign idiom (`let x = null; if c { x = f() }`) still requires a null check across the branch. An UNCONSTRAINED `let x = null` (never reassigned/used to pin `T`) resolves to the plain `null` type — a lone `null` IS fully determined, so the inference floor (`reportUninferredBinding`) does NOT raise "cannot infer" for it (distinct from an empty `[]`, whose element is genuinely unknown). A `const x = null` (immutable) keeps the exact `null` type, and an explicit `let x: T | null = null` is unchanged. Also fixes a latent bug exposed here: typing an assignment EXPRESSION (a block-tail `{ x = v }`) whose target is any `T | null` cell (`let n: i32 | null` too) no longer spuriously fails the scalar `=` operator-method lookup. (`tests/cases/types/infer-null-*.vl`)
- **A-infer-empty Usage-driven element inference for empty `[]`** — an un-annotated empty array literal now infers its element type from DOWNSTREAM usage, exactly like a generic resolves its type arg from arguments: `const xs = []; xs.push(1)` infers `xs: i32[]` (and `xs.push("a")` → `string[]`), as does passing `xs` to a `T[]` param, an annotated assignment (`const ys: i32[] = xs`), a `T[]`-returning tail, or an index-set (`xs[i] = v`). The constraint is threaded back to the binding's shared element hole (`constrainElementHole` for `push`; the existing index-signature unify in `ensureType` for param/return/assignment), then the hole is collapsed in place so codegen sees a normal typed array. The "cannot infer — annotate" diagnostic (the robustness floor) is now deferred to scope-close, so it fires ONLY for a genuinely-unconstrained empty (no resolving use). (`tests/cases/arrays/infer-empty-*.vl`, `error-empty-uninferred.vl`)
- **B-infer-empty-emit: an inferred `string[]` empty `[]` lowers when its element comes from a NON-LITERAL push** — the emitter's usage-driven element inference for an un-annotated `const out = []` followed a push of a string LITERAL (`out.push("hi")`) but not a string-typed VARIABLE / index (`out.push(s)`, `out.push(self[i])`): the binding's SLOT typed string-list (kind 7) while the `[]` literal still built the i32 list, so the store trapped wasm validation (`expected (ref $type), found (ref $type)`). A richer `letInfStrListByUseV(letIx, fnIx)` — with the enclosing function in hand — recognises a push of any STRING-typed expression via `exprString` (a `gStrInfLet` re-entrancy guard breaks the `out.push(out[0])` self-reference), and the `LetDecl` emit seeds `pendingListKind = 3` from it so `emitArr` builds the SAME string-list wrapper the slot expects (the two sites now agree). This is the codegen dual of `A-infer-empty` (the checker already inferred `string[]` from such a push); it unblocks a generic build-loop — `slice<T>` / `reverse<T>` over a `string[]` (and `i32[]`, the default rep) lowers, where the loop accumulator `const out = []; out.push(self[i])` previously trapped. (An `f64[]` / struct-element accumulator stays a separate follow-up — its non-literal-push inference is not yet usage-driven.) Native fixpoint byte-identical, deno corpus 542, native sweep PASS=409. (`tests/cases/arrays/infer-empty-push-var.vl`)
- **A-infer-map-set Usage-driven key/value inference for `Map()` / `Set()`** — the `Map()`/`Set()` analogue of `A-infer-empty`. An un-annotated `const m = Map()` now infers its key/value type from a downstream `m.set(k, v)` (`const m = Map(); m.set("a", 1)` infers `m: {[string]: i32}`), and `const s = Set(); s.add("x")` infers `s: Set<string>`. The `Map()`/`Set()` constructor binds a bare `Infer<Unknown, mapCtor>` hole (the same object the binding holds); the first `.set`/`.add` MATERIALISES that hole IN PLACE (`constrainMapHole`) into the concrete string-keyed index-signature object — `{[string]: V}` for a Map, `{[string]: boolean}` for a Set — byte-for-byte identical to what an annotated `const m: {[string]: i32} = Map()` produces, so `.get`/`.has`/`.length`/`.keys()`/`.values()`/iteration/codegen all work unchanged. Every later `.set`/`.add` then type-checks STRICTLY against the now-pinned key/value, so a conflicting `m.set("b", "x")` after `m.set("a", 1)` (resp. `s.add(1)` after `s.add("x")`) is a clean type error, never a crash. Map/Set keys must be `string` (the only hash key the rep supports today); an inferred i32 key is rejected with the same message the annotated `{[i32]: …}` path uses. The "cannot infer — annotate" floor stays deferred to scope-close, firing ONLY for a genuinely-unconstrained `Map()`/`Set()` (no `.set`/`.add`). (`tests/cases/maps/infer-from-set.vl`, `maps/error-infer-conflict.vl`, `maps/error-uninferred.vl`, `sets/infer-from-add.vl`, `sets/error-infer-conflict.vl`)
- **A-infer-return-join Multi-return inference JOINS all returns into a union/nullable (and lowers it), not first-wins** — an un-annotated function whose `return`s disagree now infers the FLATTENED union/nullable of every return, not just the first one observed, AND the emitter lowers it end-to-end. Before, `function f(b) { if b { return 5 }; return null }` inferred `i32` (the first return), silently dropping the `null` branch — so a caller's `r != null` narrow was mis-typed (`cannot compare i32 with null`) and `function g(b) { if b { return 5 }; return "hi" }` was WRONGLY accepted as `i32`, then crashed codegen when the string flowed through an i32 result. **Inference:** the two return accumulators (`noteInferredRet`, and `noteNaturalRet` for the redundant-return rule) now fold each return through `joinTys` (which mirrors the host's `flattenType(Union(...))` — assignable-collapse, dedup, nullability folds to a `?` wrapper), so the caller sees the true `i32 | null` / `i32 | string`. **Codegen:** a VALUE union / nullable-scalar inferred return (`isValueUnionName` — ≥2 non-null value atoms, or one numeric scalar + `null`) now lowers through the SAME machinery as an annotation, driven from the checker's exported inferred name: `collectU` registers it (`registerValueUnionName` interns the `uBoxIdx` box + value-box reps even when an inferred return is the program's only value union), `buildFnMap` sets `fRetUni` (the result valtype), the return site boxes via `emitUnionCoerce`, and the caller's `unionNameOfExpr` resolves the inferred name so an `is`/`!= null` narrow unboxes correctly — all with NO annotation and NO change to the AST (`fmt`-invisible). Verified: 3-member unions (`i32 | string | boolean`), `f64 | null`, and an inferred union flowing into an annotated `i32 | string` param across functions. A ROBUSTNESS FLOOR remains only for the union shapes the value box can't yet hold — a union of STRUCT refs (variant-boxed rep) and the `boolean | null` / `string | null` niches — reported as `cannot infer a {union,nullable} return type for 'f' — annotate the return type` (mirroring the empty-`[]` floor) instead of a cryptic codegen crash; lowering those un-annotated is the remaining follow-up (`docs/vl-tech-debt.md`). Native fixpoint holds byte-for-byte; the compiler's own inference also shifted +20 bytes where a multi-return now joins to the common structural SUPERTYPE rather than the first branch — a strict correctness gain. (`tests/cases/functions/infer-{union,nullable}-return-codegen.vl`, `infer-struct-union-return-needs-annotation.vl`, `annotated-{union,nullable}-return.vl`)
- **A-nullable-bool `boolean | null` niche value type, end to end** — the `boolean | null` nullable rides a NICHE in the i32 cell (0 false / 1 true / 2 = the `null` sentinel — no box, no ref), and it now works as a first-class value type across param / let-assignment / return / call, not just in the narrow `.pop()` / optional-chain contexts it was stuck in. A `null` flowing into any of those positions emits the sentinel `2` (`pendingNulBool` seeded at the call-arg boundary `cParamNulBool`, the `x = null` assignment, and the return site `retNulBool`); a boolean stays `0`/`1`; the wasm result/param/local valtype is plain i32 (`vtKindOfType` → 0). The caller side resolves niche-ness through calls and un-annotated bindings (`exprNulBool` now recognizes a `: boolean | null` call and a `const r = f()` bound to one, via `letNulBool`), so `f(x) != null` is the sentinel compare (`== 2`) and the narrowed `if r != null { … r … }` reads the boolean. Before, every one of these failed `emitProgram: bare null needs a struct-typed context` (or rejected the param outright). The inferred (un-annotated) `boolean | null` return still hits the robustness floor (`annotate the return type` — the annotation now lowers); `??` over a niche is a follow-up. First of the nullable-value-type arc (`string | null`, `??`-over-niche, `if/else`-null-tail to follow — `docs/vl-tech-debt.md`). (`tests/cases/functions/nullable-bool-{return,param-let}.vl`)
- **A-nullable-string `string | null` value type, end to end** — `string | null` is the nullable-REF value type: `(ref null $aTypeIdx)` over the raw string array (kind 16), so `null` is `ref.null` and `!= null` is `ref.is_null` — distinct from the `boolean | null` i32 sentinel (PR1). It now works as a first-class value type across param / let-assignment / return / call / module-global, where before every position failed `bare null needs a struct-typed context` or rejected the param. A `null` emits `ref.null $aTypeIdx` (`pendingNulString` seeded at the call-arg boundary `cParamNulString`, the `x = null` assignment, the return site `retNulString`, and the let/global init); the valtype is `(ref null $aTypeIdx)` (`vtKindOfType` → 16, added to BOTH `fbValtype` and the local/global cell kinds — the original bug was a kind-16 case put in `fbValtypeNullable` instead of `fbValtype`, so the param ABI fell through to i32). The caller resolves niche-ness through calls and un-annotated bindings (`exprNullableString`/`letNulString`/`fnRetNulStr`), so `f(x) != null` and a `const r = f()` global both narrow correctly. The narrowed string is then read AS a value — `.length` (via `array.len`, which accepts the nullable ref), `==`, `+` concat, and return-as-`string` — by recovering the non-null ref (`ref.as_non_null`) at every value read, while the `!= null` check reads the RAW nullable ref (`pendingRawNullRead`) so `ref.is_null` stays meaningful. Two general fixes fell out: `retNulRefFlag` excludes a `string` base (it is the array, never a struct, so `string | null` is not mis-claimed as a nullable struct), and `exprIsStrEq` skips a `null` operand (a `== null` is a null-check, not element equality). Second of the nullable-value-type arc; `??`-over-niche and the `if/else`-null-tail follow (`docs/vl-tech-debt.md`). (`tests/cases/functions/nullable-string-{return,param-global}.vl`)
- **A-nullable-coalesce `??` over the nullable VALUE types** — null-coalescing now works over `boolean | null`, `string | null`, and `i32 | null` bindings (PR1/PR2's value types), not just map gets / list `.pop()`. Each rep lowers per its representation: `boolean | null` (the i32 sentinel) is a `select` on `x == 2` (the `null` sentinel), reusing the boolean-`.pop()` niche shape for any nullable-bool source (`exprNulBool`); `string | null` (the nullable ref) is a value-typed `if` on `ref.is_null` whose present arm `ref.as_non_null`s the string (the absent arm yields the default) — read RAW (`pendingRawNullRead`) so the test stays meaningful; `i32 | null` (the value-union box) already rode the box-tag path. `exprString` now recognizes a `string | null` `??` result, so `(r ?? "default").length` / `+` work. Scoped to an Ident/field LHS (a re-readable place); the non-Ident form `f() ?? d` needs a scratch local to evaluate the call once — tracked follow-up, with `const r = f(); r ?? d` as the workaround. Third of the nullable-value-type arc. (`tests/cases/functions/null-coalesce-niche.vl`)
- **A-coalesce-call `f(x) ?? d` — a CALL-LHS null-coalesce** — the non-Ident `??` the A-nullable-coalesce entry flagged as a follow-up: `f(x) ?? d` where `f` returns a value union with `null` (`i32 | null`, `i64 | null`, `f64 | null`) now lowers, where before it failed `emitProgram: \`??\` is only supported on a map index get`. The ident/field forms RE-READ their place in the non-null arm; a call cannot (effects + cost), so its value-union box is STASHED once in a new 1-slot `(ref $uBoxIdx)` scratch frame (`fnHasCoalesceCall` reserves it via the same scan→reserve→locals-vec plumbing as the `arrNew` frame, appended LAST so layouts without it stay byte-identical), then the tag test + unbox read that single evaluation. One slot suffices across nesting (`f() ?? (g() ?? d)`): each `??` sets-tests-uses within its own evaluation and the null arm never re-reads the box. Works in print / argument / binding / nested-default positions. Also fixes a PRE-EXISTING gap shared with the ident form: a value-union `??` is now type-classified by its unboxed atom (`coalesceAtomKind` feeds `exprIsI64`/`exprIsF64`/`exprIsF32`), so `print`-routing / numeric-context coercion are correct — `print(<i64|null> ?? d)` previously emitted a type-invalid module (i32 import over an i64 value) for BOTH ident and call. A `string | null` / `boolean | null` (niche) or a multi-member result (still a box, not one scalar) stays deferred — `const r = f(); r ?? d` remains the workaround there. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/coalesce-call-lhs.vl`)
- **A-coalesce-call-niche `f() ?? d` over a NICHE-returning call** — closes A-coalesce-call's deferred niches: `f() ?? d` where `f` returns `string | null` (kind 16) or `boolean | null` (the i32 sentinel) now lowers, where before it failed `emitProgram: \`??\` is only supported on a map index get`. Same stash-the-single-evaluation shape as the box form (a call can't be re-read), but the niches need their own rep, so the call-`??` frame now carries THREE slots instead of one — the `(ref $uBoxIdx)` box, a `(ref null $aTypeIdx)` string slot, an i32 bool slot — all appended LAST so layouts without a call-`??` stay byte-identical. A `string | null` call stashes its nullable ref then a value-typed `if` on `ref.is_null` selects the default or `ref.as_non_null`s the recovery; a `boolean | null` call stashes its i32 then a `select` on the sentinel-2 compare — the call analogues of the existing ident niche paths. Nested chains (`f() ?? g() ?? d`, left-associative) work: `emitExpr` already re-associates `(a ?? b) ?? d` → `a ?? (b ?? d)`, and the print-routing classifiers (`exprNullableString` / `exprNulBool`) now see through a `??` LHS — a chained `a ?? b` over a niche is itself that niche when the default is also nullable — so the result type stays consistent with the emitted rep (without it, print mis-routed the nested string `??` to the i32 import and emitted an invalid module). Works in print / argument / binding positions, and the recovered string is non-null (usable in `+` / `.length`). Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/coalesce-call-niche.vl`)
- **A-capture-narrow — a CAPTURED binding is now read at the narrowing the closure was WRITTEN inside (D1d)** — `function p(u: string | i32) { if u is string { function g() { print("C:" + u) } g() } }` was `vl check`-clean invalid wasm, and the nullable-box and struct-union spellings failed alongside it. **The filed mechanism was half right and overturned on the load-bearing half.** Its first clause — *"the capture FRAME is typed from the DECLARATION"* — is true and **not a defect**: `captureValKind` types the env field from the declaration, the declaration holds the box, and the WAT confirms it (`(struct (field (ref 0)))`, `local.get; struct.new`). The second clause is **backwards**: the body read assumed the DECLARED rep while every consumer around it expected the narrowed one, so the read had **no rep at all** — the same shape as A-litunion-narrowed-read, where an `is` test emitted a correct ladder and only the read was wrong. The narrowing stack is per-function-body and a lifted body is emitted in its own `emitFuncCode` pass, so inside the callee the narrowing queries answered *"not narrowed"* even though the closure is lexically inside the guard and the checker narrows there; the raw `(ref $uBox)` then reached a slot expecting the arm's rep. **`emitUnionBoxPush`'s intended floor (*"a clean reject, not invalid wasm"*) was DEAD CODE**, shadowed by `emitIdentNode` reading the env and returning before any arm could call it — which is why the comment's promise never fired. Three new homes, each delegating rather than duplicating: **`capNarrowSeed`** replays the declaration-site narrowing so every *existing* narrowing query answers for a capture with no second rep ladder (read by `computeRetInference` and `emitFuncCode`, one table so they cannot drift); **`capturedIsUnionBox`** delegates to `captureValKind` so the unbox gate and the env field cannot disagree; and `emitUnionBoxPush` gains a capture leg, making it the single box-fetch for every storage class. **A second, independent root had to be found by probing the table**: a *parameter*'s union-ness is answerable from the arena but a function-scope **local**'s is not — it resolves through `declaredUnion`, which reads the emit-time local table `buildLocals` fills one function at a time, so `emitFuncCode` re-banks its own frame right after it. A third defect fell out and is fixed: `setNarrowFromCond` runs classifiers that ask for capture lists, which before `capCacheBuild` recompute through `capScan` — unbounded mutual recursion, `wasm trap: call stack exhausted` **inside the compiler**, on a program as small as a captured `const u = "hi"` under `if u is string`. 661 cells over 26 reps × 6 capture routes × 7 narrowing forms × 8 read sites, every cell written **twice** (the direct read is the oracle) and graded on **both** runtime inputs: correct **270 → 467**, check-clean invalid wasm **185 → 31**, loud emit reject 111 → 68, silently wrong **0 → 0**, program traps **0 → 0**, **0 regressions**; `invalid_wasm→correct` 154 and `emit_reject→correct` 43. The in-callee null-comparison class was **fixed rather than floored** — 48 → **104 of 104** across `!= null`, `== null`-else, `&&`, `while` and `match` over four boxed scalar reps and a value union. Capture ROUTES are perfectly uniform (39/8/5 in all five, before and after), so the split-decision-site pattern that bit the for-in loop-var work does **not** apply here; **binding form** is the axis that still splits (param 110/130 with zero invalid wasm, local 85/130 with 25). **Newly accepted: 43 cells**, all struct-union and in-callee-narrowed shapes that master loud-rejected, each producing the oracle's value on every runtime input; **newly rejected: empty**. Write-after-capture is sound with no hole: over 160 cells every guard-*falsifying* write is a loud check reject at all three positions — 16/16 before the closure is created, 16/16 after it is created, 16/16 inside its body — so the scope-wide "the narrowed type is what the binding accepts" rule carries to captures. Sabotage was predicted before running and graded on the run value: killing the declaration-site replay moves **exactly 141** cells and lands the four sub-grids exactly on master's columns while touching **0** in the in-callee grid; killing the box-fetch leg moves 178 (19 short of prediction — the survivors are **exactly** the struct-union cells, whose unbox is a different receiver path); killing the per-frame re-bank moves 18 (24 under — a null-*comparison* guard resolves a local through `valueUnionNullCmpName` rather than `declaredUnion`, so only the `is` spellings need the emit-time table). **0 cells were better under any sabotage than in the fix.** Byte-identity holds where it can (the branch-point compiler sources compile byte-identically under master's compiler, the branch point's and this one; the five plain-scalar controls are byte-identical, never narrowed so never seeded) and is meaningless where the 197 fixed cells differ by construction, a box and an unboxed ref being different heap types. **Measured, diagnosed and NOT fixed (32 cells)**: an un-annotated local capture whose initialiser is a niche-returning call types its env field **i32** and is check-clean invalid wasm in the *enclosing* frame, because `captureValKind`'s un-annotated arm uses `letInitCellKind`, a **shortened mirror** of `collectLocals`' ladder missing all eleven niche arms — the two exist separately only because `emit_classify` cannot import `emit_collect` — and the checker then emits `redundant type annotation` on the very annotation that fixes it, so the compiler advises deleting the load-bearing annotation. Also measured and unfixed: a **string-VALUED** captured map makes the compiler's own `array.get` go out of bounds with no diagnostic and no module (4 cells; the axis is the VALUE type, not the key — `{[string]: string}` and `{[i32]: string}` both trap while `{[string]: i32}` and `{[i32]: i32}` are correct), and four R10 niches remain loud emit rejects at both binding forms. (`tests/cases/functions/capture-narrowed-value-union.vl`, `capture-narrowed-nullable-scalar.vl`, `capture-narrowed-local-value-union.vl`, `tests/cases/unions/capture-narrowed-struct-union.vl`, `tests/cases/soundness/capture-narrowed-write-rejected.vl`)
- **A-forin-map-recv — a map for-in evaluated its receiver TWICE and walked the SECOND result** — `for k in mk().keys()` printed the callee's side-effect marker twice, value-correct and `vl check`-clean, and the loop's tombstone filter and its walked list came from **different evaluations** of the receiver. `emitForInStmt` does a deliberate two-pass lowering — stash the map ref so the loop can consult `live[i]` and skip tombstones, then ask `emitForInList` for the ordered keys/vals to walk — and the two-pass shape is genuinely needed; the second pass was an **accidental re-emission**, because `emitForInList` carried three hand-copied `Call`->`Member`->`memProp` ladders that each called `emitExpr` on the receiver *again* to reach the field. `pushMapRecvRef` is now the one home for map-receiver evaluation, emitting the receiver exactly once and carrying the nullable recover; `emitForInList` reads the stash, dropping 38 lines of ladder to 6, and the keys-vs-vals selection collapses into the single classifier `forInMapWalksVals`. **No slot layout moved** — the let cursor advances only on let/for statements, never on an expression emission, and all five scratch-frame-reservation cases pass with one 42 bytes smaller. **The D2/D5 grouping HELD**: the same site's other omission is that the sibling stash `emitMapRecvKey` has always carried the nullable-map `ref.as_non_null` while the for-in copy never did, so a narrowed `{...} | null` produced check-clean invalid wasm. Master's own message localises it — `local.set[0] expected (ref 4), found (ref null 4)` — because WasmGC `struct.get` *accepts* a nullable ref, so the defect lived entirely at the `local.set`, which is exactly why `.size` (same recover, different path) was always fine. Grid over 570 cells (10 receiver forms x 5 views, 14 value reps x 2 key types, 4 reps x 3 nullable states, 64 list controls, 6 identity cells), each graded on the run value **and** the evaluation count at both a populated and an empty map: correct **262 -> 526**, **WRONG EVALUATION COUNT 186 -> 0**, check-clean invalid wasm **72 -> 0**, check-clean silently wrong **6 -> 0**, loud verdicts unmoved at 10 check / 30 emit. **The wrong count is a wrong VALUE, not merely a wasted call**: the required identity cell had master print `second` where `first` was correct, a shrinking factory print `z` instead of `p,q` (loop bound taken from the second map), and a growing factory print `p` instead of `z` (bound from one map, `live[]` from the other, silently dropping an entry). Widened beyond the filing: the **bare** `for k in <recv>` map iterable has the identical defect (68 of 68 cells) — a third view — as do closure-call, lambda-call, field-read and index-into-list-of-maps receivers. The D5 widening ships rather than a loud floor: 72 previously-invalid-wasm cells now correct on **every** runtime input (40/40 populated, 40/40 empty, 40/40 null), while an **un-narrowed** nullable receiver keeps its reject byte-for-byte. Sabotage predicted and measured 3 of 3 exactly, including 448 of 454 count-asserting verdicts moving when the expected marker count is flipped globally — the 6 wrong-value cells correctly do not move, because value outranks count — and each log fixture reddens under a sabotage of a leg it names while staying green under another, which is what located D5 at the `local.set` rather than the `struct.get`. Corpus: 1,442 of 1,481 modules byte-identical, 39 changed with **26 smaller and 0 larger** (net -187 bytes) and 13 same-size-different-index; all 244 control cells byte-identical; the compiler itself 331 bytes smaller. **Why it hid**: every map for-in in the corpus binds its receiver to a local first, and the one case that iterates a call result does so over a **list**, which takes the single-evaluation path anyway — named in the fixture comments. Also **corrected** a filed claim: an i32-keyed map does **not** trap the compiler (140 i32-keyed cells double-evaluate exactly like string-keyed). Found and **not** fixed, a distinct compiler trap: `const fq: () => i32[] = () => { return [] }` — an empty array literal returned from an annotated lambda — dies at `vl check` with a wasm backtrace, **no diagnostic and no module written**, identical on master. (`tests/cases/maps/forin-view-receiver-evaluated-once.vl`, `forin-view-receiver-identity.vl`, `forin-narrowed-nullable-map-views.vl`, `error-forin-unnarrowed-nullable-map.vl`)
- **A-alias-write-perf — the aliased-write predicate was quadratic on TWO axes, and the axis the filing named was not the one the filing measured** — a 2,000-call guarded callee took **810 ms** to check and now takes **10 ms**. I filed this as *"`findFnDeclIn` is an uncached subtree walk, therefore quadratic"*, having measured +15 / +61 / +227 ms at 750 / 1,500 / 3,000 guarded property paths. The magnitude was right and **the mechanism was wrong**: gutting `findFnDeclIn` to `return -1` outright changes that workload by ~2%. The cost was the **write-effect summary BANK** — `fnWriteEffects` / `fnWriteFreePaths` scanning parallel `weKeys`/`weVals` string arrays that grow one entry per summarised (callee, parameter), so n guarded paths against n *distinct* callees is n lookups over a 2n array. The experiment that separates them holds every axis fixed but the suspected one: **3,000 guards against ONE shared callee costs 60 ms; 3,000 guards against 3,000 DISTINCT callees costs 380 ms** — same guard count, same call count, only the bank differs — and the shared-callee case is **unchanged by the fix (1.0x)**, which is what proves the walk was not the cost there. `findFnDeclIn` *is* quadratic, on an axis the filed input could not reach: the walk runs once per call **inside the body being summarised**, and an intrinsic like `print` asks a name no root holds, so one summary is quadratic in the number of calls that body makes — invisible when every callee body is one line. Both are fixed: the bank becomes a single `weSums` map (with presence asked by `has`, since `""` legitimately means "writes nothing"), and the walk is memoised on **`(subtree root, name)`** including negative answers, because that `-1` is the answer every intrinsic call needs. **The walk is scope-sensitive and BOTH filed candidates FAIL OPEN — measured, not argued**: memoising on the name alone answers the second of two same-named nested declarations with the first one's summary, and a flat module-wide map additionally answers a function *value*'s call with an unrelated nested declaration, destroying the opaque fallback that makes the whole predicate safe. Both were **5–9% faster** and unsound, so exact keying ships; `(root, name)` cannot err, the walk being a pure function of `(P.nodes, root, name)` in a file that never pushes nodes. Measured at load 4.3, best of 5: guard-axis **380 → 120 ms (3.2x)**, call-axis **60 → 10 ms** at 500 calls and **810 → 10 ms (81x)** at 2,000, with the fixed total scaling **2.13 / 2.03 / 2.05 / 2.11x per doubling across a 16x input range** — linear, where the tip ran 2.23 / 2.29 / 2.91x. The whole-compiler workload is a reported **null result** (1,308 vs 1,300 ms, fully overlapping): it declares one type and its callee bodies are ordinary, so it cannot exercise either addition — **a null result on a workload that does not exercise the addition is not evidence**, which is why both stress inputs had to be constructed. Corpus **1,935 of 1,935 modules byte-identical**, both seed-ladder legs reaching the same fixpoint bytes, and #1444's four fixtures keep their exact verdicts with its accept-control set still running `2 5 2 4` — the read-only-callee, different-field, recursive-read-only, `print`-intrinsic and read-before-write cells that an over-invalidating cache would break. The benchmark's own sabotage proof: gating the memo off and restoring the retired bank cost, both semantics-preserving, moves f2000 by **89x** while startup does not move at all. **Both fixtures are pure INVARIANT pins and labelled as such**, because a caching change can only be pinned by a program whose verdict a broken cache flips — and such a program already has the right verdict on master; their real oracle is that each fails under a REJECTED candidate. (`tests/cases/soundness/alias-write-nested-callee-scope-sensitive.vl`, `alias-write-opaque-value-callee-name-collision.vl`)
- **A-alias-write-invalidation — a property-path narrowing now RETIRES when a call can write the field through an alias** — `if o.v != null { clr(o)   print(o.v.length) }`, where `clr` assigns `o.v = null` through its parameter, was `vl check`-clean and then **trapped**; over a BOXED field rep it instead printed a **silently wrong value** (`null` for a `boolean | null`). **The filed premise — "the checker already has the right falsifying-write rule, it just cannot see an aliased write" — is REFUTED by one probe**: `if o.v != null { o.v = null }` with **no read at all** rejects `cannot assign null to string`, because the assignment's TARGET type is read out of the narrowing overlay, not because any soundness rule fired; the same write outside the guard is accepted, proving the storage admits null. So nothing ever retired a path narrowing, the direct write was blocked by a type-identity accident, and invalidation had to be added from scratch rather than extended. The mechanism: a guard pushes `(key, narrowedTy)` onto the `npKeys`/`npTys` overlay and `popScope` unwinds it, but between push and pop nothing removed, shadowed or widened an entry; a third parallel array `npInvBy` now records the invalidating call's node index, asked once at `checkNodeReal`'s `Call` arm **after** the arguments are checked, and written **in place** rather than pushed — a shadowing push would unwind with whatever inner block held the call and resurrect the narrowing for the statements after it (`if o.v != null { if c { clr(o) } print(o.v.length) }` must stay rejected). **`callInvalidatesNarrowedPath(callIx, key)` is the one home**, a pure predicate with three ordered legs: an INTRINSIC (`print`, a string/array/map/set method) never invalidates — the leg that makes the rule usable at all; an OPAQUE callee (first-class or indirect, a struct field holding a function, an unresolved member receiver, an escaping parameter, an unfindable nested declaration) invalidates **every** live narrowing whether or not it receives the object; and a statically known body consults a free-variable write summary then a per-`(parameter, field-sub-path)` summary, where a written sub-path kills the key when it equals or prefixes the rest. **The BROAD rule was built and REFUSED on measurement**: both rules fix the identical **242 unsound cells**, and the entire difference is collateral — broad newly rejects **94 of 106 correct grid cells** and **2 of 1908 corpus files**, both of them correct code and both blaming `print` for a write it cannot perform. The self-compile did not discriminate (110k lines build clean under both), so the discriminating population had to be constructed. A third variant treating recursive cycles as opaque costs 18 correct cells; the shipped rule takes the **least fixpoint** instead (cutting a may-write cycle at ⊥ and iterating; for the queried key one pass reaches it, since `X = base ∪ X` has least solution `base`), with `weCache` refusing to cache a summary computed while a cycle was cut, and `ALIAS_CYCLE_OPAQUE` keeping the conservative reading one edit away. Grid, 348 cells over 9 field reps × 12 mutation routes × 5 guard forms × 3 path depths × 3 call positions × **both runtime inputs**: all **242 REJECT-intent cells become loud check rejects**, and **0 of 106 correct-intent cells move**. The rep axis is emphatically NOT flat and keeping TRAP and WRONG-VALUE apart is what showed it: the two BOXED reps (`boolean | null`, `K | null`) produced **6 silently-wrong values each** where the five scalar/ref reps **trapped 7 each** and the two composite reps loud-rejected at emit — merging the columns would have hidden the boxed column entirely. Per-mechanism sabotage partitions the fix exactly: the parameter channel 110 cells, the free-variable channel 18 and **nothing else**, the opaque fallback 76 — 110 + 18 + 76 = 204, the total moved. Corpus verdicts **0 of 1908 changed** and **0 bytes moved** across 1576 byte-compared modules. The diagnostic is anchored at the READ and names the path, the callee and the invalidating line: `'o.v' was narrowed by a guard, but the call to 'clr' on line 21 can write it — re-test 'o.v' after the call`. **Measured and NOT fixed**: a `const p = o` alias (`clr(p)`) still leaves `o.v` narrowed — invalidation is keyed on the syntactic argument and closing it needs a may-alias relation; `if o.v != null { print(o.v.length); o.v = null }` is a pre-existing FALSE reject from the same overlay leak as the refuted premise (accepting it would widen the language, the opposite axis from this change); and a global `let g: string | null` assigned `null` inside any function emits invalid wasm with **no guard anywhere**, a separate pre-existing defect found incidentally. **This does NOT by itself unblock the `while`-body path gate**: invalidation is a STATEMENT-ORDER rule and deliberately accepts read-then-call, which is right for `if` and unsound for a loop body that re-executes; the missing half is a back-edge pre-pass over the body's `Call` nodes, for which `callInvalidatesNarrowedPath` is already the right shape. (`tests/cases/soundness/alias-write-through-param-retires-path-narrowing.vl`, `alias-write-boxed-rep-was-a-wrong-value.vl`, `alias-write-through-capture-retires-path-narrowing.vl`, `alias-write-narrowing-boundary-sound.vl`)
- **A-narrowed-write-retires — a narrowing overlay leaked into ASSIGNMENT TARGET types; the write is now ACCEPTED and RETIRES the narrowing** — `while q != null { n = n + q   q = null }` over `q: i32 | null` was `cannot assign null to i32`, which killed the natural way to walk a nullable chain to exhaustion, and the message is the tell: the declared type DOES admit null, and the narrowed type was being read as the assignment's TARGET type.
