Same root in the property-path spelling — `if o.v != null { print(o.v.length)   o.v = null }` was `cannot assign null to string`, the residue #1444 filed as its item (d). **Both are ONE root and one function owns it**: `writeStorageTy` answers "what can this place HOLD", from the narrowing bank for a bare name (the guard's shadowing `declare` hid the declaration in an outer scope) and from the receiver's own field type for a path; it is -1 for every place no narrowing overlays, so the changed set is exactly the writes a narrowing shrank. `lt` STAYS the flow type — it is what `seedExpected` types the right-hand side with and what `nodeTyIxOf` hands the emitter for the target node — and the substitution is gated on a null-bearing right-hand side, which is what keeps every currently-accepted assignment on its exact coercion path. **`writeRetiresNarrowing(tgtKey, rt, key, npTy)` is the one home for "does this write retire this narrowing"**, three ordered legs: PLACE (the write must name the narrowed place or a PREFIX of it — `o.v = …` also kills `o.v.w`; an unkeyable `xs[i] = …` names no place and no narrowing can key one either), VALUE for an exact match (`if q != null { q = 5   use(q) }` keeps reading `q` non-null, so only a write the narrowing does NOT admit retires it), and a PREFIX match retiring unconditionally — that last leg is not optional, because the member arm answers from the overlay BEFORE it checks the receiver, so `if o != null && o.v != null { o = null   use(o.v) }` reads `o.v` off a null receiver without it. Retirement is written IN PLACE, never pushed: `npInvBy` for a path and the declared type back into the scope THE GUARD PUSHED for a bare name, because a shadowing push unwinds with whatever inner block held the write and resurrects the narrowing (`if q != null { if c { q = null }   use(q) }` must stay rejected). **The retired place is UNREADABLE for the rest of the guarded region, and that is an emitter constraint rather than conservatism**: `narrowStripNullOnly` (emit_state.vl) is documented as resting on "writing null back inside a null guard is impossible", so a read after such a write lowers against the region's stale UNBOXED rep — typing it `i32?` instead gives a check-clean module the engine rejects with `expected (ref null $type), found i32`. That shape is REACHABLE ON MASTER with no guard at all, through the assignment-narrowing kind (`let x: i32 | null = null` / `x = 5` / `x = null` / `print(x ?? 0)`), so it is a separate pre-existing root and only the new guard-kind reads are refused (`nameRetiredErr`), matching what `pathInvalidatedErr` has always done for a path. **The LOOP decision is a RE-EXECUTION BARRIER, not a back-edge pre-pass, and cells decide it**: a loop's own condition is re-tested on every back edge, so `while q != null { use(q)   q = null }` is sound with no flow analysis, while a guard from an enclosing `if` gets no re-test and `if q != null { while c { use(q)   q = null } }` reads a null on iteration two. `pushReBarrier` marks the scope depth and overlay height at entry to every loop body; a narrowing at or above the mark is per-iteration, one below it keeps master's reject with a diagnostic that names the loop. The barrier is applied to GUARD-kind narrowings only: the assignment kind's null write is accepted on master and `let x: i32|null = null` / `x = 5` / `while c { use(x)   x = null }` is a CORRECT program for a single-iteration loop, so barring it would newly reject working code. **Two pre-existing defects had to go with it, both found by the grid's own axes.** `isConstName` stops at the first scope that BINDS the name and asks that frame alone — the shadowing rule a `let` over an outer `const` needs — and a narrowing shadow is a binding with no const-map entry, so every guard silently hid the const reject: `const q = mk(true)` / `if q != null { q = 5 }` COMPILED AND RAN on master, and only the null spelling was stopped, by the accident this change removes. The narrowing now carries constness across with it. And the narrowing bank needed a scope DEPTH: `let x: i32 | null = null` / `x = 5` / `{ let x = "s"   x = null }` read the outer bank entry for the inner binding, put null into a `string` and overwrote its type with `i32?`. Grid, **516 cells over 11 reps x 8 binding forms x 8 write positions x 5 guard forms x 2 constructs x 2 read kinds x BOTH runtime inputs**: correct **0 → 108**, loud check reject **516 → 408**, and **0** in each of the other seven columns on both sides — no newly-rejected cell, no unsound acceptance. The 8 LEGAL cells that stay rejected are `{field,nested} x {i32,string} x while`, the pre-existing exclusion of property paths from a `while` body. **Sabotage partitions it exactly**: killing `writeStorageTy` reproduces master's grid CELL FOR CELL (0 of 516 moved), and its two legs split the change with nothing left over — bare name 100, property path 8, 100 + 8 = 108. Dropping the retirement moves **264** cells out of the reject column — **104 TRAP, 20 loud emit reject, 18 print extra lines** (the two BOXED reps, where `K | null` reads the null sentinel back as the atom `"b"` while the five scalar reps trap) **and 122 score as CORRECT purely because their runtime input is null and the body never runs**, which is why intent is a property of the program here and not of the run. Corpus A/B over 1935 files: **0 verdict changes, 0 diagnostic changes, and 0 bytes moved across 1592 byte-compared modules** — this is a checker-side acceptance change, so byte-identity is the expected result and it holds. Distinct names were the control: renaming either loop var made every spelling correct on master. (3) A nullable NUMERIC litunion (`1 | 2 | null`) was claimed by neither litunion rule, so `vtKindOfType` defaulted it to `"i32"` for the param/return/functype cell while the `let` ladder's NAME test built a box — ONE type, TWO reps; `nulNumLitUnionBaseName` is now its single home, beside its string twin. Separately `nullCmpRefOf` becomes the one home for "which operand of `== null` is the non-null side", a peel the three narrowing selectors had each hand-copied, and both `setNarrowFromCond` and `setNarrowFromCondElse` gain the MEMBER channel the `is` arm already had — that one is the `w.f != null` shape, which tested the box tag CORRECTLY and then read the box RAW. Three grids, 988 cells over 13 nullable reps: **520-cell bare-null grid 444 → 460 correct with check-clean invalid wasm 10 → 0**; **208-cell container grid 144 → 172, loud 64 → 36**; a **260-cell grid added after a fixture found the `== null` spelling whose narrowed arm is the ELSE, 222 → 230, invalid wasm 8 → 0**. +52 forward, **0 backward, 0 cells into either silent class**, and the two silent columns are sabotage-proved live (a wrong box null-tag and a wrong `boolean|null` sentinel put 27 of 520 and 8 of 208 into SILENT_WRONG). Both down-moves are recorded rather than shipped: fix 1 briefly turned 8 name-collision cells LOUD → invalid wasm and fix 2 closed them in the same commit (along with the pre-existing LOUD instance, reachable with no map at all), and a counterfactual boxing `return null` over the numeric litunion was BUILT, measured and REVERTED because the call-result classifier (`computeRetInference`/`criClassify`) is a THIRD name-keyed home, so the receiving `const r = f(b)` stayed an i32 — that rep keeps its loud floor, with the reason recorded at the gate. Byte-identity across reps does not apply and is not claimed (a box, a `(ref null $array)` niche and an i32 sentinel are different heap types); the bootstrap identity does and holds. Residue, stated: the `?? null` column (44 + 22 cells) is `emitCoalesce`, measured and untouched; `mapkeys` over the mono-i32-map niches is an independent map-INDEX defect; `(fn | null)` values need `nulclosure` in two more kind whitelists; `{[string]: i32[] | null}` values and `{[string]: i32} | null` as a struct field remain `mvValKindOfName`'s documented by-design `-3` rejects. (`tests/cases/maps/forin-values-nullable-and-box-value-loop-var.vl`, `tests/cases/loops/forin-same-named-loop-vars-union-box.vl`, `tests/cases/literal-unions/nullable-numeric-litunion-param.vl`, `tests/cases/objects/union-field-null-compare-narrows-the-path.vl`)
- **A-litunion-is-narrowed-read — the `is` TEST over a nullable numeric litunion was already correct and the narrowed READ had no rep at all** — the filed diagnosis ("the `is` path is a third consumer never wired to `nulNumLitUnionBaseName`") was OVERTURNED by disassembly: master emits the right membership ladder (box tag 0, then a per-member payload compare) and then pushes the union BOX into the i32 consumer, so `if p is N { print(p) }` over `p: N | null` (`type N = 1 | 2`) is `vl check`-clean INVALID WASM while `p != null` on the same type is right one line away — because the COMPLEMENT narrowing banks the base scalar's own name (`i32`) and the `is` narrowing banks the tested SPELLING (`N`, or a bare `1`). Four per-rep ladders, each missing exactly one arm, each now reading an existing single home. (1) **`narrowedValueAtomOf`** — "which rep does an `is`-narrowed ident read as" — had only the STRING litunion leg (`nameIsLitUnionType` is `litKind == "str"` only); it now reads `numLitUnionBaseName` off the arena TYPE the same push banked, and separately answers for a TRANSPARENT alias of a scalar atom (`type N = i32`, `if p is N`) from that type's `TyPrim.primName` — the string `unMemAtomKind` itself hands `valueAtomKind`, so the two legs cannot disagree about which atom an arm is. (2) **`nodeTyIsUnionAlias`** contradicted `vtKindOfType` about ONE type: `isValueUnionBox` rejects a `TyNullable` over a homogeneous litunion, so the predicate said "not a union" for a slot that already had the box valtype — and THREE consumers needed that one answer (`paramUnion`, hence `exprUnion`, hence the membership arm, which loud-rejected at a PARAM while the `let` spelling compiled; `retAnnKindChain`, hence the stored `fRetKind`, hence the CALL-RESULT classifier; and `emitReturnValue`/`emitNullReturn`, hence `return null`). Moving the shared predicate is what makes all three agree, which is why #1439's narrower counterfactual at `retUNm` alone measured WORSE and was declined: it left the receiving `const r = mk(b)` an i32. (3) **`tyKindOf`** — "THE single typed-IR → emitter-kind mapping" — answered -1 for a numeric literal type or union, so every fast path over it (`exprIsF64`, `exprIsI64`, `exprString`, the list classifiers) fell back to a syntactic derivation with no litunion case either: a field `W | null` over `type W = 1.5 | 2.5` unboxed to an f64 and `print` routed it to `__print_i32__`. It now resolves through `numLitUnionBaseName` and then its OWN `TyPrim` arm; string literals still answer -1, which is required (a string litunion member is an interned i32 atom). (4) **`repTyScalarMask`'s `TyLit` arm** assumed a literal type's inhabitants are always SPELLED in source — false for the one inhabitant that is not a member, `null` — so `type W = 1.5 | 2.5` used only as `g(null)` declared no `__print_f64__` import while the narrowed read printed an f64; it descends through `litBaseTy`. THREE grids, 2776 cells, every cell graded on the RUN VALUE with both a present and a `null` runtime input: **2016-cell rep × spelling × storage-position × test-form grid 914 → 1422 correct, check-clean invalid wasm 562 → 126**; **480-cell RETURN-axis grid 120 → 480 correct, invalid wasm 72 → 0, loud 288 → 0**; **280-cell grid over the ELEVEN nullable niches 240 → 252, invalid wasm 7 → 0**. +1000 forward, **0 backward and 0 cells into either silent class**, and both silent columns are sabotage-proved live from this same fix: comparing every member against the LAST member's literal puts 84 of 2016 and 108 of 480 into SILENT_WRONG, and claiming the STRING rep for a numeric arm puts 200 of 2016 and 108 of 480 into TRAP. Per-mechanism sabotage, one config per leg: 400/216 cells redden with leg 1 off, 120/288 with leg 2, 84/0 with leg 4, 24/24 with leg 3. **365 previously-REJECTED programs are newly ACCEPTED** (72 + 288 + 5 across the three grids — the PARAM and RETURN positions) and all 365 print the correct value on their runtime input; the boundary that stays loud is `is` on a BARE CALL RESULT, which a membership ladder cannot re-read, pinned as a reject. Byte identity: all **1575** pre-existing corpus programs that build under both compilers build BYTE-IDENTICALLY, 0 of them regressed to a failure, and the bootstrap fixpoint from master's published seed holds byte-for-byte. Residue, stated: a CAPTURED nullable numeric litunion is still invalid wasm (the documented un-wired `emitCapturedRead` narrowed-atom path), the `let` position with a `null` initialiser plus a later `p = null` reassignment is an independent pre-existing hole, `match` over a union with literal members is a standing checker reject, and `print` of a MIXED litunion (`1 | "a"`) remains the documented "narrow it first" reject. (`tests/cases/literal-unions/is-nullable-numeric-litunion-narrowed-read.vl`, `tests/cases/literal-unions/is-nullable-numeric-litunion-is-at-param.vl`, `tests/cases/literal-unions/nullable-numeric-litunion-return.vl`, `tests/cases/literal-unions/float-litunion-print-import-through-null-only.vl`, `tests/cases/literal-unions/nullable-numeric-litunion-is-on-call-result-reject.vl`, `tests/cases/unions/is-transparent-scalar-alias-narrowed-read.vl`)
- **A-nullable-if-tail `if`/`else`-expression tail with a `null` branch** — `function f(b): i32 | null { if b { 5 } else { null } }` (the implicit-return form of `return null`) now lowers, where before it failed `emitProgram: unsupported statement in body` even ANNOTATED. Two parts: a trailing `null` is now a tail value (`stmtIsTailValue` gained the `NullLit` case — it was omitted, so the `if` fell to the void-statement path that can't emit a bare `null`); and the three implicit-return tail paths (`emitFuncBody`'s trailing value, `emitStmtTail`, the `if`-tail arms) now route through a shared `emitReturnValue` helper extracted from the `RetStmt` emitter — so a tail `null` / nullable value gets the SAME box / sentinel / `ref.null` seeding as `return null`, per the function's return type. Works for all three nullable reps (i32|null box, string|null ref, boolean|null niche, incl. `else if` chains). The helper takes a `widenNum` flag: explicit `return e` keeps numeric (f32/f64/i64) literal widening, the tail passes `false` (a tail value's type already matches the return, and forcing the float-widen mis-converts an already-f64 closure `call_ref` the classifier doesn't recognize — surfaced by routing the tail through the shared path). Native fixpoint byte-identical (the `RetStmt` refactor is behavior-preserving). Fourth of the nullable-value-type arc. (`tests/cases/functions/if-else-null-tail.vl`)
- **A-missing-return Fall-through totality — implicit `null`, not a trap** — a function whose body can fall off the end without yielding a value implicitly produces `null` there. That is sound exactly when `null` is part of the return type, so the rule is assignability-based, not a blanket reject: a **non-nullable** value return with a reachable fall-through is an error (`function f(n: i32): i32 { if n > 0 { return 1 } }` — the TS `noImplicitReturns` case, which used to type-check and then TRAP at the wasm result-type fallthrough), while a **nullable** return lowers the fall-through AS that null. An un-annotated function widens its inferred return to `T | null` on fall-through (host byline: "types hidden by aggressive inference") — `function f(n: i32) { if n > 0 { return 1 } }` infers `i32 | null`, no annotation needed — and a declared `: T | null` may fall through too (previously a false-positive reject). The totality test (`nodeIsTotal`) is the dual of the definite-assignment `daBranchDiverges`: a block is total iff its last statement is — an explicit `return`, a `break`/`continue` (divergence), an `if`/`else` whose BOTH arms are total (`else if` chains recurse), a `while true { … }` (never falls through), or a bare trailing VALUE expression. It is computed while the body scope is still live (a discriminated param/local's type would otherwise be out of scope) and consumed once the return type is finalized. The emitter pads a live fall-through with the return rep's null via `emitNullForRet` (boolean-niche sentinel `2`, string-niche `ref.null $array`, nullable struct `ref.null $S`, value-union null-box) instead of the dead `unreachable` it still uses for a proven-dead non-nullable fall-through. DISCRIMINATING `is` / literal-equality chains stay DEFERRED to the exhaustiveness check. Box-able value types (`i32`/`i64`/`f64`/`f32` and unions) infer un-annotated; a NICHE (`boolean | null` / `string | null`) inferred return is lifted in **A-niche-inference** below. (`tests/cases/soundness/missing-return-{reject,inferred-nullable,nullable-ok,niche-inferred,ok}.vl`)
- **A-niche-inference — un-annotated `boolean | null` / `string | null` returns lower** — an un-annotated function whose returns join to a NICHE nullable now infers and lowers WITHOUT an annotation, closing the floor (`cannot infer a nullable return type — annotate`) the value-union box could already skip. `function f(n: i32) { if n > 0 { return "hi" } }` infers `string | null`; `{ return true }` infers `boolean | null`. The checker stops flooring a nullable whose inner is `boolean`/`string` (a nullable STRUCT ref still floors — it needs the variant-boxed rep). The A20 inferred-return table exports the canonical niche name (`boolean|null` / `string|null`), and the emitter seeds the rep from it by NAME rather than a `fn.fnRet` annotation node: `buildFnMap` sets `fRetNulStr` for `string|null` (driving the kind-16 `ref null $array` result valtype, same as the annotated path; `boolean|null` stays plain i32 — its null is the sentinel `2`), `emitReturnValue` seeds `retNulString`/`retNulBool`, `exprNulBool` classifies a CALL of an inferred niche fn, and `fnRetNullBearing`/`emitNullForRet` emit the niche null on a fall-through. (`tests/cases/soundness/missing-return-niche-inferred.vl`)
- **A-recursive-operator-inference — un-annotated self-recursion through an operator** — an un-annotated DIRECTLY self-recursive function whose recursive call feeds an operator now infers from its base case instead of erroring. `function fib(n: i32) { if n < 2 { return n }  return fib(n - 1) + fib(n - 2) }` used to fail `operator '+' is not defined for i32 | string and i32 | string` and demand a `: i32` annotation. The cause: during cycle inference the recursive call's pending return (`-2`) resolved to `TY_ERR`, and since `assignable(TY_ERR, string)` is vacuously true, `+` took its string-concat form and polluted the inferred return to `i32 | string`. Fix: at the call site, a direct self-recursive call (the callee is the inference-stack top) resolves to the function's PROVISIONAL inferred type — the concrete base-case returns accumulated so far (`inferObserved`) — rather than `TY_ERR`, so `fib(n-1) + fib(n-2)` sees `i32 + i32`. The base case is checked before the recursive tail, so the provisional is already concrete; a recursive call before any base case (`inferObserved < 0`) and MUTUAL recursion (callee ≠ the accumulating function) keep `TY_ERR` and are still settled by the binding-group fixpoint. The base case decides the form — `rep(n) = "" ; "a" + rep(n-1)` stays `string`. (`tests/cases/functions/infer-self-recursive-operator.vl`)
- **A-if-expression-value — `if`/`else` as a value types as its branch join** — an `if … then … else …` (or braced `if { v } else { w }`) used in VALUE position now has the type of the JOIN of its two branch tail values, instead of always `void`. This closes a family of soundness holes where the checker accepted an if-expression-as-value and codegen then crashed with a wasm type mismatch: assigning one to a typed binding (`const x: i32 = if c then 1 else 2`), passing one as an argument (`g(if a > b then a else b)`), and — the headline — un-annotated SELF-RECURSION through a bare-value if-branch (`function fib(n) if n < 2 then n else fib(n-1) + fib(n-2)` and the braced `{ n } else { … }` form), which A-recursive-operator-inference fixed only for the explicit-`return` shape. The checker now threads a return-tail position flag: the function-body tail statement (and, propagated, the tail of each branch of a tail `if`) is a return position, so a base-case branch value seeds the inferred return (`inferObserved`) BEFORE the recursive sibling branch is checked — reaching the #487 provisional-type mechanism through an implicit-return branch. A `Block`'s trailing value type is carried out-of-band (`blockTailTy`) so the `if` can read each branch's value without re-checking it. Seeding is gated to a genuine value-`if` (one WITH an `else`) and a CONCRETE branch value (not `void`/`TY_ERR`), so a void procedure whose tail is a no-else `if` ending in a recursive call still infers `void`, and a base-case-less cycle still defers to the fixpoint. Disagreeing branches join to a value union that narrows with `is`; an else-less `if` as a value stays `void` outside tail position (A-else-less-if-nullable below lifts the tail case). (`tests/cases/functions/{infer-if-expr-recursion,if-expr-value-position}.vl`)
- **A-else-less-if-nullable — an else-less tail `if` returns `T | null`** — `function f(c) { if c then 5 }` now returns `i32 | null` (the false condition is a fall-through with no value — `null`, exactly the #484 function fall-through), where before it was a `void`-typed crash. The then-arm returns its value and the false path is lowered as the rep's null by the function-end padding (`emitNullForRet`), so no `else` arm is synthesized — it reuses #484 wholesale. Covers the value-union box (`i32 | null`), the string niche (`ref.null`), and the boolean niche (sentinel `2`). The checker types an else-less `if` as `thenTy | null` only in RETURN-TAIL position (the `retTailPos` flag from A-if-expression-value); an inline `const x = if c then 5` stays `void`, pending typed-blocktype if-expression lowering. Two emitter subtleties: (1) the else-less tail is routed to `emitIfTail` only at EMIT time (`elseLessNullableTail`, gated on `fnRetNullBearing`) — NOT through `ifTailIsValue`, which feeds the `fRetVoid` fixpoint in `buildFnMap` and so must keep ignoring else-less ifs (a void procedure's `if n>0 { rec(n-1) }` must not look like a value); (2) `fRetVoid` consults the checker's nullable inferred-return name (`inferRetNameOf` with a `null` atom) so it agrees `f` is non-void, while a non-nullable i32-valued tail the emitter can't lower as a value (a Member-target assignment `state.x = …`) still classifies `void`. An annotated `: i32` else-less tail stays a totality error; `: i32 | null` is allowed — same rule as a bare fall-through (#484). (`tests/cases/functions/else-less-if-nullable.vl`)
- **A-typed-if-expr — value-position `if` lowers with the branch-join's scalar rep** — a value-position `if … then … else …` whose branch-join is a NON-i32 SCALAR rep (`f64`/`f32`/`i64`) now lowers with that rep's single-byte blocktype (`0x7c`/`0x7d`/`0x7e`) instead of the hardcoded i32 one, and both arms are emitted COERCED to the join — so `if c then 1 else 2.5` widens its i32 then-arm to f64, and `if c then big else 7` extends the i32 arm to i64. Before, these type-checked (A-if-expression-value gives the if-expr its join type) and then produced a TYPE-INVALID module (an i32 blocktype over f64/i64 arm values, trapping at instantiate). `emitIfExpr` now computes the rep once (`ifExprValtype`, via the same `exprIsF64`/`exprIsF32`/`exprIsI64` classifiers — which gained an `IfStmt` case so the WHOLE expression also classifies correctly for print routing / context coercion) and threads the blocktype byte through the `else if` recursion (one shared rep down the chain) and into each arm's coerced emit (`emitBlockValueAs`). A function-LOCAL binding infers its slot valtype from the if-expr init: `letIsF64` now mirrors `letIsI64` (infers through the initializer when `fnIx >= 0`); an un-annotated GLOBAL from a non-literal init stays out of scope (annotate `: f64`), exactly as for an i64/call-returning global today. REF-typed arms (string/struct/union) still need an interned blocktype functype and remain deferred. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/if-expr-typed-value.vl`)
- **A-nullable-if-binding — a nullable/value-union `if`-expression as a binding init** — `const x: i32 | null = if c then v else null` (and `else if` chains) now lowers, where before it failed `emitProgram: bare null needs a struct-typed context` / emitted a type-invalid module. The result is a NULLABLE REF rep (the value-union box / the `string | null` ref), which a single-byte blocktype cannot carry; rather than an interned-functype typed blocktype (avoided everywhere in the emitter) or a scratch stash, each arm writes the binding's OWN local inside a VOID `if` — the binding local IS the destination. Routed by the slot kind `collectLocals` already chose: kind 4 the value-union box (`i32 | null`, `i64 | null`, `f64 | null`, multi-member `i32 | string | null` — boxed per-arm via `emitUnionCoerce` with the annotation's union name), kind 16 the `string | null` ref (a non-null string passes through, a bare `null` lowers `ref.null $aTypeIdx` via `pendingNulString`). The non-null box local is pre-seeded with its null before the `if` — it is non-defaultable and a void-`if` merge does not satisfy the validator's definite-init check; the nullable `string | null` ref is defaultable and needs no pre-seed. The binding-position counterpart of the return-tail nullable `if` (#484/#489). Scoped to ANNOTATED bindings (the annotation drives the slot kind + union name + box registration); INFERRED `const x = if c then 5 else null` (needs local-union registration/naming), a `boolean | null` local (a separate pre-existing gap — not yet a supported local rep at all), and non-binding value positions (print/arg/return — no destination local) remain follow-ups. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/nullable-if-expr-binding.vl`)
- **A-infer-if-binding — an UN-annotated value-union `if`-expression binding** — `const x = if c then 5 else null` (and the else-less `const x = if c then 5`, whose false path is the implicit `null`) now infers + lowers as a value union, where before it failed `emitProgram: bare null needs a struct-typed context`. A-nullable-if-binding lowered the ANNOTATED form; this drops the annotation, fulfilling the "types hidden by aggressive inference" byline. A static pre-pass — `nliInferIfLet`, beside the `let x = null` hole's `nliInferLet` and run from the same `collectInlineUnionsIn` body walk — derives the value-union NAME from the if-expression's arms (literal / annotated-call atoms, via the same `nliAtomOfExpr`), registers it (`registerValueUnionName`, which interns the union box + value-box reps even as the program's ONLY union), and records it in the same `nli` tables, so the slot classifies (kind 4 box via `letIsUnion`→`nliNameOf`) and the binding emits (`emitNullableIfBinding` via `letUNm`) through the SAME path as an annotation — no AST change, `fmt`-invisible. Covers `i32 | null`, `i64 | null`, `f64 | null`, an `else if` chain, a non-null `i32 | string`, and the else-less form. MIXED-numeric arms (`if c then 1 else 2.5`) are NOT a union — they WIDEN to one numeric (f64, #490); the name derivation bails on ≥2 distinct numeric atoms (the static atom list cannot do that lattice join) so they stay on the numeric value-position-if path. An arm the pre-pass cannot classify (a bare ident `if c then v else null`), a pure `string | null` (the niche, not the value box), and `boolean | null` locals remain follow-ups. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/inferred-nullable-if-binding.vl`)
- **A-infer-let-export — the emitter reads the CHECKER's inferred binding type** — closes A-infer-if-binding's "an arm the static pre-pass cannot classify" follow-up: `const x = if c then v else null` where the non-null arm is a NON-literal (a param `v`, an inferred local `const v = 9`, a call `g(d)`, an `i64`) now infers + lowers, where before the emitter failed `bare null needs a struct-typed context` even though the checker typed it `i32 | null` cleanly. The checker already computes each binding's inferred type with the scope live; it now EXPORTS the un-annotated value-union ones by LetDecl NODE INDEX (`inferLetNameOf` / `inferLetCount` / `inferLetIxAt` / `inferLetTyAt` — the binding analogue of the inferred-RETURN export `inferRetNameOf`, rendered via the same `valueUnionRetName`). `nliInferIfLet` now reads that name instead of re-deriving from the arm literals (`ifExprNliName` stays a fallback for any binding the export does not carry), so ANY arm the checker can type works, and the numeric-widening join (`if c then 1 else 2.5` → `f64`, not a union) is correct for FREE — the checker did the lattice join, so the emitter no longer needs its own distinct-numeric bail. Emit-time boxing classifies each arm through the normal local-registry classifiers (which the pre-pass lacked), so a param/local/call arm boxes correctly. Scoped to the value-union box (kind 4); a pure `string | null` (niche) inferred binding renders "" from `valueUnionRetName` and stays a follow-up. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/inferred-if-binding-ident-arm.vl`)
- **A-infer-nulstr-if — an inferred `string | null` if-binding (the niche follow-up to A-infer-let-export)** — `const x = if c then "hi" else null` with NO annotation now infers + lowers, where before the emitter failed `bare null needs a struct-typed context` even though the checker typed it `string | null`. Unlike a value union (the `uBoxIdx` box, kind 4), `string | null` is a NICHE rep (`ref.null $aTypeIdx`, kind 16) — so `valueUnionRetName` renders "" for it and A-infer-let-export's `inferLetNameOf` table skips it, and the emitter's STATIC arm derivation (#492) can't classify it from the arms either. The checker now ALSO exports each inferred `string | null` if-binding by LetDecl NODE INDEX (`isInferLetNulStr` — the niche analogue of `inferLetNameOf`, recorded with a cheap `isNulStringTy` shape test, no `tyToStr`), and the emitter's `letIsNulString` consults it FIRST so the slot classifies as kind 16 and reuses the ANNOTATED `string | null` if-binding lowering (A-nullable-if-binding, rep 1) with no annotation. Any arm the checker can type works — a literal, an else-less tail, a param, a `: string`-returning call. GATED to if-expr inits (the A-infer-let-export perf lesson — the shape test never runs on the AST-node union that every `const e = P.nodes[i]` binds). Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/inferred-nullable-string-if-binding.vl`)
- **A-nulbool-local — `boolean | null` is a real LOCAL rep, plus its inferred if-binding** — `boolean | null` (the i32 niche: a cell holding 0/1 with the spare value 2 = `null`) was supported as a param / return / global / assignment target, but NOT as a local binding — even an ANNOTATED `let x: boolean | null = true` failed `emitProgram: only i32 locals are supported`, because the slot classifier had no kind for it and `checkLocalI32` rejected the niche annotation. It is now **kind 17**: a plain i32 cell (same valtype as a `boolean`, so `fbValtype` renders it via the i32 default — no new valtype), with a dedicated classifier branch (`letNulBool`) so the annotation/init is accepted and a later `x = null` write stays valid. On top of that the INFERRED if-binding works — `const x = if c then true else null` with no annotation now lowers, the `boolean | null` companion of A-infer-nulstr-if: the checker exports each inferred niche if-binding by node index (`isInferLetNulBool`, a cheap `isNulBoolTy` shape test gated to if-expr inits, mirroring `isInferLetNulStr`), `letNulBool` consults it so the slot classifies as kind 17, and the if-binding lowers via `emitNullableIfBinding` **rep 2** (a bare `null` arm is the sentinel `2`). Every arm the checker can type works — a literal, a param, a `: boolean`-returning call — and every null-check idiom (`??`, `!= null`, `== null`) is at parity with an annotated `boolean | null`. (`is boolean` narrowing of the niche — distinct from a null check — is a SEPARATE pre-existing gap shared with the annotated form, untouched here; an else-less `const x = if c then true` binding is rejected in BOTH forms, the else-less `T | null` tail being return-position only.) Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/inferred-nullable-bool-if-binding.vl`)
- **A-infer-struct-union-return — an UN-annotated STRUCT-union return** — `function pick(c) { if c { return {meow:1} } return {woof:2} }` (no annotation) now infers + lowers as `Cat | Dog`, where before the checker floored `cannot infer a union return type for 'pick' — annotate the return type`. The ANNOTATED form already lowered (the variant-boxed rep) + `is`-narrowed; only the inference was missing. The obstacle was nominal vs structural + phase ordering: the checker is purely STRUCTURAL (it infers `{meow: i32} | {woof: i32}`), but the emitter's union machinery is keyed by the NOMINAL struct-table name — `registerInlineUnion` / `structIndexByName` resolve `Cat`, not a `{…}` shape — and the union must be registered at `collectU`, BEFORE `collectS` builds the struct table, so the emitter can't derive the name from the return expressions either. Resolved checker-side: a reverse map (`structUnionRetName` → `nominalNameOfObj`) matches each inferred object member to the named type whose shape it structurally equals (bidirectional `assignable`) and exports `Cat|Dog`; the emitter then registers + classifies it through the EXACT annotated path (`registerInlineUnion` in `collectU`'s inferred-return loop, `unionNameOfExpr` for the binding, `fRetUni` for the result valtype). The floor uses the SAME `structUnionRetName` predicate, so it accepts EXACTLY what lowers — a member matching no named type (an anonymous / PARTIAL shape: `{meow:1}` against `Cat = {meow, foo}` differs in field count, so it does NOT misattribute) or a struct mixed with a scalar still floors with the clear "annotate". One emit fix: the un-annotated expression-based return classifier (`criClassify`) would ALSO flag each single-object-literal return as a plain struct return (`fRetRef`), conflicting with the union rep — now gated on `fRetUni == 0` so the union flag dominates. Covers `is`-narrowing (incl. an `else if` variant chain), a field COMMON to every variant read without narrowing, and floors anonymous/partial/mixed unions. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/inferred-struct-union-return.vl`, `inferred-struct-union-common-field.vl`)
- **A-heterogeneous-union — a union mixing STRUCT variants with SCALAR atoms** — `A | B | i32 | f64 | boolean | string | null` is now representable as a variable, where before it failed `emitProgram: only i32 locals are supported`. Two union machineries share the SAME `uBoxIdx` `{tag, value}` box — a VALUE union (`i32 | string`) boxes scalar atoms (per-rep value boxes), a STRUCT union (`A | B`) boxes variant structs — both tag-discriminated. The gap was the CLASSIFIER/registration rejecting a union that MIXED the two: a struct-ref variant with a bare scalar fell between `isValueUnionName` (all-scalar) and the all-struct path, so neither claimed it and it dropped to the scalar-local classifier. `isInlineStructUnion` is generalized to `isVariantBoxUnion` (≥2 non-null members, each a struct variant OR a value-atom, ≥1 struct), the registration pushes only struct members as variants AND `markValueUnionAtoms` interns the scalars' value boxes, and `emitUnionCoerce` boxes a scalar arm (`unionHasValueAtom`) while an object/struct arm auto-boxes as a variant struct. `is`-narrowing then dispatches each member by its globally-stable tag — exactly as the homogeneous unions already did (no new `is`/unbox code). Works as a local, parameter, annotated/inferred return, and binding; each member type narrows + reads. The byte-identical all-struct / all-value paths are untouched (the mix is the only new shape). A numeric atom reached by a DOUBLE-complement `else` (`x is A` else `x is i32` else → read f64) and a `??` whose non-null result is still a multi-member box are PRE-EXISTING limitations shared with value unions, so they stay out of scope (each numeric arm uses an explicit `is`). Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/heterogeneous-union.vl`)
- **A-nulstr-module-scope — a `string | null` VALUE read at module scope** — a top-level `x ?? d` / `x != null` over a `string | null` (kind 16) binding now recovers the non-null string ref, where before it emitted a `(ref null $array)` where a `(ref $array)` was expected (a wasm validation error — `expected (ref $type), found (ref null $type)`). The non-null recovery (`ref.as_non_null`) was gated on the global being NON-const, on the assumption that a const ref cell is already non-null; but a `string | null` cell is ALWAYS nullable (a const can be initialized to `null`), so the kind-16 recovery now applies regardless of const-ness. Function-scope reads already worked (the local path recovers); this closes the module-scope gap. `i32 | null` / `boolean | null` were unaffected (their cells are i32 — no ref recovery). Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/nullable-string-module-scope.vl`)
- **A-field-type-parity — struct FIELDS gain the full variable type capability** — a struct field may now be a niche nullable `string | null` (code 20, a `(ref null $aTypeIdx)` slot) or `boolean | null` (code 21, the i32 sentinel), a HETEROGENEOUS union (`v: A | i32`), or a STRUCT union (`v: A | B`) — all of which previously failed `emitProgram: only i32 / boolean / string / array struct fields are supported`. This brings fields to parity with the variable reps (the `A-heterogeneous-union` arc). The variant-box unions ride the SAME kind-16 `uBoxIdx` box a value-union field already used, so the field read / write / `??` / `!= null` lowerings reuse the existing box + niche machinery — `fieldTypeCode` / `nameFieldCode` admit `isUName` (a registered struct/heterogeneous union) and the niche names, the two niche codes add a `pushFieldStorage` valtype + a `ref.as_non_null` recovery on value read + null seeding on write, the `??` niche paths were hoisted to fire for a member field (not just an ident, gated `!(lhs is Call)` so the call-stash path is unaffected), and `exprNullableString` / `exprNulBool` / `exprString` learned the field cases. An inline-union FIELD annotation is registered in `collectU` via a new `TypeDecl` scan (BEFORE `collectS` interns the variant heap types). Nested variant field access through a field path (`s.v.a`) narrows via the member path key (`memIsNarrowed` / `emitNarrowedMem` handle a Member receiver). Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/field-type-parity.vl`)
- **A-field-completion — a struct literal may OMIT a nullable field** — `let cat: Cat = { meow: 7 }` against `Cat = { meow: i32, foo: i32 | null }` no longer errors `cannot assign {meow: i32}`; the omitted `foo` is materialized as `null` at construction (the "completion coercion"). Scoped to the object-LITERAL assignability path (`assignableExpr` — a known target type, where `tyAdmitsNull` permits the omission), NOT general structural subtyping, so it never widens a value through a container / variance. A non-nullable omitted field is still required. Works in binding, return, and argument position; the emitter fills the omitted field via `emitOmittedFieldNull` (the per-rep null — `ref.null` / sentinel / null box). SOUNDNESS holds: a value built as the NARROWER shape is physically that shape, so `cat is WiderType` is false (it lacks the slot) — no value masquerades with fields it does not have. (`tests/cases/functions/field-completion-coercion.vl`)
- **A-fn-type-annotation — function-type annotation syntax** — `(i32) => i32`, `(i32, i32) => i32`, `() => i32` now parse and check as a TYPE, where before the type grammar had no arrow form (`(i32) => i32` failed to parse — `expected RPAREN but found EQUAL`). The parser adds the `(params) => ret` production (a parenthesized comma-list, `=>` as `EQUAL` `GT`, then a return atom; `((i32) => i32) | null` keeps a top-level `|` OUTSIDE the arrow — parenthesize the return for a unionized result), emitting the synthetic name `"(params)=>ret"`. The checker's `nameToTy` resolves a top-level `=>` (depth/quote-aware `topLevelArrowIndex`) into a `TyFunc`. The emitter classifies a function-typed param / field / global / module-binding as the closure fat-pointer (kind 14 — `fieldTypeCode` / `globalCellKind` / `nameFieldCode` learned the `=>` name), a source annotation flips `fnValUsed` so its signature interns (`collectCloSigs`), and the call lowers to `call_ref` over the interned signature — the SAME rep an inferred function value already used. **Scope:** all-i32-param, value-returning signatures; `() => void` and f64/struct signatures need the internal sig key threaded and stay a follow-up, a function INSIDE a union (`((i32) => i32) | null`) and `is`-by-signature are separate next steps, and a lambda bound to a LOCAL inside a function is a PRE-EXISTING bug (an un-annotated local lambda binding fails there too — works at module scope). Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/function-type-annotation.vl`)
- **A-void-binding — reject binding a void result** — `const x = f()` / `let x = f()` over a VOID function is now a clean, source-located type error (`` cannot bind the void result of 'x' — a void function returns no value ``), where before it slipped past `vl check` (only an "unused variable" warning) and CRASHED codegen with an opaque wasm translation error. The assignment path (`x = f()` → "cannot assign void to T") and the expression / argument positions (`f() + 1`, `g(f())`) already rejected void; the BINDING path was the hole — an un-annotated binding had no assignability check, and `assignableExpr(void, T)` is lenient. The `LetDecl` checker now rejects a `void`-typed initializer (a new `isVoidTy`, compared STRUCTURALLY — `void`, like `null`, may intern at more than one arena index, so `== TY_VOID` is unreliable), EXCEPT an if-EXPRESSION init (a statement-`if` is context-free `void`, but the if-binding inference resolves `const x = if c then 5` to `T | null` — that path owns it). `null` is unaffected: it is a VALUE (a `T | null` result binds + reads normally), unlike `void` which has no value. (`tests/cases/functions/void-binding-rejected.vl`)
- **A-closure-builtin-capture — a function calling a BUILTIN can be a value** — `const f = g` / passing `g` as a callback where `g`'s body calls a builtin (`print`, `toString`, `Map`, …) now works, where before it failed `emitProgram: captured variable not found in enclosing frame`. The closure free-variable scan (`capScan` → `capRecord`) excluded bound vars, module globals, and USER functions from the capture set, but NOT builtins — so a builtin CALL TARGET (`print`) was mistaken for a captured variable and the closure-env build then failed to resolve it. A new `isBuiltinFnName` excludes the builtin call targets (they lower directly, never as values). NOT void- or return-type-specific — an `i32` function calling `print` hit this exact bug; it just surfaced while probing void function values. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/closure-calls-builtin.vl`)
- **A-inferred-f64-i64-return — an inferred f64/i64 return lowers when CALLED** — `function dbl(n: f64) { return n * 2.0 }` (no return annotation), once CALLED, now lowers, where before it crashed codegen with a wasm type mismatch IN the callee (`expected i32, found f64`). The inferred-return functype-result switch picked the kind from the `fRet*` tables for string / array / union / nullable / struct / `string | null` — but NEVER `fRetF64` / `fRetI64` — so an inferred f64/i64 return DECLARED an i32 functype result while the body returned f64/i64. The flags were already set (`buildFnMap`'s A20 pass reads the checker's inferred scalar name, exactly as for `string` / `i64`); only the two scalar cases were missing from the result-valtype switch (`infKind = 11` f64, `10` i64). The annotated `: f64` / `: i64` path always worked (via `vtKindOfType`), which is why every existing f64/i64 corpus case — all explicitly annotated — masked this. (Found while threading f64 closure VALUES — it's the prerequisite, and NOT closure-specific: a plain inferred-f64 callee hit it.) Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/inferred-f64-i64-return.vl`)
- **A-f64-closure-value — an f64-signature function VALUE lowers when called** — an `(f64) => f64` (or `(i32, f64) => f64`) function held as a VALUE — bound, passed as a callback, or in a struct field — now lowers when called, where before the `call_ref` used the all-i32 arity sig (`function-value call arity has no interned signature`, or a wasm mismatch routing the f64 result as i32). The call site derives the callee's REAL signature — from a function-typed PARAM / LOCAL / GLOBAL annotation (`(f64) => f64` → the `"d>d"` key via `calleeCloSigKey` → `annSigKey`, the readable `(params)=>ret` analogue of the monomorphized `"=>sigkey"` pin) or, for an INFERRED binding (`const f = dbl`), from the underlying function's own `fnSigKeyOf` — and the f64 result classifies through `calleeReturnsF64` (and `cloRetKindOf` for a field-closure call `t.op(x)`) so print / numeric-context routing is correct. f64 `$fnsig`s were already interned by the value-eligible pass; this threads the right one through the call site + the result type. Builds on A-inferred-f64-i64-return (the callee's own functype). Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/f64-closure-value.vl`)
- **A-module-scope-f64-i64-binding — an un-annotated module-scope f64/i64 ARITHMETIC binding lowers** — a top-level `const r = 2.0 + 4.5` (f64 / i64 arithmetic, or an f64-returning call `dbl(2.0) + 4.5`) now lowers, where before it crashed codegen `expected i32, found f64`. A single float literal (`const a = 1.5`) already classified, but a COMPOSITE init did not: top-level `const`s run in the start function, and the local `letIsF64` / `letIsI64` init-inference was gated to INSIDE a function (`fnIx >= 0`), while the module-cell classifier `globalKind` had no scalar-f64/i64 case in its expression family. Both now classify it — `globalKind` gains the `exprIsF64` → kind 11 / `exprIsI64` → kind 10 cases (the cell valtype), and the `letIsF64` / `letIsI64` gate is lifted so the READ (`globalIsF64` / `globalIsI64`) agrees. A cross-global ref (`const b = a + 1.0`) recurses through `globalIsF64` and terminates for an acyclic program — the same shape `globalKind`'s `exprString` / `exprStruct` cases already rely on. (Inside a function, and the explicit `: f64` / `: i64` annotation, always worked.) Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/module-scope-f64-i64-binding.vl`)
- **A-void-closure-value — a VOID function VALUE lowers when called** — a `void`-returning function used as a VALUE — bound (`const f = hi`), passed as a `() => void` callback, or held in a struct field — now lowers when called, where before it failed `function-value call arity has no interned signature` (only OBJECT-FIELD void closures had their `>v` sig interned) or hit a wasm stack mismatch. Three pieces: (1) the value-eligible sig pass interns a void function's `>v` sig for non-field values too (usage-gated by `fnValUsed`, `>v` only — struct-/string-returning closure values stay a separate slice); (2) `calleeCloSigKey` resolves it — `annRetKind(\"void\")` → `>v` for a `() => void` param annotation, `cloSigKeyExt` for an inferred void binding — and a void closure-call STATEMENT skips the result `drop` (`calleeReturnsVoid`, since `fnIndexOf` can't see a param/binding callee); (3) `stmtIsTailValue` (now threaded `fnIx`) treats a void closure-call tail as a STATEMENT, not a value, so `run(g: () => void) { g() }` infers void — the value-call analogue of the named-`fRetVoid` tail rule. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/void-closure-value.vl`)
- **A-struct-closure-value — a STRUCT-returning function VALUE lowers, and its result types as that struct** — a struct-returning function used as a VALUE — bound (`const f = mk`) or passed as an `(i32) => S` callback — now lowers when called, and the call RESULT types as that struct so `f(5).x` / `const p = f(5); p.x` resolve the field, where before it failed `function-value call arity has no interned signature`. The value-eligible sig pass interns a struct function's `>sN;` sig for non-field values too (it already did for object-field closures — the same `cloSigKeyExt` fallback the void slice generalized); `calleeReturnsStructIdx` parses the struct index back out of the resolved `>sN;` key (params never contain `>`, so the last `>` begins the return), mirroring `fnRetStructIndex` for a named function, and feeds BOTH `structIndexOfExpr` and the `exprStruct` predicate so the closure-call result classifies as a struct for the binding cell (`letIsStruct` → `collectLocals`) and the field read. The struct-result functype was already emitted (the field-closure `>sN;` REP path). Builds on A-void-closure-value. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/struct-closure-value.vl`)
- **A-string-closure-value — a STRING-returning function VALUE lowers, and its result types as a string** — a string-returning function used as a VALUE — bound (`const f = greet`), passed as an `(i32) => string` callback, or held in a struct field — now lowers when called, and the call RESULT types as a string (print routing, concat, a string binding cell), where before it failed `function-value call arity has no interned signature`. A string return is the raw i32 array `(ref $aTypeIdx)` — a NEW value-call result kind `>S`, the string analogue of the void (`>v`) and struct (`>sN;`) slices: `cloSigKeyExt` (inferred `fRetStr` / annotated `vtKindOfType == 3`) and `annRetKind(\"string\")` mint it, the `$fnsig` functype emits `(ref $aTypeIdx)` as its 1 result, and `calleeReturnsString` (an Ident binding/param callee) + `fRetStr` (a field-closure Member callee) classify the result so `exprString` routes it. Builds on A-struct-closure-value (the same non-field interning + result-classification shape). Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/string-closure-value.vl`)
- **A-i64-closure-value — an i64-returning function VALUE lowers, and its result types as i64** — an i64-returning function used as a VALUE — bound, passed as an `(i32) => i64` callback, or held in a struct field — now lowers when called, and the call RESULT types as i64 (print routing, i64 arithmetic, an i64 binding cell), where before it failed `function-value call arity has no interned signature`. i64 is the `0x7e` scalar — a new value-call result kind `>I`, the i64 analogue of the f64 (`>d`) / string (`>S`) slices: `cloSigKeyExt` (inferred `fRetI64` / annotated `vtKindOfType == 10`) and `annRetKind(\"i64\")` mint it, the `$fnsig` functype emits `0x7e` as its 1 result, and `calleeReturnsI64` (an Ident binding/param callee) + `fRetI64` (a field-closure Member callee) classify the result so `exprIsI64` routes it. Builds on A-string-closure-value. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/i64-closure-value.vl`)
- **A-union-nullable-closure-value — a UNION / NULLABLE-returning function VALUE lowers, and its result narrows** — a function whose return is a union (`A | B`) or nullable (`S | null`, `string | null`), bound as a VALUE (`const f = g`), now lowers when called, and the call RESULT types as that union / nullable so it narrows (`r is i32`, `p != null`) and `??`-coalesces — before, each failed `function-value call arity has no interned signature`. New closure-call result kinds `>u` (union box), `>nN;` (nullable struct), `>N` (`string | null`), the union/nullable analogues of the scalar (`>d`/`>I`) and struct (`>sN;`) slices. The result-valtype mapping is unified into `cloRetValKind`/`cloRetValSlot`/`cloRetKeySuffix` (shared by `cloSigKeyExt` and the `$fnsig` functype, replacing the per-kind special-casing — byte-identical), and `calleeRetKind` decodes the trailing `>X` token to drive `exprUnion` / `exprNullableStruct` / `exprNullableString` (`calleeReturnsStructIdx` now also reads `>nN;`). Scoped to the BOUND form: passing such a function as an annotated `(…) => A | B` PARAM or struct FIELD needs checker support for complex return types in a function-type annotation, and list (`i32[]` / `string[]` / `f64[]`) and closure returns stay un-minted (a clean "no interned signature") — separate slices. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/union-nullable-closure-value.vl`)
- **A-closure-call-in-is-operand — a function-value call used inline as an `is` operand lowers** — `f(x) is i32` (the closure-call result tested directly, never bound to a `const` first) now lowers, where before it crashed codegen `unknown local 0: local index out of bounds`. The closure `call_ref` reads a 1-slot scratch local (`callRefSlot`), reserved only when `blockHasCallRef` finds a function-value call in the body — but that scan recursed into `BinExpr` / `Member` / `Index` / arg lists, NOT into an `IsExpr` operand, so an inline `f(x) is T` left the slot un-reserved and the `call_ref` read a non-existent local 0. The scan now recurses into the `is` operand. NOT result-kind-specific: the same scratch slot backs every closure call (this surfaced via a union-returning closure, shipped in A-union-nullable-closure-value). Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/closure-call-in-is-operand.vl`)
- **A-union-closure-narrow-use — a union from a closure call narrows and unboxes** — a union value produced by a CLOSURE call, bound and then NARROWED and USED (`const r = f(x); if r is i32 { … r … }`), now lowers, where before it crashed codegen `expected i32, found (ref $type)` — the narrowed read kept the raw union box instead of unboxing to the scalar. The narrowing/unboxing is driven by the binding's union NAME (its atom set), resolved by `unionNameOfExpr`, which handled a NAMED-function call but fell through to "" for a function-VALUE callee — so a closure-sourced union local had no atoms to narrow against. It now resolves the name from the underlying lifted function (mirroring the named path via `identClosureFe`), so a union from a closure call narrows exactly like one from a named call. The bound `print(r is i32)` test (A-union-nullable-closure-value) already worked; narrow-and-USE — the primary way to consume a union — did not. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/union-closure-narrow-use.vl`)
- **A-fntype-complex-return — a function-type annotation may return a union / nullable** — a `(i32) => A | B`, `(i32) => S | null`, or `(i32) => string | null` annotation now type-checks and lowers, so a union/nullable-returning function passes as a callback PARAM or a struct FIELD (not only the bound form), and the call result narrows / `??`-coalesces. Before, the checker rejected the annotation outright (`called value is not a function`). Two pieces: (1) **checker** — a top-level `=>` binds LOOSER than `|`/`&`, so `(i32) => A | B` parses as a function RETURNING a union, not `((i32) => A) | B` (a new `isTopLevelFuncTypeName` guard on the union/intersection split in `nameToTy`; a genuine `((i32) => i32) | string` keeps its arrow nested in parens, so it still splits as a union); (2) **emitter** — `annRetKind` maps such a return to its closure-sig suffix (`>u` / `>nN;` / `>N`, via a depth-aware `annSplitPipe`), matching `cloRetKeySuffix` for the underlying function so the value call resolves, and `unionNameOfExpr` gains a FIELD-closure (`Member`) case (the analogue of A-union-closure-narrow-use's binding case) so a field-closure union result narrows too. (A genuine union of a function — `((i32) => i32) | null` — parses correctly as a union; CALLING such a value is a later slice.) Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/fntype-complex-return.vl`)
- **A-nullable-function-value — a `((i32) => i32) | null` local holds a function or null, narrows, and calls** — a NULLABLE function value — `let c: ((i32) => i32) | null` — now holds a function OR `null`, narrows with `if c != null` / `c == null`, and CALLS the recovered function, where before the `null` and the value call both failed (`bare null needs a struct-typed context`). It is the closure analogue of the nullable struct (kind 9): a new cell **kind 19** `(ref null $cloStruct)`, `null` → `ref.null $cloStruct` (a `pendingNulClosure` seed), `== null` / `!= null` → `ref.is_null` (`exprNulClosure`), and the narrowed call recovers non-null (`ref.as_non_null`) before `call_ref`. Crucially this required a **checker** fix to keep the type NAME distinct: `((i32) => i32) | null` (a nullable function) vs `(i32) => i32 | null` (a function returning `i32 | null`, A-fntype-complex-return) — `canonEmitName` now preserves the grouping parens around a function union-member AND no longer splits a top-level function type on `|` (the #516 `isTopLevelFuncTypeName` rule, now applied to canonicalization too), so the two keep distinct canonical names. Scoped to LOCAL bindings; a nullable-function PARAM / struct FIELD / global is a later slice (it errors cleanly, not crashes). Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/nullable-function-value.vl`)
- **A-nullable-function-param — a `((i32) => i32) | null` PARAMETER accepts a function or null, narrows, calls** — the param-form completion of A-nullable-function-value: a function parameter typed `((i32) => i32) | null` now accepts a function OR `null` argument, narrows with `if c != null`, and calls the recovered function. `vtKindOfType` maps a `fn | null` annotation to the nullable-closure **kind 19** (before the bare-function kind 14), so the param slot is `(ref null $cloStruct)`; a `null` ARGUMENT lowers as `ref.null $cloStruct` (a `cParamNulClosure` seed at the call boundary, mirroring `cParamNulString`); and `paramNulClosure` feeds `exprIsClosure` / `exprNulClosure` so the narrowed `c(x)` recovers non-null before `call_ref`. Still deferred: a nullable-function struct FIELD / global. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/nullable-function-param.vl`)
- **A-nullable-function-field — a `((i32) => i32) | null` struct FIELD holds a function or null, narrows, calls** — the field-form completion of A-nullable-function-value / -param: a struct field typed `((i32) => i32) | null` now holds a function OR `null`, narrows with `if recv.op != null`, and calls the recovered function. The field carries the same nullable closure fat-pointer as a kind-19 local, encoded as a NEW **field code 22** — `fieldTypeCode` maps a `fn | null` field annotation to it (before the bare-function code 14, mirroring `vtKindOfType`), `pushFieldStorage` emits `(ref null $cloStruct)` storage, a `null` field value lowers as `ref.null $cloStruct` (a `pendingNulClosure` seed in `emitObj`, and `emitOmittedFieldNull` for an OMITTED field — the completion coercion), and the field read narrows with `ref.is_null` / recovers non-null (`ref.as_non_null`) before `call_ref` — both driven by a new Member case in `exprNulClosure`, with `structFieldIsClosure` routing `recv.op(x)` to the `call_ref` path. Still deferred: a nullable-function global. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/nullable-function-field.vl`)
- **A-nullable-function-global — a `((i32) => i32) | null` MODULE GLOBAL holds a function or null, narrows, calls** — the global-form completion of the nullable-function arc (after the local-binding, parameter, and struct-field forms): a module global typed `((i32) => i32) | null` now holds a function OR `null`, narrows with `if handler != null` / `== null`, calls the recovered function, AND reassigns between states (`handler = inc` / `handler = null`). The global carries the same nullable closure fat-pointer as a kind-19 local — `globalCellKind` maps a `fn | null` annotation to **kind 19** (before the bare-function kind 14); the non-const cell is declared `(ref null $cloStruct)` (`fbValtypeNullable`) and zero-initialized `ref.null $cloStruct` (`fbRefNullForKind`); a bare `null` init / RHS lowers as `ref.null $cloStruct` (a `pendingNulClosure` seed in the start-fn init AND `emitAssign`'s global + local paths — closing a latent gap where reassigning a kind-19 LOCAL back to `null` also failed); the read stays RAW nullable (NOT auto-recovered, like a kind-9/15 global — a new `gck0 != 19` guard) so `!= null` is `ref.is_null` and the narrowed call recovers non-null (`ref.as_non_null`) before `call_ref` — both driven by `exprNulClosure`'s new global case, with `exprIsClosure` routing `handler(x)` to the `call_ref` path. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/functions/nullable-function-global.vl`)
- **A-list-returning-closure — a function VALUE may return a list (`(i32) => i32[]` / `string[]` / `f64[]`)** — a list-returning function value now passes as a callback PARAM or a bound value, is CALLED, and its returned list flows through the list machinery (`.length`, indexing, `for-in`). The reps were explicitly DEFERRED in #513 (adding them then crashed `string[]`); this revives them, narrowed to a VALUE call. Two pieces: (1) **name disambiguation** — a function type whose RETURN is a list ends in `[]` (`(i32) => i32[]`), so the emitter's array-name scan would mis-read the whole annotation as an array; an `annArrowAt` guard at the top of `collectA`'s `TypeRef` branch detects a TOP-LEVEL arrow (a function type — it then forces only the RETURN's backing types), so `((i32) => i32)[]` (a NESTED arrow — an array OF functions) is untouched and stays a later slice; (2) **result suffix** — `annRetKind` and `cloRetKeySuffix` mint `>a` (i32[]) / `>A` (string[]) / `>D` (f64[]) so the closure `$fnsig` result is the list-wrapper ref (`fbValtype` kinds 2 / 7 / 12, recovered non-null at the call site), and `exprArray` / `exprStringArray` / `exprF64Array` recognize a closure-value call result (via `calleeRetKind`'s `>a`/`>A`/`>D` decode) so a `const xs = g(x)` binding types as that list. Native fixpoint byte-identical; corpus PASS=398, no `string[]` regression. (`tests/cases/functions/list-returning-closure.vl`)
- **A-array-of-functions — an array of function values (`((i32) => i32)[]`)** — a ref list whose ELEMENTS are function values now builds from a literal of named functions (`[inc, dbl]`), is indexed, and the indexed element is CALLED via `call_ref` — as a LOCAL or a function PARAM, with constant or variable index, `.length`, and `for`/`while` iteration. A new ref-list **element kind 5**: the backing is `(array (mut (ref null $cloStruct)))`, each slot a closure fat-pointer (`rlElemHeap` backfilled to `cloStructIdx` after it is interned). Pieces: `nameIsRefArray`/`refArrElemKind`/`refArrElemName` recognize a closure-element array (`nameIsClosureArray`); `tyAnnRefListKind` threads kind 5 so `emitArr` builds the closure backing (the literal's bare-ident elements evade `arrLitIsRef`, so the annotation is authoritative); `exprIsClosure` gains an `Index` case so `xs[i](a)` routes to `call_ref`. The crux is again NAME disambiguation: `((i32) => i32)[]` (a NESTED arrow under a top-level `[]`) is an array OF functions, vs `(i32) => i32[]` (a TOP-LEVEL arrow) a function RETURNING a list — `canonEmitName` keeps the element's grouping parens AND no longer treats a top-level function type's trailing `[]` as an array suffix, so the two stay DISTINCT canonical names, and `vtKindOfType` classifies the array as kind 5 BEFORE the bare-closure kind 14 (whose `=>` test would else claim the whole array). Still deferred: a module-GLOBAL array of functions, and a non-all-i32 element signature (the indexed call rides the all-i32 arity sig). Native fixpoint byte-identical; corpus PASS=399. (`tests/cases/functions/array-of-functions.vl`)
- **A-definite-assign Definite assignment for uninitialized locals** — an uninitialized `let x` / `let x: T` now starts UNassigned and NON-null (NOT implicitly nullable); a READ where `x` is not provably written on every preceding path is a `` `x` is used before it is assigned `` error. This closes a silent-`0` soundness gap (`let x: i32; return x` used to compile and fabricate a `0`). The check reuses the parser's single-pass flow machinery — the same `is`-narrowing fork/join that scopes through `if`/`else`/`while` and lets a diverging branch drop out: a per-`Binding` "unassigned" bit is forked at each branch, joined at the merge (assigned only if written on every surviving path — a `return`/`break`/`continue` branch contributes nothing, reusing `divergesStatement`), and reset to the pre-loop state after a `while`/`for`/`for-in` body (zero-iteration). The declaration itself is fine; only the reads are gated, and the binding's type is unchanged (`let x: i32` stays `i32`, not `i32 | null`). Initialized `let x = …` and every `const` are assigned from the start and untouched. (`tests/cases/variables/definite-assign-*.vl`)
- **A11 Recursive structural types** — `type Tree = { value, left: Tree | null, … }` constructs/traverses/compiles (cycle-safe traversals + self-referential WasmGC rec-group). Mutual recursion across *separate* `type` decls (`type A = { b: B | null }` ↔ `type B = { a: A | null }`) now resolves: a top-level type-name pre-pass hoists every `type` name before any body is parsed, so a forward/mutual reference stays a lazy `Alias` leaf and both aliases tie into one rec-group. (`tests/cases/types/mutual-recursive-type.vl`) Recursion through an **array element type** (`type List = { value: i32, rest: List[] }`) now compiles too — the element struct, its growable-list `{backing,len,cap}` wrapper, and the backing array are discovered as one strongly-connected component and built in a single WasmGC rec group (forward-referencing each other), instead of unboundedly re-entering type lowering and overflowing the stack. (`tests/cases/types/recursive-array-element.vl`) Recursion through a **map value type** (`type Tree = { value: i32, children: { [string]: Tree } }`) now compiles as well — the map's hash struct and its `vals` backing array join the same rec group, so the value-type back-edge is threaded via forward refs instead of overflowing through `mapType`→`mapArrayType`→`refHeapType`. (`tests/cases/types/recursive-map-value.vl`)
- **A12 Soundness corpus (started)** — must-error/must-not-error corpus under `tests/cases/soundness/`; strict by default; known-unsound corners `xfail`-marked.
- **A13 Operator-constraint inference (core)** — fully-inferred structural functions monomorphize per call shape (i32 & f64 from two call sites).
- **A13 fix: the deferred binary-op capability check covers EQUALITY** — a binary op with a hole operand records a deferred `(lt, op, rt)` constraint that each generic call site re-validates under the substituted argument types (`binOpDefinedFor`), but its `==`/`!=` arm returned true for every operand pair. So `function cmp(a, b) a == b` / `cmp(1, "x")` type-checked clean and then emitted an i32 where the module declares a `(ref $type)` — a compile-clean program the engine rejects at instantiation — while the annotated twin `a == "x"` was already a `cannot compare i32 with string` error. The arm now applies the same mutual-compatibility criterion, over what the operands ERASE to (`nomErasedTyIx`), so `id == 0` on a newtype stays comparable while `EntityId == string` rejects. A `null` operand is exempt: a nullness test asks about the storage, not about type agreement. Rejects `i32`/`f64`/`boolean` against `string` and `boolean` against `i32` in all eleven hole-provenance shapes (two-hole, one-hole-one-annotated either side, two-level chain, captured operand, array element, struct field, second instantiation); no program in the 1,770-file corpus changes verdict. (`tests/cases/soundness/equality-hole-operand-reject.vl`, `equality-hole-operand-sound.vl`, `equality-hole-null-test-sound.vl`)
- **A13 fix: a string ORDERING with one annotated operand defers to the call site** — `function f(a, b: string) { a < b }` called `f("a","b")` was rejected *"comparison expects numeric operands, got any and string"* while both its bare twin `f(a, b)` and its fully-annotated twin `f(a: string, b: string)` accepted and printed the same value — so adding an annotation removed a capability, inverting the aim of types hidden by aggressive inference. The ordering arm demanded `isStringTy` on BOTH sides, so a hole on either side fell to `isNumeric`, which refuses a `string`; hole/hole never reached the arm because `isNumeric` admits a `TyVar`. A concrete-string-against-a-hole ordering now DEFERS, the way `+` already does for `s + h`: `noteBinCstr` records the constraint and each call site adjudicates it under substitution through `binOpDefinedFor`, whose ordering rule admits exactly `string`/`string` and numeric/numeric. Population re-derived at **24 cells**, not the filed 8 — the four ordering operators × the two half-annotated directions × **three** string-typed spellings (`string`, a string literal type, a string literal union; the last two reach the arm through `softenLitTy`, which a `string`-only grid never exercises). All 24 print the same value as their bare and fully-annotated twins, as do the same shape through a lambda, a nested generic, a `let` init, a loop condition and an uncalled body — so every newly-accepted program has an accepting fully-annotated twin. Every non-string binding of the hole still rejects (i32, f64, boolean, list, object, `null`, `string?`, and through two generic hops), now at the call that pins it and naming the ARGUMENT types rather than the hole; no program in the 237-case `@error` corpus changes verdict. (`tests/cases/soundness/ordering-hole-string-operand-sound.vl`, `ordering-hole-string-operand-reject.vl`)
- **A14 Named/opaque type crash fix** — bodyless-`type Point` infinite-recursion crash fixed (cycle-guarded `getConcreteType`; now errors cleanly). → `DECISIONS.md`
- **A15 Structural equality** — `==`/`!=` by value; functions by reference; arrays + nested structs recurse via `valueEq`/`isEquatable`. → `DECISIONS.md`
- **A15 fix: structural `==`/`!=` on a value-union operand** — `v == 7` where `v: "x" | 7` no longer crashes codegen (`Have only handled i32 with ==, got Union`); it lowers structurally by reusing the boxed-union `{tag, value}` rep and the `is`-literal machinery — tag-select the matching arm, then `valueEq` the unboxed payload against the other operand (a cross-type arm is structurally unequal, e.g. the `"x"` arm vs an `i32` → `false`). `union == union` lands too (same arm + payload-equal, short-circuiting each unbox on its tag). Reuses `unionInfo`/`findVariant`/`unboxPayload`/`valueEq`; no checker change. (`tests/cases/operators/union-eq.vl`)
- **A15 fix: symmetric `==`/`!=` type-checking for a value-union operand** — `7 == v` (where `v: "x" | 7`) now type-checks and runs just like `v == 7` did; equality is symmetric, but the checker previously gated the structural value-union branch on the *left* operand being the union (`leftType.type === "Union" | "Nullable"`), so the reversed form fell through to the numeric `==` path and reported `expected i32, got "x" | 7`. The branch now fires when *either* operand is a value-union; the comparability test (`validateType` either direction) is already order-independent, and the reversed form additionally tests the union against the *un-softened* left operand so a bare literal (`7`, softened to `i32` for arithmetic) still matches one of the union's arms. A plain mismatch with no union (`"a" == 7`) still errors as before. Codegen already handled both orders (PR #200) — no `toWasm.ts` change. (`tests/cases/operators/union-eq-reversed.vl`, `eq-no-union-mismatch.vl`)
- **A16 Literal-union types (type-level)** — annotations constrain (`"a"|"b"` rejects `"c"`); `==`/`!=` discriminate + narrow; covering `if/else if` chain exhaustive. `flattenType` dedup uses an O(n) value-key set when every variant is a literal (the common case; distinct literals never subsume), replacing the O(n²) pairwise `validateType` scan — a left-folded N-member literal union now type-checks in O(n²) not O(n³) (160 members 485ms→3.8ms). Shared front-end logic (carries to `typecheck.vl`). (`tests/cases/types/literal-union-dedup.vl`)
- **A7b `boolean`-where-`i32`-expected coercion** — a `boolean`-typed VALUE coerces to `i32` (true→1, false→0) at an assignment boundary (binding/argument/return); no-op in codegen (shared i32 rep). One-directional (`i32` ⊄ `boolean`); excludes a bare boolean literal and membership/narrowing queries (`x is boolean` on `string | i32` still rejected).
- **A7b fix: an `i32[]` binding built from boolean-typed elements PRINTED `true`** — `const t = true` / `const e: i32[] = [t]` is check-clean, because the A7b coercion lives at `assignableExpr` — the ONE seam every binding / argument / return / field / element / store / push flows through — so an array LITERAL adapts ELEMENT-WISE into an `i32[]` destination. The checker then types every `e[i]` read as `i32` and says so in its own words (`const x: i32 = e[0]` draws `` redundant type annotation: `x` is inferred as `i32` ``). The EMITTER disagreed: `print(e[0])` rendered `true` while `print(e[0] + 0)` rendered `1` — the same expression over the same value, two formats, `vl check` rc 0. `listElemIsBool` consulted the DECLARED element name only as a positive claim (`== "boolean"` → true) and then walked to the binding's INITIALIZER regardless, where the first element is a boolean-typed value; annotation and initializer legitimately disagree exactly where the coercion fires, and the walk outvoted the annotation. `i32` is now claimed beside `boolean` at that same seam, so the declared element decides in BOTH directions and the initializer walk stays the route only for an un-annotated binding. Only the depth-1 array element was ever wrong — `i32[][]` reads through an `Index` (no binding to walk to), an object-literal field goes through `Member`, and `.push` / index-store leave the initializer alone, so all three already printed `1`. Blast radius: **0 of 1,970** corpus check-verdicts move, **1,901 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored** on the `@log` oracle, **0** self-host reliance (the fixed compiler is its own self-compilation fixpoint byte-for-byte at 1 compile, and bootstraps from the published master seed in 2). Sabotage: with the fix reverted to the saved pristine artifact, exactly **1 of 1,901** reddens — the new positions fixture, as predicted. (`tests/cases/types/boolean-to-i32-container-positions.vl`, `boolean-to-i32-container-reject.vl`)
- **A17 Forward / mutual-reference return-type inference** — demand-driven (lazy + memoized, cycle-detected) return-type inference; a function calling a later-defined or mutually-recursive function no longer infers `any`; only a genuinely base-case-less inferred cycle still needs an explicit annotation. (#105; detail: `docs/selfhost-gaps.md` §A17)
- **A18 Order-independent module scope + TDZ** — top-level `let`/`const` VALUES are now hoisted (names + types) in a checker pre-pass, exactly as top-level FUNCTIONS already were, so a function body resolves a global declared LATER in the file. This removes the last reason a forward-referenced / demand-inferred function needed an explicit RETURN annotation (its body could previously not resolve a not-yet-declared global, so inference failed — `A17` covered the call graph, not the global it reads). Initialization order is still enforced: each global joins the definite-assignment dead-zone at hoist time and is marked assigned only when its initializer is reached, so a read before initialization — straight-line top-level code, OR one global's initializer reading a later global — is a `` `x` is used before it is assigned `` (temporal-dead-zone) error, NOT a silent read of uninitialized memory. A function body runs at call time, after module init, so its reads are correctly exempt (its own definite-assignment context is cleared). An annotated global hoists its annotation type; an un-annotated one hoists a side-effect-free LITERAL peek (a non-literal initializer stays order-dependent — it carries no manifest type to hoist). A genuine top-level redeclaration is still an error. (`tests/cases/scope/`)
- **A19 Binding-group return inference (recursive cycles)** — return-type inference now resolves a mutually-recursive GROUP whose return is determinable, with NO annotations: `even`/`odd`, or a chain whose return passes *through* a recursive cycle but bottoms out in a concrete value. `A17`'s demand inference is single-pass — at a cycle it commits a provisional `void` and memoizes it, so a function whose return only becomes known AFTER the cycle resolves inferred `void` and then errored. `A19` adds a suppressed return-inference PRE-PASS (before the body-checking pass) that iterates the cycle-poisoned ("tentative") functions to a FIXPOINT — the structural-typing analogue of HM's unify-the-binding-group, iterating the return lattice instead of unifying (VL has subtyping, not unification). It's standard SCC / binding-group inference (cf. *Typing Haskell in Haskell*, Haskell Report §4.5), realized lazily through the existing demand stack — which already visits callees first, i.e. dependency order — so no explicit call graph is built. Genuine VOID recursion (no value return position) still infers `void`; a genuinely base-case-less value cycle (`ping`→`pong`→`ping`, no concrete basis) still reports `cannot infer a return type`. The pre-pass cost scales with un-annotated functions and actual cycles — negligible on annotated code (no measurable change on `vl check compiler/`). (`tests/cases/functions/infer-*.vl`)
- **A20 Elaborate inferred return types for codegen** — the emitter classifies an UN-annotated function's return by re-deriving it from the return EXPRESSION syntactically (`exprString`/`exprStruct`/…); that misses a return which is a plain VARIABLE (a `let`-bound, concatenation-built string), so the function is mis-classified and a caller's field access crashes codegen (`field access receiver is not a struct`) — a checker-accepts / emitter-rejects soundness gap (the type checker proves a clean `string`, the emitter can't lower it), exposed by inferring more returns. The checker now exports each un-annotated function's inferred concrete SCALAR/STRING return name in a side table the emitter consults, and the emitter sets the classifying flag directly from that inferred TYPE — matching the declared-annotation path. It is kept OUT of the AST (`fnRet` is untouched), so `fmt` never re-adds the annotation: inference stays invisible in the source. Conservative on purpose: only scalar/string returns (names that round-trip unambiguously) and only NON-generic functions (a `self`-style `TyVar` param has a per-call-site return, so its definition-time inferred type is a placeholder — `sumsq(self)` rendered as `string`); structural / array / union returns keep the existing expression-based classification. (`tests/cases/functions/infer-string-return-codegen.vl`)
- **A21 An un-annotated PARAMETER is SOLVED from the usage that constrains it (D1e)** — `function p(u) { const r: K = u   print(r) }` left `u` an inference HOLE, so the parameter's REP came from whatever the call site's argument happened to be.
