That is not a free choice for a NAMED string litunion: `K` is an interned i32 ATOM while a plain `string` is a `(ref $array)`, and a bare `"a"` argument widens to `string` — so the CORRECT program `p("a")` was `vl check` rc 0 and then `Invalid input WebAssembly code: type mismatch: expected i32, found (ref $type)`, while the NON-member `p("zz")`, which the annotated `p(u: K)` rejects, passed the checker and printed `zz` at run time in the spellings whose reps happened to agree. The filed escalation offered only "keep the reject" or "lower it with the atom tower" (whose last member is an unconditional default, so a non-member would silently BECOME a member); the owner's ruling is a third option that needs neither — `u` is narrowed to `K` BY INFERENCE OF THE USAGE, after which the ordinary call-site argument check does the rest. A quiet body probe (checkProgram pass 1.6, before any expression is checked, so the answer is independent of source order) reads the flow constraints the holes accumulated and records the FIRST fully concrete destination; `assignableExpr` is the ONE seam every binding / argument / return / field / element / store flows through, so a single hook there sees every constraining usage, and it walks source and destination in PARALLEL so `{f: u}`, `[u]` and a `K`-valued map store reach the hole at the position that actually constrains it (a composite source short-circuits `assignable` whole). `unannotParamTy` is the single producer of an un-annotated parameter's type — the pass-1 hoist, `checkFuncDeclNode`, and through the `nodeTyIx` it records the emitter's `synthParamAnnots` all read it — and `recordedParamPinName` is its emitter-side twin, which the MONOMORPHIZER now consults instead of deriving a second answer from the argument type (`monoArgTyName` names no boxed union at all and had no litunion arm, so a `K`-typed argument fell to the `"i32"` catch-all, which mints no annotation by design — the instance's parameter lost its type entirely and a bare `print(u)` printed the raw atom id **0** on master). A DEMANDED SHAPE is deliberately not a solution: `{bar: _}` is a `TyObj`, not a bare `TyVar`, and adopting it turned the working relay `relay({foo: "RF"})` into `expected {bar: _}, got {foo: string}` — `tyReachesHole` is the fully-concrete test that keeps a lower-bound-from-usage out. Two probe side effects had to be suppressed rather than tolerated: the inferred-return patch (pass 1.7 must re-derive the return against the SOLVED parameters) and the deferred-join PARKING, which is keyed by CALL NODE rather than by hole name, so a probe row made the real pass decline to re-park and silently dropped the escaped-join reject `soundness/hole-join-two-level-chain-reject.vl` pins. **840-cell grid** (12 constraint shapes × 8 constraining types × 5 argument shapes, plus a 5-form axis: `function`, arrow `const`, UFCS receiver, nested, generic), each cell graded against its ANNOTATED TWIN as the oracle: **all 660 flow-constraint cells at parity, from 583 on master** — 150 invalid-wasm-where-the-oracle-rejects and 100 check-clean-accepts-of-a-program-the-oracle-rejects became the oracle's own reject, 43 invalid-wasm valid programs and 24 false rejects became correct, 0 regressions. Soundness stated as a measurement rather than an argument: of the 252 non-member cells, **0 with a flow constraint runs at all**, and every one of the 100 newly REJECTED cells is rejected by the annotated twin too. Byte-identity with the annotated twin on **224 of 342** cells where both build and run; the differences are 80 UNCALLED functions (no argument to monomorphize from), 20 `is`/no-constraint shapes where the twin is a different program, and the single-member-alias / map-value spellings, all with identical observable output. Corpus A/B over **1,846** files: **0 output changes, 0 verdict changes**, and one MESSAGE change — `soundness/hole-arg-concrete-param-reject.vl` keeps its verdict and position and takes the annotated spelling's wording, because `x`'s only usage solves it and the reject is the ordinary argument check; the deferred-constraint route stays the reject for a hole no usage solves. (`tests/cases/inference/unannotated-param-solved-from-usage.vl`, `tests/cases/soundness/unannotated-param-solved-nonmember-reject.vl`, `tests/cases/inference/unannotated-param-solved-composite-and-nested.vl`)
- **A22 fix: the compiler TRAPPED with no diagnostic and no module — TWO roots behind one `out of bounds array access`** — three filed shapes (an empty `[]` returned from a lambda; a captured map; the `silent-class-inventory` D4 program) share the outcome `wasm trap: out of bounds array access` INSIDE `vl-compiler.wasm` — no message, no source location, no artefact, the worst outcome class in the tree. Disassembling the named seed (`vl build compiler/entry.vl --names`, then `wasm-tools dump` at the backtrace offsets) puts them at **two** faulting sites, not one and not three. **Root 1 (CHECK stage, `typecheck.nulElemListRetName` frame 0 / `checkFuncDeclNode` frame 1)** — `TyArray.aElem` is `-1` for an empty `[]` whose element type is not yet known, which the type's own comment DECLARES, and the read `T.tys[t.aElem]` did not decline it. It was the ONLY member of its own thirteen-function `*RetName` ladder without the guard; the file dereferences `aElem` at 22 sites and 18 spell `if x.aElem < 0 { return … }` inline above the read. `tyArrayElemIx` is now the one home for the question (upper bound included, which NO inline guard had) and the four holed sites route through it — `nulElemListRetName`, `unionArrayArmForLit`, `refArmUnionRetName`, and `structElemFieldsPlain`, whose `>= 0` comparison sat ONE LINE BELOW the read it needed to gate. **Root 2 (EMIT stage, `emit_classify.sFieldIndex` frame 0 / `memberUnionFieldName` / `exprHasStrOp` / `emitFuncCode`)** — `captureValStructIdx` is the `fbValtype` COMPANION slot and is POLYMORPHIC IN THE CAPTURE'S KIND: for a map capture it is the value type's `mv` SHAPE slot, not a struct-table row. `capturedStructIndex` returned it as a struct index, so `sFieldStart[mvSlot]` read past the end. It is the SHORTENED MIRROR of two functions that get this right — `localStructIndex` gates on `localIsRef[slot] == "struct"/"nulstruct"`, `retCapturedMapShape` on `captureValKind == "map"` — and it now applies the same gate; `sFieldIndex` additionally floors an `si` past the struct table, the same answer its `si < 0` arm already gives. **Shape C reduces to Shape B** (byte-identical 13-frame backtraces, and its program is a member of Shape B's own grid), so the three shapes are TWO roots. **The filed AXIS was wrong**: D4 named the map's KEY; the key is FLAT (48 traps per spelling) and the VALUE decides, because `mapAnnShape` answers the mono sentinel `-1`/`-4` for an `i32`/`boolean` value and only a value needing its own `$mapStruct` yields the slot that was mis-spaced. Unmasking Root 2 exposed its SECOND HALF — `mapShapeOfExpr`'s captured-map arm covered only the `LetDecl` binding, so a captured map PARAM read the mono shape against a typed env field (`(ref $mapStruct)` stored, `(ref null $mapStruct)` expected): **48 check-clean invalid-wasm cells, now 0**. Unmasking Root 1 exposed 12 `indirect call type mismatch` PROGRAM traps, and those ship as a LOUD source-located floor rather than a silent one: a lambda annotated `() => string[]` / `() => i32[][]` returning an empty `[]` has no element rep to build from, and `scalarLeafListRetName`'s exclusion note (`string[]` "self-classifies identically to the annotation") is true of a NON-EMPTY literal and false of an empty one; the `i32`/`boolean` shared-backing elements are exempt and stay correct. Measured on a **608-cell grid** (A 384 = 8 element types × 4 producer forms × 2 literals × 6 consumers; B 220 = 11 map value types × 2 keys × 5 capture routes × 2 runtime inputs; C 4 = the inventory's own cells), every cell run through `check` + `run` + a third `build` stage that records WHETHER A MODULE WAS WRITTEN — the signal that separates a compiler trap from a program trap and the only reason this class was ever visible: **COMPILER TRAP 172 → 0**, program trap 0 → 0, invalid wasm 64 → 28, correct 352 → 532, loud check reject 20 → 48, silently-wrong value 0 → 0, wrong evaluation count 0 → 0, hint-only rc 1 0 → 0. The COMPILER-TRAP column was proven LIVE, not merely non-zero: an out-of-bounds arena read injected into `checkFuncDeclNode` and gated on a function NAME present in exactly 44 cells moved **44 cells (predicted 44 before the run)** into that column and **0** into program-trap or invalid-wasm, after which the grid returned to its pre-sabotage classification on all 608 cells from an `md5sum -c`-verified saved artefact. - **A9 (cont.) — a container whose ELEMENT changes storage class was `vl check`-clean invalid wasm, and the axis is the element's STORAGE, not the element's type** — `objShapeAdapterless` recursed through an array element and a map value looking for an OBJECT pair, so it saw `i32[]` into `(i32 | null)[]` as a nullable peel over two equal `i32`s and declined. It is not a peel: an array's wasm type is fixed by how its element is stored, so a plain-i32 element array and a boxed-union element array are unrelated types with no element-converting copy to insert. Live at HEAD as rc 0 then `Invalid input WebAssembly code`, in every position measured (parameter, binding, return, one container deeper), and for the map value as well as the array element. **THE RULE IS A CLASS TEST, NOT A TYPE-IDENTITY ONE, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE MEASUREMENT** — a type-identity rule rejects working code on its first line. Widening is FREE whenever the storage class holds: a REF element takes `| null` for nothing, because a nullable ref is the same wasm reftype (`C[]` into `(C | null)[]`, `string[]` into `(string | null)[]`, `i32[][]` into `(i32[] | null)[]` all RUN), and a NICHE-nullable scalar keeps its base class, because the sentinel lives in the element's own value space (`boolean[]` into `(boolean | null)[]` and `K[]` into `(K | null)[]` RUN — i32 sentinel 2 and interned-atom sentinel -1). What has no lowering is the class CHANGE: a scalar boxed by the destination union (`i32[]`, `i64[]`, `f64[]` into their `| null` twins), an interned litunion atom swapped for a ref (`K[]` into `string[]`), and a bare width change (`i32[]` into `f64[]`). `elemStoreClassOf` is that ladder and `ESC_UNKNOWN` always DECLINES — an inference hole, a type variable or any unnamed shape is never claimed on a guess, which is what keeps `first<T>(ys: T[])` applied to an `i32[]` working. **The nested pair is tried FIRST**, so a container carrying an object pair keeps the precise object wording and `i32[][]` into `(i32 | null)[][]` reports the INNER array pair with the outer named as what it was reached through. The diagnostic gets its own wording rather than reusing the object one, whose whole vocabulary is wrong here (no field is dropped, and the two shapes may be the same shape); `shapeAdaptElemPair` is banked beside the pair the walk already banks, for the same reason the pair is — one of two rules claimed it and the pair does not say which. **Corpus A/B over 2,005 files: 2 verdict changes and 1,640 of 1,640 emitted modules BYTE-IDENTICAL.** Both movers are `@emit-error` closure fixtures whose own headers describe this exact defect (a lambda self-inferring the bare list arm `>A` where the annotation's element is the union box `>u`), so both are emit-reject → CHECK-reject TIER MOVES with the verdict unchanged, and each records that the emit message it used to assert is now the backstop behind this gate. Nothing in the corpus exercised the hole in a program that runs. - **Band 1 destringify — a THIRD name-grammar mis-order, same oracle, and it moves no coverage** — pointed at the last 10 leaves, the diagnostic named a third class, and every remaining witness is one name shape: a name with a TOP-LEVEL `|` reaching the composite arms (`MAPMISS nm={[string]:()=>i32}|{w:i32}|null`, `ELEMMISS nm=(boolean|string)[]|(i32|f64)[]`). The paren-carrying union branch interned its arms and then FELL THROUGH — deliberately, per its own comment ("closure unions etc. keep their existing path") — into the `[]`, map and inline-shape peels, each of which claimed the whole union as though it were the composite its FIRST arm happens to be; the arena says `TyUnion`, declines the step, and that is how it surfaced. The fall-through now returns as the paren-free branch always did, and the path its comment protected runs ABOVE this point since the functype arm moved. **And it moves NO coverage — still 743 of 753 (98.7%), residue still 10**: the mis-peels were not producing hinted leaves, they were producing WORK on names the descent had mis-parsed, so this is a hygiene fix in the same family rather than a coverage win, and saying otherwise would be the kind of claim this board exists to catch. **Three mis-orders, one diagnostic, and the residue is now the point**: the last 10 leaves are what the descent genuinely cannot type, not mis-parses. `internShapeDeepTy`'s grammar now orders arrow → nullable → union → array → map → shape, which is `internFuncTypeShapes`' order — the two twins agree for the first time. Corpus A/B 0 of 2,010 on all three channels; suite 2,159/0; `cases_wasm` 1,939/0; native fixpoint byte-exact at 1,251,334.
- **Band 1 destringify — the arena hint caught a name-grammar mis-order; 31% of the descent's leaves were SPURIOUS** — chasing the last 25 unhinted leaves, the step-miss witnesses named themselves once the arena type was printed beside the name: `ELEMMISS nm=()=>(f64|null)[] ty=() => (f64 | null)[]`. **The arena is right and the NAME PEEL is wrong** — `nameIsElemArray` only asks whether a name ENDS in `[]`, so it claimed a FUNCTYPE whose result is a list and peeled the whole name as an array, descending a type the name does not denote; `internShapeDeepTy` ordered its grammar array-before-arrow while `internFuncTypeShapes`, its own twin, has always ordered it arrow-before-array. **It had no detector until the hint arrived**: the mis-peel interns extra shapes rather than wrong ones, so nothing reddened — not the corpus, not the suites, not the fixpoint — and what surfaced it was a LOST HINT, the arena declining a step the name had taken. Fixed by moving the functype arm above the array arm, and the effect is larger than the residue it was chasing: leaf resolutions in the descent **1,097 → 753 (−344, −31%)**, hinted **1,071 → 737 (97.6% → 97.9%)**, dual-run **737 / 0**. **344 of 1,097 leaf resolutions were spurious** — work done on names the descent had mis-parsed — and corpus A/B **0 of 2,010** on emitted-wasm sha256, exit code and diagnostic text confirms they were no-ops, which is why nothing had caught them. This is the hand-over paying for itself in a way the programme did not predict: the hint was built to remove parses, and what it did here was act as a CHECK on the name grammar — two independent derivations of the same structure, disagreeing exactly where one is wrong. Residue now 16 leaves of 753 — **and the oracle found a SECOND mis-order immediately**: `NULMISS nm=(i32)=>string|null ty=(i32) => string | null`, where `nullablePartOf` claimed a name whose top-level ARROW binds the `| null` to its RESULT. That is a rule `internFuncTypeShapes`' own header states **with a fuzz-found invalid-wasm consequence attached** (*"the naive peel treated it as a nullable CLOSURE and the `f64 | null` result never surfaced"*) — the twin had the fix, the descent did not. The functype arm moved once more, above the `| null` peel as well as the `[]` one, giving coverage **737 → 743 of 753 (97.9% → 98.7%)**, dual-run **743 / 0**, residue **10 leaves**. **Two name-grammar mis-orders in one sitting, both found the same way, neither visible to any existing channel**, and the pattern is now explicit and reusable: **where the arena declines a step the name took, the name grammar is wrong** — a standing diagnostic, and the strongest argument this programme has produced for carrying the type alongside the spelling. Corpus A/B 0 of 2,010 on all three channels; suite 2,159/0; `cases_wasm` 1,939/0; native fixpoint byte-exact at 1,251,331.
- **Band 1 destringify — "what is a struct row's identity?" enumerated, and the enumeration is COMPLETE** — I closed the last round saying a row's identity "is enumerated NOWHERE, so every attempt to replace a spelling comparison guesses at a list nobody has made", and called that the blocking design question. **The list is derivable from the comparison that was already making it**: `annShapeIndexOf`'s own body names all five dimensions — field-name sid (the count + name-set conditions), field code, element-name sid with `-1` for none (the element comparison, now speaking one vocabulary), the map-KEY bit (`recordSFieldElemRow`: *"an mv slot's identity is the (KEY, VALUE) pair"*), and the litunion-ATOM bit (the two arms that keep `{f: K0}` off a `{f: boolean}` row). `shapeFieldSetIdOf` now hash-conses exactly that tuple per field, sid-sorted so field ORDER cannot change the key, and the interner's bucket keys on it — **the identity is a written-down list in one function, which is what did not exist**. **And it is COMPLETE, checked rather than asserted**: the refinement still runs and still decides, so the key only narrows candidates and an incomplete enumeration would surface as a rejected candidate — measured over the corpus, **2,360 bucket candidates accepted, 0 rejected**. The refinement is provably redundant on everything available and stays anyway, because "checked everywhere, asserted nowhere" is what makes the claim worth having. **What this settles**: the question was never a language or ABI ruling — it was a list nobody had written down, and the reason nobody had is that four of its five dimensions were only expressible as string comparisons, so it could not be stated until the vocabularies agreed and the sids existed. Corpus A/B 0 of 2,010 on emitted-wasm sha256, exit code and diagnostic text; suite 2,159/0; `cases_wasm` 1,939/0; native fixpoint byte-exact at 1,251,331.

What is left is not leaf work: the remaining 40.8% are codes that depend on what the emitter has interned (a ref-list's element slot, a map's value kind, a union's box, a nullable niche's backing), which is the same question the interner's own name-keying asks and moves when it does. - **Band 1 destringify — the shape dedup's primary key is now a structural id** — `annShapeIndexOf` walked the whole struct table and re-derived the same three conditions on every row: the field COUNT, every queried name being present, and the codes matching. Those three ARE a key — "the row has the same multiset of (field name, field code)", and a struct's field names are unique — so they are computed once as a hash-consed id over `(sid(name), code)` pairs in canonical order (integers, no spelling composed), and the scan becomes a walk of one bucket. **Exactly equivalent by construction**: a row in a different bucket fails conditions 1–3 and the old scan rejected it; a row in the same bucket passes them and reaches the element / atom-identity refinement, which is untouched — that refinement stays per-candidate because it compares stored element SPELLINGS and its atom split reads the query's text, the genuinely name-shaped part of this dedup. **Dual-run 3,112 agree / 0 disagree** over the corpus, with the chain walk shown load-bearing (stopping at the bucket head disagrees on **75** of 3,112). **The sort is not exercised by the corpus and that is recorded rather than banked**: keying in written order instead of sorted gives 0 disagreements, so no two rows here share a field-name set in a different order where dedup matters; it stays sorted because the scan it replaces looked each name up via `sFieldIndex` and was order-insensitive BY CONSTRUCTION, and an order-sensitive key would silently mint a duplicate row for `{a: i32, b: i32}` against `{b: i32, a: i32}`. Corpus A/B 0 of 2,010 on emitted-wasm sha256, exit code and diagnostic text; suite 2,159/0; `cases_wasm` 1,939/0; native fixpoint byte-exact at 1,247,601.
- **Band 1 destringify — the interner measured, and its cost is NOT the parse: `structIndexByName` is now an index** — with the rep tables done I sized the interner before converting it, and the census re-ranked it. On the compiler's own source the shape interner's field SPLIT runs **0** times and `annShapeIndexOf` **0** — every annotation the compiler writes about itself is nominal, so the parse the terminal item is named for is not what the interner costs there. What it costs is the name-keyed row LOOKUP: **258,112 calls and 2,394,078 row comparisons** on the self-compile (207,375 calls over the corpus), each a linear walk over `sNames` with a string compare per row. So `structIndexByName` is now an incremental index — incremental and not rebuilt-on-demand because `sNames` grows throughout collect, where a rebuild-on-length-change index would be O(n) per growth and restore the same O(n²); `sNames` is push-only within a program (four push sites, no in-place rename), so only rows past the watermark are ever inserted, first match wins, preserving the scan's duplicate-spelling semantics. **The per-program reset is load-bearing and measured**: a watermark cannot see `collectS`'s `sNames = []` (program B pushing as many rows as A leaves it satisfied with A's spellings), and removing the explicit `structNameIxReset()` fails **438 of 1,939** in the shared-instance wasm harness — the same hazard `sidKeyedTablesReset`'s header describes for its own tables. **Perf: NOT a measured speedup, shipped anyway** — min-of-8 self-compiles 1.55 s before, 1.54 s after, and the corpus channel is pure noise; removing 2.4M string comparisons is invisible, the eighth time this session that deleting a large string-shaped population moved the clock by nothing. The justification is complexity, not time: an O(rows)-per-query scan on the emitter's hottest name query is worth removing on a program larger than anything in this corpus. A method note that cost a run: sabotaging the reset and testing with `VL_COMPILER_WASM` set showed 1,939/0, because the Deno harness loads `build/vl-compiler.wasm` directly and ignores that variable — the sabotage only reddened once the seed was swapped on disk. - **Band 1 destringify — the rep-column rewrite, step 2: the first consumer keys on the identity, not the spelling** — the slot cache's twin index keyed on `repCanonKey(di)` in two `{[string]: i32}` maps. It now keys on `repCanonId(di)`, and since ids are dense from 0 the two maps become two plain arrays — **the rendered key is no longer built at that site at all.** A change of REPRESENTATION with the partition held fixed, which is why it cannot move a byte and does not. **One hazard had to be closed first, and it was not hypothetical**: `hcReset` lives in `sidKeyedTablesReset`, which the driver calls INSIDE the module-parse loop (its own header calls that "the sharpest edge the carrier has"), so the id space restarts mid-compile — the two `ty -> id` memos hold ids as values and the slot cache holds them as indices, and both would index the previous space's rows. `hcReset` now clears the memos and stamps the slot cache stale. **And the byte channel is blind here, measured rather than assumed**: flipping the unique-twin gate from `== 1` to `== 2` — turning every structural-bridge answer into a decline and inventing new ones — moves **0 of 2,010** files, while the bridge is nevertheless LIVE (reached 664 times, answering 223 on the corpus). So the byte gate proves nothing about this conversion; what proves it is the identity equivalence measured in step 1, which is the whole reason that was built first. - **Band 1 destringify — the rep-column rewrite, step 1: the structural identity exists and is proven over 283,052 pairs** — `repCanonKey` / `repElemKey` RENDER a structural string from an arena type, and every consumer uses it for one thing: equality. So the string is a REPRESENTATION of a structural identity, not the identity. `repCanonId` / `repElemId` build the identity directly — every node interns `(tag, args…)` into a hash-consed table (FNV-1a, intrusive bucket chains, rehash at 3/4 load) and returns its INDEX, so structurally-equal types get the same i32 and **no type spelling is ever composed**. The recursion mirrors the string builders arm for arm, with the mix-widening rule now written ONCE (a shared `hcUnionId`) instead of the string builders' two copies. **`hcLen` is the sharp part**: it carries what the string answer WOULD be in characters, computed without rendering, which reproduces the 262,144 runaway cap exactly and is a far tighter check than partition agreement — a length matching at every node says the two recursions have the same SHAPE. It caught the only real bug in the first draft (the functype arm started at 2 for `(` and `)` while the string's closer rides inside `")=>"` — an off-by-one on 19,260 of 53,622 keys that the partition check could not see, because every functype was off by the same one). The proof runs inside the existing `repShadowSweep` harness (armed by `$VL_REP_SHADOW`, off by default, one boolean when unarmed), asserting per key and per vocabulary: **53,622 pairs on the self-compile and 229,430 on the corpus, 0 false merges, 0 false splits, 0 length mismatches**. Sabotage (drop the nullable wrapper from the identity) gives **606 merges and 693 length mismatches** with witnesses naming the class — live in both directions. The id→spelling assertion is deliberately PER VOCABULARY: the two builders share one table and an id reached from both is correct, and asserting across them produced 14,355 false "merges" that were all the harness being wrong. The table is sid-keyed, so `hcReset` goes in `sidKeyedTablesReset`, the function whose header says it exists to be that pairing's one home. **Not wired to any consumer yet, deliberately** — switching `repSlotKeySi` / `repSlotKeyN` / `slotCanonKey` onto it is the next slice, with this harness already in place to catch drift. It is not that. The counter sat after the `cUserTypes` rung and BEFORE the `nameIsShapeSpanEnds` test, so it counted reaches past the nominal rung, most of which return -1 without resolving anything. Re-instrumented at `annotResolve` itself — the actual parse — the emit-side census is `declTyIxOfName` **1,175 → 1,248**, `repElemKeyOfNameTy` **576 → 590**, `sTyIxOfNameTy` **207 → 40**, emitter total **1,958 → 1,878**, with the checker at **17,459 in both builds** (the identical figure is the control that says the attribution is stable, and it is B1's "17,832 of 17,834 are tree walks" holding on this tip). **And the two neighbours went UP**: `annotNameMemo` is keyed on the SPELLING and shared by every site, so a name `sTyIxOfNameTy` used to resolve was a memo entry the other two rode for free — take the resolution away and they pay the miss. Of the 167 parses removed at `sTyIxOfNameTy`, **87 (52%) relocated and 80 (48%) were genuinely removed**: a net **−4.1%**, not the −77.8% claimed. The same-tip A/B agrees — ignoring the hint on the current tip gives 556/416/1,137 = **2,109**, against 590/40/1,248 = **1,878** with it, so the whole hand-over mechanism is worth **231 parses (−11.0%)**. **The standing lesson is bigger than this slice: per-site parse counts in this programme are NOT ADDITIVE.** Every "site X does N parses" figure is an upper bound on what removing site X saves, and a slice must be priced by an A/B on the emitter TOTAL with the hand-over toggled on one tip — never by the site's own counter falling. None of this touches correctness, which is what the slices were for: the dual-runs are unchanged and still exact (526/0, 888/0, 1,018/0, 1,044/0, each with a live sabotage), and leaf hint coverage 37.3% → 95.2% is a coverage measurement that stands. The board says destringify is a correctness programme, not a speed one; this is the first time it caught me quoting a speed number for it anyway.

- **Band 1 destringify — the TYPE ARENA is now string-free for structure and kind** — three raw `string` fields in `T.tys`'s own variants encoded a type's KIND, each with its closed set written out in a comment over the field; all three are now declarations. `TyPrim.primName` and `TyLit.litKind` became literal unions (`PrimName`, `LitKind` — 25 of `litKind`'s comparisons keep their source text unchanged, and its 9 producers all pass literals through one minter), and **`TyErr.errKind` became an `i32` because it was WRITE-ONLY**: `grep -rn errKind compiler/*.vl` is two lines, the declaration and one construction, and nothing reads it — it exists only because `is` discriminates the arena union on field NAMES, so the variant needs one field to be distinguishable and never needed that field to be a heap string. `litKind`'s one string-sentinel local (`let litKind = ""`, meaning "no member seen yet") becomes `LitKind | null`, the same absence idiom `primNameOf` took. What remains in the arena is TEXT rather than structure — `objFieldNames`, `tvName` and `litText` are user-authored identifiers and lexemes that a type legitimately carries, none of them encoding a shape or a kind. 0 verdict changes and 0 emitted-byte changes over 2,005 corpus files, full suite 2,159/0, native fixpoint holds, seed 68 bytes smaller.
- **A bare `return` in a void function had NO LOWERING** — `function g(c: boolean) { if c { return } print("after") }`, the ordinary guard clause, was `vl check`-clean and then `emitProgram: bare return is not supported`, so an early return from a void function could not be written in VL at all. **It was completely unpinned**, which is why it survived: the corpus A/B that shipped the fix moved **0 verdicts and 0 emitted bytes over 2,005 files**, because not one fixture used the construct. The lowering is the wasm `return` opcode with nothing pushed, and it needs no arity reasoning of its own because the CHECKER is already the gate — a bare `return` in a value-returning function is refused there by name of the type it owes (`return needs a value of type i32`), so an EMPTY result type is the whole population the emitter arm can see. `fRetVoid` is read position-keyed through `fnStmtsPosOf`, the same way `emitFuncBody`'s fall-through reads it, rather than re-derived from the annotation; anything that channel cannot answer for keeps the old loud reject rather than guessing an arity, since a `return` that leaves the stack wrong is invalid wasm and strictly worse than the message it replaces. Pinned on both sides and over four shapes — a guard clause, inside a loop's block structure, a lambda body, and TAIL position where the terminator still has to be written even though the fall-through would have ended the body. Found by probing underneath the `void`-as-unit-type ruling rather than from any filed row. (`tests/cases/statements/bare-return-void-early-exit.vl`, `statements/error-bare-return-in-value-fn.vl`)
- **Band 1 destringify — `TyPrim.primName` was a closed vocabulary held as a raw `string`, spelled out in a COMMENT over the field** — *"`primName` is one of \"i32\" | \"i64\" | \"f32\" | \"f64\" | \"boolean\" | \"string\" | \"void\" | \"null\" | \"never\""*. That comment is now the declaration: `export type PrimName = …`, and every one of the compiler's `primName == "i32"` tests is an `i32.eq` against an interned atom **with its source text unchanged** (F2/#1402's template, applied to the type arena itself). **Censused in the unit that decides the work**: 176 `.primName` sites over 5 files (typecheck 97, emit_rep 48, emit_classify 29, check_query 1, wasmEmit 1), of which **154 are comparisons against a literal**; **9 producers, every one a literal `mkPrim("…")`** at arena init; and **0 sites compare the readers to `""`** — which is what made the absence marker a non-question, so `primNameOf`/`tyPrimNameOf` return `PrimName | null` and the empty-string marker nothing ever tested for is gone. Four consumers spell the name instead of deciding on it (two rep-KEY builders, the `as`-target spelling canon writes back onto the node, one niche render) and route through an exported `primNameStr`, the only new surface. **0 verdict changes and 0 emitted-byte changes across 2,005 corpus files**; full suite 2,157/0; native fixpoint holds. **PERF IS MEASURED NEUTRAL and that is the entry's main finding**: interleaved min-of-5 reads `vl check compiler/` 4,692 → 4,710 ms (−0.4%) and the self-compile 1,548 → 1,566 ms (−1.2%), both inside noise, with an earlier run of the same pair reading +1.6% in the other direction. **F2's −9.4% is not the expected shape for this class** — `tok.kind` is compared once per TOKEN and `primName` once per TYPE DECISION, so a closed-vocabulary conversion is worth what its site FREQUENCY is worth, not what its site COUNT is (154 vs 561 was never the axis). Kept on the other axis: the checker is now a COMPLETE ORACLE for the vocabulary — a mistyped primitive name is a hard type error at all 176 sites rather than a silently-false comparison.
- **A24 — an un-annotated object literal with a NON-LITERAL field value could not compile at all, and one root wore two messages** — `const a = { legs: 4, purr: v }`, a bare variable in a field, is `emitProgram: ref valtype with no interned shape`, while `{ legs: 4, purr: 1 + 1 }` — the same shape with a constant-foldable field — compiles; a call, a field read, a parenthesised read and an array index reject the same way, and the field COUNT and field TYPE are both inert. Inside a function body the SAME root reports `emitProgram: object literal but no struct type declared`, because a global cell resolves its valtype in `emitGlobalSection` before `emitObj` ever runs while a local reaches `emitObj` and meets its `sDeclared` guard first — the pair of messages is why this looked like two defects. `collectAnonShapes` leaves a whole shape un-interned when any field's `anonFieldCode` answers -1, and that ladder read the value's SYNTAX only, so an ANNOTATION anywhere was what rescued an ordinary program (it interns the shape on the other pass, `collectAnnShapes`). The syntax ladder keeps its place FIRST — a literal knows context-adopted reps a type cannot name, such as a small integer literal in an f32 field — and a second rung behind it resolves the CHECKER's recorded type through `nameFieldCode`, the one home every ANNOTATED field position already resolves through. **The second rung is GATED on a measured invariant**: a shape interned here fills `sFieldElemName` from `anonFieldElemName`, which answers for a map field and a nested-struct field and "" for everything else, so a code carrying a COMPANION column cannot be minted on this path — an ungated first cut admitted code 5 (`S[]`) and turned 24 grid cells and **two green corpus files** into `emitProgram: ref-list field element type is not interned`. `anonCodeIsSelfContained` admits exactly the codes whose wasm slot the code alone decides (four scalars, the string ref, five scalar-backed list wrappers, the closure fat pointer). A GENERIC's structural-return literal still declines for the right reason rather than by accident: `collectAnonShapes` runs inside `collectS`, BEFORE `monomorphize`, so `{ first: b, second: a }` has unsolved type PARAMETERS in its fields and `nodeTyName` answers "" for a `TyVar`. Measured over a constructed **360-cell grid** (15 field types × 6 value forms × global/local × 1-or-2 fields, each cell's own annotated twin as its oracle): **48 → 288 correct, 240 newly accepted, 0 regressions**, 0 silently-wrong, 0 wrong-evaluation-count, 0 compiler traps, and the 44 still-rejecting cells report the SAME message as before; 28 further cells are ORACLE-BROKEN in both directions and are generator mis-shapes rather than results (24 print a map read without `??`, 4 spell `(i32) => i32[]` for `((i32) => i32)[]`). Corpus: **1,970 files, 0 verdict changes**, every module that builds in both byte-identical except the two fixtures whose own source this branch edits (**1,612/1,612**) — no heap type is newly interned in any unedited program. The two `@run` cases are LOG-MISMATCH fixtures, red on master with `emitProgram:`; the value-form file's oracle field names are deliberately DIFFERENT from its cells' (`o`/`olead` vs `g`/`lead`), because `collectAnonShapes` skips a literal whose field-name SET already matches an interned shape — an oracle spelled with the cell's own names interns that shape and RESCUES the binding it is judging, which a first cut did, passing on master. Sabotage: the recorded-type rung made inert reddens **exactly the 2 files predicted**; the GATE opened reddens **exactly the 3 predicted** (the ref-element pin plus the two closure corpus files). (`tests/cases/objects/anon-shape-nonliteral-field-values.vl`, `objects/anon-shape-nonliteral-field-reps.vl`, `objects/anon-shape-ref-element-field-reject.vl`)
- **E8 The checker's INTERNAL absence marker `<none>` reached users on the diagnostic channel — and the sibling `<error>` is a MEASURED non-change, not the other half of the same fix** — `tyToStrAt`'s no-arena-entry bail-out (`ix < 0`) wrote `<none>` into user-facing messages: `unknown property \`add\` on {[<none>]: <none>}` for a bare `Map()`, `argument 1: expected string, got <none>[]` for an empty `[]`. It now writes the blank `_` — E7's token, for E7's reason: an un-annotated param and an empty literal's element are ONE sentence to a reader ("no type was determined here; say it"), and the split between them is arena bookkeeping (an unsolved entry vs no entry at all) that made the second kind look like a different, internal condition. `holeDemandStr`'s "demands nothing" answer moves with it, because it lands in the `expected …` half of a message whose `got …` half comes from that renderer. **CORPUS, re-derived on the tip rather than inherited**: 4 marker occurrences in 4 of the **300 error-producing** files (of 1,137 diagnostic-producing, of 2,004 `.vl`; 13,510 diagnostic lines) — the board's "3 named files of 240 diagnostic-producing" is stale in both terms, and its shape→file table mis-assigns two of the four (`types/unknown-type-in-map-value.vl` is an `<error>` site; the `<none>[]` site is `soundness/hole-is-guard-return-join-reject.vl`). The corpus is not the population, so the render was reached from **74 CONSTRUCTED cells** (3 waves; 2 non-reproducing probes re-shaped after VL rejected `function(){}` for arrow syntax): **`<none>` 35 of 38 cells across 12 distinct message TEMPLATES** (`argument N`, `return type mismatch`, `cannot assign … to 'x'`, `cannot assign … to T`, `comparison expects numeric operands`, `operator '+' is not defined`, `unknown property`, `no method '.p' on array`, `print of …`, `set:`, `add:`, `push:`) and every shape of it (`_[]`, `_[][]`, `{[_]: _}`, `{[_]: boolean}` for `Set()`, `{a: _}`, `(_)[]`, `() => _[]`, `_ | null`). **`<error>` reaches the user through EXACTLY ONE template — 12 of 12 hits over 36 cells, plus 2 of 2 in the corpus, all the `print of …` codegen gate, and in all 14 the errored component sits inside a FUNCTION type**; the other 24 cells report only `unknown type '…'`, because an errored type is assignable both ways and every assignability-shaped check ABSORBS it. **Three answers for `<error>` were priced and all three refuted.** `_`: collapses a distinction `soundness/hole-renders-as-blank-reject.vl` pins, and misdirects — the reader has already been told `unknown type 'nosuchtype'` by name, and `() => _` sends them after a missing annotation instead of the one they wrote. SUPPRESS the second diagnostic: refuted by a CONTROL, not by the fixture comment that claims it — `function mk(): i32 { return 0 }  print(mk)` is rejected `print of () => i32 …` identically, so the gate is not a cascade and only its RENDER is polluted. RENDER THE OFFENDING NAME (`() => nosuchtype`, strictly the most useful): impossible without a rep change — `TY_ERR` is a SINGLETON arena entry (one `mkErrTy()` call, `errKind` a constant), and `emit_rep.vl` keys `TyErr` by arena INDEX, so minting one per failure multiplies rep keys. `<error>` therefore stays, and with `<none>` gone the angle brackets become a CLEAN one-meaning vocabulary: they mark a type the reader named and the checker rejected. **BYTE-IDENTITY, the primary oracle for a text-only change: 2,004 of 2,004 corpus files produce an identical `(rc, md5)` record, all 1,640 emitted modules byte-for-byte** — the `tyToStructStr` hazard (it shares `tyToStrAt`, and its output names shapes the emitter stores) did not fire, measured rather than assumed. The diagnostic channel moves **2 of 13,510 lines**, both `<none>` → `_`. **LSP**: `ABSENT_TYPE_MARKERS` drops `"<none>"` — no producer emits it, and the blank could not replace it, being a SUBSTRING of ordinary identifiers (`{foo_bar: i32}`) in a substring sentinel test. Both `KNOWN_CLEAN_DROPS` exceptions in the corpus sweep disappear (`: _ | null`, `: () => _[]` are displayable), so **no diagnostic-free file loses a hint any more** and the clean side is now an absolute zero, with `dropsOnErrored ≥ 1` carrying non-vacuity. A `TyNullable`-arm answer of `null` for the lone-`null` case was **refuted by measurement, not scope**: 4 of 12 constructed cells reach `_ | null` and only 2 are the lone null — `function mk() { return [] }` makes `mk().pop()` one too, where the blank is honest. **Pins retargeted (4) and predictions**: `sets/error-add-non-boolean-value.vl` (`{[<none>]: <none>}` → `{[_]: _}`), `soundness/hole-is-guard-return-join-reject.vl` (`got` → `got _[]`, a pin that stopped one token BEFORE the marker and so could not see it), plus header-only retargets on `soundness/hole-renders-as-blank-reject.vl`, `generics/type-arg-inline-object-field.vl`, `types/unknown-type-in-inline-object-field.vl` and the two `tail-assign-*` files whose blocked empty-`[]` rows named the display filter as blocker (it no longer is; the checker-side half at `fnAssignRetTargetName` is untouched). Predicted 1 fixture red before retargeting — measured 1. SABOTAGE with the SAVED pre-fix artefact (`md5sum -c`, never a rebuild): predicted 3 corpus fixtures red — measured exactly 3, and the LSP suite predicted GREEN and measured 8/8, because its corpus sweep observes the FILTER and not the producer. No log-mismatch fixture is available for a diagnostic-text slice; the new file is a PIN. (`compiler/typecheck.vl`, `lsp/src/typeFeatures.ts`, `tests/cases/soundness/absence-markers-render-reject.vl`, `tests/lsp_undisplayable_type_test.ts`)

## Codegen, memory & runtime (Track B)

- **A MAP VALUE riding a type parameter was never substituted AT ALL — `monoSubstAnn` had no map arm, and neither did its gate** — `function mk<T>(v: T): {[string]: T}` is check-clean INVALID WASM (`vl run` "expected i32, found (ref $type)") in all four value reps, and has been since long before this programme. `monoSubstAnn` carries arms for the arrow, the union, a generic application, an object shape, a function type, a grouping paren and a `[]` run; it had none for a MAP, and neither did `monoAnnHasTyParam`. So the gate answered false, the substitution was never ATTEMPTED, the instance kept the ORIGINAL annotation, and the type parameter survived into it as a nominal name. **The pair of omissions is ONE bug** — an arm in the substituter with no arm in the gate is dead code, and the gate alone substitutes nothing — so both are added together. THE VALUE ONLY: a map KEY is `string` or `i32` by construction, so a parameter cannot reach that position. The key PREFIX rides as the slice it already is (everything through the `]:`), never rebuilt from parts, and the re-render is gated on the value actually MOVING, so a concrete map annotation returns VERBATIM and never becomes a second intern key — the discipline the `[]`-run and grouping-paren arms already carry. **Found by widening the grid, not by reading**: `scripts/mono-tyaram-grid.sh` reported 0 BAD throughout while this was broken, because its twelve constructors did not include a map. A sweep over twelve MORE — two-parameter generics, application-of-application, arrays of applications, closure returns, deep shapes, nullable applications — turned up exactly one BAD cell in 96, and it was this. The tool now carries all 24 (216 cells), its sabotage against the pre-#1475 seed fires on 13 rather than 6, and its header records that **a grid is only ever evidence about the cells it contains**. Corpus A/B 1 of 2,024 — the fixture. Gates: suite 2,173/0, cases_wasm 1,953/0, native 2,190/0, fixpoint byte-exact, rep-fuzz exact, extended grid 0 BAD. (`tests/cases/generics/generic-return-map-value-tyaram.vl`)
- **B9 CLOSES — the monomorphizer's union rejoin folds, and the defect needed BOTH halves; neither moved anything alone** — a generic whose return union COLLAPSES under its binding, read into an INFERRED binding, was `vl check --codegen` rc 0 and `vl run` "expected (ref null $type), found (ref $type)". `monoSubstAnn`'s rejoin is a blind concat, so the instance's return NAME was the spelling `string|string` — a name no source program can produce and no interner has seen. It now flattens each substituted atom (one may itself be a union), dedupes by FIRST OCCURRENCE so a union whose arms do not collapse re-renders byte-identically and never becomes a second intern key, and returns the BARE atom when one survives. **The other half was the checker's `substTyDeep` fold**, shipped separately. Each was measured at **0 outcomes moved** on its own — the emitter half over a 16-cell grid at B9, the checker half re-tested against B9 and still failing. Both measurements were right and the conclusions drawn from them were wrong: *a 0-effect measurement rules out "this change alone fixes it", not "this change is not part of the fix"*. Measured: 20-cell union-return grid vs pre-#1481 — **2 moved, both from INVALID WASM to correct output**, the other 18 unchanged; the B12 audit grids (10 lambda-union, 10 param-pin) 0 moved; corpus A/B **2 of 2,023** — the new fixture plus `closures/generic-nulclosure-return-sig-skip.vl`, whose output and diagnostics are byte-identical and whose module GREW 6 bytes at the same 2 type rows. Gates: suite 2,172/0, cases_wasm 1,952/0, native 2,189/0, fixpoint byte-exact, rep-fuzz exact, `mono-tyaram-grid` 0 BAD. (`tests/cases/generics/generic-return-union-collapses-inferred-binding.vl`)
- **REGRESSION FIX — #1481's return hand-over must decline a UNION, because `substTyDeep` does not fold a degenerate one and the annotation path does** — `T | string` at `T := string` substitutes to a union whose two members are the SAME type. `nameToTy("string|string")` collapses that to plain `string` (which is also why the SOURCE spelling `const v: string | string` compiles and runs); `substTyDeep` rebuilds member-wise and stops. Handing the un-collapsed union over recorded a UNION BOX on a binding whose instance returns a bare `(ref $str)`: `const r: string = f("a")` over `function f<T>(v: T): T | string` ran correctly before #1481, and after it was `vl check --codegen` rc 0 with `vl run` "expected (ref null $type), found (ref $type)". **TWO CHANNELS PASSED IT AND BOTH ARE WORTH NAMING.** The dual-run compared RENDERS and filed the union cells under its 22 "differs only nominally" — the wrong reading, because for a union the recorded TYPE is what picks the box, so a differing render is not cosmetic there. And the corpus A/B read **0 of 2,021**: no corpus program has a generic whose return union COLLAPSES under its binding. That is the fourth time on this programme a clean measurement has been blind to the case that mattered, and the reason the fix ships with a FIXTURE rather than a measurement. Unions are the only constructor with a folding step, so excluding them keeps every other shape converted — verified over a 20-cell grid (5 arms x 2 bindings x annotated/inferred) at **0 moved versus the pre-#1481 build**. Re-widening needs `substTyDeep` to fold a degenerate union the way `nameToTy` does, not a looser gate at the call site. The INFERRED binding over the same collapse is a separate and older defect (B9), check-clean invalid wasm before #1481 and after this fix alike, and is deliberately not pinned here. Corpus A/B 1 of 2,022 — the new fixture. Gates: suite 2,171/0, cases_wasm 1,951/0, native 2,188/0, fixpoint byte-exact, rep-fuzz exact, `mono-tyaram-grid` 0 BAD. (`tests/cases/generics/generic-return-union-collapses-under-binding.vl`)
- **The instance's parameter pin stops re-deriving a type the node already held — `recordClonedNodeTy` has ONE live caller and 109 of its 118 calls were redundant** — the arm is reached only when `pinned[pj] == tyNameOf(p3.parType)`, i.e. the pinned name IS the written annotation, so the checker's recorded type for that node is usually the pin itself and the call re-parsed the spelling to re-derive it. `nameToTy` not being hash-consed, that mint is a duplicate row. **Measured: 109 of 118 calls re-derive a type rendering IDENTICALLY to the one already on the node, minting 26 rows for nothing.** **The 9 that DO differ are why the call cannot simply be deleted**, and they are exactly the case the site's own header describes: the original declaration's node carries the checker's resolution taken in the GENERIC BODY's type-param frame, so a `T` or an `Inner<string>` sits there as a type VARIABLE and the instance needs the concrete pin. `tyHasTyVar` (added in #1481) is that distinction, and the guard is measured rather than argued — **0 cases** over the corpus where the recorded type is TyVar-free AND the re-parse renders differently, so the predicate separates the populations completely. **Cumulative across #1478 / #1479 / #1481 / this: re-parses 1,318 -> 149 (-89%), arena rows minted by a parse 955 -> 99 (-90%).** Corpus A/B 0 of 2,021. Gates: suite 2,170/0, cases_wasm 1,950/0, native 2,187/0, fixpoint byte-exact, rep-fuzz exact, `mono-tyaram-grid` 0 BAD.
- **The monomorphized RETURN stops re-parsing its substituted name — the emitter's string bind-and-substitute finally answers through the CHECKER's type-level pair, gated on completeness** — `nret = synthTypeRef(rs, -1)` was 239 of the 452 remaining corpus re-parses and **117 of the 210 rows they minted**: over half of the whole remaining cost, on one line. `monoBindFromAnn` + `monoSubstAnn` are hand-written STRING twins of `bindGenWalk` + `substTyDeep`, which already work on types; the input those needed was the ARGUMENT types, and `pinTys` — added by #1474 for an unrelated `$fnsig` pin — already carried them. **THE GATE IS WHAT MAKES IT SAFE RATHER THAN MERELY AGREEABLE.** `substTyDeep` rebuilds only what CHANGED, so a parameter it could not bind is left in place and never reported — at the result, indistinguishable from a full substitution. Ungated, the dual-run left a residue whose type RENDERS EMPTY, i.e. partly substituted, which recorded as an instance's return type is simply wrong; the obvious guard (`tyc == orig`) caught 11 of them and not the rest. New `tyHasTyVar` is the structural predicate that does separate them, depth-bounded and failing TOWARD the name path (its bound returns true), so an unknown keeps master's behaviour. With the gate: **172 of 239 render identically to the string answer, 22 differ, 45 are skipped** — and all 22 are the nominal-vs-structural class this programme keeps meeting (`P` against `{x:i32}`, `Pair<i32>` against `{a:i32,b:i32}`, `Inner` against `{deep:{n:i32}}`), harmless for #1478's reason: the hand-over changes the recorded TYPE and keeps the recorded NAME. Each class was built by hand and compared against master — a generic returning a nominal alias, returning `T` bound to one, returning `P | null`, and a nested nominal — all identical. **Cumulative across #1478 / #1479 / this: re-parses 1,318 -> 258 (-80%), rows minted by a parse 955 -> 118 (-88%), hand-overs 2 -> 1,062.** Corpus A/B 0 of 2,021. Gates: suite 2,170/0, cases_wasm 1,950/0, native 2,187/0, fixpoint byte-exact, rep-fuzz exact, `mono-tyaram-grid` 0 BAD.
- **The parameter-pin site was PAYING FOR A PARSE IT THEN THREW AWAY — 534 of them, the largest remaining re-parse site; `emit_rewrite` now resolves no type from a name at all** — `synthParamAnnots` synthesized an un-annotated parameter's pin with `synthTypeRef(upin, -1)`, where `-1` means *resolve the name*; `nameToTy` is not hash-consed, so that mints a structurally identical `Ty` row. The very next statement, `recordNodeTyFrom(un, ps[pi])`, then OVERWROTE the result unconditionally with the parameter's own recorded type. The funnel's answer was not merely duplicated — it was discarded, every time, and the site's own header already asserted the type was in hand ("`upin != \"\"` means the param HAS a recorded type"). This is the shape the programme doc names at B8 (*a funnel that records can make its caller's record a duplicate*) in its pure form. **`recordNodeTyFrom` is KEPT rather than deleted, and that is what makes the change exactly equivalent instead of nearly so**: where the handed type is >= 0 it now rewrites the same value, and in the arm the header argues is unreachable (-1) the hand-over declines, `recordClonedNodeTyKnown` falls to its placeholder, and the copy overwrites that placeholder with -1 exactly as before. Deleting it would have changed that arm from -1 to a placeholder. With this and the lambda-return ladder, **`emit_rewrite.vl` imports `synthTypeRef` no more** — all 16 of its synthesis sites hand the type over, and the unused-import warning is what proved it. **Corpus (1,646 files), cumulative across the two slices: re-parses 1,318 -> 986 -> 452 (-66%), rows minted by a parse 955 -> 261 -> 210, hand-overs 2 -> 334 -> 868.** This slice's own 534 parses mostly resolved to EXISTING rows, so it buys compile-time work rather than arena rows — stated because the two are different currencies and only one of them was large here. **Corpus A/B: 0 of 2,021 rows.** Gates: suite 2,170/0, cases_wasm 1,950/0, native 2,187/0, fixpoint byte-exact, rep-fuzz exact, `mono-tyaram-grid` 0 BAD.
- **Band 1 destringify — the lambda-return pin stops re-parsing a name the checker had already resolved; 694 duplicate arena rows retired, 0 corpus rows moved** — `synthRetAnnots`' ladder pins an anonymous lambda's inferred return through 15 arms, every one of them `fn.fnRet = synthTypeRef(ctx, -1)`, and `-1` means *parse the name back*. `nameToTy` does not hash-cons, so each parse MINTS a structurally identical `Ty` row and answers with its index — the hazard `synthTypeRef`'s own header states. The type was never missing: `inferRetTyIx` has been recorded beside `inferRetTy` since #1046 and had no keyed reader, so the emitter re-derived what the recorder had banked. New `inferRetTyIxOf` / `inferRetTyIxByNode` are that reader, and the ladder hands the index over. **Measured over the corpus (1,646 files): re-parses 1,318 -> 986, arena rows minted BY a re-parse 955 -> 261 (-73%), total arena rows 115,595 -> 114,900.** The re-parse surface is 91% `synthTypeRef` (1,200 of 1,318) against 118 for `recordClonedNodeTy`, and 15 of its 31 call sites are this one ladder — which is why one change moves three quarters of the rows. **Zero on the compiler's own source**, so this is a cost paid by generic- and closure-heavy user code (175 of 1,646 files reach it; the worst spends 40 of its 482 rows on duplicates), not by the self-compile. **The dual-run disagrees 211 times in 1,007 and the hand-over is still right** — because it changes the recorded TYPE and not the recorded NAME. The disagreements are alias-vs-expansion (`K0` renders `string`, `P | null` renders `{x:i32}|null`) and rendering WHITESPACE (`{x: i32}` vs `{x:i32}`); the name is what preserves the local spelling, exactly as the 2026-08 ruling requires, and `synthTypeRefTy` keeps it. Verified rather than argued: the litunion-alias lambda return, the litunion-arm value union, the nullable-struct-alias return, the inline-shape return and the map return all behave identically to master, and **the corpus A/B moves 0 of 2,021 rows**. Gates: suite 2,170/0, cases_wasm 1,950/0, native 2,187/0, fixpoint byte-exact, rep-fuzz exact, `mono-tyaram-grid` 0 BAD.
- **The ALIAS-APPLICATION half of the generic-original hole, left open by #1473 — `Box<T>` at a type parameter was check-clean INVALID WASM, in TWO spellings** — `function len<T>(b: Box<T>)` over `type Box<X> = { v: X[] }` is a generic ORIGINAL's annotation: it denotes no concrete struct, so `gaeEnsure` must intern nothing, because the original is replaced by its instances and each instance re-runs the pass over its PINNED name (`Box<i32>`). Without the skip `gaeApplyFieldTy` substituted the parameter FOR ITSELF — the alias body `X[]` under `X := T` is `T[]` — and the row interned with the type parameter read as a NOMINAL element name. #1473 closed exactly this for the INLINE spelling `function len<T>(b: { v: T[] })`, which is a loud reject; both alias spellings were `vl check --codegen` rc 0 with `vl run` "expected (ref null $type), found i32". **The second spelling produces BYTE-IDENTICAL substituted field text**, which is what makes it one hole and not two: `{ v: X[] }` at `X := T` and `{ v: X }` at `X := T[]` both give `v: T[]`. The guard peels to the leaf exactly as `genTyParamArr` does — its header states the rule for its own half ("`T[][]` is as much a type-parameter array as `T[]` is") and both halves peel or neither is right. **THE SHADOW RULING NEEDS THE UNION OF TWO SETS, AND EITHER ALONE IS A REGRESSION.** `gaeParamShadowed` is the binding question's own bound — a parameter named for a PRIMITIVE binds nothing — and without it `Box<f64>` beside `function idF64<f64>(…)`, a CONCRETE application, was skipped and failed with "only i32, i64, f64, f32, boolean, struct, union, array, or string parameters are supported" on a program master runs. `genTyParamArr`'s array accept set is NOT a substitute: its own header says it works only inside `collectA`'s cascade, whose earlier arms already claim the `string[]` / `f64[]` / `i64[]` / `f32[]` spellings, so lifting it out silently drops them. It is still needed for what `gaeParamShadowed` does not cover — a parameter shadowing a DECLARED type. Both directions are pinned by the control file, which now carries the shadowed application, a concrete `Box<i32>`, a shadowed application with no generic function present, the `f64` and `string` primitive-named parameters, and a concrete `Cell<i32[]>` argument the leaf peel must not claim. **Corpus A/B: 3 of 2,021** — two new fixtures, plus `memory/flat-generic-rows-stride.vl`, whose output and diagnostics are byte-identical and whose MODULE SHRINKS BY 6 BYTES: its `type Rows<T> = { base: i32, count: i32 }` never mentions `T`, so master interned a redundant generic-original row beside the real `Rows<TValue>` / `Rows<Node>` instances. - **An inline shape over a type PARAMETER crashed the compiler, because the SHAPE-field classifier could not see type parameters and the ARRAY one could** — `function f<T>(v: { g: T[] })` is a generic ORIGINAL: it declares no concrete struct, exactly as a generic ALIAS declares none, so its shape must never be interned (the instances carry pinned concrete names, and it is those that reach the type section). `collectA`'s `genTyParamArr` arm has skipped a bare `T[]` PARAMETER since #1218; the shape-FIELD classifier in `internInlineShapeTy` never asked, so it resolved the field's element through the NOMINAL NAME PATH — and a type parameter is not a nominal name. **Two outcomes, neither a diagnostic.** With no type named `T` in scope the element resolved to `""`, the field's ref-list slot was never interned, and `pushFieldStorage` read `rlWrapIdx[-1]` — an out-of-bounds CRASH of the compiler, under `vl check --codegen` too; that read is written to fail LOUDLY (`ref-list field element type is not interned`) and the `-1` slipped past its `refSlot >= rlWrapIdx.length` bound on the low side, the one side it does not test. With a REAL `type T` also in scope the element resolved to THAT UNRELATED TYPE and the field got a wrapper: `vl check --codegen` rc 0, `vl run` invalid wasm. **ORDERING WAS THE WHOLE OF THE BUG and the classifier arms were already written.** `genTyParamNames` was gathered INSIDE `collectA`, which the pass table runs AFTER `collectS` — the pass that interns shapes. At intern time the registry was empty, so every membership test short-circuited to `false` on its own first line. The gather is now its own pass (`collectTyParams`) ahead of `collectU`/`collectS`, with `collectU`/`collectS`/`collectA` declaring the dependency; `collectA` still calls it, so the `collectA#2` re-run re-gathers over the instances monomorphization minted. **The skip is the ARRAY form and the FuncDecl half only, and both restrictions are rulings rather than caution.** A parameter that also names a real type binds nothing (`genTyParamArr`'s header states it; `generics/type-param-shadows-alias-through-constructors.vl` pins it at eight constructors), and `genTyParamArr` carries that shadow guard as its `nameIsI32Array`/`nameIsRefArray` arms — a BARE-name membership test has none, and using one skipped `{a: Id}` beside `type Id = i32`, a shape whose field is a genuine `i32`, reddening that fixture. The generic-ALIAS half needs no arm either: `collectGenAliasShapes` interns each application's substituted shape, and routing it through this skip reddened the same fixture at `type ShapeInArrK<K>` — a litunion array is outside `checkArrName`'s accept set, so the shadow guard cannot see it. The bare `{g: T}` form never reached a struct row at all, `monoArgTyName` declining a bare parameter and monomorphization rejecting the call. **What lands is the diagnostic, not the capability**: an inline-shape argument is still not a supported generic argument type and now says so, positioned, at both `vl check --codegen` and `vl run`. **Corpus A/B vs master over all 2,015 `tests/cases` files** ({exit, wasm sha256, diagnostic text}): 6 rows moved, and the 5 that are not this fixture predate it on the branch (two storage-class rejects, two diagnostics whose new text is what their own `@error` directive asks for, one the void ruling). - **A union with TWO function-typed arms now answers `is` by SIGNATURE — `x is F` had been a CONSTANT TRUE across every function arm** — `type U = ((i32) => i32) | ((i32) => string)` with a value built through the second arm took the `is F` branch, `vl check` rc 0, valid module, no diagnostic. The cause is one line: `unMemAtomKind` answers value-atom kind 11 for EVERY `TyFunc` whatever its signature, and `scalarTagOfKind(11)` is the box tag, so both arms boxed and both tests compared the same constant. **This shares ROOT A with #1340 (one tag cannot separate two arms) and reaches a DIFFERENT branch of it, so #1340's fix could not have moved it**: the membership arms #1340 and #1380 added are gated on a tested type with literal MEMBERS (`tyLitMemberTexts` / `tyNumLitMemberTexts`, both empty for a `TyFunc`), so a function tested type is not a candidate for them — it falls to the bare `struct.get 0 0 / i32.const 11 / i32.eq`, which the disassembly confirms. **Membership does not transfer either**, and for a structural reason rather than a gate: `K ⊆ string` gives a literal union a runtime-observable member set to test against, while a function value's signature is not observable in the fat pointer at all (`{env, table index}` — two fields, no discriminant). What DOES transfer is the shape the REF-ARRAY band already had — `F[] | G[]` has always discriminated because `refArrSlotTag` keys on the element's interned reflist SLOT — so a bare function arm now tags on the slot its CANONICAL STRUCTURAL RENDER interns at (`cloSigTagSlot` → `cloSigSlotTag`), with the box side reading the value's own checked type (`nodeTyIxOf`) and the test side the tested type, through one composition (`cloArmTagOfTy`) so the two cannot drift. The tag space above the value-atom band now interleaves THREE slot-keyed tables by residue mod 3 instead of two by parity (ref-arrays `+13+3s`, maps `+17+3s`, closure signatures `+15+3(s−1)`, with the FIRST signature keeping kind 11's offset so a single-signature program's bytes do not move). Measured on **112 constructed cells** across four grids — what differs between the arms (return / param / arity / zero-arity / both), which arm the value is BUILT through and which is TESTED (every cell pinned in both directions), the receiver (module global / local / parameter / struct field / call result), the carrier (lambda literal / named-function reference / a box passed through without re-boxing), the arm count (2 and 3), the partner arm (`string`, `i32`, `null`, a struct, `{[string]: i32}`, `i32[]`, `F[]`) and the spelling (alias / inline / an alias-typed param): **51 RUN-WRONG + 1 wrong-branch `indirect call type mismatch` trap → 0**, and every masked-correct cell stays correct. The wrong answer was CONSTANT TRUE in both directions and at every receiver — all 51 are cells whose correct answer is `no`. **Three measured negatives, each moved out of the family by its own control and each unchanged by this fix**: boxing an i32 into a closure-armed union emits invalid wasm (`expected anyref, found i32`) with ONE function arm too, so it is not about two arms; a map VALUE boxed into a closure-armed union is the loud `a map value is not a supported union member` (the family's 1 EMIT-REJECT cell, a union-member restriction and not an `is` lowering); and `==` over a closure-armed union is already the loud `` `==` over a struct union is not supported yet ``, so the union-equality tag sites cannot see a closure arm. **Byte identity: 1966 of 2002 corpus modules unchanged, 36 moved, all 36 still building rc 0.** The attribution was measured rather than argued, with a third build carrying ONLY the band restride: **34 of the 36 move under the restride alone** (a union arm at reflist slot ≥ 1, or a typed map-value slot), **3 under the closure-signature band** (the files that intern two or more closure signatures), 1 under both. The prediction going in was "the same 36 either way" and it was wrong in exactly that way, which is what identified the two closure-signature files. (`tests/cases/closures/is-two-function-arms-both-directions.vl`, `is-function-arm-signature-tag-receivers.vl`, `is-function-arm-partner-discrimination.vl`)
- **A wide-scalar PARAMETER decided the width of everything INSIDE the argument, not just the argument — and the axis is the i32 SUB-POSITION, not the array index** — `pendingI64` / `pendingF32` / `pendingF64` say what a bare integer LITERAL standing in an i64 / f32 / f64 slot lowers as. They belong to that ONE literal position, never to the tree around it. `emitCallDirect`'s argument spine seeded the flag from the callee's parameter (`vtKindOfParam(cParams[i]) == "i64"`) and handed it to `emitExprAsI64`, which delegates to plain `emitExpr` for every shape that is not a bare literal — so the seed rode the WHOLE argument subtree and every integer literal anywhere inside took the wide form while its own slot was still i32. `function idi(x: i64) { print(x) }` / `const a: i64[] = [7]` / `idi(a[0])` was `vl check` rc 0, "Checked 1 file, no errors", and a module that does not validate: the disassembly shows `i64.const 0` pushed as the ARRAY INDEX into an i32 bounds check. **The array index is one member of the family and not the axis.** `idi(1 + 2)` and `idi(-7)` carry no index at all and failed identically (an `i32.add` / `i32.sub` handed `i64.const` operands); so did an i32-keyed map KEY (`idi(m[0] ?? 0)`), a `.slice` BOUND (`idi(a.slice(0, 1)[0])`), an index at two depths, an index through a struct-element array, and an index EXPRESSION that merely contains a literal (`a[k - 1]`, `a[a.length - 2]`). The fix is one home — `emitExprWideCleared`, which saves, clears and restores all three seeds around an emit whose width is decided at that emit — used by `emitExprAsI64` and `emitExprAsF32` for the arms that are not the bare-literal peel; `emitExprAsI64`'s literal peel moves ABOVE its `exprIsI64` delegation so the seed still reaches the one shape it exists for, width-identically (`emitNumLitNode` reaches the same `parseI64Pair` + `fbI64Const` pair through its own `exprIsI64` / out-of-i32-range arms). `emitMemIntrinArg`, which was that body spelled out, now IS that call. **The named `pendingI64`/`pendingF32`/`pendingF64` shape was right in two thirds and its named LOCATION was wrong in both directions.** Measured: f32 leaks too, but NOT at the array index — `emitIndex`'s four index arms already clear `pendingF32` per arm, so an f32 element read was correct while `zf32(mf[0] ?? 0.0)` and `zf32(fs.slice(0, 1)[0])` were check-clean invalid wasm; and f64 has no reproducing cell at all in 8 of 8 sub-positions, having no seeding site at a call boundary. Clearing the three flags at the `nd.idxIndex` emissions would therefore have been simultaneously too NARROW (it reaches none of the no-index cells, nor the map key, nor the slice bound) and partly DEAD — and there are SEVEN of those emissions, not ~10: six in `emitIndex` plus the index-ASSIGN target, of which three (the nullable-scalar-list, ref-list and scalar-list arms) already clear the f32 flag and three (the two string-list arms and the raw-string arm) have a string receiver no wide-scalar seed can ever be about. **Measured over 128 cells in four grids, each cell its own compile-and-run against its own `@log` oracle: 94 → 126 correct, 32 moved, 0 backward.** The dimensions MOVED are the i32 sub-position (7 forms), the seeding FLAG (i64 / f32 / f64), the consuming CONTEXT (8 forms: call argument, both argument slots, module-global init, local binding, return, element assign, `.push`, lambda argument), and the element REP (6). Dimensions the filing held fixed and this moved: the index literal's value and form, one and two index depths, where the array lives (module `const` / module `let` / local / parameter), where the call sits (module scope / function body / `while` body / lambda body), and the callee's shape. **The controls are what put the axis on the sub-position rather than on the index**: `idi(1 + 2)` fails with no array present, and `print(a[0])` / `const v: i64 = a[0]` / `return a[0]` / `b[0] = a[0]` / `xs.push(a[0])` are all correct, because only the direct-call spine seeds. The two remaining non-OK cells are correct LOUD checker rejections (`idf(1 + 2)` — i32 → f32 is lossy and wants an explicit `as`). **Every one of the 2,006 corpus files is BYTE-IDENTICAL** (`vl build` digest A/B against the saved pre-fix artifact; 1,641 buildable modules identical byte-for-byte, the 365 non-building files failing identically) — so the flag that this fix "would move corpus bytes" is refuted, and the new fixture is the only file that reddens when that saved artifact is restored as the compiler: 1 predicted, 1 actual. **Measured negatives, each a result**: a LAMBDA callee never reproduced at any cell — `emitCallRef`'s own argument spine already saves and clears all three seeds — and neither did a CAPTURED nested callee; the const-GLOBAL constexpr init path (`emit_sections`' `gck` seeding, the one remaining live consumer of the three flags) is unmoved and correct at every literal shape; and `idi(-9000000000)` was already correct before the fix while `idi(-7)` was not, the wide magnitude reaching `i64.const` through `numLexFitsI32` rather than through the seed. (`tests/cases/numerics/wide-param-context-argument-interior.vl`)
