The annotation ladder had no map/Set arm and no scalar / scalar-list arm, so the argument fell to the `"i32"` catch-all, the instance declared an i32 parameter over a body doing map / string / list work, `vl check` was clean and the module failed validation (`current function requires result type [(ref …)] but callee returns [i32]`). **This settles the inventory's "same root as D4" claim in the direction that matters: same decision site, and D4's fix was PARTIAL.** D4 was closed by adding `nodeTyMapName` / `nodeTyName` arms to the recorded-type branch — the same instrumented probe shows D4's own program never reaches the catch-all now — and that branch cannot answer inside a generic body by construction, so the other branch stayed open. `monoAnnPinName` now answers for a BARE map/Set spelling (`nameIsBareMap`) and for the canonical scalar / scalar-list spellings (`monoScalarAnnName`, EXACT spellings only — an alias, a nullable and every composite still decline, because the pin becomes the instance's parameter annotation and a non-canonical name would rename the rep). **The measured axis is wider than the filing in one direction and narrower in another.** Over a **26-rep × 9-shape grid (234 cells)** the filed shape (a forwarded PARAMETER) fails for the map/Set spellings only — six of them, both KEY flavours and four VALUE reps, not the four filed — while a `T`-ANNOTATED LOCAL inside the same body (`const y: T = x`, a form the filing held fixed) fails for **every** non-i32 rep, because a local has no `paramString` / `paramF64Array`-style annotation probe for the cascade to fall back on. Four dimensions the filing held fixed are each **flat**: two generic levels and three fail alike, arity 1 and 2 alike, `T`-in-return and `T`-in-parameter-only alike, and binding the result to a `const` or reading it straight through alike. The concrete-forwarder control is correct in every cell, before and after. **234-cell grid: 146 → 198 correct, 52 moved (46 from check-clean INVALID WASM, 6 from a loud `monomorphize: expected an array argument` — an `f32[]`-annotated local was the loud half of the same missing arm), 0 backward.** — **The second family is not generic at all.** An f32-element ARRAY LITERAL had no route to the f32 list without an annotation to thread the kind through: the literal family `arrLitIsStr` / `arrLitIsF64` / `arrLitIsI64` **had no f32 member anywhere in the tree**, so `exprF32Array` was the only scalar-list classifier with no `ArrayLit` case, the collection scanner had no f32 arm beside its f64/i64 ones, and `emitArrLit`'s f32 path was reachable only from `pendingListKind == 7`. `[fv]` fell past every list arm to the i32 catch-all and built an i32 backing under f32 elements. The filed case (`gfirst([a])`) is one consumer; **`const xs = [a]` with no generic in the file is the same defect**, and so are a module global, `.push`, `for`-in and `.map`. New `arrLitIsF32` claims a literal whose elements are **all** `exprIsF32` — deliberately narrower than the two siblings' any-element rule, because a MIXED literal is a checker-level union (`[fv, 1]` types `(f32 | i32)[]`) and a bare `[1.5]` is f64 (a float LITERAL is not `exprIsF32`), so every mixed spelling keeps the answer it had. **65-cell element-rep grid (6 reps × 11 argument spellings): 56 → 65, and the axis is the element rep ALONE** — f32 failed at an annotated local, a parameter, a global, a call result and a field read, in 1- and 2-element literals, at both a `T[]` and a `T` parameter, and with both a `T` and an i32 result; every other element rep was correct at all eleven. **20-cell POSITION grid: 15 → 20**, moving four check-clean invalid-wasm cells (inferred local, inferred global, `.push`, `for`-in) and one loud one (`.map result is f32[] but f32 list type not collected`). **D5 and D6 do not share a root**: one is an annotation ladder in the monomorphizer, the other a rep classifier three files away that bites with no generic in sight, and neither fix moves a cell of the other's grid. **The grid caught the one backward cell, which is why the guard exists**: self-identifying the literal made `const xs: f64[] = [fv]` invalid wasm, because `letIsF32Array` fell THROUGH a settled non-f32 annotation to its initialiser — the binding classified f32 while the literal built f64. A settled `f64list`/`i64list`/`strlist`/`list` annotation is now authoritative there, matching what `emitArrLit`'s own threaded-kind rule already says; the nullable niches keep the fall-through their own classifiers need. **1,990 of 1,990 corpus files build byte-identically** (`vl build` digest A/B, base = the published `seed-latest` this branch bootstraps from), and the three new fixtures are the only files that redden when the saved base artifact is restored as the compiler — 3 predicted, 3 actual. Both seed-ladder legs hold, `NATIVE FIXPOINT` byte-for-byte at both. **Measured negatives, each still open and each pre-existing**: a generic forwarder passing an ELEMENT READ of its own `T[]` parameter (`gid(xs[0])`) is unmoved at 10 invalid-wasm + 8 loud of 26 reps — `monoArgTyName`'s `Index` arm claims a STRUCT element only (`isSName`), which is the same shape of hole one arm further along; an UN-ANNOTATED local bound to a generic call returning `i64[]`, inside a generic body, is 1 invalid-wasm cell of 234 with a concrete-outer control that is correct; and a **newly measured** silent cell outside both families — `function wrapc(x: string | null): string | null { return gid(x) }` is `vl check` rc 0 and `expected (ref $type), found (ref null $type)`, with the generic-forwarder spelling of the same program a LOUD reject. (`tests/cases/generics/mono-forwarded-annotation-pin.vl`, `tests/cases/arrays/f32-array-literal-self-classifies.vl`, `tests/cases/arrays/f32-array-literal-map-callback.vl`)
- **A REF-ELEMENT array (`reflist`) captured by a nested function or lambda now works — at ANY capture position, and the deciding axis is the REP, not the array's nesting depth** — a captured `i32[][]` reported `emitProgram: ref valtype with no interned shape`, and so did a captured depth-ONE `S[]`, `U[]`, `((i32) => i32)[]` and `{[string]: V}[]`: all five are the `reflist` VKind, whose `fbValtype` needs the ref-list TABLE SLOT beside its kind, while the scalar-backed arrays (`i32[]`, `string[]`, `f64[]`, `f32[]`, `i64[]`, `boolean[]` — the `list`/`strlist`/`f64list`/… kinds) were always fine. TWO ENDS had to move together, and each alone is worse than neither: `captureValStructIdx` was a SHORTENED MIRROR of `paramStructIdxOf` (it knew the map and struct tables, not the ref-list one), so the env FIELD got no slot; and `exprRefArray`/`refListElemNameOfExpr`/`exprNullableRefArray`/`nulRefArrayInnerSlot` each had local/param/global arms and no CAPTURE arm, so the READ inside the callee did not know it was indexing a ref list — fixing only the read end emits a module that fails to VALIDATE (measured: 4 cells). The kind→table pairing now has **one home**, `annValtypeSlotOf` (`reflist`/`nulreflist` → the ref-list slot, `map`/`nulmap` → the map shape, `variant` → the variant index, else `retStructIndex`), which `paramStructIdxOf` and the capture-env field both resolve through; the capture channel is `capturedRefListSlot`, the ref-list sibling of `capturedStructIndex` on the other index space that one companion number ranges over. The nullable niche (`i32[][] | null`, `S[] | null`) is included. Measured over a constructed 976-cell grid (17 reps × 5 binding forms × 5 capture routes × 8 read forms × populated/empty input, each with the uncaptured read as its oracle): **250 → 878 correct, 628 cells newly accepted, 0 regressions, and 0 cells in the silently-wrong / wrong-eval-count / invalid-wasm / trap / compiler-trap columns before or after**. Every one of the 628 computes the uncaptured control's value on BOTH runtime inputs. Every pre-existing corpus module is BYTE-IDENTICAL (2040/2040) — no heap type is newly interned, so no type index space moved. Still loud, pinned as rejects: ASSIGNING INTO a captured binding (rep-independent), and an UN-ANNOTATED `nulreflist` capture (`letInitCellKind` has no `letIsNulRefArray` arm where `collectLocals` does). A captured nullable MAP/SET is a DIFFERENT decision site (`exprNullableMap`'s own missing capture arm) and is unmoved. (`tests/cases/closures/capture-ref-array-storage-classes.vl`, `capture-nullable-ref-array-narrow.vl`, `capture-ref-array-assign-reject.vl`, `capture-unannotated-nullable-ref-array-reject.vl`)
- **The i32-keyed Map/Set floor listed "a map value" among the positions it supports, then declined one** — `{[string]: {[i32]: boolean}}` raised `an i32-keyed Map/Set is supported as a binding / parameter / return / `| null` / an ARRAY ELEMENT / a closure result / a map value — not inside '{[string]:{[i32]:boolean}}'`, a message that contradicted itself in one line. The five remaining positions are each verified to lower; the false one is gone and the message now names the workaround. (`compiler/emit_collect.vl`, `tests/cases/maps/error-i32-keyed-position-*.vl`)
- **`print(<literal-union atom>)` evaluated its argument ONCE PER MEMBER — a call argument ran once per member of the type** — the atom→string widen had TWO homes: the shared `select` tower (`emitAtomToStr`, reached by `+` concat, a `string` argument position, a `string` return), which stashes the interned id once and re-reads the STASH per member; and a hand-rolled per-member `if id == <member> { print "<member>" }` chain in the print handler, which re-read the RECEIVER per member. Re-reading a place is free; re-reading a CALL is another call. So `type K = "a" | "b"` with `function f(): K { print("S") return "a" }` printed `S` / `a` / `S` — `vl check` clean, module valid, right value, **wrong number of evaluations**. The count tracked the member count EXACTLY (2 members → 2 calls, 3 → 3, 4 → 4, 5 → 5), and the `K | null` niche re-read once more for its sentinel arm (members+1: 3 for a 2-member niche, 4 for a 3-member one) — which is what identifies the re-read as the mechanism rather than a guess about it. Print now routes through the same tower every other atom consumer already used, and the niche's sentinel test and member widen both read the ONE stashed id (`emitStashAtomId`, the single home for "read the atom"). Measured on **1800 cells** (24 receiver reps incl. all eleven `VKind` nullable niches × 8 receiver forms × 9 consumer positions), graded on the EVALUATION COUNT as a first-class column: **check-clean silently wrong 20 → 0, correct 868 → 888**, with loud reject (829), check-clean invalid wasm (49), traps (2) and N/A (32) all **unchanged** — the fix accepts nothing new. An independent 70-cell grid probing a GLOBAL MUTATION instead of a print marker moves the same way (17 → 0). **Every affected cell is a receiver that is not a re-readable place** — a call, a closure call, a UFCS method call, and a `??` whose LHS is a call — at the `print` position only; the other 8 consumer positions were already correct because they reach the shared tower. **The loud-reject alternative was built and rejected on its numbers**: gating print on `unionEqOperandOk` turns the same 20 cells into loud rejects instead of correct ones AND breaks **42 of 1904 corpus files** that build today (RC-DIFF 42 → the fix shipped is RC-DIFF **0**). A byte-preserving variant that keeps the if-chain for places and stashes only for non-places was also built: behaviourally identical (0 of 1800 cells differ) but it keeps two homes, costs **+166 compiler bytes**, and gives up 26 KB of the emitted-code saving. Collapsing N per-member streaming loops to one makes **119 of 1906 corpus modules smaller, −33,140 bytes total, +0 larger**, and the compiler itself 4 bytes smaller. 1454 of 1906 corpus modules are byte-identical; the `string`, `i32`, inline-litunion, concat and `==` controls are byte-identical, and the bind-first control (`const v = f(); print(v)`) is deliberately NOT — it is a named-atom print and takes the tower too, 49 bytes smaller. Remaining cost, measured in the disassembly: a member text that is not also a source string literal is not in the string pool, so the tower materializes it with a 1-element `array.new_fixed` on every print even when another arm is selected (N−1 unused tiny allocations, the same trade `emitAtomToStr` already made everywhere else). Pooling litunion member texts would remove it. (`tests/cases/literal-unions/atom-print-call-evaluated-once.vl`, `atom-niche-print-call-evaluated-once.vl`)
- **`%` is INTEGER-ONLY — a float operand was type-checking clean and emitting `i32.rem_s` over an f64** — the f32 and f64 slices of `emitBinExprNode` ask their opcode table for the operator, get `-1` because wasm has no `f64.rem`/`f32.rem`, and **fell through to the i32 tail at the bottom of the function**, which emitted an i32 opcode over an operand already on the stack as a float. In a 272-cell grid (17 operators × 16 ordered scalar pairings, annotated params) `%` was the **only** operator with broken cells — **6 of 272**: `f64%f64`, `f64%f32`, `f32%f32`, `f32%f64`, `f64%i32`, `i32%f64`, plus every literal (`5.5 % 2.0`) and inferred-local spelling of the same. Both operands annotated, so this was never an inference gap. **The lowering that looks obvious is not fmod, and measuring it is what decided the fix**: against Rust's `%` on f64 (which IS C `fmod`) the identity `a - trunc(a / b) * b` disagrees on **86,066 of 200,000** random pairs at ordinary magnitudes (43.0%) and **98,980 of 200,000** across |exp| ≤ 300 (49.5%), and on 10 of 33 curated edge cases — every `|a / b|` past 2^53 (`1e17 % 3` → 0, not 1), a `-0.0` dividend (sign lost), an infinite divisor (NaN instead of the dividend), and `f64::MAX % 3` overflows to `-inf`. Accepting through it would have traded a loud invalid-wasm reject for silent numeric corruption. So `%` joins the bitwise/shift family's integer-only rule and reports in its diagnostic shape, naming both operands — `operator '%' is integer-only, got i32 and f64` — and the three mixed pairings that previously said `mixes f32 and i32` now name the actual reason. **Verdicts move in one direction only: the grid's CLEAN count is unchanged at 128 and REJECT is +6, exactly the six formerly-broken cells**; no corpus program uses float `%`. Independently, both wide-scalar slices now `emitFail` on a `-1` opcode instead of falling through, so the i32 tail is unreachable with a float on the stack however that state is entered — verified by removing the checker rule and watching `operator '%' has no f64 form` / `no f32 form` fire at both widths. Exact float remainder needs a scaled shift-and-subtract helper (verified exact: 0 mismatches over the same 400,000 pairs), and when it lands it belongs beside `sqrt`/`trunc`/`copysign` as a float intrinsic — the shape every float operation with no operator spelling already uses. (`tests/cases/arith/float-remainder-reject.vl`, `float-remainder-f32-reject.vl`, `integer-remainder-control.vl`)
- **A CAPTURE's type now reaches the code that reads it — the env FIELD and the READ were typed by different answers** — `captureValKind` types each env-struct field, and the classifiers type the read out of it; the scalar classifiers (`exprIsF64`/`exprIsI64`/`exprIsF32`) had no capture arm and `capturedKindOf` was gated on `fnEnvIdx`, which `mAssignTypeIndices` mints only after every classification pass. Two `vl check`-clean invalid-wasm families followed. **(1)** `function outer(n) { function inner(x: f64) { x + n } inner(1.5) }` emitted a correct `(struct (field f64))` env and then widened the read through `f64.convert_i32_s` — "expected i32, found f64", one spurious instruction. **(2)** `function joinCaps(a: string, b: string) { function catCaps() a + b; catCaps() }` — every binding annotated, nothing generic — inferred `catCaps` at `(result i32)` around a body producing a string ref, because return inference could not see a capture at all. **This is not what the item (workboard D8) was filed as.** It was filed as the function-value ABI's un-annotated-parameter i32 default; re-gridding **4 operand types × 11 operators × 2 spellings × 4 annotation variants = 352 cells** shows annotating the NESTED parameter leaves the f64 half **11 of 11** broken in both spellings while annotating the OUTER binding fixes it, and the capture-only spelling fails identically with everything annotated. The capture-only spelling goes **12 invalid-wasm cells → 1 in every annotation variant** and is now annotation-neutral; the param-taking spelling goes **11 → 1**; the 1 left in each is `f64 %`, which is D7's emitter fall-through and fails fully annotated. **150 running cells print exactly what their fully-annotated twin prints.** What remains is the **i32 arm's loud value-floor reject** (`emitClosureValue`) for a nested function whose own parameter is un-annotated — that one IS the parameter default, it fires whether or not the outer binding is annotated, and it is correct. Each of the three scalar arms was measured failing with it removed, so none is dead. (`tests/cases/functions/capture-scalar-kind.vl`, `capture-only-return-kind.vl`)
- **A top-level scalar `let`/`const` becomes a START-FUNCTION LOCAL, not a module global (#1321)** — the script spelling of a hot loop was **4.13× slower** than the identical loop inside a function, because a top-level binding emitted `global.get`/`global.set` per iteration where an in-function one emitted `local.get`/`local.set`; wasmtime keeps locals in registers but must treat a mutable global as observable state. `wasm-opt --closed-world -O3 --gufa` does NOT promote them (measured: 16.5 s after, 7 global ops still in the loop, and the globals were neither exported nor read by a second function), so this belonged in the emitter. A 10^9-iteration f64 accumulate goes **1,767 ms → 497 ms** and now **converges with the in-function spelling** (572 ms) — the two emit byte-identical loop bodies, which is what makes the convergence the instrument rather than the ratio. **182 of 1,442 buildable corpus programs promote, 538 bindings**; corpus bytes net **−974** with **+0 on every one of the 1,260 programs that promote nothing**. A binding stays a global if it is a ref cell, if its module has no top-level statements, or if its name appears in ANY function body (name-keyed, so a same-named local vetoes conservatively — the walk runs in the direction where a hole costs population, not soundness). Refuted while building: relaxing the no-top-level-statements gate does NOT help the compiler — a probe rebuilt with it relaxed still emits 2,280 globals unchanged, because module state read from function bodies is what a compiler's top level IS, so the self-compile qualifies zero bindings. Also: the behavioural corpus is BLIND to this (a sabotage disabling promotion leaves every `@log` correct), which is why the new suite counts global-section entries; and 8 corpus RUN divergences are the hex address in a wasm trap backtrace, identical after normalizing `0x<hex>` (verified: 27 trap fixtures, 8 differ, 8 address-only, 0 real). (`docs/internals/perf-program.md` §11, `tests/vl_global_promotion_test.ts`, `tests/cases/globals/promoted-scalar-start-locals.vl`)
- **The union box is built at ONE site per function, not one per ARM (#1322)** — a two-armed producer emitted a `struct.new` in each `return`, and binaryen's Heap2Local scalarizes PER ALLOCATION SITE, so two sites merging into one returned value meant neither could own it and the box survived every optimization level. The return path now writes a tag local and a payload local and branches to a single exit that constructs the box once: **box sites 2 → 1 per two-armed producer, 78 removed corpus-wide across 76 functions in 54 modules.** **The win is entirely downstream of `wasm-opt` and must be quoted with its level** — on a 100 M-trip loop, min-of-5: plain `vl build` 535 → 530 ms (a WASH), `-O` 304 → **173 ms (1.76×)**, `-O3` 293 → 176 ms (1.66×). The sink removes a *merge point*, not an allocation — A and B both allocate exactly two objects per trip before the optimizer, since only one of A's two sites executes on any given trip. Four claims of the design doc it implements were refuted by building it: the 2.0–2.2× figure is **V8-only** (natively 1.7×); the box melts at plain **`-O`**, a rung earlier than "closed-world type refinement" predicted, because the ceiling was never binaryen's but the site count; the 309-site population is ~4× what is removable (a *k*-return function loses *k−1*, a single-exit function loses none); and the cost estimate touched none of the 18 construction sites — the fix is a peephole at three return terminators. Bytes +18 per sunk function (corpus +1,379, +0.069%) while the `-O3` artifact gets SMALLER. Does not sink: a `return` of an if-expression (`emitUnionIfValue` still boxes per arm — the largest remaining shape), a `let` assigned on two branches, passthrough returns, single-exit functions. (`docs/internals/unboxed-union-rep-design.md` §10, `tests/vl_union_return_sink_test.ts`)
- **The union box sinks past a BINDING's if-arms too — and this one is worth something WITHOUT `wasm-opt`** — `const u: A | B = if c { a } else { b }` had **three** construction sites, not two, and the third is the finding: the binding's slot is a non-null `(ref $uBox)`, which is not defaultable, and a void-`if` merge does not satisfy the validator's definite-assignment check, so the rep's NULL BOX was built before the `if` and overwritten on every path — allocated every trip, read by nothing. Each arm now writes the tag/payload pair and the single `struct.new` follows the `if`; that one store dominates every read, so the pre-seed is not merely redundant but unnecessary and the shape drops from `arms + 1` sites to ONE. **Box sites 1,551 → 1,498 corpus-wide (53 removed over 6 modules), +55 B (+0.0022%), 6 up 0 down**, and the six byte movers are exactly the six site movers. **The rung story is NOT #1322's and must not be quoted as it** — that sink removes a merge point, so A and B allocate identically and plain `vl build` was a wash; this one removes an ALLOCATION, so on a 100 M-trip loop (CPU ms, min-of-7 interleaved, load 80–88) plain `vl build` is **1.36×** (750 → 551), `-O` **1.68×** (594 → 354), `-O3` 1.50× (558 → 372), distributions non-overlapping at every rung. The two sinks SHARE one reserved local pair, because their live ranges cannot interleave — a sunk `return` branches straight to the exit block, a sunk arm falls straight to the `if`'s `end`, and an arm's value is an expression so no `return` can be emitted between an arm's write and the construction that reads it (witnessed both nestings by `tests/cases/unions/let-if-binding-sink-with-returns.vl`). Two arm shapes DECLINE and the gate is static: a nested if-expression arm ends in a frame `end` rather than a construction, and a PASSTHROUGH arm builds nothing — unlike a declining return, a declining arm cannot be tolerated per-arm, since the single join would have to unbox and rebuild it, trading no site for an allocation that executes every trip. **What is left of §10.4's `let` row is not a sink**: `let u = a; if c { u = b }` writes the slot from two separate statements with no merge point, and collapsing it means holding the pair across a liveness window — a rep question, filed with its before-number rather than forced. (`docs/internals/unboxed-union-rep-design.md` §12, `tests/fixtures/opt-melt/union-sink-let-if.vl`, `tests/cases/unions/let-if-binding-arms.vl`)
- **B0 Numeric literals, i32/f64 arithmetic, if/while, direct calls, start fn, memory builtins.**
- **B1 Allocation = WasmGC** (binaryen 116→130 for GC API). → `DECISIONS.md`
- **B2 Numeric codegen (core)** — i64 & f32 arithmetic + float `/` & comparisons; i64/f32 type mappings; range-aware integer-literal defaults.
- **B2 Value-level bitwise/shift operators** — `& | ^ ~ << >> >>>`, integer-only, i32/i64 → native wasm instructions (#99; H4.2 self-host gap).
- **B2 Explicit numeric casts — `x as T`** — a postfix, CHECKED conversion between numeric scalars (`i32`/`i64`/`f32`/`f64`). Unlike the implicit lossless-widening rule (`numWidensName`, unchanged), `as` permits EVERY direction: widenings + the lossy widenings (`i32→f32`, `i64→f64/f32`), narrowings (`i64→i32` wrap, `f64→f32` demote) and int↔float. Float→int uses the TRAPPING `trunc` family — NaN / out-of-range aborts (traps-are-for-bugs). `as` is a CONTEXTUAL keyword (stays an identifier; `import { a as b }` untouched), binds tighter than every binary operator (`a + b as f64` is `a + (b as f64)`), and is chainable. The lossy-implicit reject now points at `as`. Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/numerics/as-cast-*.vl`)
- **B3 First-class functions / indirect calls + per-shape monomorphization** — fat-pointer closure `{ tableIndex, env }`; each call site gets a fresh signature keyed by wasm param signature.
- **B4 Closures (first WasmGC codegen)** — nested decls, capture analysis, env struct, escaping closures.
- **B5 Objects (core)** — literals, read/write, nested, f64 fields, args/returns, reassignment, captured-in-closures, excess-property width subtyping, function-valued fields + member-call; method-shorthand `{ add(a,b){…} }`.
- **B6 Growable `T[]` (core)** — `{backing,len,cap}` WasmGC struct monomorphized per element type; 2× growth; `[...]` seed; `a[i]`/`a[i]=v` trap on OOB; `.length`/`.get(i)`/`push`/`pop`/`clear`/`+`; for-in + structural equality updated. (`compiler/builtins/lists.ts`; → `docs/collections-design.md`) `.capacity` removed (leaky growth detail; `cap` stays internal). → `DECISIONS.md`
- **B6b Build-loop fusion** — a loop building a fresh local list by one push per iteration — `for i in A to B [step S] { a.push(e) }` or the counter-`while` `while i <cmp> N { a.push(e); i = i ± C }` — lowers to a pre-sized indexed fill, ~3–4× faster than per-element push (elides the frontier bounds-check the engine can't); unified recognizer → one fill core, guard-gated, falls back to push otherwise. (`tests/cases/lists/build-fusion-*`; → `DECISIONS.md`)
- **B6a `Map` + `Set` (core)** — ordered open-addressing hash maps (Python-dict shape) over WasmGC; string keys; `Map[k]: V|null`; `.size`/`.get`/`.has`/`.set`/`.delete`/`.keys()`/`.values()`; `Set` `.add`/`.has`/`.delete`; deterministic insertion-order iteration; tombstone-aware probing + compaction. (`compiler/builtins/maps.ts`) A map whose VALUE type is the enclosing recursive type (`{ [string]: Tree }`) now compiles — the hash struct + its `vals` array thread into the type's WasmGC rec group (mirrors A11 array-element recursion). (`tests/cases/types/recursive-map-value.vl`)
- **B6a perf: per-entry stored hash** — the open-addressing map now stores a parallel `hashes` i32 array (Python-dict model): a probe hashes the key once and gates the expensive (string) `keyEq` behind a stored-hash `i32.eq`, so the full key compare runs only on a genuine hash match; a resize re-places from the stored hash instead of re-hashing every key. `set`/grow/compact maintain the array; appended as map-struct field 6 at both `toWasm.ts` sites (standalone `mapType` + the recursive-map-value rec-group path), so existing field indices are unchanged.
- **B6a i32-keyed `Map`/`Set` (webcraft P2)** — `{[i32]: V}` and `{[i32]: boolean}` `Set` ship the whole string-keyed surface (`m[k]`, `m[k] = v`, `.set`/`.get`/`.has`/`.delete`/`.length`/`.keys()`/`.values()`, `.add`, and the bare `for k in m`) over the SAME ordered open-addressing rep with ONE field re-typed: `keys` is the i32 list instead of the string-ref list. Only the hash (a murmur3 finalizer mix — an identity hash clusters fourCC tables into a linear scan) and the compare (`i32.eq` instead of a call into `__str_eq__`) differ; the probe's tombstone skip, the stored-hash gate, `__map_resize__`, the compaction and the insertion-ORDERED iteration are literally shared, so a delete/re-insert interleaving prints the same order under both keys. The emitter threads the key as TWO new shape sentinels (`-4` map, `-5` Set) disjoint from the string-keyed ones and both `< 0`, so every VALUE-side classifier that reads "slot < 0 ⇒ the mono i32/boolean rep" stays correct untaught — which is exactly the value set this rep supports. The op-name SCANS (`exprHasMapOp`/`blockHasMapOp`/`fnHasMap`/`forInMapRecv`/`startFnDetectScratch`) are key-BLIND by construction and needed nothing added; `tests/cases/maps/i32-keyed-scratch-frame-reservation.vl` is the measurement rather than the argument. Everything is usage-gated (`mI32Used`): the extra map struct, the extra `__map_probe_i32__` helper and the 5 extra scratch locals appear only in a module that has an i32-keyed map, and the whole 1,665-file corpus builds BYTE-IDENTICAL. **Deferred, loud-reject:** a REF/WIDE value (`string`/struct/list/`f64`/`i64`/`f32`) and the composition positions (struct or variant FIELD, array ELEMENT, map VALUE) — each needs a per-value i32-keyed map struct; `vl build` exits 1 naming the value type or the position. (`tests/cases/maps/i32-keyed-*.vl`, `tests/cases/sets/i32-keyed-basics.vl`, `tests/cases/maps/error-i32-keyed-*.vl`)

THE FIX IS ONE COLUMN, NOT A SECOND MACHINE: an mv slot's identity was the VALUE type and is now the (KEY, VALUE) PAIR — `mvKeyI32` beside `mvValName`, with a `K`-suffixed sibling of each slot resolver taking the key beside the value, and `mvShapeOfMapName` reading the key off the map spelling so no caller had to be taught. The map struct's only key-varying field is field 0 (`keys`), so `repMapValSlotsTwin` gained a key guard and the type-section emit reads the same column. Downstream the slot is SELF-DESCRIBING — `mapTypeIdxOf`, the `cm*` emit accessors and the typed per-slot scratch frames needed no second parameter; the ~5 sites that tested the `-4`/`-5` mono sentinel directly (`forInElemKind`'s two arms, the i32-list and string-list `.keys()` claims, `emitMapKeys`, `emitMapRecvKey`) route through one predicate, `mapShapeKeyI32`. FIXED ON THE STRING SIDE TOO: `m[k] = Map()` never seeded the shape the inner `Map()` builds, which was silently right only while the inner map was mono — `{[string]: {[string]: string}}` was invalid wasm at `vl check` rc 0 (`tests/cases/maps/nested-map-ref-value-store.vl` fails on master). The whole 1,678-file corpus still builds BYTE-IDENTICAL (field 5 1,678 same). **Still a loud reject:** a struct/variant FIELD (the field ROW erases the key — needs a key column on both field tables) and an array ELEMENT (kept deliberately: the string-keyed list-of-maps store is itself invalid wasm for 12 of 14 value types). (`tests/cases/maps/i32-keyed-ref-value*.vl`, `tests/cases/maps/error-i32-keyed-position-*.vl`)

THE ROOT IS THE CONSTRUCTOR, NOT THE STORE — #1286 filed this as a list-of-maps STORE family (`xs[0][k] = v`), and the store is innocent: build the map first (`const m: {[string]: string} = Map()`, then `[m]`) and the identical store RUNS on master. `[Map()]` ALONE, with no store in the program, is the invalid module. The element `Map()` reached `emitMapNew` with the ambient `pendingMapSlot` (-1, the MONO struct) and `struct.new`'d that into a backing typed to the element's own per-value struct — the SAME one line #1286 fixed one composition position over, at the map VALUE. It was silently right for exactly the three values whose struct IS the mono one (`i32`, `boolean`, and a literal-union ATOM — the third the filing's "2 of 14" count missed; a NULLABLE atom is not mono, it interns a box-backed slot). The seed is `rlElemMapShape`, the ref-list twin of `mvInnerMapShape`, applied at `emitArr` (a literal) and `refElemValueCtx` (`.push` and `xs[i] = Map()`) — and the two boundaries' element-context gates, which had each been hand-taught "nested list" plus the nullable-CLOSURE niche and neither taught MAP, become one `rlElemNeedsValueCtx`. THE MECHANISM WAS THE ONE #1286 FILED, unchanged on measurement: a field ROW records the map's VALUE and nothing else (`sFieldElemName` holds `V`, the D5 sidecar holds V's arena type) while an mv slot's identity is the (KEY, VALUE) pair, so an i32-keyed map field would have resolved its STRING-keyed twin's map struct — a string-ref keys list read as an i32 list, invalid wasm that `vl check` cannot see. Byte delta +291. **Still a loud reject, each at its real condition:** a plain-`i32`-valued map's narrowed bare read (every i32 is a legal value — there is no spare sentinel, and `m[k] ?? d` is the spelling), and a BARE `m.get(k)` with no `??` in any position (`callee is not a function name` — only the FUSED method form lowers). - **B6a the INDEX-WRITE spelling pins an inferred map, and an inferred key is gated in the same channel as an annotated one (E2)** — two checker-side changes over the usage-driven `Map()`/`Set()` pin. (1) **`m[k] = v` did not pin the hole `m.set(k, v)` pins.** A bare `const m = Map()` written only through the index spelling fell to the `cannot infer a type for 'm' — add a type annotation` floor while BOTH its `.set` twin and its annotated `{[i32]: string}` twin ran — the array arm sitting three lines above it (`xs[i] = v` pins an empty `[]`'s element) is the same rule for the other collection. The pin is **key-BLIND**: the filing named the i32 key, but the string key was refused identically, and the measured population is symmetric. (2) **An inferred key skipped the unsupported-key gate.** The map rep hashes exactly two key types (`mapKeyTySupported`), and only the ANNOTATION was checked against it — so `const m: {[f64]: V} = Map()` is a clean type error while `const m = Map(); m.set(1.5, v)` passed `vl check` and died in codegen as `emitProgram: map key is not a string`. The pin now raises the annotation's own diagnostic (one wording, `mapKeyUnsupportedErr`, shared by both sites; the constructor word follows the flavor, so a `Set()` says `Set`). A hole key (an un-annotated parameter reaching `m.set(k, v)`) is deliberately NOT adjudicated — it is unsolved, and the call site owns it. **The rule for the pinned key is the BASE type**: `m[1] = "x"` pins `i32` and `m["a"] = 1` pins `string`, matching what `.set` has always pinned, so a later `f64`/`string` key is a clean `map key must be i32, got …` rather than a widen. Grid: **1,728 cells** (key type × creation × write spelling × operation × key origin), each twinned against its annotated oracle — **432 → 288 oracle-backed defect cells**, 240 inferred programs newly accepted with stdout **byte-identical to an accepting annotated oracle 240/240** (144 against their own twin, 96 against the base-key twin), 464 cells move from a codegen error to the actionable check error, **0 cells regress**, and the grid found **0 invalid-wasm and 0 silently-wrong-output cells** on either side. Reject parity: **0 of 251 corpus `@error` cases changed verdict** (1,747 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored). **Still open, measured here and filed rather than folded in — two EMITTER shape defects that the `.set` spelling already exposed** (the 288 residue, all `ann=OK`): an un-annotated map-returning function (`function mk() { return Map() }`) emits the default shape for the hole and rejects `emitProgram: map values must be i32 / boolean`, and a bare `Map()` inside an object literal (`const s = { m: Map() }`) rejects `emitProgram: ref valtype with no interned shape` — 144 cells each. - **B6b a nominal newtype can key a `Map`/`Set` — the key gate tests the LOWERING, not the spelling (E2k)** — `mapKeyTySupported` was `k == TY_STR || k == TY_I32`, an ARENA-INDEX compare. A `type Id = new i32` brand is minted as a SECOND arena index over the same `Ty` value and is erased before the emitter runs (`docs/internals/newtype-design.md` §3), so `{[Id]: V}` hashes byte-for-byte as `{[i32]: V}` — yet the index compare refused it, and refused it in BOTH spellings (the annotation and the `m[k] = v` / `.set` pin, which share the gate since E2). The gate is now `mapKeyRepTy`, **THE ONE HOME** for "which hash does this key take": it answers the arena type the keys are hashed AS (`TY_I32` / `TY_STR`, else -1) by reading the STRUCTURE (`tyPrimNameOf`, which also peels a one-member union wrapper, so a brand, a transparent alias and an alias-of-an-alias all reach their base). The two emitter predicates that pick between the string-keyed and the i32-keyed map struct — `nodeTyMapKeyIsI32` (which was itself an index compare, and would have said "string-keyed" about a map whose emit name canons to `{[i32]: V}`) and `emit_rep.tyMapKeyIsI32` (a second, independent structural test) — now project that same answer, so the checker and the emitter cannot drift about it. **The filed shape is refuted and the defect was bigger than filed**: on master an annotated `{[Id]: V}` and its inferred twin agreed in **208 of 208** same-program pairs — both rejected — so this was never "an annotation removes a capability", it was "a newtype cannot key a map at all". Grid **976 cells** (16 key spellings × 7 positions × 7 operations × annotated/inferred): **226 newly accepted, 0 regress, 0 silently-wrong-output, 0 invalid wasm** (452/452 clean modules revalidated under `wasm-opt -O3`); annotated-vs-inferred **byte-identical 100/100**; derived-key-vs-base-key **byte-identical 325/339**, the 14 differing only by an `as`-cast in a GLOBAL INITIALIZER defeating a const-fold, reproduced with no map in the program at all. Reject parity **0 of 299 corpus error-directive cases** changed verdict or message. **Measured negatives, reported rather than forced**: a LITERAL UNION key stays refused — a named string litunion reps as an interned i32 ATOM while its map-key spelling stays `K` at emit, so admitting it would leave the key expression lowering as an atom while the map struct resolved string-keyed (a silent wrong hash, strictly worse than the reject); an INLINE litunion key (`{["a" | "b"]: V}`) is a PARSE error (the key slot is a bare identifier token); and a type VARIABLE in the key slot (`function f<K>(m: {[K]: i32})`) is refused for `i32` as much as for a newtype — key-blind and pre-existing. - **B7 Strings (core)** — WasmGC i32-array of code points; literal, `.length`/`s[i]`, `+`, `==`/`!=`, `print`.
- **B8 Loops (core)** — `for…in` over arrays, direction-aware `step`, single-line block bodies, empty-range warning.
- **B9 `break` / `continue` in codegen** (incl. labelled `break outer`).
- **B10 Unary / prefix / postfix ops** — `-`, `++`/`--`, `!`. → `DECISIONS.md` for `!`-not-`not`.
- **B11 `while true` return analysis** — non-escaping `while true` types as `Never`; no spurious `| null` on functions tail'd by one.
- **B13 Well-known-symbol dispatch (operators + index)** — operator overloading via `"+"` field or free `self` function; `"[]"`/`"[]="` index traps dispatched statically. → `DECISIONS.md`
- **B14 Methods via explicit `self` + UFCS (core)** — free `self`-first function callable as `o.f(args)`; field-then-self-fn resolution; non-`self` functions not instance-reachable. → `DECISIONS.md`
- **B14 Free INDEX OPERATORS (`function "[]"` / `function "[]="`, webcraft P1.1's bracket)** — `x[i]` and `x[i] = v` on a user type lower to a DIRECT call to a free function whose NAME is the operator. A quoted string is the spelling, because `[` and `]` open an index expression everywhere else so the bracket operators have no operator TOKEN (the form accepts every operator name, so `function "+"(self, b)` and `function +(self, b)` are the same function). **Overloadable by receiver**: a program may declare several `"[]"`, one per receiver type — the parser mints a declaration name carrying the receiver (`[]@F32View`; `@` cannot occur in an identifier, so the encoding is total and a genuine duplicate still collides on the ordinary redeclaration rule), and **dispatch is by the receiver's TYPE** (`assignable` to the `self` parameter, the same test UFCS uses), never by the spelling in the name — which is what lets `F32View` and `I32View`, structurally identical and distinguished only by their A14 brands, carry different operators. The `self` annotation is required. **Merge-safe with no alias**: unlike a UFCS member-call, a bracket names nothing, so the checker's operator registry — built from the already-MERGED program — holds mangled names from the start. The checker BANKS its resolution on the site node and the emitter's rewrite reads it back, so the two can never disagree. Nesting composes (`g[i][j]`); `x[i] += v` works and inherits the native-array rule that a compound assignment re-evaluates receiver and index (measured, not new). **The swallow**: array / map / string / hole indexing is untouched — six-channel corpus differential over 1,638 files reads zero rows differing on any field, with a live inverted control. `std:buffer` gains four one-line operators forwarding to its accessors, which lands P1.1's spelling; `x[i]` vs `getF32(x, i)` build modules differing in TWO bytes (the `call` immediate — one extra forward, which `-O3 --closed-world` inlines). (`docs/internals/index-operator-design.md`, `tests/cases/index/operator-*.vl`, `tests/cases/modules/index-operator-import/`, `tests/cases/std/buffer-view-bracket.vl`)
- **B15a Default + optional parameters (webcraft "API ergonomics")** — `p: T = <literal>` and `p?: T` parse, check and lower. **Defaults subsume optionals**: `p?: T` is desugared IN THE PARSER to `p: T | null = null`, so one mechanism serves both spellings and only a faithful-surface marker (`Param.parOpt`, the `IsExpr.isNeg` pattern) survives for the printer to reprint the `?`. A call that omits trailing arguments — or, when NAMED, skips a middle one (`add(1, c: 5)`) — is normalized to the callee's full arity by `emit_rewrite`'s `drwFillDefaults`, which runs BEFORE monomorphization and signature collection, so mono, `collectCloSigs` and the emitter's per-parameter coercion all see an ordinary full-arity call. **The `$fnsig` value-call ABI is untouched, measured not asserted**: `fnSigKeyOf` derives a closure signature key exclusively from the DECLARATION's parameter list, never from a call site's argument count, and the rewrite resolves its callee with the same `fnIndexOfInScopeChain` classifier `emitCall` uses to choose the direct path — so a function VALUE keeps full arity and `const kv = k; kv(1)` stays the arity error it was. The design's four leak points are closed at the declaration, each with a pinned reject: a default must be a **literal** (it is emitted in the CALLER's frame, so a name in it would either read a parameter that does not exist there or capture a caller-local of the same name — this is what makes an earlier-parameter reference and a call/allocation default rejections rather than surprises); a default must be **annotated** (an un-annotated parameter is an inference HOLE each call site pins, so keeping holes and defaults disjoint leaves the monomorphizer's instance keying untouched); defaults are **trailing-only**; and a defaulted parameter's annotation may not name one of the function's own **type parameters** (an omitted argument would drop a binding site). The call-site arity gate becomes a RANGE and the diagnostic names it (`expected 1 to 2, got 0`). A call's declaration is now resolved through `fnDeclScopes`, a stack parallel to `T.scopes` in the shape `constScopes` already establishes, so a NESTED function takes its defaults too and a local binding shadowing a function name answers "not a declaration" and keeps the exact-arity gate. Out of scope by ruling, with a pinned reject: **UFCS** dispatch still matches a `self`-function by exact arity, because widening it there changes what counts as a method call rather than only which arguments get filled. (`tests/cases/functions/default-params.vl`, `optional-params.vl`, `error-default-param-*.vl`, `error-optional-param-*.vl`)
- **std:array — generic array SEARCH helpers (`indexOf`/`lastIndexOf`/`includes`/`count`)** — a new pure-VL std module (`std/array.vl`, the array dual of `std:fmt`), built ENTIRELY on the intrinsic list floor (`.length` / indexing / `==`) — no emitter changes. Each is `<T>`-generic over the element type and takes a `self` first parameter, so `import { indexOf, includes } from "std:array"` then `xs.indexOf(3)` / `ss.includes("b")` read as UFCS methods, one definition serving `i32[]`, `string[]`, … (`==` resolves per element type). This is the SEARCH family (each returns i32 / boolean, building no new collection); the array-BUILDING methods (`slice`, `reverse`, `reduce`, index-aware `map`) are deferred — a generic `const out = []` / a `(x: T) => …` callback inside a generic body does not yet propagate the element type `T` through monomorphization (three distinct generics-monomorphization gaps), so they would mis-lower for a non-i32 element rather than ship i32-only. Native fixpoint byte-identical, deno corpus 542, native sweep PASS=407. (`std/array.vl`, `tests/cases/modules/std-array/entry.vl`)
- **A10 generics: a generic CLOSURE parameter `(A, T) => A` monomorphizes (+ `std:array` `reduce`)** — a `<T>`-generic function may now take a closure parameter whose type rides the type params, so `fold<T, A>(self: T[], f: (A, T) => A, init: A)` instantiates per call shape. The monomorphizer's three type-substitution helpers — `monoAnnHasTyParam` (does the annotation carry a type param?), `monoBindFromAnn` (bind them from the argument's concrete type), `monoSubstAnn` (substitute through the binding) — previously handled bare `T` / `T[]`, a generic-alias application `Head<…>`, and an inline object `{a: T}`, but NOT a FUNCTION-TYPE annotation `(A, T) => A`, so a generic closure param kept its un-substituted annotation and the instance body's `f(…)` value-call resolved no interned `$fnsig`. Each helper gains a function-type branch (recurse into each parameter + the return). The binding is derived from the closure ARGUMENT's function-type NAME, built by a new `monoArgFnTypeName` (`(i32, i32) => i32` from a lambda's concrete params + `cloRetTypeName` result) — distinct from the `"=>sigkey"` pin an UN-annotated closure param wants. This lands `std:array`'s `reduce<T, A>(self, f, init)` (an i32 sum, a string fold) on top of the search family. (An `f64` / `i64` accumulator stays a clean emit error — the non-i32/string closure-value floor, not invalid wasm.) Native fixpoint byte-identical, deno corpus 543, native sweep PASS=408. (`compiler/emit_base.vl`, `compiler/wasmEmit.vl`, `std/array.vl`, `tests/cases/functions/generic-closure-param.vl`)
- **A10 generics: a `boolean[]` argument printed 0/1, and a `string` / `{[K]: V}` LOCAL pinned i32** — two independent roots, each a classifier ladder whose ARM SET was a shortened mirror of a sibling's, measured over a **550-cell grid** (14 argument reps × 8 argument forms × 5 generic shapes, every cell carrying its CONCRETE TWIN in the same file as the oracle and BOTH runtime inputs): **48 check-clean SILENTLY-WRONG-VALUE cells and 15 check-clean INVALID-WASM cells → 0, with 0 cells moved out of `correct`** (286 → 352 correct; the 4 residual invalid-wasm cells are an unrelated concrete accept — an un-annotated `[5]` into an `i64[]` parameter — and the 7 COMPILER TRAPS, a `{[string]: i32}[]` under `<T>(xs: T[])`, are untouched and filed). (1) `listElemIsBool` is the ONE place a boolean element's PRINT formatting is recovered, the i32 list being the shared rep (`nameIsI32Array` says so) — and it resolved its receiver from a `let` local or a module global only, with no PARAM arm and no nested-INDEX arm, the two its rep siblings (`exprStringArray` and family) both carry. Outside a generic the checker's node-type bank answers first and MASKS the gap; inside a monomorphized instance the body is a clone the checker never typed, so the substituted parameter ANNOTATION is the only boolean-ness left and the gap is the whole answer — `gtake<T>(x: T) { print(x[0]) }` over a `boolean[]` printed `1` while `x[0] == false` was correct, so the VALUE was right and only the formatter was wrong. `listAnnNameOf` is one home for the four storage classes a list receiver has (param, local, global, one INDEX level). (2) for that annotation to SAY `boolean[]`, `monoArgTyName` has to spell the pin, and the rep cascade cannot — `exprArray` claimed the argument and answered `i32[]`; three arms now read the CHECKER's own record instead (the element name for a list argument, the whole array type for a nested boolean literal, and the recorded type for a `string` / `{[K]: V}` read, whose `exprString` / `exprMap` Ident arms consult `declaredString` / `declaredMap` — tables `buildLocals` fills POST-mono, so a `const s: string` local, a string FIELD read and a `s ?? d` result reached the `"i32"` catch-all and the instance declared an i32 parameter over a string body, while the same program with the annotation DELETED worked). `monoAnnPinName` collapses the annotated-LOCAL and PARAM ladders, which carried the same three arms verbatim — the drift they were one edit from is exactly the 8 forwarder cells the grid found still wrong when the direct call was right. The faithful cut is measured, not assumed: the other three i32-backed list spellings (`K[]`, `(boolean|null)[]`, `(K|null)[]`) keep master's pin and reach a LOUD floor, so declining them fails closed. Corpus **1,497 `@run` files: 0 output changes, 0 verdict changes**; byte A/B **1494/1495 identical**, the one mover being `generics/nested-generic-call-two-types.vl`, whose own comment records that `boolean` had been sharing i32's instance ("the mis-shared callee happened to be the right one anyway") and whose output is unchanged. Bootstrap from master's published seed reproduces the new compiler byte-for-byte. (`tests/cases/generics/bool-list-arg-print.vl`, `string-and-map-local-arg.vl`, `error-litunion-list-arg-not-pinned.vl`)
- **A10 generics: a generic function binds `T = f64` from an `f64[]` ARGUMENT** — calling a `<T>`-generic with an `f64[]` argument (`head([1.5, 2.5])`) instantiated `head<i32>`, not `head<f64>`: the monomorphizer's argument-type classifier (`monoArgTyName`) named a `string[]` / struct-array / `i32[]` argument but had no `f64[]` case, so an f64 array literal fell through to the `exprArray → "i32[]"` default. The instance's functype then declared an i32-list param while the caller passed the f64-list, trapping wasm validation (`expected (ref $i32list), found (ref $f64list)` — pinpointed with `wasm-dis`). An `exprF64Array` check before the i32 default binds `T = f64`, so generic element reads / returns over `f64[]` lower correctly — `head<T>` / `last<T>` / `count<T>` over `f64[]` now work, alongside the `i32[]` / `string[]` instances. (A generic BUILD-loop over `f64[]` — an inferred `const out = []` accumulator — is still a follow-up; element ACCESS is fixed here.) Native fixpoint byte-identical, deno corpus 543, native sweep PASS=409. (`tests/cases/generics/f64-array-arg.vl`)
- **A10 generics: a generic function's BODY may annotate its type parameter (`const x: T`, `const out: T[]`)** — a `<T>`-generic body that names its type param in a `LetDecl` annotation no longer fails `unknown type 'T'`, and lowers per instance against the CONCRETE element type. Two halves: (1) **checker** — the function's type params are kept on the `tpEnv` stack for the WHOLE body (not just around each param/return annotation as `resolveAnnot` did), so `const out: T[] = []` resolves `T` to the same opaque `TyVar` the signature binds; (2) **emitter** — the monomorphizer rewrites the instance body's `LetDecl` annotations through the binding (`const out: T[]` → `const out: f64[]`), via a new `monoCloneBody` that clones only the statement SPINE (blocks / control-flow / `LetDecl`s) and SHARES every leaf expression — INCLUDING a nested lambda, so no function node is duplicated (a clone would not be re-lifted, `collectFns` having run) and a `T`-independent body shares its arena wholesale (byte-identical, usage-gated). This reuses the already-working CONCRETE annotated path for every element type instead of per-rep usage inference, so a generic build-loop (`reverse<T>`) and a generic typed local lower for `i32` / `string` / `struct` (and `f64` LOCALS). (A generic `T[]`-returning BUILD over `f64[]` is still blocked by a SEPARATE bug — a function RETURNING `f64[]` is not yet recognised as an array at the index site — the next follow-up.) Native fixpoint byte-identical, deno corpus 544, native sweep PASS=410. (`tests/cases/generics/body-type-param.vl`)
- **B15 Lambdas / declaration-vs-value (typed)** — `FunctionDeclaration` in value position lowers to its closure value (let-bound, capturing, higher-order, inline object fields). → `DECISIONS.md`
- **B15 UN-ANNOTATED function as a value: module scope, struct fields, and a diagnostic that names the signature** — `const f = add` over a generic (un-annotated-param) named function now monomorphizes per call site at MODULE scope, not only inside a function body (the exemption was gated on being in a frame; a module binding is a global whose init neither the const-cell nor the start-fn path handled), and the "used only as a direct-call callee" test runs program-wide because a global is visible from every body. A function-typed struct FIELD joins the typed boundaries `monoCoerceFnValue` materializes an instance at — alongside the annotated binding, the concrete function-typed argument, the annotated return and the array element — for a declared alias (read off its `TypeDecl`'s field nodes) and an inline shape alike, composing with the array arm so a field holding an array of generic functions pins each element. Closes an invalid-wasm hole in the pre-existing exemption: with NO call site the template stays un-pinned and emits at the i32 default, which for `function exclaim(s) { return s + "!" }` was `vl check` rc 0 and a module the engine rejects; an un-pinned alias now takes the floor's loud reject. That reject names the function, the un-annotated parameters, and the concrete signatures the program's own call sites already pin (`Call sites pin \`add\` at (i32, i32), (f64, f64)`), or says plainly that nothing pins it. REMAINING: a generic passed to a HOF whose callback parameter is itself un-annotated. (`tests/cases/functions/value-flow-mono-module-scope.vl`, `generic-fn-struct-field-boundary.vl`, `error-generic-alias-never-called.vl`, `error-generic-fn-value-inferred-hof.vl`)
- **B17 Diagnostics + lint (started)** — `severity`, `@warning` directive, empty-range warning, stable `code`s; lint pass (`compiler/lint.ts`): unused-variable, unreachable-code (`unnecessary` tag = VS Code grey-out).
- **B17 Export-aware top-level unused** — exported top-level bindings exempt from unused-variable lint; landed with `export` keyword (H0 phase 1).
- **B17 fix: unused-import diagnostic + remove fix** — an unused imported name now squiggles the imported NAME (`a`, or the `as`-alias `y` in `{ x as y }`), not the `import` keyword (`parseImport` threads each specifier's local-identifier token span to `declareBinding`), and is reported under a dedicated `unused-import` code with the message "Unused import `a` (remove it)". Imports get NO `_`-prefix suggestion (prefixing would require aliasing, not a bare `_`); the only quick-fix removes the specifier — deleting the whole `import` line when it was the sole specifier, else dropping just that specifier and one adjacent comma (first/middle/last handled with no dangling comma; an `{ x as y }` alias clause is removed whole). Import bindings carry an `isImport` flag (`Binding.isImport`, set in `parseImport`) that the lint branches on.
- **B17 lints: unused-function, one generic walker, binding-keyed tracking, per-module attribution** — a never-referenced non-exported top-level function is flagged at its NAME token; the five per-rule AST walkers collapsed into one generic child-iterator (fixing match-arm blind spots); use/write tracking became binding-keyed via a scope-aware resolver (shadowed findings unmasked); the lint pass runs per resolved module with file attribution and a real `unused-import` rule over the new `ImportDecl` AST node. (#759, #765, #785, #794, #799)
- **B17 lints: dead/constant branch + degenerate `for` step** — two more lint rules (`compiler/lint.ts`). **Constant condition** (`code: constant-condition`, `warning`, `unnecessary` tag = grey-out) flags a branch whose literal-boolean condition makes it unreachable: an `if false { … }` body, the `else` (and later arms) of an `if true { … }`, and a `while false { … }` body. Literal-only — no general constant folding; recurses into nested bodies. **Degenerate `for` step** (`code: for-step-zero`, `error`) flags a range `for` written with a literal `step 0` — it never advances the loop variable (an infinite loop), the case the parser's empty-range warning deliberately excludes (an empty-range check can't fire when the step is zero); points at the `step 0` expression. (`tests/cases/lint/dead-branch-if-false.vl`, `dead-branch-if-true-else.vl`, `dead-branch-while-false.vl`, `for-step-zero.vl`)
- **B17 lint: unused-function** — a non-exported top-level `function` that is never referenced anywhere in its file is dead code in a whole-program compile; flagged with a `warning` (`code: unused-function`, `source: vital`, `unnecessary` tag = grey-out) — `Unused function \`f\` (prefix with \`_\` to suppress, remove it, or \`export\` it)`. EXPORTED functions are NEVER flagged (public API — VL is multi-file, so an `export function` may be imported and called by another module). A `_`-prefixed name is the "intentionally unused" convention and downgrades to a `hint` (greyed, not a warning), mirroring the unused-variable rule. Reuses that rule's reference model (the symbol table's non-decl occurrences) and additionally consults an AST-level call/value-reference set so FORWARD/mutually-recursive calls (resolved against a hoisted signature before the real binding is declared) and UFCS/operator-method calls (`x.m(…)`, `a + b`, which the parser desugars to a named `FunctionCall`) count as uses — a function reachable from any call site is never falsely flagged. The LSP offers the `_`-prefix quick-fix (no multi-line removal). (`tests/cases/lint/unused-function.vl`, `exported-function-no-warn.vl`, `called-function-no-warn.vl`, `mutual-recursion-no-warn.vl`, `tests/lint_unused_function_test.ts`)
- **B17 fix: `run`/`build` suppress info/hint advisories** — `vl run` and `vl build` now apply a `warning` display floor (via the existing `meetsThreshold` helper): errors (still blocking) and warnings (dead code, empty intersections) print, but info/hint-level style lints (e.g. prefer-`const`) are suppressed — those are the job of `vl check` and the editor, not noise on every run. `vl check` is unchanged and still shows the full set.
- **B19 `return` / early returns** — early, from loops, fall-through; bare `return` yields null.
- **B-debug Name section + source maps + trap-to-source** — wasm name section + Source Map v3 (binaryen debug locations, survives `optimize()`); trap → precise VL `file:L:C` error message. (#76)
- **B6 Nullable-list narrow-then-use codegen fix** — a `T[] | null` narrowed to non-null (`if xs != null { … }`) then read into a list op (`xs[i]`, `xs[i]=v`, `xs + …`, `for…in`, `map`/`filter`) no longer crashes binaryen's `LocalSubtyping` (`isSubType` assertion): narrowing is type-level only, so the value keeps its nullable `(ref null $list)` slot, and stashing it in the op's non-null `(ref $list)` local was a subtype violation. Each such read now `ref.as_non_null`s the list (the narrowing guarantees non-null). Reproduces non-recursively with `i32[] | null`; found by the A11 array lane. (`tests/cases/types/nullable-list-index.vl`)
- **B-arrow-lambdas — arrow `(params) => body` is the SOLE anonymous-function syntax; `function(…)` expression form removed; lambdas debug-name from their binding** — an anonymous function is now written `(n: i32) => n * 2` (bare-expression body) or `(n) => { … }` (block body), and the old `function(n: i32) n * 2` EXPRESSION form is REJECTED — a `function` token in expression position reports `anonymous functions use arrow syntax \`(params) => body\`, not \`function(...)\`` (a named `function f(…)` DECLARATION is a statement, dispatched before expression parsing, so it is untouched; object method shorthand `{ m(a) {…} }` is untouched). The change is a pure SURFACE swap: an arrow lambda parses to the SAME nameless `FuncDecl` the old form produced, so the checker (un-annotated param inference from callback position, capture analysis) and the emitter (the fat-pointer closure value + `call_ref`) are byte-for-byte unchanged — only the front-door syntax moved. **Parser:** a `(` in expression position scans to its matching `)` and branches to a lambda iff `=>` immediately follows (a grouped expression's `)` never is), so `(a + b) * c` stays grouped and a function-typed param `(f: (i32) => i32) => f(3)` resolves the OUTER `)` past the nested arrow; the param list and bare/block/object body are shared with the `function`-keyword tail (factored into `parseParamList` + `parseFuncBodyAndBuild`). **Formatter:** anonymous functions now print as arrow — MANDATORY, since emitting `function(…)` would no longer re-parse; the 27 corpus fixtures + object method/field values were migrated via `vl fmt` and the result is idempotent. **Debug naming:** a directly-bound lambda `const foo = (n) => …` takes the binding name in the wasm name section, so a wasmtime trap backtrace reads `vl!foo` instead of `vl!__lambda_N` (an unbound `.map((n) => …)` callback keeps `__lambda_N`). The display name is DECOUPLED from resolution — a parallel `fnDispNames` array, populated at lift time, read ONLY by the name section, never entering `fnNameMap` (reusing `fnName` collided: the checker saw `redeclared foo`; and a `{[string]: string}` map mis-lowers with `?? ""`, so an array is used). Native fixpoint byte-identical, deno corpus 535, native sweep PASS=402. (`tests/cases/functions/arrow-lambda.vl`, `lambda.vl`, `arrays/map-filter-arrow.vl`)
- **B-closure-nonref-params — a function VALUE may take a function-typed / string / list / struct-list parameter** — the closure-call ABI's PARAMETER spine, previously limited to i32 / f64 / struct, now mirrors the already-broad RESULT spine. A lifted lambda used as a value (`const call3 = (g: (i32) => i32) => g(3)`) whose param is a closure (`(i32) => i32`), `string`, `i32[]` / `string[]` / `f64[]`, or a struct list (`P[]`) now lowers: its env-leading `$fnsig` encodes the param valtype via a new `cloParamTok`, so the call site `ref.cast`s the funcref to the RIGHT functype. Before, any non-i32/f64/struct param made `fnSigKeyOf`/`cloSigKeyExt` yield "", so no `$fnsig` was interned and the value call fell back to the all-i32 ARITY signature — casting e.g. a `(ref $cloStruct)`-param funcref to a `(…, i32) → i32` functype, which trapped wasm validation (`expected i32, found (ref …)`). The functype EMITTER already wrote `fbValtype(vtKindOfParam, …)` per param, so only the interning KEY needed widening; the annotation-string path (`annParamKind`, used to resolve a value call over a `g: (string) => i32` higher-order param) was widened in lockstep so both producers mint the identical key. **Soundness floor:** the kinds still outside the param ABI — an `i64`/`f32`, a nullable-ref (`T | null`), a union, or a map parameter (and an `f32` return) — keep `cloSigKeyExt` "" and are now REJECTED at the value site (`emitClosureValue`) with a clear message (`a function value's signature is not yet supported — …`) instead of mis-casting to the arity signature and emitting invalid wasm. (A named function with these types, called directly, is unaffected.) Native fixpoint byte-identical, deno corpus 536, native sweep PASS=402. (`tests/cases/functions/closure-value-nonref-params.vl`)
- **B-lambda-inferred-return — an anonymous lambda's inferred non-i32 return through a VARIABLE classifies correctly** — an arrow lambda with an un-annotated return whose tail is a plain `let`-bound variable of a non-i32 type (`const f = () => { const x: i64 = 42; return x }`, and the `f64` / `string` / `i32[]` / `string[]` counterparts) no longer emits invalid wasm (`expected i32, found i64`). The emitter's return classifier reads the checker's `A20` inferred-return table to type a return the expression-based fallback misses (a bare variable), but that table was keyed by function NAME — and a lambda is anonymous at check time, lifted to a `__lambda_N` name the checker never assigned, so `inferRetNameOf("__lambda_N")` missed and the return defaulted to i32 while the body produced the wider type. The checker now ALSO records an anonymous lambda's inferred return under a NODE-INDEX sentinel key (`#<nodeIx>`, read by `inferRetNameByNode`) — recorded in `checkFuncDeclNode` exactly where the named path patches the hoisted `TyFunc`, gated to the real (non-quiet) pass — and the emitter consults it (by `fnStmts[i]`) when the name lookup misses. The classifiable-name set is now a shared `isClassifiableRetName` helper so the named loop and the lambda recording stay in lockstep. A NAMED function with the same body always worked; this brings lambdas to parity. (The bug predates arrow lambdas — the old `function(…)` expression form had it too.) Native fixpoint byte-identical, deno corpus 537, native sweep PASS=404. (`tests/cases/functions/lambda-inferred-nonref-return.vl`)
- **B-arrow-return-annotation — an arrow lambda may carry a return-type annotation `(params): Ret => body`** — completing parity with the `function` form (which always allowed `: Ret`). `const inc = (n: i32): i32 => n + 1`, `(): i64 => { … }`, `(): string => …`, `(): i32[] => …` all parse; the annotation flows into the SAME `fnRet` the named form uses, so the checker and emitter are unchanged. **Parser:** `looksLikeArrowLambda` now commits to a lambda when the token after the matching `)` is a `:` (a return annotation) as well as `=>` — unambiguous because `vl` has no `?:` ternary, so a `(group):` never arises in expression position (the only other place `:` follows a parenthesised thing); `parseArrowLambda` consumes the optional `(":" type)` before `=>`. **Formatter:** `arrowHeader` now prints the `: Ret` verbatim — MANDATORY, since dropping it would change meaning (a load-bearing return type, e.g. one pinning the wider `i64`/`f32` result) and the round-trip would be lossy; idempotent. The closure-RETURN kinds still outside the value ABI (`: f32`, `: i32 | null`, a closure return) parse but hit the existing `emitClosureValue` soundness floor (a clean message, not invalid wasm). Native fixpoint byte-identical, deno corpus 538, native sweep PASS=404. (`tests/cases/functions/arrow-return-annotation.vl`)
- **B-bare-expr-return-coercion — a bare-expression body returning a narrower numeric literal coerces to the declared return type** — `function f(): i64 5`, `function f(): f32 1.5`, and the arrow-lambda equivalents (`(): i64 => 5`, `(): i64 => 10 + 5`) no longer emit invalid wasm (`expected i64, found i32`). The implicit-return tail (a bare value expression, vs an explicit `return e`) shares `emitReturnValue`, which promotes a narrower literal (`5` → i64, `1.5` → f32) to the declared float/i64 result — but the implicit path passed `widenNum = false`, deliberately SKIPPING that widening to avoid mis-widening an already-typed tail (a closure `call_ref` result the float-classifier once didn't recognise), so the literal emitted at its DEFAULT type (i32 / f64) and mismatched the result valtype. That skip is now obsolete: `exprIs{F64,F32,I64}` recognise a closure-call result (and a variable, field read, etc.), so `emitExprAs{I64,F32,F64}` is a no-op pass-through on an already-correct value — making the widening safe unconditionally. The `widenNum` parameter is removed; explicit and implicit returns now share one path. A general fix (it applies to NAMED bare-expression bodies too, not just lambdas). Native fixpoint byte-identical, deno corpus 539, native sweep PASS=405. (`tests/cases/functions/bare-expr-return-coercion.vl`)

- **Rep-bug burn-down, wave 1 (fuzzer-found)** — all ten confirmed bug families from the rep fuzzer's first sweeps fixed at their roots: map value kinds across the return boundary + atom-valued maps (#814); nullable/union rep seams — `f64|null` print import, nullable-scalar-element list constructs, variant scalar-list fields — + the boolean print classifier reading the checker's recorded type (#815); i64 widening seams — lambda contextual returns adopt the expected type, numeric prims invariant inside function types, union-box tags adopt the union's atom (#816); composite-global field-code drift — atom-array fields, union-with-closure-member fields (#817); scalar leaves in literal-inferred shapes (#818); parenthesized conditions narrow like bare ones — one peel at the `setNarrowFromCond` seam + checker exhaustiveness (#819), with fuzzgen paren coverage + baseline re-pin (#820). Fixed shapes graduated from `scripts/rep-fuzz-baseline.txt` into `tests/cases/`.
- **Rep-bug burn-down, wave 2 — zero unsound outputs (soundness milestone)** — every unsound class (INVALID-WASM, TRAP, MISMATCH) driven to **0** at the pinned CI seeds; the residual is 36 shapes, ALL fail-loud REJECT (coverage gaps refused cleanly, never silent miscompiles). Waves: structural heap-type dedup so structurally-identical declared/inline types share one WasmGC heap type (#833/#834/#835); value-union box read — narrowed member extracted into a typed local (#855); map-as-closure-return heap-type identity — the captured map's env field, the closure `$fnsig` result, and the returned map all dedup to one `$mapStruct` (#860); scalar-union-arm boxing in composite position (closure return + array element) with a function type promoted to a value-union REF atom (kind 11), which also closed the last union-closure-member-field xfail (#861). Remaining residual is coverage-only (union-with-map/array member, map reader path, niche nullable-scalar lists) — see `docs/internals/rep-fuzz-findings.md` + `ROADMAP.md`.
- **Union with an array member — real rep (#863)** — a union whose arm is an array (`S[] | {w:i32}`, `(() => f64)[] | i64`) now lowers end-to-end instead of rejecting: param acceptance for a ref-element array arm (`isVariantBoxUnion`), a deterministic additive tag band above the fixed value-atom kinds (`refArrSlotTag = uVariants.length + 12 + rlSlotByName(elem)` — zero blast radius on existing tags, fixpoint-stable), boxing the reflist wrapper into the union anyref (`emitUnionCoerce`), `is <ref-array>` tag compare, and a narrowed reflist read (`ref.cast` the payload to the wrapper). Struct-array / closure-array / scalar-array arms across param/local/return/global, read + construct. (One neighbour — an array whose ELEMENT is a paren union, `(C | D)[]` — stays a loud reject: `splitUnionAtoms` lacks paren-depth tracking; never unsound.)
- **Union with a map member — real rep (#865)** — a union whose arm is a map (`{[string]: string} | f64`, `{[string]: K} | i64`) now lowers instead of rejecting, mirroring the array-member feature (#863): `isVariantBoxUnion` accepts a map arm, an interleaved additive tag band (`mapSlotTag = uVariants.length + 15 + 2*slot` on ODD offsets, ref-arrays moved to EVEN `12 + 2*slot` so a `S[] | {[string]:i32}` union can't collide), `emitUnionCoerce` boxes the map struct ref into the union anyref, `emitIs` compares the map tag, and a narrowed read `ref.cast`s the payload to the map heap type (so `t["k"]` / `t["k"] ?? d` work); the checker's `floorMapUnion` guard is removed. Scoped to scalar/string/mono map VALUES: a ref-value map arm (struct/nested-map/union/closure value) would be a #860-style heap-type-identity hazard (silent invalid-wasm), so it stays a **loud registration reject** (pinned `error-map-member-refvalue.vl`), deferred to a follow-up.
- **Map-member union in a LOCAL binding + nullable-atom map values (map-union composition)** — two root fixes in the union-with-map / composite-map-value family the rep fuzzer flagged. (1) A local `const v: {[string]: i32} | boolean = h` (a map arm boxed into a union whose OTHER arm is a scalar/boolean — not the struct/map cases #865/#868 covered) emitted invalid wasm: the local-slot classifier (`letIsMap`) excluded the map-member-union ANNOTATION but fell through to `exprMap(init)` — and the init IS the bare map arm — so the cell was typed as the map struct while the RHS boxed into the union box (`struct.new $box` stored into a `(ref $map)` local). `letIsMap` now returns false for any registered `isUName` annotation (map-FIRST `{[..]}|X` and map-LAST `X|{[..]}`), so the cell rides its union box and the map arm narrows through the union. Graduates the 18 p0 (local) construct+read shapes — scalar/boolean/struct/nested-map value map-unions. (2) A map whose VALUE is a nullable-ATOM niche (`boolean | null` — 0/1 with the spare `2` = null) was mis-classified as a ref-VALUE union map (`mvValKindOfName` counted `boolean`+`null` > 1 and BOXED the values into a `(ref $box)` vals cell); it now reps as the MONO i32 map. `K0 | null` (a nullable LITUNION whose atom values are StrLit-syntax) rides the mono map too but hits the atom-map store guard and is now a clean LOUD REJECT (INVALID-WASM → REJECT) rather than plumbing nullable-atom-map store/read across every site. (`tests/cases/unions/map-member-local-narrow.vl`, `tests/cases/maps/map-nullable-atom-value.vl`)

Graduates `p2r {[string]: () => i32} | {w: i32} | null` from the baseline. (`tests/cases/unions/struct-arm-grouped-union-list-field.vl`, `map-member-null-narrow-rebind.vl`, `struct-list-arm-map-field-read.vl`, `map-member-closure-value-null-narrow.vl`, `tests/cases/maps/error-union-map-member-value.vl`, `error-nulstr-value.vl`)

- **repOf hot path memoized (roadmap repOf item (c)) — self-compile ~26% faster, byte-identical** — `repCanonKey` gains a generation-stamped per-arena-index memo (consulted/written at TOP-LEVEL calls only: an interior de Bruijn back-edge fragment is context-dependent, so only a root's key is reusable verbatim), and `repSlotOfTy`'s per-query linear rescan of `sNames` (re-rendering every declared struct's key — 41.8% of a profiled self-compile) collapses into a stamped cache (nominal `di→slot` array + canonical-key→unique-twin maps; exact-match-wins order preserved). The stamps are two new checker generations: `tyMutEpoch` advances at EVERY in-place `Ty`-arena mutation (empty-collection pins, return-inference patches, 0a-placeholder fills, generic-alias instantiation fills, hole-shape field pushes) and `cUserTypesVer` at every `cUserTypes` write, so a mid-emit re-check can never serve a stale key. `removeAtomFrom` (25% of the same profile) becomes a pure-function memo (the dominant `removed == "null"` case keys on the set alone) over a slice-run rebuild (kept atoms are verbatim contiguous slices of the set, so the complement is ≤2 slices in the common case instead of two concats per atom — byte-identical by the `splitUnionAtoms` tiling invariant). Receipts: `repSlotOfTy` subtree 41.8% → 0.0%, `removeAtomFrom` 24.5% → 7.1% of guest samples (total −43%); `refresh-compiler.sh` 19.9s → 14.6s; `rep-fuzz-check.sh` 105.7s → 54.0s (identical failure set). Native fixpoint holds and the memoized compiler reproduces master's fixpoint artifact byte-for-byte from master's source. Also chartered: the fused `m[k] = (m[k] ?? 0) + 1` missing-key map-RMW miscompile the cache build tripped (ROADMAP B-bug-maprmw; comment-marked workaround at the `repSlotKeyN` build).
- **B-bug-maprmw — map SET evaluates receiver, key, and VALUE before the probe; fixes the fused missing-key RMW + the shared-scratch clobber family** — `m[k] = (m[k] ?? 0) + 1` on a MISSING key trapped (OOB array access — the append arm linked `index[slot]` and pushed the key BEFORE evaluating the RHS, so the RHS's probe of the same key hit the half-inserted entry and read `vals.backing[count]`) or, once the backing had grown, silently stored `0 + 1` and skipped the `??` default. Same root for every value shape (boolean/i64/f64/string/struct-field receiver). The same lowering also parked the receiver/key in the SHARED per-shape scratch locals across user code, so a same-shaped map op nested in the KEY or VALUE expr clobbered them: `b[a["x"] ?? "d"]` probed `a` (reads returned the default; a SET's write was silently lost), and `m["a"] = (n["x"] ?? 0) + 1` on a missing key appended into `n`. Fix: `emitMapRecvKey` leaves receiver + key on the OPERAND STACK (immune to nested ops) and `emitMapSetV` evaluates the value ONCE — before the probe touches anything — into a reserved stash (the mv slot's typed `valStash`; a new mono frame slot 14), which both arms consume; a value-expr CALL that mutates the map is now correctly ordered before the write too. #917's split-form workaround at the `repSlotKeyN` build stays ONE seed generation — the published seed's lowering predates this fix, so a fused spelling in compiler source traps when that seed code-gens it (the bootstrap ordering constraint); fuse it once the seed containing this fix publishes. (`tests/cases/maps/rmw-fused-missing-key.vl`, `rmw-fused-value-shapes.vl`, `rmw-cross-map-clobber.vl`)

- **List-of-a-UNION arm — real rep (roadmap rep-burn-down item (b)); fixes a LIVE SILENT MISCOMPILE (#906 multi-obs MISMATCH)** — a union whose arm is a list of a union (`(i32 | string)[] | {w: i32}`, `N[] | {w: i32}`, `(C | D)[] | {w: i32}`) now lowers end-to-end. The root: `splitUnionAtoms`/`unionMemberCount` split `|` on brace/bracket depth only, so the paren-grouped arm mis-split into garbage atoms — the whole annotation silently dropped its union rep (the initializer's own type won the local slot) and `is (i32 | string)[]` const-folded FALSE: `["other"]` took the ELSE branch with no trap and no validation error (masked from the single-carrier oracle because the else path and the inner union's non-carrier arm print the same sentinel). Fixes: (1) the split/count are now all-grouper paren-aware + quoted-atom-safe (closure `cloParen` special case subsumed); (2) `parenUnionArrElemName` requires the whole name to be ONE atom (a two-ref-array-arm union `(boolean|string)[] | (i32|f64)[]` used to slice an unbalanced "inner" and derail registration); (3) `emitUnionCoerce` routes a list LITERAL whose own classification is a scalar list or a concrete struct list into the union-box-element arm's build (`pendingListKind = 2` + the arm slot, elements boxed per `emitUnionBoxArg`) tagged `refArrSlotTag` — the union's own exact list atom still wins; (4) `registerInlineUnion` recurses into a ref-array ARM's element union so a struct element union's variants intern (`objVariantName`, `x[0] is C`) and a value element union's box reps mark. Graduates 9 baseline REJECTs (`(f32|string)[]`/`(i32|string)[]`/`(i64|{w:i32})[]` + `{w:i32}` at p1c/p1r, `(boolean|{w:i32})[] | string | null` at p2c/p2r, and the nested `(boolean|null)[] | i32` struct-field shape). Byte-identical: fixpoint, corpus, rep-fuzz exact, strong-form differential + 3 fuzz seeds vs master. (#910)
- **B21 `match` (phase 1: litunion match) shipped** — exhaustive-by-default `match` over literal-union scrutinees: OR-patterns, `_` wildcard, redundant/invalid-pattern errors, cross-module rename support (`tests/cases/match/*`); payload-binding match over tagged unions is unshipped and stays on the roadmap.
- **B21 `match` (phase 2a: VALUE-union scrutinees) shipped** — `match` now dispatches over **every** union, not only literal ones: struct unions (`C | D`), scalar unions (`i32 | string`), mixed (`Box | i32 | string`), and both `| null` spellings (declared `TyUnion`, inline `TyNullable`).
