An arm's pattern is a TYPE (`C =>`, `i32 =>`, `{c: i32} =>`, `null =>`, or the OR-form `A | B =>`) and the parser mints it as a real `IsExpr` over the scrutinee, so the arm rides the EXISTING `x is T` machinery end to end — `collectThenNarrows`/`collectElseNarrows` narrowing, the `isVarTyIxOf`/`narrowTys` emit ABI, the module-merge rename, the lint type-name scan — and the desugar reuses the pattern node as the arm's condition. Every arm binds the narrowed member, the last one included: it lowers to the chain's bare `else`, whose union COMPLEMENT is that member. **Exhaustiveness is the point**: a missing member is a compile error naming it (`non-exhaustive match — missing {c: i32}`), where the `is`-chain this replaces has none. Still refused, each with its own reason instead of the old blanket "must be a literal union": a non-union scrutinee, a union with LITERAL members (an arm's `is 0` does not lower), and a pattern that resolves to a union (one `is` cannot test it). Also fixes a **compiler TRAP on master**: a zero-arm `match k {}` read `bodies[-1]` for the chain's bare `else` and died as a bare `wasm trap: out of bounds array access` with no diagnostic; it now reports its missing members. Corpus byte-, message- and run-identical (1,288 pre-existing files); fuzz A/B identical over 25,200 programs/side. (`tests/cases/match/value-union-*.vl`, `scrutinee-reject-messages.vl`, `zero-arm-error.vl`, `tests/cases/modules/match-type-pattern-cross-module/`)
- **B21 `match` (phase 2b: PAYLOAD BINDING) shipped** — a variant pattern may carry a punned field list, and the arm narrows AND binds in one form: `match cmd { Move{x, y} => f(x, y), Attack{target} => g(target) }` — the command-dispatch shape webcraft's order pipeline wants. Punning only (`{x, y}`); renaming (`{x: a}`) and nested destructuring stay deferred, with the one-branch cost of each recorded in `docs/internals/match-design.md`. **The lowering is the recorded one and nothing more**: the arm is already `if scrut is Move { <block> }`, so the desugar PREPENDS `const x = scrut.x` statements to that block — no new chain, no new narrowing, no new emitter path, and the exhaustive-last-arm bare `else` unchanged. Measured, not asserted: 18 grid cells (payload types i32/f64/string/struct/list/nullable x union shapes 2/3/5/aliased x punned/subset/empty/no-payload-arm/`_`-default x statement/expression) each compile BYTE-IDENTICAL to their hand-written if-chain twin, with two inverted controls reading DIFFER. The clause lives on a new `matchBinds` column PARALLEL TO `matchPats` (a `Block` of `LetDecl`s per pattern, or -1) rather than in the arm body, so `nodeTyIx` still covers every minted node (it is sized at `checkProgram` entry) and `vl fmt` still prints the arm the user wrote. Three walks had to be taught it: the FORMATTER renders the clause structurally (the pattern's source span stops at its type atom, so the verbatim slice phase 2a round-trips with would have silently DELETED every binding from a formatted file), the module MERGE shadows the bound names over the arm body (a module-level `const x` otherwise captured an arm binding `x`), and lint needs nothing because the splice has already run by lint time. Four rejects, each ONE error: a clause on an OR arm (forced — a multi-pattern arm gets no then-narrowing, so the field read cannot type), a duplicate binding, a body declaration that shadows a binding (both exist because the desugar SPLICES: post-splice the two share a scope the checker saw as two), and an unknown field. **Exhaustiveness is untouched**: coverage is decided from `matchPats` alone, so the same match with and without bindings produces the identical phase-1 `non-exhaustive match — missing …` error — pinned as one fixture and proved by sabotaging the coverage rule (both the new and phase 1's fixtures go green-when-they-should-be-red). Corpus six-channel A/B over 1,673 files: every pre-existing file identical on all six channels including BYTES; fuzz A/B identical over 14,400 programs/side. (`tests/cases/match/payload-binding-*.vl`, `tests/cases/modules/match-payload-shadows-global/`, `tests/vl_fmt_test.ts`)
- **B21 `match` — a binding arm can be a VALUE: the if-expression arm gained a PRELUDE (C6)** — `const r = match u { A{a} => a, B{b} => b }` reported `emitProgram: if-expression arm is not a single value`. The grid that localises it (binding/non-binding × statement/tail/value position, for `match` and for the hand-written `if` twin) reads 8 of 10 cells lowering on master, with both failures at binding × VALUE position — so the BINDING broke value position, not `match`-in-value-position, and not phase 2b: the desugar's output IS the `if` twin, and the twin failed identically. **An arm's value is now its LAST statement** (the rule `blockTailIsValue` already applies in tail position and `emitStmts` in statement position), and everything before it is the arm's PRELUDE — exactly what a payload clause produces. `emitIfArmOpen`/`emitIfArmClose` lower the prelude in the arm's own lexical frame; the five arm emitters (numeric join, ref join, union box, concrete variant, return sink) and the two binding-init sinks (nullable local, union-box pair) each still lower the VALUE in their own rep, so `match` and `if` alike now work in binding-init and `return` position across i32/f64/boolean/string/struct/list/nullable/`string | null`/value-union joins and `else if` chains. **The constraint is the local-slot PRE-ORDER**: wasm locals are function-scoped and `emitLetDeclStmt` claims them off a linear cursor, so a prelude may only be lowered where the collect pass allocated its locals in the same order — `collectIfExprLocals` walks the two statement positions whose order the emitter replays (a binding INITIALIZER, a `return` operand), and `armPreludeBlocks` records the arms it walked. `emitIfArmOpen` requires that membership, so the two unwalked positions — an if-expression buried deeper in an expression (`print(match u { A{a} => a … })`) and a TOP-LEVEL binding (a module global, whose initializer the start function's local collection never descends into) — are LOUD rejects naming the supported spelling. Both were compiler TRAPS (`out of bounds array access`) the moment the arm emitters accepted a multi-statement arm, so the mark is what converts a silent-or-crashing slot misalignment into a diagnostic. `armPreludeBlocks` holds arena NODE indices and so resets in `emitProgram`'s sidecar block with the other index columns — a stale index naming a node in the next program grants permission collect never gave, reachable only when one instance lowers several programs, which is why the corpus driver caught it and the CLI and the fixpoint ladder did not. Native fixpoint byte-identical; both seed-ladder legs green. (`tests/cases/match/binding-arm-in-value-position.vl`, `error-binding-arm-nested-in-expression.vl`, `error-binding-arm-global-initializer.vl`, `docs/internals/match-design.md` "A binding arm in value position")
- **The expression-statement DISCARD applies by node class, and the void-if-arm reject names its actual cause** — `emitStmt` implemented VL's "a bare value expression in statement position is evaluated and its value dropped" rule for a hand-listed few classes: a non-`=` `BinExpr` and a non-void `Call`. Every other value-shaped node reached `emitProgram: unsupported statement in body` with `vl check` rc 0. One cell per node class read **6 of 21** honouring the rule — `42`, `"s"`, `x`, `q.length`, `q[0]`, `-q`, `!q`, `{a: 1}`, `[1, 2]`, `(1 + 2)`, `q is i32`, `q as f64` all rejected. The rule was never in question: `tests/cases/statements/discarded-call-return.vl` pins it for a call, and `emitStmt`'s own `BinExpr` arm spells out evaluate-then-`drop`; only its coverage was. The discard now routes through **`stmtIsTailValue`** — the one classifier that already answers "this statement IS a value" — so it applies by MEMBERSHIP rather than by spelling, and a prefix `-q`/`!q` joins it (retiring `emitProgram: unsupported unary statement`). **This is also what a value-producing `if`/`match` in statement position needed**: such an `if` lowers as a VOID `if` whose arm blocks each end in a value, so the failure was INSIDE the arm on the arm's own last statement — which is why a hand-written `==` ladder reproduced it identically and why `match` needed nothing of its own (`desugarMatchAt` lowers it to the same chain before emit sees it). Measured on a 300-cell grid (node class × construct × position × arm types × value type × flag polarity): statement-position value-producing branches **5 → 51 of 51**, bare expression statements **6 → 22 of 23**, with **0 regressions** and **both silent classes 0 of 300 before and after** — the 84 cells where a VOID arm is the TAKEN one in a value-demanding position are loud in every case (57 emit-reject, 17 check-reject, 8 runtime trap on the emitter's `unreachable` poison, 2 invalid wasm), so nothing fabricates a value. The one class that still rejects is a bare `null` statement, correctly: it has no rep without a typed slot, and it says so. Separately, `emitProgram: if-expression arm is not a single value` **misnamed its own cause** — the common case is an arm ending in a VOID call, which IS a single expression and simply yields nothing. `ifArmNoValueFail` now distinguishes empty arm / void call / statement across all five arm emitters (plain, union, variant). **The layer that owns a void arm is the CHECKER**: VL types the arms and joins them, void included, and rejects `void` in a value slot on every ANNOTATED path (`return type mismatch: expected i32, got i32 | void`, `cannot bind the void result`, `argument 1: expected i32, got void`, `push: cannot add void`, `operator '+' is not defined for void and i32`). It applies that rule on annotated paths ONLY, so the emit reject is a BACKSTOP for an inference-path hole, pinned as two files that are one shape differing only by the annotation. That hole is `if`-independent and independently reachable — `function sv() { print(9) } print(sv())` is `vl check` rc 0 and invalid wasm — measured at **4 of 16** value-demanding positions uncovered (`print(<void>)`, `[<void>]`, an un-annotated `return <void>`, and a call to a void-tailed function). (`tests/cases/statements/discarded-if-expression-value.vl`, `discarded-expression-statement-classes.vl`, `error-if-arm-ends-in-void-call.vl`, `error-if-arm-ends-in-statement.vl`, `error-if-arm-is-empty.vl`, `error-void-if-arm-annotated-return.vl`, `error-discarded-bare-null.vl`)
- **`__trap__("reason")` — the abort grows a human-readable message (std-design OD2 / error-handling-design)** — the deliberate-abort intrinsic now takes an OPTIONAL single string argument: `__trap__("boom")` streams the reason to the host boundary through the print-of-string path (`__print_char__` … `__print_str_flush__`), THEN emits the same `unreachable`, so a bug aborts with a reason visible on stdout before it dies. Bare `__trap__()` is byte-identical to before; no new intrinsic name and no keyword (intrinsics stay dunder-only). The checker rejects a non-string arg or >1 arg (`__trap__ takes an optional string message`). `std:test` still uses the bare form (threading messages through it is a follow-up). Native fixpoint byte-identical. (`tests/cases/intrinsics/trap-message.vl`, `error-trap-args.vl`, `error-trap-arity.vl`)

- **Numeric-opcode intrinsics — the 44 operations that ARE one wasm instruction (webcraft P0.3+P0.4)** — the four IEEE bitcasts `f32bits`/`f32fromBits`/`f64bits`/`f64fromBits` (`*.reinterpret_*`, previously absent entirely, so a float's bits were unreachable — `as` converts), the f32/f64 `sqrt abs floor ceil trunc nearest min max copysign`, and the i32/i64 `clz ctz popcnt rotl rotr divU remU ltU leU gtU geU`. Free functions with the width taken from the operands under the binary operators' own rule (a bare float literal adapts down, a genuine f64 widens), so an intrinsic and an operator over the same operands compute at the same width; `clz` at i64 yields an i64 because `i64.clz` does. **Shadowable**: the checker's arm is skipped when the name is a declared function or an in-scope binding and the emitter's is reached only where the direct-call and closure-value lookups both failed, so a program's own `min`/`max` still wins. No import, no runtime, nothing for the linker — the host ABI is unchanged. Transcendentals are a deliberate non-ask (no opcode computes one; a std `sin` would be a determinism trap). Also fixes a latent import-scan hole this exposed: `scanPrintUse` now reads an intrinsic call's own recorded type, since an intrinsic RESULT scalar appears nowhere as an annotation (`print(f32fromBits(k))` alone emitted a call to a `__print_f32__` that was never imported). Corpus byte-, message- and run-identical over 1,342 pre-existing files; fuzz A/B identical over 25,200 programs/side; every opcode verified by `wasm-tools print`. (`docs/internals/numeric-intrinsics.md`, `tests/cases/numerics/bitcast-*.vl`, `float-opcodes-*.vl`, `int-*.vl`)
- **The linear-memory load matrix, `memory.grow`/`memory.size`, and the EXPORTED memory (webcraft P0.2 + the load half of P0.1)** — the seven missing load widths `__load_i8__`/`__load_u8__`/`__load_i16__`/`__load_u16__`/`__load_i64__`/`__load_f32__`/`__load_f64__` (`i32.load8_s`/`i32.load8_u`/`i32.load16_s`/`i32.load16_u`/`i64.load`/`f32.load`/`f64.load`, each one instruction at its natural alignment), plus `__memory_size__` and `__memory_grow__` (`memory.size`/`memory.grow`) — which were DECLARED and lowered by nothing, so a program could not address past the one hardcoded 64 KiB page and two 32 KiB buffers already trapped. `memory.grow` returns the PREVIOUS page count, or `-1` on failure, passed through unchanged. The narrow loads differ only in extension (`__load_i8__` of `0xFF` is `-1`; `__load_u8__` is `255`), the alignment immediate is a hint so every width reads correctly at an odd address, and a fresh page — including a newly grown one — is zero. **The module's linear memory is now exported as `memory`**, automatically, gated on the same `memUsed` flag as the memory section: a program that never touches linear memory is BYTE-IDENTICAL to before, while a memory-only module now emits an export section it previously skipped entirely. A host overlays `Float32Array`/`DataView` on `instance.exports.memory.buffer` and reads guest bytes in place — proved from the JS host and from a wasmtime 47 embedding, neither of which needed any change. A user `export function memory` in a memory-using program is a loud reject (export names must be unique); the same name without linear memory still compiles. **`memory.grow` DETACHES every host view** (`byteLength` 0, indexed read `undefined`, no throw), so the JS host's dead `imports.memory` and the two sinks that read it are deleted — censused dead across all 1,149 corpus modules that build — and the re-read-after-grow contract is asserted by test rather than described. NO `Buffer` type: O1/O5/O6 are unruled and the name is hard to change once shipped. Known limitation, censused across all ten memory intrinsics in four call positions: they work at top level and in a plain named function, and fail with `captured variable not found in enclosing frame` inside a lambda or in a named function in a module using a function value — so a `Buffer` METHOD surface is blocked until `isBuiltinFnName` is fixed. Also: `--enable-bulk-memory` added to `wasm-opt`/`wasm-dis` ahead of any `memory.copy`/`memory.fill` emission (binaryen 130 exits rc=1 with NO output without it); and the JS host no longer reports a linear-memory fault as "array index out of bounds". Every opcode verified by disassembly; suite 2,122/0/8. (`docs/internals/buffer-design.md` §H, `tests/cases/memory/`, `tests/vl_exported_memory_test.ts`)
- **Six queued defects: the numeric-literal-union ALIAS, the two nullable-print niches, the memory-intrinsic operand context, the else-less `if` binding, the `unknown type` component, and the dead-intrinsic census** — five fixed, one refuted in half. **(1)** `type Z = 0 | 1` + `const z: Z = 1` emitted SILENT INVALID WASM at every READ (top level, function body, struct field, array element, alias-of-alias, three-arm) with `vl check` clean: the cell was the `{tag, value}` union BOX while every read lowered as the plain i32 the checker's own algebra says it is, because a bare alias NAME has no digit/quote/pipe in it and `nameNeedsCanon` returned it untouched, so `collectU`'s `unNames` row claimed the annotation. `canonEmitName` now resolves such an alias to its BASE SCALAR through `softenLitTy` — the checker's "a numeric-literal union is its base type for arithmetic" rule, not a second answer. Not a `print` bug: `z + 1` and `toString(z)` failed identically and `z == 1` named it — "`==` over a struct union is not supported yet". The STRING sibling `type K = "a" | "b"` is deliberately untouched (there the alias name IS the interned-atom identity; a sabotage that softens it too reddens 45 corpus files), and MEMBERSHIP is unaffected — canon runs after every membership decision. **(2)** `print(<string | null>)` TRAPPED on an actual null (`array.len` on a null ref) and `print(<boolean | null>)` printed **`true`** for null (the niche sentinel 2 is non-zero, and `__print_bool__` renders any non-zero as true) — both `vl check` clean. Both now print `null`, via the MISSING ARMS the corpus had already diagnosed in prose: the string arm recovers the ref with `br_on_null`, the boolean arm branches with `br_table` on the niche value (which is already the arm index), each evaluating the argument exactly once and reserving no scratch frame. A checker GATE was built first and refuted by the corpus — it rejected four working programs including the boundary-controls fixture whose whole purpose is to pin what `print` must keep accepting. A fourth silently-wrong cell fell out and was ENTOMBED IN A FIXTURE: `print(r[1][0])` over a `(boolean|null)[][]` holding `false` pinned `@log 0` five lines below its `boolean[][]` sibling — the same value — pinned `@log false`. **(3)** A linear-memory intrinsic's OPERAND inherited an enclosing WIDE-SCALAR context, so `const q: f32 = __load_f32__(0)` emitted `f32.const 0` before `f32.load` — invalid wasm in 9 of 11 f32 positions and 1 of 4 i64 positions, `vl check` clean. Every such operand is an i32 BY DECLARATION, so they now clear the pending flags, the discipline the `call_ref` argument spine thirty lines away already applied. **(4)** An else-less `if` as a `let`/`const` INITIALIZER typed `TY_VOID`, so `const r = if c { 5 }` refused at every use; the decline was documented and its stated reason ("the emitter can't yet leave a nullable value on the stack") was FALSE — `nliInferIfLet` lowers exactly this binding, and its gate IS this position. Widened, POSITION-GATED by measurement: an ungated probe fixes 7 of 10 cells and the three it does not (call argument, explicit `return`, module-scope binding) answer `union if-expression requires an else arm`, so those keep the checker's clean positioned refusal. Filed as a `??` bug; `??` is not involved. **(5)** The `unknown type` diagnostic named the whole composite (`unknown type '{foo:string}|{bar:any}'`) instead of the offending component; the resolvers now bank the innermost failing spelling and six sites read it (`unknown type 'any' within '{foo:string}|{bar:any}'`), with the leaf case byte-identical. **(6)** The five DECLARED-BUT-UNLOWERED memory intrinsics (`__store_i64__`/`__store_f32__`/`__store_f64__`/`__store_string__`/`__log_string__`, re-censused in three call positions) now say so at `vl check`, positioned, instead of `emitProgram: call to unknown function` — a message `docs/internals/memory-gc-design.md` §1.2 had itself filed as reading like a typo'd identifier when the identifier is real and the implementation is what is missing. Shadowing is preserved: unlike the seven emitter-intercepted names, a program that declares one of these still runs it. **Refuted:** the report that the union case additionally "loses the second diagnostic entirely" — graded over 3 annotations × 7 bodies, the second error is decided by the BODY and identical across all three, and a corpus fixture has pinned both for that exact union shape since it was written. Suite 2,122/0/14 (master seed on the same tree: 2,112/10, the 10 being exactly the new and changed pins; ignored NAME SETS identical); corpus A/B 1,396 files with 1,356 pre-existing byte-, message- and run-identical and all 40 differences named; fuzz A/B 25,600 programs/side 0 differing; seven named sabotages (45/17/18/1/1/1/0 files). (`docs/internals/destringify-types-program.md`, `tests/cases/literal-unions/numeric-litunion-alias.vl`, `types/print-nullable-niche-null-value.vl`, `run/memory-intrinsic-arg-wide-context.vl`, `functions/else-less-if-expression-binding.vl`, `types/unknown-type-names-the-component.vl`)
- **A union-annotated GLOBAL resolved its struct layout from its object-literal INITIALIZER, so any same-fieldset struct row hijacked the narrowed read** — `structIndexOfLet` answered from the annotation when that named a struct and otherwise fell through to the INITIALIZER. A VALUE-UNION annotation pins the cell to the `{tag, value}` box, which has no struct-table layout at all, so the fall-through asked `structIndexOfExpr` about the arm's object literal — a question an object literal answers by FIELD-NAME SET (`structIndexOfObjCtx`) — and handed the binding whichever interned struct row shares those names. `structIndexOfExpr`'s Ident arm then reported that row for the union global and its `msi >= 0` branch short-circuited the narrowed-variant resolution the read actually needs, so `u.v.v` reported `emitProgram: field access receiver is not a struct` while binding the field alone (`const g = u.v`) reported `emitProgram: ref valtype with no interned shape` — **ONE root at two read depths, and the single guard clears both**. **THE AXIS IS THE FIELDSET TWIN, not the nesting and not the identifier**: measured cell by cell over union arm × nested field type, `{v}`/`{v}` and `{w}`/`{w}` rejected, while `{v}`/`{v,k}` and `{a,b}`/`{a}` — the same "the outer field name is also an inner field name" reading — worked before and after; `{a,b}`/`{b,a}` (twin by set, different declaration order) rejected, and `{v: string}` under `{v: Inner}` (twin by set, different field TYPES) rejected. The partner row need not be the field's own type at all: an UNRELATED same-fieldset struct reproduces it, and takes the whole `{n}` family down with it rather than only the narrowed read. **THE STORAGE CLASS IS THE OTHER HALF**: the identical shape as a PARAM, as a LOCAL, or as a global initialized from a CALL all worked throughout — only the global-with-object-literal spelling reaches the fall-through, and dropping the union works too (a plain `const u: Outer` resolves by annotation). `letAnnIsUnion` is the declared-spelling half of `letIsUnion` given one home; both call it, and `structIndexOfLet` now answers -1 for a union-annotated binding — the discipline `paramStructIndex` already held (annotation ALONE, never a value). Corpus A/B **2,075 of 2,078 `.vl` files byte-identical** (`tests/cases/` + `std/` + `bench/`, records == cells), the 3 differences being exactly the fixtures below, each rc 1 → rc 0. Suite **4,233 / 4,233, 0 failed, 7 ignored**, ignored NAME SET unchanged; with the guard made inert against the SAVED master artifact, exactly the predicted **6 tests redden** (3 fixtures × the corpus-oracle and native-align suites) and nothing else moves. Both seed-ladder legs green, the published-seed leg reproducing the fixed compiler byte-for-byte. **Retires the verdict pin `unions/error-struct-typed-variant-field-read.vl`**, whose header stated the axis as "a union ARM that is a struct carrying a STRUCT-TYPED field" and asserted "THE SPELLING IS NOT THE AXIS" — the grid refutes both. The generic `Box<Box<i32>> | i32` spelling shares this root and is fixed with it, but the fully INLINE `const u: {v: {v: i32}} | i32` never had it (its annotation names no union ALIAS, so it never took the fall-through), and `type Outer = { v: { v: i32 } }` stops earlier still, in the variant-field collector (`only i32 / boolean / string / array union-variant fields are supported`) — a separate gap, left standing. Also left standing and now measured: an UN-ANNOTATED struct global whose object literal fieldset-twins a union VARIANT never interns a struct row at all (`emitProgram: field access but no struct type declared`), the collect-side twin of this root and unreached by it. (`tests/cases/unions/union-global-literal-fieldset-twin-nested-read.vl`, `union-global-literal-twin-unrelated-struct.vl`, `union-global-literal-twin-field-bind.vl`)
- **A named union variant's INLINE-SHAPE field had no code, while the same field spelled as a declared NAME always did — and the "struct-typed variant fields are unsupported" reading of that floor is refuted by its own control** — `type Outer = { v: { v: i32 } }` used as a union arm reported `emitProgram: only i32 / boolean / string / array union-variant fields are supported`, and the message was read as "the collector supports only i32 / boolean / string / array fields, so a STRUCT-typed field on a union variant has no support". **THE AXIS IS THE FIELD TYPE'S SPELLING — inline shape vs declared name — AND THE NOMINAL CONTROL IS INVERTED**: `type Inner = { w: i32 }` + `type Outer = { p: Inner }` as a union arm is exactly a struct-typed variant field and has ALWAYS lowered, at one field and at three, at depth 2 and depth 3. Two recorders fill the variant-field table and they part on that one question. `collectU` is the FIRST emit pass, so the struct table does not exist yet and an inline shape's only resolution (`structIndexOfTypeName`) needs it, while a declared name resolves off the ARENA (`nameIsStructDecl`), which does — so `fieldTypeCode` answered 15 for the nominal spelling and -1 for the inline one. The INLINE-ARM recorder (`{a: {b: i32}} | i32`, no `type` declaration) never had the floor: `collectShapeVariantFields` carries an intern-free acceptance check (`variantNestedShapeOk`) and defers the intern to `collectA`'s code-15 variant-field pass. `collectVariantFields` now takes the SAME fallback, with the elem name derived from the field TYPE TEXT when it fires (`fieldRefElemName` re-derives `fieldTypeCode` internally and would answer `""` for a shape that entry point still cannot code, starving the deferred intern of its key). **Dimensions moved, each its own cell against the saved pre-fix artifact**: field identifiers (all-`v`, all-distinct — both floored), field-set cardinality (1 and 3 — both floored), nesting depth (2 and 3 — both floored, both lower now), leaf type (i32 / string / boolean / f64 / `i32[]` / map — all floored, all but the map's read path lower now), arm count and the other arm's kind (`Outer | i32`, `A1 | B1` — both floored), and nullability of the shape field (floored, lowers now). **Parity with the arm recorder brought one of its PRE-EXISTING residuals with it, so that is repaired here too**: `collectA`'s deferred code-15 intern discarded the index `internInlineShapeTy` returns, and the intern DEDUPS — `annShapeIndexOf` treats one wasm code as one layout, so `{b: boolean}` resolves to an existing `{b: i32}` row (field name equal, code 0 equal, no elem name to part on) and no row spelled `{b: boolean}` is ever minted, while the row's one code-15 consumer looks its element up BY NAME (`structIndexByName`) and read -1 — `nested-struct field element type is not interned` for a shape whose LAYOUT was interned, under its twin's name. The loop now re-keys the row to `sNames[<interned index>]`, the discipline `internInlineShapeTy`'s own nested-field arm already applied to its field texts. **The floor was the ARM recorder's first** — reproduced on the pre-change compiler in a single program, and ACROSS the corpus oracle's shared compiler instance, where a `{b: i32}` shape in one case file reddened a `{b: boolean}` shape in the next. Storage-correct by construction (the two spellings dedup precisely because they store identically) and inert wherever the names already agreed. Axis measured cell by cell: one code + two texts at the same field name floors; different field NAME, identical shape, and different CODE (`{b: string}`) all worked before and after. What still stands, unrepaired and unchanged: an inline shape whose field-name SET twins a DECLARED struct's reports the same message from `internInlineShape`'s dedup, byte-for-byte as the inline-ARM spelling does, before and after. Corpus **1,522 of 1,522 pre-existing `@run` modules BYTE-IDENTICAL** (`vl build` md5 A/B over all 1,526 `@run` files, records == cells; the 2 non-`SAME` rows are the new fixtures and the 2 `BOTHREJECT` rows are the pre-existing soundness xfails), and the pass/fail sweep is line-for-line identical (1,522 PASS / 2 CHECKFAIL, both before and after). 19 frontier cells validated through binaryen with the host's own flags (`--enable-reference-types --enable-gc --enable-bulk-memory --enable-tail-call -O3`): 18 valid, 0 invalid, 1 loud build-reject. Sabotage, twice, against SAVED artifacts: inert at the pre-change compiler, exactly the predicted **3 of the 5 new fixtures redden** and both verdict pins hold; inert at the collector fix ALONE (the re-key backed out), exactly the predicted **1 reddens** — the twin-code one — which is what separates the two repairs. **The separately-filed second face does NOT share this root, and the fix proves it: it moved all 9 of that face's cells ZERO, message for message.** It splits into two floors of its own, each now pinned with its measured axis. (a) An UN-ANNOTATED binding whose literal's field-name set twins a union VARIANT gets no layout from either table — `collectAnonShapes` skips it (`objVariantName != ""`) and a variant's `TypeDecl` is deliberately absent from the struct table. **Depth 1 with a flat `i32` field reproduces it**, which is what refutes the struct-typed-field reading; a deeper shape only changes WHICH wrong struct the orphan literal collides with, and `nested struct fields are not supported` is that collision's artifact, not evidence about field types. The guard is LOAD-BEARING: deleting it lowers every cell of that grid and reddens **7 of 1,524 `@run` corpus rows**, among them `types/variant-as-value.vl`, which PINS a variant type as a first-class value — minting an `#anon` row for a variant-twinned literal makes later same-fieldset literals resolve to it instead of the variant, breaking variant construction. (b) A module GLOBAL annotated with a variant name reports `ref valtype with no interned shape`: `buildLocals` has a `variant` rung ahead of its union/struct rungs and `globalCellKind`, whose header says it mirrors that ladder, has none — so **STORAGE CLASS is that cell's axis** (the identical binding as a local or a param lowers), and union mixedness and nesting are not (struct|struct floors identically; a nested field as a local lowers). Lifting it is a rep slice across 33 `globalCellKind` sites that no corpus row exercises, so it is pinned rather than guessed at. Measured negative, unrelated to either root and unmoved by this change: a map field read through a narrowed variant arm reports `bare null needs a struct-typed context` in the UNNESTED control and in the inline-ARM spelling alike, before and after. Native fixpoint byte-identical; both seed-ladder legs green. (`tests/cases/unions/variant-inline-shape-field.vl`, `variant-inline-shape-field-composite-leaves.vl`, `variant-shape-field-code-twin-rekey.vl`, `variant-fieldset-twin-anon-shape-floor.vl`, `variant-annotated-global-floor.vl`)

## CLI (Track C)

- **C1 Headless `compile(source) → { ast, wasm, diagnostics }`** (`compiler/compile.ts`), shared by LSP, test runner, and CLI.
- **C2 `vl run`** — compile + run a file / `-e` snippet / stdin (`deno task run`).
- **C3 `vl build <file> [-o out.wasm] [--wat]`** — emit wasm bytes (+ optional `.wat`).
- **C3b `vl build -O3` — the RELEASE PROFILE (webcraft P1.3)** — runs `wasm-opt --closed-world -O3 --gufa -O3` plus the shared GC/reference-type/bulk-memory enables. `-O` is unchanged (one open-world `-O`), `-O3` wins when both are passed, and a missing `wasm-opt` stays a soft no-op — a note naming the flag, the unoptimized module on disk, exit 0 — exactly as `-O` already behaved. The spelling is `-O3` rather than `--release` because unknown `vl build` flags were silently IGNORED, so `vl build f.vl -O3` used to write an unoptimized module and exit 0: any other spelling would leave a live rc=0 trap. **Two measurements invert the ask that motivated it.** (1) *Heap2Local is the wrong lever*: open-world `-O3` DOES inline the producer, leaving both `struct.new`s provably non-escaping in the loop, and Heap2Local still leaves them because the box reaches its use through an `if`-result of reference type — naming `--heap2local` changes nothing at any rung, while `--closed-world -O` melts all four. The box melts by closed-world type refinement + DCE. (2) *`--gufa` is measurably inert on VL output* — zero allocations and zero `ref.cast`s removed across all fixtures and on the 1.1 MB `vl-compiler.wasm` (4,503 casts either way); it is shipped because P1.3 names it, and the trailing second `-O3` is the member that earns its place (the only one moving the grown-list row 4→2). **What does NOT melt, pinned as a fixture at 4/4/4**: a union box whose narrowed value is READ. The headline 4→0 row is allocate-tag-test-discard; `if u is i64 { acc + (u as i32) }` keeps all four allocations at every rung, and a struct union melts only to 2 even tag-only — so the "union boxes melt in per-tick scratch code" half of P1.3 is NOT delivered, and the fix is an unboxed union rep rather than A16 (which prices every payload encoding at one allocation for the box itself). `--closed-world` soundness is now measured rather than assumed: all 1,338 corpus `@run` cases give identical stdout AND exit status through the profile. (`docs/internals/opt-profile-design.md`, `tests/selfhost_native_release_test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/opt-melt/`)
- **C4 `vl check <file>`** — diagnostics only, non-zero exit on errors; `--codegen` opt-in for full binaryen pipeline.
- **C5 Distribution via `deno compile`** — native `vl` binary (binaryen embedded); `deno task compile`/`smoke`; 11 smoke checks pass. → `DECISIONS.md`
- **C-redundant-type — lint + `--fix` for redundant explicit annotations (lean on inference).** VL's design hides types and surfaces them only where required, but hand-written annotations accrete; this is the first rule of a cleanup family. `vl check` now emits a `hint` for a `let`/`const` whose explicit annotation is EXACTLY what the initializer infers on its own (`let x: i32 = 5`, `const c: string = "hi"`), and `--fix` rewrites `let x: T = e` → `let x = e`. **Provably conservative — no functional change, never a false positive.** The check lives in the type checker (`compiler/typecheck.vl`, the `LetDecl` arm): the initializer is typed by `checkNode` BEFORE the annotation is consulted, so its context-free type is in hand; a finding is recorded only when that type RENDERS identically to the annotation. Widening / hole cases are excluded for free — `let x: i64 = 5` (the literal defaults to i32, the annotation widens), `: f64 = 5`, `: i32[] = []` and `: i32 | null = null` (the hole-bearing init type never renders equal to the concrete annotation), so the annotation is load-bearing and kept. The fix span runs from just after the name to the assignment `=` (found by a depth-aware token scan, so a multi-token or function-type `=>` annotation is handled), and the initializer — even `1 + 2` / `[1, 2, 3]` — is preserved byte-for-byte. cli.vl reads the finding stream (joined module, no marshalling) for both the report and `cliApplyFixes`. Gated to SINGLE-MODULE checks for now (a graph compile merges modules, so a dependency's findings would mis-attribute against the entry — per-module attribution à la inlay hints' `symOccModuleAt` is the follow-up, with the return-type and parameter rules). Validated: native fixpoint holds; corpus 468/0, LSP 36/0, native suite 513/0 (the finding stream is cli-only, never in the adjudicated diagnostic count); **0 false positives across 472 single-file corpus/std files** (already inference-first). (`tests/vl_check_redundant_type_test.ts`)
- **C-redundant-type: module-aware `--fix` + applied to the compiler (−150 annotations).** Drops the single-module gate. Two changes make the rule safe and reportable across a module graph: (1) **per-module attribution** — each finding records its name token; the driver's `redunModuleAt = modOfTok(redunTokAt(i))` (mirroring `inlayModuleAt`) resolves the owning module, and the cli keeps only the ENTRY's (module 0), de-mangling the merge's `$mN` suffix for the message + anchoring the fix span at the `:` token (robust to a mangled binding name). (2) **module-aware `--fix`** — the fix moves from a pre-fetch, entry-ALONE check (which gave unreliable types under unresolved imports — it once mangled `let x: i32[] = []` into `let x: []`) to AFTER the resolved module compile in `cliCheckCurFile`: the now-trustworthy redundant-annotation + lint streams drive the edits, the file is written, and a module-aware re-check produces the report (`cliComputeFixes` no longer re-checks; error diags are captured BEFORE the lint re-parse so module-compile errors aren't dropped). So an import-DEPENDENT redundancy (`let e: i32 = importedFn()`) is now removed too, while empty-collection holes stay correctly excluded. **Dogfooded:** `vl check --fix compiler/ std/` removed **150 redundant local-variable annotations** across 5 files, and the seed rebuilt from the cleaned source is **byte-identical** to before — proving every removal was semantics-neutral (the strongest possible check). Validated: native fixpoint holds; lint-self + fmt-check clean; native suite 518/0, corpus 468/0, full suite 612/0. Remaining follow-ups: the return-type and parameter rules (`docs/vl-tech-debt.md`). (`compiler/cli.vl`, `compiler/typecheck.vl`, `scripts/vl-compiler-driver.vl`)
- **C-redundant-type: redundant RETURN-type annotations + applied to the compiler (−1180 annotations).** Extends the family to `function f(): T { … }` whose body's inferred return is exactly `T`. A `retNatural` accumulator (the first non-void/non-error return, mirroring the inference's `noteInferredRet`) is gathered while checking an ANNOTATED function, and a finding is recorded when it renders identically to the declared return; `--fix` deletes the `: T` between the params `)` and the body `{` (span ends found by token-index scans — robust in a merged compile). Conservative exclusions: an annotation-influenced return (empty `[]`/`Map()`/`Set()`, `null`) is load-bearing and kept; and the rule is scoped to SCALAR/STRING returns of NON-generic functions — exactly the categories the emitter (default + `A20`) lowers for an un-annotated function. Object / array / union / nullable returns and `self`-generic functions keep their annotation (the emitter re-derives those from the return expression and can mis-lower; array support is a follow-up `A20` widening). This was the hard one: it needed `A18` (order-independent scope), `A19` (binding-group recursive inference) and `A20` (elaborate inferred returns for codegen) to land first — removing an annotation makes the function inferred, and earlier the inference (forward globals, recursive cycles) or codegen (string/variable returns) couldn't keep up. **Dogfooded:** `vl check --fix compiler/ std/` removed **1180 redundant return annotations**, the seed rebuilt from the cleaned source is **byte-identical**, and the native fixpoint holds — every removal provably semantics-neutral. Validated: lint-self + fmt-check clean; corpus 477/0; native suite 518/0. (`compiler/typecheck.vl`, `tests/vl_check_redundant_type_test.ts`)
- **C-redundant-type: classify inferred `i32[]` returns for codegen (`A20` widening).** Extends `A20` + the redundant-return rule from scalar/string to `i32[]` returns: the checker exports the inferred `i32[]` return name and the emitter maps it to `fRetArr` directly (no synthesized node — `fmt`-safe), so an un-annotated function returning a `let`-bound i32-array emits correctly when indexed, and the rule now flags such a return as redundant. The compiler-wide APPLY (removing the ~22 now-redundant `i32[]` annotations) is a SEPARATE follow-up — it must bootstrap on THIS seed, since the release seed can't compile a source with those annotations removed until the i32[] classification is itself in the seed (each seed self-compiles the next). `string[]` stays out — `string` is internally `{[i32]:i32}`, so `string[]` is a ref-list whose backing wasm type the bare `fRetStrArr` flag doesn't drive (the annotated path registers it via the real `TypeRef`); follow-up in `docs/vl-tech-debt.md`. (`tests/cases/functions/infer-i32-array-return-codegen.vl`)
- **C-redundant-type: applied `i32[]` return cleanup (−22).** The compiler-wide apply that rides on the previous entry's seed: `vl check --fix compiler/` removed **22** now-redundant `i32[]` return annotations (the big-int math, byte-buffer builders, etc., across `ast`/`parser`/`typecheck`/`wasmEmit`). Seed rebuilt from the cleaned source is **byte-identical** and the native fixpoint holds — every removal provably semantics-neutral. Split from the classification change because the release seed must carry the i32[] classification before it can compile a source with those annotations removed (each seed self-compiles the next). Validated: lint-self + fmt-check clean; corpus 478/0; native gated suite 519/0.
- **C-redundant-type: classify inferred `string[]` returns for codegen (`A20` widening).** Extends `A20` + the redundant-return rule from scalar/string/`i32[]` to `string[]` returns — the last array case the rule can lower safely. The earlier i32[] entry parked `string[]` on the theory that a `string` is internally `{[i32]:i32}` so its list needs a per-function ref-list slot the bare flag can't drive; in fact `string[]` lowers to the SAME module-global string-list wrapper (`mkListIdx`, valtype kind 7) the annotated path uses, so the only gap was the un-annotated **function-signature result valtype** — it handled `fRetStr/Arr/Uni/Ref/Nul/RArr` but not `fRetStrArr`, so an inferred `string[]` return fell back to an i32 result and a caller indexing it trapped (`index receiver is not an array or string`). The checker now exports the inferred `string[]` return name (same side table as scalar/string/`i32[]`), the emitter sets `fRetStrArr` directly from it (no synthesized node — `fmt`-safe), and the signature path maps that flag to kind 7. The rule now flags such a return as redundant. The compiler-wide APPLY (removing the 7 now-redundant `string[]` annotations) is a SEPARATE follow-up — it must bootstrap on THIS seed, since the release seed can't compile a source with those annotations removed until the classification is itself in the seed (each seed self-compiles the next). Remaining array/return kinds kept annotated: `f64[]`, ref-arrays (`T[]`), unions, nullable — `docs/vl-tech-debt.md`. (`tests/cases/functions/infer-string-array-return-codegen.vl`, `tests/vl_check_redundant_type_test.ts`)
- **C-redundant-type: applied `string[]` return cleanup (−7).** The compiler-wide apply that rides on the previous entry's seed: `vl check --fix compiler/` removed **7** now-redundant `string[]` return annotations — the line-splitters (`cliSplitLines`/`splitLines`), `cliPopLast`, the definite-assignment snapshot (`daSnapshot`), the `&`-type splitter (`splitTopAmp`), and the param/capture name lists (`fnParamNamesOf`/`captureNamesOf`), across `cli`/`format`/`typecheck`/`wasmEmit`. Seed rebuilt from the cleaned source is **byte-identical** and the native fixpoint holds — every removal provably semantics-neutral. Split from the classification change because the release seed must carry the `string[]` classification before it can compile a source with those annotations removed (each seed self-compiles the next). Validated: lint-self + fmt-check clean; corpus 479/0; redundant-type unit suite 6/0.

- **C6 `vl test` — the in-language test runner (webcraft P1.6).** `vl test [path]` walks for `*.test.vl`, compiles each module-aware (so `std:` and relative imports resolve), and runs them **one wasm instance per file across a host thread pool**. Measured 1.11 s serial → **0.31 s at `--jobs 4`** on four CPU-bound files (118% → 425% CPU). **All POLICY is VL** — discovery, compilation, the plan, `-t` filtering, the report and the exit code live in `compiler/cli.vl` as a third subcommand (`cliCmd == 2`) on the existing command-queue pump — and exactly three raw mechanisms cross to Rust (`CMD_TEST_STASH` keeps an emitted module, `CMD_TEST_COLLECT` instantiates + reads each registry, `CMD_TEST_RUN` runs the plan), because instances, threads and trap catching are the engine's and the engine is not reachable from a wasm program. This is the `CMD_RUN_WASM` capability `docs/internals/cli-design.md` reserved. **`std:test` grew its registration half**: `describe`/`it`/`itSkip`/`beforeEach`/`afterEach` register into module-level parallel arrays at instantiation (the start function IS the collect pass), and the `vlt*` protocol exports let the host drive one test per call — which is what buys per-test trap isolation, output attribution and single-pass filtering. **A test file writes no boilerplate**: the runner APPENDS the protocol re-export to the source before compiling (appended, never prepended, so every diagnostic position in the user's file is unchanged — asserted). **Trap isolation is real**: a failed expectation, a raw `__trap__()` and an out-of-bounds index each fail their own test and nothing else — the host catches the trap and re-instantiates, and the fixture proves a test declared after two traps still runs and passes. **Failure messages are structural, not scraped**: the matcher records `expected 7 to equal 8` in a module global and the host reads it back off the unwound instance (`vltFailLen`/`vltFailAt`); a trap carrying no message falls back to the engine's text. A test file that does **not compile** is one failing entry with the compiler's own positioned diagnostics and the run continues. Exit 0 green / 1 any failure / 2 usage. Known v1 gaps, all filed: void-return covariance on function values (a body ending in an ASSIGNMENT needs a trailing `done()` — VL types a function by its tail), f64 failure rendering (`<f64>` + the values printed into the captured output, until `std:fmt` grows f64→string), no per-test timings/timeouts, no `--no-capture`/`dot`/`json` reporters. Two ROADMAP premises were refuted on the way: `std:fs` was **not** the gate (the walk was already in `cli.vl`), and "the linker stays EMPTY" was already false (every printing program imports `__print_*__`). (`docs/internals/vl-test-design.md`, `tests/vl_test_runner_test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/vl-test*/`)
- **C-fmt: the trailing-lambda exception (`vl test`'s top follow-up).** `vl fmt` exploded `it("adds", () => { … })` into a six-line vertical argument list **structurally, not on width** — a 34-column call broke exactly as a 100-column one did, because the list rule is "an argument spans lines ⇒ break every argument" and a block-bodied lambda always spans lines. Now the call **hugs** when its FINAL argument is a block-bodied lambda, every preceding argument fits on one line, and the head line fits `fmtWidth`. The three deferred design questions get one guard set rather than three rules: an EARLIER lambda renders multi-line, so a two-lambda call fails the "preceding arguments fit" guard and breaks vertically; a lambda FOLLOWED by another argument fails "the last argument is a lambda"; both rulings are what the corpus already spells (of the 38 block-lambda call sites in the repo, **36 are trailing**, **2 are the `callIt(lambda, k)` shape and are already written broken**, and **0** pass two lambdas). Expression-bodied lambdas are untouched. Implementation discipline that is load-bearing: `hugTrailingLambda` **decides before it renders**, because a lambda body is rendered through the formatter's CONSUMING comment cursor and a speculative render thrown away would delete every comment inside it at rc 0. **Blast radius zero** — all 33 gated `.vl` files stay `fmt --check` clean, so nothing in `compiler/`/`std/`/`scripts/` is reformatted and the compiler's own bytes are the only delta. All **8** `tests/fixtures/vl-test*/` fixtures are now fmt-clean AS WRITTEN (their "deliberately not fmt-normalized" comments are gone, and `tests/vl_fmt_test.ts` pins that they stay clean). Validated: idempotence over 1,675 parseable corpus files (0 failures); round-trip over all 1,693 (`vl build` of the original and of the formatted text byte-identical, 0 differences); the formatter's reject set unchanged (26 = 26); 4 corpus files format better and none worse; two sabotages (hug-nothing → 3 fixtures red, hug-everything → 1). (`docs/internals/fmt-trailing-lambda-design.md`, `tests/vl_fmt_test.ts`)

## LSP / editor (Track D)

- **D0 Diagnostics on change.**
- **D1 Hover types** — `onHover` resolves cursor via D2 symbol table + `receiver.member` (object fields, array/list/string members via `typeFromExpression`/`listMemberType`/field); rendered via `stringifyType`.
- **D2 Go-to-definition / find-references** — symbol/binding table (`compiler/symbols.ts`); `textDocument/definition` + `textDocument/references`; locals, params, function decls, type aliases, single-document. → `DECISIONS.md`
- **D3 Autocomplete (core)** — scope-aware identifier completion + structural member completion (`lsp/src/typeFeatures.ts`); tested (`tests/lsp_completion_test.ts`).
- **D4 Formatter (core)** — AST-driven source formatter (`compiler/format.vl` via the seed, `vl fmt`, LSP `textDocument/formatting`); idempotent, round-trip-AST-equivalent, comment-preserving, 80-col reflow.
- **D5 Semantic tokens** — `textDocument/semanticTokens/full`: identifiers via D2 table, literals/keywords/operators via lexer, comments by source scan, `receiver.member` names (→ `property`/`method`).
- **D6 Inlay hints** — inline `: <type>` at unannotated `let`/`const`/params and omitted returns; annotated positions and unresolved holes suppressed.
- **D6 fix: an inferred array displays as `T[]`, not `{i32: T}`** — an array is internally an `{[i32]: T}` index-signature object; an array LITERAL (`[1, 2]`) and an `A-infer-empty` empty `[]` were keyed by the integer type `i32` and so fell through to the generic object render `{i32: i32}` in hover / inlay / error messages, while a `T[]` annotation already printed `i32[]`. Array construction now CANONICALIZES on a single `number`-keyed index signature (`number` = the i32-width array-index alias) across all three paths — the `T[]` annotation suffix (`parseArrayType`), the array literal (`ArrayLiteral`), and the intrinsic `listOf` (`.push`/`.map`/`.values()` results) — so a literal, an annotation, and an inferred-empty `[]; .push(1)` yield the SAME type shape and all render `i32[]` (`["a"]` → `string[]`, `[1.0]` → `f64[]`, `[[1]]` → `i32[][]`). `arrayElementType` / `isListType` / `getConcreteType` / `ensureType` treat the `number` key as the i32-width index (resolving to `i32`), so element inference, indexing, `.push`/`.length`, assignability, and codegen are unchanged; an explicit `{[i32]: V}` index-signature annotation keeps its `i32` key (still a native array, rendered `{i32: V}`), and a `string`-keyed map (`{[string]: V}`) stays a distinct map. (`tests/cases/arrays/render-i32-array.vl`, `tests/lsp_inlay_hint_test.ts`)
- **D3 Keyword + snippet completions** — 26 keywords + 10 snippet skeletons with tab-stops; after-dot completions suppress keywords/snippets; trigger-character tuning. (#143)
- **D4 Formatter: collapse short if/if-else** — a single-conditional `if { stmt }` or `if { a } else { b }` that fits 80 cols folds to one line; comments/multi-statement bodies stay block. (#138)
- **D4 Formatter: trailing comment on `type` alias** — a trailing comment on a `type X = …` line now stays on that line instead of being displaced. (#146)
- **D4 Formatter: verbatim-fallback for fn with commented expression body** — a function whose body is a single expression spanning multiple lines with an own-line comment is reproduced verbatim, preventing the comment from being displaced after the closing brace.
- **D4 Formatter: trailing comment on bare-body loop header** — a trailing comment on a `while`, `for`, or `for-in` header with a bare (brace-less) body (e.g. `while r < 10 // cond`) now stays on the header line instead of being displaced outside the enclosing function.
- **D4 Formatter: trailing comment on block closing brace** — a trailing comment written on the same source line as a closing `}` (e.g. `} // done`, `} // end of f`) now stays on that line for `if`, `while`, `for`, `for-in`, function, and free-standing blocks instead of being displaced to an own line before the next statement; the `if`-block case was also not idempotent (second pass re-moved the comment).
- **D4 Formatter: trailing comment on verbatim-fallback function last line** — a trailing comment on the last source line of an operator-named (`function +(self,b){…}`) or method-shorthand-body function (reproduced verbatim because the AST discards the surface syntax) now stays attached to that line instead of being displaced as an own-line comment before the next statement.
- **D7 Doc-comment cross-references (single-file)** — `` [`Name`] `` / `[Name]` rustdoc-style intra-doc links in `///` comments resolve via D2's symbol table and render as clickable markdown links in hover and completion `documentation`; unresolved names left verbatim; cross-import resolution deferred (needs H0 phase 3 module graph).
- **Module-aware diagnostics (H0 phase 3)** — the open file is analyzed as the entry module through a workspace `ModuleReader` (open buffers + disk, `lsp/src/moduleGraph.ts`): `import`ed names resolve to their real types instead of flagging "undeclared", and genuine import errors (bad path / not-exported / cycle) surface on the import line; hover/completion seed the same imported-name types; no-import files unchanged.
- **Cross-file navigation (H0 phase 3)** — go-to-definition and doc-comment `` [`Name`] `` xrefs on an imported name jump to the EXPORTING sibling module's declaration (`lsp/src/moduleGraph.ts` resolves an imported local name → exporting module + the export's decl span via the symbol table); find-references gathers a symbol's occurrences across the current file + other OPEN documents (graph-seeded so importer uses are recorded); single-file go-to-def/refs/doc-xref unchanged; refs over unopened on-disk siblings deferred.
- **Find-references: unopened on-disk sibling modules (H0 phase 3)** — find-references now crawls `.vl` files on disk that are not open in the editor: project root is detected from the LSP workspace folder or by walking up to the nearest `deno.json`/`package.json`/`.git` ancestor (max 6 levels); `.git`, `node_modules`, `dist`, `.claude`, `reference` dirs are skipped; at most 500 files per request; open-buffer text wins over disk; on-disk results are deduplicated against already-found open-doc refs by URI.
- **D8 (partial) Type-alias name preservation** — hover/inlay render the alias name (`Point`/`I32`) not its expanded body; `Type` node carries the source alias name (`aliasSymbol`-style), `stringifyType` `maxDepth=0` default preserves all names. (Verbosity step-expansion still ahead — needs proposed LSP 3.18.)
- **D — Project-wide unused-export hints (two signals)** — a debounced workspace pass (triggered on document save and after a 3-second idle) builds a global use-map over up to 500 `.vl` files and publishes `hint`/`unnecessary`-tagged diagnostics for two export conditions: (1) **fully dead** (`code: "unused-export"`) — exported symbol with zero references anywhere (not imported by any other module AND not used within its own file) — greys the export **name**; (2) **redundant export** (`code: "redundant-export"`) — exported symbol used only within its own module (e.g. a recursive function or a locally-consumed value) but never imported by any other module — greys the **`export` keyword** itself with message `` `export` is redundant — `foo` is only used within this module, never imported ``; the `export` keyword span is captured in `Binding.exportKeywordSpan` (stamped in `compiler/parser.ts` alongside `binding.exported = true`); the use-map tracks cross-module imports and same-file local uses separately (`ExportRefCounts { cross, local }`); struct fields are out of scope (structural typing makes field-level analysis fuzzy); merged with regular lint/type diagnostics; tests in `tests/lsp_unused_export_test.ts`.
- **kill-TS: semantic tokens off the self-hosted checker (lexical slice)** — the last TS dependency in the wasm semantic-tokens path is gone: new driver exports (`lexScan`/`lexClassAt`/`lexLineAt`/`lexColAt`/`lexLenAt` in `scripts/vl-compiler-driver.vl`) tokenize the staged source and classify each COLOURED token (keyword / operator / numeric+boolean literal / comment) natively — the counterpart of the host's TS `tokenize` + `lexicalTokenType` + comment scan. `wasmChecker.lexicalTokensAt` wraps them (`WasmLexicalToken`); `semanticTokensDataFromWasm` (typeFeatures) assembles a whole document from the wasm identifier + lexical + member slices with NO `tokenize`/`checkOnly`/AST, and the `"wasm"`-mode `textDocument/semanticTokens` handler now takes that path (TS only in `"ts"`/`"both"`/no-seed fallback). Incidentally fixes an operator-classification drift: the TS `lexicalTokenType` matched stale kind tags (`DIV`/`MOD`/`EXCLAMATION`/`EQUAL_TO`/…) the lexer never emits, so `/`, `%`, `!`, `==`, `!=`, `<`/`>`/`<=`/`>=`, `<<`/`>>`/`>>>`, and the compound-assigns went uncoloured; the native classifier keys off the real lexer kinds, so every operator now gets a token. (`tests/lsp_semantic_tokens_test.ts` — `wasm-lexical: …`)
- **kill-TS: member completion off the self-hosted checker** — `receiver.` member completion now resolves natively: new driver exports (`memberScanAt` + the per-index name/type/`memberScanIsFn` accessors) over a new `symMemberScanAt` in `compiler/typecheck.vl` resolve the receiver binding under the cursor, soften a nullable, and enumerate its members — a `TyObj`'s identifier-named fields (with `tyToStr` detail), or a `string`'s builtin methods (`slice`/`indexOf`/`includes`/`charCodeAt`); arrays/maps surface none, matching the TS path. `wasmChecker.memberCompletionsAt` wraps them (`WasmMemberCompletion`) and `memberCompletionsFromWasm` (typeFeatures) maps them to completion items. The native parser isn't error-tolerant for the incomplete `receiver.`, so `server.ts` strips the trailing `.` (a `removeCharAt` repair) and resolves the receiver as a bare expression at its own position; the `"wasm"`-mode `onCompletion` `.`-branch takes this path, falling back to the TS `receiverObjectType` + `memberCompletions` only when wasm yields nothing (no regression). (`tests/lsp_member_completion_wasm_test.ts`)
- **kill-TS: imported names complete under their source name** — in a multi-module compile the merge renames a cross-module binding to `name$mN`, so wasm-mode completion in an importer surfaced an imported name under its internal mangled form (`add$m1`). `symScopeAt` now de-mangles to the source name (`add`) for display and de-dups on it (`$` is not a legal identifier char, so the suffix is unambiguous). (The broader cross-module scope leak — a dependency's nested locals whose per-module line span overlaps the cursor — is a separate, pre-existing issue tracked in `docs/vl-tech-debt.md`.) (`tests/lsp_crossfile_wasm_test.ts`)
- **kill-TS: completion fully self-hosted in `"wasm"` mode** — the builtin half of identifier completion (the numeric/string TYPE names + builtin FUNCTIONS `print`/`Map`/`Set`/`toString`/`fromCodePoint[s]`) now comes from a native `builtinScan` export (driver) instead of the TS `defaultScope`; the checker defines these as special-cased arms with no enumerable scope, so the completion list is maintained in the native LSP surface, the single source of truth co-located with the compiler. `wasmChecker.builtinCompletions` + `builtinCompletionsFromWasm` (typeFeatures) wrap it. `server.ts`'s `onCompletion` now has a dedicated `"wasm"`-mode branch — native `scopeAt` (in-scope user bindings, incl. imported names) + native builtins + native member completion — that uses NO `checkOnly`/`parseSymbolsSeeded`/`importedScopeFor`/`defaultScope`; the TS path remains only for `"ts"`/`"both"`/older-seed fallback. (`tests/lsp_member_completion_wasm_test.ts` — `wasm-builtins: …`)
- **kill-TS: hover fully self-hosted in `"wasm"` mode** — `onHover` in `"wasm"` mode now resolves through a TS-free chain: native value-binding type (`hoverTypeAt`, incl. imported names) → member-access type (`memberTypeAt`) → user `type`-alias body → builtin. The new `typeAliasAt` export (driver) + `symTypeAliasAt` (typecheck) close the type-alias-hover gap by scanning the token stream for the identifier under the cursor and resolving it through the checker's declared-types map (`cUserTypes`) — no new occurrence table; builtin hover reuses the PR-4a `builtinCompletions` list. No `checkOnly`/`parseSymbolsSeeded`/`importedScopeFor` on the wasm hover path (TS retained for `"ts"`/`"both"`/older-seed). Source `///` docs remain absent on wasm-mode hover (a pre-existing limitation now tracked in `docs/vl-tech-debt.md`). (`tests/lsp_wasm_checker_test.ts` — `wasm-symbols: typeAliasAt …`)
- **kill-TS: inlay hints off the self-hosted checker (+ inferred-return retention fix)** — `inlayHint` in `"wasm"` mode now sources the inferred types + declaration positions from the native checker (`inlayScan` export over `symInlayScan`, wrapped by `wasmChecker.inlayHintsAt`); the source-scan filters (skip an annotation the user wrote, place a function's hint after its `)`, honor the request range) stay host-side in `inlayHintsFromWasm` (typeFeatures), reusing the same helpers as the TS `deriveInlayHints`. No `parseSymbols` on the wasm inlay path. **Checker fix:** an unannotated function's demand-inferred return is now written BACK into its retained symbol-table type (`checkFuncDeclNode` re-retains after inference) — previously the retained `fnRet` kept the `-2` infer-later sentinel, so hover rendered `(i32, i32) -> <none>` and a function-return inlay hint couldn't be produced; both now show the inferred `-> i32`. (`tests/lsp_inlay_hint_test.ts` — `wasm-inlay: …`; `tests/lsp_wasm_checker_test.ts` — `inferred return is retained`)
- **kill-TS: neutral diagnostic-type leaf (`compiler/diagnostics.ts`)** — the LSP-facing diagnostic vocabulary (`VLDiagnostic`/`VLDiagnosticTag`/`VLRange`/`VLPosition`/`VLSeverity`) moved out of `compile.ts` (which drags in the whole compiler) into a dependency-free leaf; `compile.ts` re-exports it for its existing consumers. The LSP imports the shapes from the leaf, so `wasmChecker.ts` now imports NOTHING from `compile.ts` (`server.ts`/`moduleGraph.ts` still use its TS functions, pending the final teardown). Pure type relocation — no behaviour change.
- **kill-TS: the LSP server runs entirely on the self-hosted checker** — `server.ts` is now wasm-only. The `vital.checker` `"ts"`/`"both"` modes and their live parity instruments (`diffDiagnostics`/`diffHoverType`/`diffSemanticTokens`/`diffDefinition`/`diffReferences`) are gone, along with every TS fallback path (`checkDocument`/`checkOnly`/`parseSymbols`/`parseProgram`/`tokenize`/`defaultScope`) and the helpers that fed them (`importedScopeFor`/`parseSymbolsSeeded`/`buildDocRefResolver`/`ctxToRange`). Each handler now runs the native path and degrades to an empty/no-op result when no seed is loaded (rather than a TS fallback). `server.ts` imports NOTHING from the compiler core — only the diagnostic types (the leaf) and `format` (the formatter fallback, its finale separately gated on the playground migration). The `vital.checker` setting is removed (there's no TS checker to select); `vital.compilerWasm` stays. Net −490 lines. The authoritative batch parity oracle (`scripts/checker-parity-sweep.ts`) is untouched.
- **kill-TS: moduleGraph off the compiler core** — with `server.ts` no longer calling them, moduleGraph's orphaned TS front-end functions are deleted: `checkDocument`/`analyzeEntryImports`/`resolveModuleExports`(+cached)/`exportCache`/`diagnosticFromError`/`GraphCheckResult`/`EntryAnalysis` (the graph-aware diagnostics path) and the TS `importedNameSources`/`importedNameSource`/`exportedDeclRanges`/`cachedParseSymbols` (the import/export resolver). The surviving cross-file functions (`crossFileReferences`, `canonicalExportOf`, `buildUnusedExportUseMap`, `unusedExportHints`) are wasm-backed. `moduleGraph.ts` now imports only the `ModuleReader` glue type + the diagnostic leaf — nothing from the compiler core (lexer/parser/typecheck/compile/defaultScope/lint/ast). Obsolete TS-path tests removed (`lint_import_range_test.ts`, `lsp_module_diagnostics_test.ts` — module diagnostics are covered by the wasm checker tests); `lsp_crossfile_test.ts` trimmed to its wasm `crossFileReferences` cases (go-to-def/doc-xref covered by `lsp_crossfile_wasm_test.ts`). `server.ts`, `moduleGraph.ts`, and `wasmChecker.ts` now import nothing from the compiler core; `typeFeatures.ts` is the last holdout.
- **kill-TS: the wasm checker is now environment-agnostic** — `wasmChecker.ts` no longer touches `node:fs`: its loader splits into `createWasmChecker(instantiate, wrapReader?)` (the env-agnostic driver protocol — types, the symbol/token/scope/member/inlay + format/lint query families, the diff helpers) and a new `wasmCheckerNode.ts` that owns the `node:fs` seed load (path → bytes, mtime-reload) + the `withStd` reader wrapper and delegates to the core. Node consumers (`server.ts`, the wasm-checker tests, `checker-parity-sweep.ts`) import `loadWasmChecker` from `wasmCheckerNode.ts` (which re-exports the whole core surface); the std overlay is now an injected `wrapReader` rather than a hard `withStd` import, so the core has no `node:fs`/DOM coupling. This unblocks the browser playground driving the SAME self-hosted checker (fetched seed + embedded-std reader) — the keystone for retiring the playground's TS LSP transforms. Pure refactor — no behaviour change. (existing `tests/lsp_*wasm*`/`lsp_wasm_checker_test.ts` cover it via the relocated import.)
- **kill-TS: the editor layer imports nothing runtime-bound from the compiler core** — the LSP/playground (and the type-importing crossfile tests) reached into `ast.ts`/`symbols.ts`/`modules.ts` for three type-only leaves — `Position`, `BindingKind`, `ModuleReader` — files that vanish when `compiler/*.ts` is deleted. Those types move to a new dependency-free leaf `compiler/coreTypes.ts` (mirroring the `diagnostics.ts` precedent); the original homes re-export them so the compiler's internal imports are unchanged, and the editor layer imports them from the leaf. `lsp/src` now references only the two neutral leaves (`diagnostics.ts`, `coreTypes.ts`); the sole remaining `compiler/*.ts` runtime importer outside the core is `tests/std_resolution_test.ts` (the TS module-loader test). Also deletes the fully-orphaned `compiler/wasmDebug.ts` (zero importers). Pure type relocation — no behaviour change; `deno check`/`deno lint`/LSP+playground builds clean.
- **D4 Formatter (self-hosted) quality pass** — `format.vl` gains first-line-only indent on wrapped object literals, trailing-comment alignment within a block (opt-in when any comment in the block sits >1 space off its token), forced-paren wrapping for over-wide `if`/`while` conditions, per-clause `if`/`else if` chain reflow, parameter-list wrapping, and inline-collapse refinements. (#445, #447–#452)
- **`vl fmt` is ~linear** — fixed O(n²) hotspots (the `joinOut`/`joinLines`/`normalizeProgram` string accumulators and the `lineOf`/`exportPrefix` per-node scans) that crashed (`allocation size too large`) or hung on large files; now divide-and-conquer joins + binary-search lookups. (#455)
- **Parser: newline after `(` / before `)`** — `parsePrimary` skips newlines around a parenthesized expression (porting the host's behavior), so `vl fmt`'s forced-paren condition output re-parses. (#454)
- **`vl fmt --check` gate** — the whole `compiler/`, `std/`, `scripts/` tree is `vl fmt`-clean, enforced in `scripts/lint-self.sh` + CI (tests/ excluded by construction); landed via a one-time tree reformat. (#456)
- **cleanup: `0 - N` literal idiom → unary `-N`** — ~915 operand-position numeric-literal sites in the compiler source; semantically identical (unary `-` lowers to the same `push 0; <x>; i32.sub`, so the self-compiled seed is byte-identical). (#457)
- **kill-TS: retire the TS formatter (`compiler/format.ts`)** — the self-hosted `format.vl` (via the seed) is the sole formatter for `vl fmt`, the LSP, and the playground; `format.ts` had no remaining importers and is deleted. `lsp/src` and `compiler/cli.ts` reference no TS formatter.

## Browser playground (Track E)

- **E1 Browser bundle** — esbuild + `esbuild-deno-loader`; binaryen@130 runs client-side ESM; `deno task playground`.
- **E2 Playground UI (core)** — `<textarea>` + Run + diagnostics/log/WAT panes + sample picker (`playground/`).
- **E2 Playground redesign** — branded light/dark theme pair (system-default with revert-to-system toggle, lock-step Monaco theme), results-as-tabs (Output/WAT/Diagnostics + size/count badges), multi-file projects (per-file diagnostics, whole-program `compileProgram` run/WAT), opt-in auto-run (always-on debounced analysis), full-width status bar, real Share + Format wiring.
- **E4 Shareable links** — source encoded in URL hash (#94).
- **Playground↔LSP parity test** — `tests/playground_lsp_parity_test.ts` enumerates the language features `lsp/src/server.ts` exposes (handlers self-validated against the live source) and asserts, per feature, that `playground/src/lspAdapter.ts` exports the pure binding AND `playground/src/main.ts` wires the matching Monaco provider — so an LSP feature can't be added without wiring the playground (the gap that left "Auto Fix" silently missing). Three current gaps are documented as asserted known-TODOs: code actions (`registerCodeActionProvider` unwired in `main.ts` though the `codeActions` adapter binding exists), find-references, and completion.
- **kill-TS: the playground's LSP runs on the self-hosted seed** — the playground's language features (semantic tokens, hover, inlay hints, go-to-definition, completion, format) no longer run the TS `typeFeatures` transforms / symbol table; they drive the SAME `build/vl-compiler.wasm` seed the Node LSP and `vl check` run, through the environment-agnostic `createWasmChecker` core. New `playground/src/wasmCheckerBrowser.ts` fetches the seed (`new URL("vl-compiler.wasm", import.meta.url)` — copied next to the bundle by `build.ts`) and instantiates it with an embedded-std reader (the `std:` half of `withStd`, no workspace dir in a browser); `lspAdapter.ts` is rewired to drive it (mirroring `server.ts`'s wasm-mode handlers) + the kept `*FromWasm` assembly helpers, with `main.ts`'s providers now awaiting the async results. The features degrade to empty (format to a no-op) until the seed loads, and stay disabled if it can't be fetched — Run is unaffected (it still uses the TS compiler + binaryen, pending the codegen-export migration). Diagnostics stay on the TS `checkOnly`; quick-fixes stay pure string surgery. The GitHub Pages deploy (`pages.yml`) fetches the seed (`fetch-seed.sh`) and ships it in the artifact so the live site keeps these features. `playground/verify.ts` exercises the full bundled path; `tests/playground_lsp_wasm_test.ts` pins it in CI (and `playground_completion_test.ts` moved to the seed-backed path). This makes `playground/src/lspAdapter.ts` the last consumer of the TS `typeFeatures` transforms to migrate — clearing the way to delete them.
- **kill-TS: the TS feature transforms are deleted (`typeFeatures.ts` off the compiler core)** — with the Node LSP and the playground both on the wasm checker, the TS analysis transforms are dead and gone: `classifyDocument`/`classifyTokens`/`semanticTokensData`/`semanticTokensDataFromIdentifiers` (semantic tokens), `deriveInlayHints` (inlay), `identifierCompletions`/`memberCompletions`/`receiverObjectType` (completion), `resolveMemberAt` (+ `ResolvedMember`/`MemberTokenKind`), and every helper they alone used (`classifySymbolTokens`/`lexicalTokenType`/`classifyLexicalTokens`/`classifyMemberTokens`/`commentTokens`/`spanToken`/the member-typing internals). What remains is pure assembly over the wasm checker's output (`semanticTokensDataFromWasm`/`inlayHintsFromWasm`/`builtinCompletionsFromWasm`/`memberCompletionsFromWasm`/`scopeCompletionsFromBindings`, the keyword/snippet lists, `docMarkdown`/`linkifyDocRefs`, the legend + `encodeSemanticTokens`). `typeFeatures.ts` now imports NOTHING executable from the compiler core — only two TYPE aliases (`Position`, `BindingKind`); its `../../compiler/typecheck.ts` value imports and the `spanOf` import are gone (1588 → 812 lines). Obsolete TS-path tests removed (`lsp_completion_test.ts`, `lsp_member_hover_test.ts`, `lsp_type_features_test.ts` — covered by the wasm suites) and the mixed ones trimmed to their wasm cases (`lsp_inlay_hint_test.ts`, `lsp_semantic_tokens_test.ts`). The LSP (`server.ts`/`moduleGraph.ts`/`wasmChecker.ts`/`typeFeatures.ts`) and the playground are now entirely off the TS compiler front end for editor features — only the playground's Run path still uses the TS compiler + binaryen (the codegen-export migration is the remaining step).
- **kill-TS: the LSP formatter drops its TS fallback** — `server.ts`'s `onDocumentFormatting` formatted via the seed's `wasmChecker.formatSrc` but fell back to the TS `format()` (`compiler/format.ts`) on a parse error / missing export. That fallback is removed: format runs on the self-hosted formatter only; `undefined` (parse error / no seed) → no edits, as before. `lsp/src` now imports NOTHING executable from `compiler/` — only type-only leaves (`VLDiagnostic`/`ModuleReader`/`Position`/`BindingKind`), which relocate when `compiler/*.ts` is deleted.
- **kill-TS: the playground's Run path compiles on the self-hosted seed** — `Run` no longer uses the TS compiler's back end (`compile`/`compileProgram` → binaryen). A new `createWasmChecker.compile(source, entryKey, read)` turns source into wasm bytes via the driver's `compileSrc` + `rbyte*` reads (the SAME path `vl build` runs), alongside the error-tier diagnostics; `playground.ts`'s `runProgram`/`runProject` take an injected checker and feed those bytes to the pure `runWasm` (a `WebAssembly.instantiate` over the VL host-import ABI — no binaryen, no front end), and `main.ts` gates Run on the seed (no TS fallback). The seed's emitted module is byte-ABI-identical to the TS compiler's (the fixpoint equivalence), so `runWasm`'s import object runs it unchanged. Two TS bits remain on the playground, each its own follow-up: diagnostics still use the codegen-free `checkOnly`, and the WAT pane is rendered by binaryen (`wasmToWat`, now lazily imported) disassembling the seed's bytes — binaryen no longer compiles VL. `tests/playground_lsp_wasm_test.ts` gains seed-backed `compile`/`runProgram` cases; `playground/verify.ts` drives the Run path through the seed.
- **kill-TS: the playground's diagnostics run on the seed (playground fully off `compile.ts`)** — squiggles no longer use the TS `checkOnly`/`checkProgram`; `lspAdapter.diagnostics` now runs the seed `check` (error tier, whole-program via the workspace reader — so cross-module import errors surface and resolved imports don't read as "undeclared") merged with `lint`. `main.ts`'s per-file pass drops the whole-program `checkProject` + the "undeclared import" filter (each file checks as its own entry, reader-resolved); `codeActions` is now async. With Run/WAT already lifted (P1a), `playground.ts`/`lspAdapter.ts` import NOTHING executable from `compiler/` — only the `VLDiagnostic`/`ModuleReader` type leaves. Also restores the `unnecessary` tag (greyed unused/unreachable/constant-condition spans) on the wasm lint path — `wasmChecker.lint` reconstructs it from the lint `code` (the driver store carries no tag), fixing it for the Node LSP too. (`playground/verify.ts` drives diagnostics + quick-fixes through the seed.)
- **fix: cross-file semantic tokens + go-to-definition (LSP + playground)** — two cross-file bugs the editor surfaces share (the wasm checker drives both). **(1) Highlighting:** the semantic-token occurrence table spans EVERY committed module with module-local line/col, so a dependency's decls (`./mathx`'s `add`/`square` + params, at *mathx's* lines) bled onto an importer's `import` lines. `wasmChecker.tokensAt` now keeps only the entry module (table index 0, via `symOccModuleAt`) — single-file is unaffected (no module table → all module 0). (A dependency's MEMBER-access tokens bleed the same way; that table lacks a module tag and needs a driver export — a follow-up.) **(2) Go-to-definition:** `onDefinition` tried the local `definitionAt` first, which for an imported name returns the canonical decl's span in the DEPENDENCY — and with no module the host mis-attributed it to the current file (landing on the `import` line). The cross-file `importedNameSources` jump (correct file + range) now precedes `definitionAt` for imported names, on both `server.ts` and the playground (`lspAdapter.definition` gains the cross-file jump — returning the target module key — and `main.ts` resolves it to the sibling model). (`tests/lsp_semantic_tokens_test.ts` — module-0-only tokens; `tests/playground_lsp_wasm_test.ts` — cross-file go-to-def.)
- **fix: cross-file inlay hints (LSP + playground)** — the same module-bleed class as the semantic-token fix, now in inlay hints: `symInlayScan` records a hint for EVERY committed module's unannotated decls, each with module-local line/col, so in a multi-file project a dependency's hints (`./mathx`'s `add`/`square` RETURN types, its params, `TAU`) rendered against the importer — landing on the `import` line and inside the `"./mathx"` string literal. The inlay candidate now carries its occurrence index (`symInlayOccAt` in `typecheck.vl`), the driver maps it to the owning module (`inlayModuleAt = symOccModuleAt(symInlayOccAt(i))`, mirroring the semantic-token slice), and `wasmChecker.inlayHintsAt` keeps only the entry module's hints (table index 0) — single-file is unaffected (module 0 throughout), and an older seed without `inlayModuleAt` degrades to keeping every hint. Seed refreshed + native fixpoint re-proven (stage3 == stage4, 636083 bytes). (`tests/lsp_inlay_hint_test.ts` — a dependency's hints don't bleed onto the importer.)
- **kill-TS full-delete P4a: the standing test oracle drops its TS-compiler dependency** — before retiring the TS-vs-`.vl` parity suites, the tests that STAY are cut loose from `compiler/compile.ts`. The behavioral corpus oracle (`cases_wasm_test.ts` — every `tests/cases/**` `@directive` adjudicated by the seed) imported only `runWasm`/`VLRuntimeError` from `compile.ts` (harness plumbing — V8 instantiate + the `print`/`log` host-import ABI + trap mapping, not the compiler front end); that's lifted verbatim into a compiler-free `tests/support/runWasm.ts` (minus the source-map path — the corpus asserts trap REASONS, not positions).
