The numeric-conversion matrix test (`selfhost_host_parity_test.ts`) ran the TS host `checkOnly` as a third parity column; with the TS compiler retired there's nothing to parity-check against, so it now pins the NATIVE `vl check` verdict directly against the documented spec lattice (renamed `selfhost_native_conv_test.ts`, joining the `native_*` family the `ci-native` job runs). No deletions and `compile.ts` is untouched — both suites stay green — clearing the way for P4b to delete the parity scaffolding.
- **kill-TS full-delete P4b-1: the TS-vs-`.vl` parity suites are retired** — with the self-hosted seed now the compiler (and the behavioral corpus + native fixpoint + golden byte-tripwire the standing oracle), the transitional suites that compiled the SAME source through BOTH the TS compiler and the `.vl` compiler and asserted equality have served their purpose and are deleted: `selfhost_corpus`/`selfhost_corpus_run` (verdict + runtime TS≡`.vl`), `selfhost_lexer`/`selfhost_parser`/`selfhost_typecheck`/`selfhost_wasm_emit`/`selfhost_spans` (per-stage parity), `selfhost_emit_fixpoint`/`selfhost_emit_fullfixpoint`/`selfhost_emit_program` (TS-built emitter fixpoint — superseded by the TS-free `scripts/native-fixpoint.sh` + `native-golden-check.ts`), `selfhost_pipeline`, `selfhost_format`/`selfhost_format_corpus`, and `selfhost_self_typecheck`, plus their `.vl` harness fixtures (`tests/selfhost/{lexer,parser,pipeline,typecheck,wasm_emit}_harness.vl`, `sample.vl`, `probe_format_corpus.ts`). KEPT: `tests/selfhost/goldens.ts` (the golden source set `native-golden-check.ts` pins) and `tests/_selfhost_cache.ts` (still imported by the `--ts-genesis` break-glass `scripts/build-compiler-wasm.ts`, retired in P5). The remaining `selfhost_native_*` suites (align / opt / conv / diag-pos) drive the native `vl` binary, not the TS compiler. Full suite stays green (1688 passed); native golden 14/14 byte-identical.
- **kill-TS full-delete P4b-2: the TS-internal unit tests are retired** — the unit tests that drove `compiler/compile.ts` (and `ast.ts`/`lexer.ts`/`format.ts`/`toAST.ts`) internals directly are deleted, as their subjects are going away: `cases_test` (the TS corpus runner — the seed-backed `cases_wasm_test` is now the SOLE corpus oracle, run in `ci-native`), `symbols_test`, `stringify_type_test`, `ast_spans_test`, `check_only_test`, `export_function_test`, `format_test`, `lexer_comments_test`, `lint_{prefer_const_range,severity,unused_function}_test`, `reprint_readiness_test`, `source_map_test`, and `lsp_alias_hover_test` (TS symbol-table alias hover — the seed path is covered by `lsp_wasm_checker_test`'s `typeAliasAt` cases). Two seed-backed keepers were decoupled rather than deleted: `lsp_docxref_test` drops its `parseSymbols`/`stringifyType` end-to-end block (the pure `linkifyDocRefs`/`docMarkdown` helper tests stay), and `lsp_wasm_checker_test` repoints its `VLDiagnostic` type import to `compiler/diagnostics.ts` and replaces the TS-`format()` host-parity assertion with a literal canonical-source stability check. The `ci` job's `deno check` drops the deleted `tests/cases_test.ts` (now `deno check compiler/*.ts`). With this, the only remaining `compiler/*.ts` test/script consumers are the CLI suites (P3) and the `--ts-genesis`/parity-sweep scripts (P5). Full suite green; seed-backed corpus 466 + LSP suites pass.
- **kill-TS full-delete P3 (step 1+2): `vl fmt` + the compiler CLI off the deno tasks** — the native `vl` binary (`scripts/vl-host`) gains a `fmt` subcommand driving the seed's `formatSrc` (the formatting logic lives in `format.vl`; the Rust host is just I/O), matching the TS CLI's surface: `vl fmt <file>` → stdout, `-w/--write` → rewrite in place when changed, `--check` → non-zero exit on drift, `<dir>` → recurse `*.vl` (skip-list: `node_modules`/`.git`/`dist`/`reference`), stdin → stdout; a parse error leaves the source unchanged. The seed-load + `.cwasm` sidecar cache is factored into a shared `load_compiler` helper. The four compiler-facing deno tasks (`run`/`build`/`check`/`fmt` → `compiler/cli.ts`) are deleted from `deno.json` — `vl` is now the CLI (`test`/`lsp:*`/`playground:*`/`gen-std`/`perf*` stay deno-driven). The VS Code "Run Current File" command (`lsp/src/extension.ts`) no longer shells to `deno task run`; it invokes the `vl` binary, resolved from a new `vital.compilerPath` setting (relative paths against the project root) → `vl` on PATH. The binary is spawn-probed before the run, so a missing or wrong-platform binary surfaces a clear, actionable "build it for this platform" error instead of leaking a cryptic shell error (`exec format error`) into the terminal. A committed `.vscode/settings.json` points `vital.compilerPath` at the in-repo `scripts/vl-host/target/release/vl` so repo devs get zero-config resolution without baking a repo-specific path into the shipped extension. `cli.ts` itself stays for now (P3 step 3): richer conveniences not yet in `vl` (`run -e`/stdin, `build --wat`, `check` over a dir / `--fix` / `--severity`) remain reachable via `deno run -A compiler/cli.ts …`.
- **kill-TS full-delete P3 (step 3a): `vl build --wat`** — `vl build` gains `--wat`, writing a `.wat` text dump beside the emitted module via binaryen's `wasm-dis` (the purpose-built disassembler), the native counterpart of the TS CLI's binaryen-JS `wasmToWat`. Like `-O`, it's an OPTIONAL external tool resolved `$VL_WASM_DIS` → PATH (a shared `binaryen_tool` helper now backs both `wasm-opt` and `wasm-dis`), enables exactly the GC + reference-type features WasmGC output needs (not `-all`, which wasmtime would reject), and is a soft no-op when absent. The "binaryen missing" note is unified into one `cfg!`-tailored helper: the macOS build suggests `brew install binaryen`, other platforms point at the package manager / prebuilt releases — so it never suggests `brew` on Linux. README/AGENTS document the binaryen dependency for `-O`/`--wat`.
- **kill-TS full-delete P3 (step 3b): `vl run` from `-e`/stdin** — `vl run` reaches TS-CLI parity for its input sources: `-e "<snippet>"` (inline source), stdin when piped (`… | vl run`), a file, or a prebuilt `.wasm` (magic-byte detection — runs straight through wasmtime). Like `fmt`, `run` is dispatched ahead of the positional `<cmd> <input>` parsing (its arg shape has no file with `-e`/stdin). An interactive TTY with no input shows usage instead of blocking on stdin forever; an empty snippet/stream is a clean usage exit (2). The compile+run tail is factored into a shared `compile_and_run` (seed compile with the `name` section for legible traps → DRC-engine run).
- **kill-TS full-delete P3 (step 3c): `vl check` reports lint + severity, pretty output** — `vl check` moves from type-errors-only toward TS-CLI parity. It now MERGES the error tier (`checkSrc`) with the lint tier (`lintSrc` — `unused-variable`/`prefer-const` warnings, intentionally-unused hints), the same diagnostics the editor shows (the `cargo check`-shows-warnings model; a heavier `vl lint` can come later). `--severity <hint|info|warning|error>` (and the `--severity=` form) does double duty — it gates the exit code (default `error`: only errors fail) AND raises the display floor (default: show everything); an unknown level is a clean exit 2. Output is rustc/Deno-style PRETTY by default (`[SEVERITY]: msg` + source line + caret, ANSI color auto-gated on a TTY and `NO_COLOR`), with `--concise` for the grep-friendly `file: severity [L:C] msg` one-liner. `--codegen` runs the full `compileSrc` so emit-tier errors surface. On a rejection the summary NAMES the failing stage — `Found N errors. (parse|type|emit error)` — so tooling (the native-align reject suite) can classify WHERE the front end rejected. The seed staging (module-fetch loop + `srcReset`/`srcPush`) is factored out of `compile_vl` into a shared `stage_program`, reused by the new check path. (Single-file for now; a directory walk + `--exclude`/`--ignore` is the next slice. The TS `cli_codegen` test's binaryen-specific `recursion limit exceeded` message is a TS-path artifact the self-hosted emitter won't reproduce — it'll be retired with `cli.ts`, not ported.)
- **playground fix: cross-file LSP (imported names + dependent locals)** — the playground's editor features ran single-file (no sibling reader), so in a multi-file project hovering an imported name (`add`, `square`) or anything whose inferred type depended on one (`let r = add(square(3), 4)`) showed no tooltip — only builtins like `print` resolved (VS Code, with its workspace reader, was unaffected). `lspAdapter` now takes the active file's KEY + a workspace reader over the live project buffers (wired by `main.ts` via `setWorkspace(projectFiles)`), the browser counterpart of the Node LSP's `workspaceReader`, so an importer's graph resolves: `add` → `(i32, i32) -> i32`, `r` → `i32`. Every feature (hover/semantic tokens/inlay/definition/completion) threads the entry key; single-file queries (and the unit tests) default to `main.vl` with an empty reader. (`tests/playground_lsp_wasm_test.ts` — cross-file hover case.)
- **playground: code-split binaryen off the initial load** — with codegen on the seed, binaryen (~13 MB) is reached ONLY via the lazy `import("binaryen")`/`import("./toWasm.ts")` behind `wasmToWat` (the WAT renderer). `build.ts` now enables esbuild `splitting` (`outdir` + object-form `entryPoints` → `playground.js`), so binaryen lands in its own `chunk-*.js` fetched on demand when the WAT pane is first shown — not on page load. The entry `playground.js` drops from ~22 MB to ~2 MB (the rest is lazy chunks: Monaco's on-demand features + the binaryen WAT chunk). `pages.yml` ships the chunks (`dist/*.js`); `index.html` still loads only `playground.js` + `playground.css`. WAT stays a feature (binaryen disassembling the seed's bytes), just off the critical path.

## Infrastructure (Track F)

- **F1 Test harness** — `deno task test` over the `.vl` corpus with `// @directive` expectations.
- **Rep-composition fuzzer (roadmap Next#2)** — a VL-native property tester over the non-i32 rep matrix (leaves i32/i64/f64/f32/boolean/string/litunion-atoms × containers struct/multi-field-struct/list/nullable/map/union/closure × positions local/param/return/global, read + construct-only variants), with a sound round-trip oracle and failure classes REJECT/INVALID-WASM/TRAP/MISMATCH. CI runs a pinned-seed smoke against a known-failure baseline (`scripts/rep-fuzz-baseline.txt`) — a NEW failing shape is a regression, a newly-passing shape graduates into `tests/cases/`. Triage log: `docs/internals/rep-fuzz-findings.md`. (#667, #674, #805)
- **F3 Retired `ts-interpreter/` → `reference/`.**
- **F8 Dropped binaryen patch + `patch-package`** — LSP server is ESM; binaryen's TLA is legal there.
- **F9 Perf baseline** — `deno task perf`; compile-time (front/codegen split) + wasm size over corpus; best-of-N. Finding: literal-union compilation is ~cubic in member count (200 → ~2 s). (→ `docs/perf-findings.md`)
- **F9c Memoize `structSig` in `toWasm.ts`** — the structural-signature walk was uncached and dominated IR-build on the self-host module (~6 s of ~7.7 s total; 268k calls). Caching by type-node identity (empty `nameStack` only) cut selfhost-suite wall time ~107 s → ~30 s with byte-identical wasm. Post-fix: binaryen `optimize()` is only ~0.8 s on this module, not the bottleneck. (#107; detail: `docs/perf-findings.md`)
- **F10 Bare `deno check` passes** — lsp sub-project excluded; playground + lsp-test type errors fixed so top-level `deno check` exits clean. (#140)
- **F11 CI skips heavy suite for docs-only changes** — an in-job `git diff` gate (not a workflow-level `paths` filter) `if`-guards the heavy steps, so docs-only PRs go green fast while the `ci` check still reports. (#142)
- **F12 GitHub Pages deploy** — `.github/workflows/pages.yml` builds the playground (`deno task playground:build`) and deploys the static bundle to `https://voces.github.io/vl/` on every master push that touches `playground/`, `compiler/`, or build-config files.
- **F — Deleted stale `samples/` sketches** — the 7 `samples/*.vl` early design sketches (manual malloc / linear-memory, pre-WasmGC) are removed and dropped from the test corpus (formatter walk, `smoke-binary.ts`, repo-layout docs). The one idea not already tracked — expression `step` on a counter range (`for i = 1 to 5 step i * 2`) — was captured under B8; the rest were obsolete or already covered.
- **F — Seed-backed tests run in CI again (+ a rot guard)** — a seed-backed test (one that loads `build/vl-compiler.wasm`) self-ignores when no seed is present; the `ci` job never builds a seed, so such tests run only in `ci-native`, and only if it globs (`vl_*`/`selfhost_native_*`) or names them. Six had drifted into running NOWHERE in CI — `lsp_wasm_checker`, `lsp_crossfile{,_wasm,_refs_wasm}`, `lsp_member_completion_wasm`, `lsp_unused_export_wasm` (92 cases) — now wired into the `ci-native` editor-features step (and `lsp_wasm_checker`'s two `formatSrc` expectations refreshed to the current inline-collapse output). `tests/ci_seed_coverage_test.ts` is a new guard (runs seedless in the `ci` job) that fails the moment any seed-backed test isn't covered, so this can't silently recur. `cli_fmt_test.ts` is retired — it drove the doomed TS `cli.ts fmt` (needs both binaryen and a seed, which neither job has) and the native `vl fmt` is fully covered by `vl_fmt_test.ts`.
- **kill-TS: retire the TS-driven dev scripts** — the three `scripts/perf*.ts` benchmarks all drove the TS `compile()` (the compiler being deleted), so they're retired (with their `deno task perf*` entries); the findings are preserved in `docs/perf-findings.md`, and a baseline can be rebuilt against the native binary if regression-tracking is wanted again. `tests/std_resolution_test.ts` (a unit test of the TS host's `resolveSpecifier`/`loadProgram`) is replaced by `tests/vl_std_resolution_test.ts`, which drives the SHIPPING native resolver (`vl-compiler-driver.vl`) through the `vl` binary over `VL_STD` fixtures — same coverage (well-formed `std:` chains, the std-internal relative guard, malformed/bare specifiers), now auto-run in `ci-native` (the `vl_*` glob). This leaves the TS compiler core's only out-of-core consumers as `cli.ts`/the release binary, the `--ts-genesis` seed break-glass, and `checker-parity-sweep.ts`.
- **kill-TS: retire the `--ts-genesis` seed break-glass** — the TS stage-0 emitter that minted a seed from pure source (`scripts/build-compiler-wasm.ts` + its `tests/_selfhost_cache.ts` whole-compile cache) is DELETED, along with every `--ts-genesis`/`VL_SEED_TS_GENESIS` path in `fetch-seed.sh`, `refresh-compiler.sh`, `publish-seed.yml`, and `ci.yml`. The published `seed-latest` release (self-compiled from the prior seed each master push, fixpoint-gated) is now the seed's sole source of truth, with the immutable `seed-v0` anchoring the lineage; a seed too stale to compile current source is recovered by re-fetching `seed-latest` / landing the enabling change one seed-generation early, not by a TS re-mint. This consciously drops the "reconstructable from pure source without trusting a binary" guarantee `docs/genesis-design.md` previously called a deliberate keep — the project does not maintain a second compiler. The automatic seed pipeline is unaffected (it never used genesis): `refresh-compiler.sh` + `native-fixpoint.sh` hold byte-identical (660816 bytes). Docs updated (`genesis-design.md`, `vl-tech-debt.md` debt item resolved, README, the design-doc seed-build references).
- **kill-TS: the CLI test suites move onto the native `vl`** — the four test files that imported `compiler/cli.ts` (the doomed TS CLI) are retired and re-pinned against the shipping native `vl` binary instead: `cli_severity_test.ts` → `vl_check_severity_test.ts` (the `--severity` exit-code gate + display floor), `cli_codegen_test.ts` → `vl_check_codegen_test.ts` (`--codegen` surfaces an emit-stage error the fast path misses), and `cli_excludes_test.ts` → `vl_check_exclude_test.ts` (the `--exclude` matcher edges — basename glob, the `attests`-vs-`tests` prefix collision, `**` crossing separators, `./`+trailing-`/` normalization — pinned end-to-end via excluded files carrying type errors, complementing the basic case already in `vl_check_dir_test.ts`). `cli_fix_test.ts` is simply deleted: the native `vl check --fix` deliberately implements a NARROWER policy (prefer-const only — it drops the TS path's unused-var `_`-prefix auto-fix as a human judgment call), and its surviving behaviour is already covered by `vl_check_fix_test.ts`. The new tests follow the seed-gated harness (`SELFHOST_NATIVE_ALIGN=1` + binary + seed), so they self-ignore in the `ci` job and run in `ci-native` via the `vl_*` glob (the `ci_seed_coverage_test.ts` guard confirms the wiring); the TS host's `run`/`build` advisory-floor display cases, which the native host does not mirror, retire with `cli.ts` rather than port. This leaves the TS compiler core's only out-of-core consumers as `cli.ts`/the release binary and `checker-parity-sweep.ts`.
- **kill-TS: the native `vl` host can embed its seed (self-contained release binary)** — groundwork for retiring `compiler/cli.ts` as the `deno compile` release binary (H-M2). The Rust host (`scripts/vl-host`) gains a `CompilerSource` (`Path` | `Embedded`) and a single `resolve_compiler()` that folds the three duplicated `--compiler`/`$VL_COMPILER_WASM`/`./build/vl-compiler.wasm` resolution sites into one chain with a new **final fallback: a seed baked into the binary**. The embed is behind an `embed-seed` cargo feature (a `build.rs` stages `build/vl-compiler.wasm` — or `$VL_EMBED_SEED` — into `OUT_DIR` for `include_bytes!`), OFF by default so normal dev/CI builds need no seed present at compile time (`ci-native` builds the host before it mints the seed). Resolution honours an explicit `--compiler`/env STRICTLY (a missing file errors, never silently uses the embedded copy); only the default falls back, with the on-disk seed still winning when present (so a dev checkout / CI drives its fresh seed and a release binary's embedded copy stays overridable). `cargo build --release --features embed-seed` yields a single `vl` that runs `build`/`check`/`run`/`fmt` anywhere with no out-of-band asset. A new `ci-native` step builds with the feature (the seed exists by then) and smoke-tests the embedded fallback from a seedless dir, guarding the distribution path against rot every push. The seed embed is byte-identical across targets (platform-independent WasmGC), so it's cross-compile-agnostic — the prerequisite for swapping the release matrix off `deno compile cli.ts`. `cli.ts` is untouched here; its retirement + the release swap is the next step. (`scripts/vl-host/src/main.rs`, `scripts/vl-host/build.rs`, `scripts/vl-host/Cargo.toml`, README)
- **kill-TS: the release binary goes native — `compiler/cli.ts` deleted** — the `deno compile cli.ts` distribution path is retired in favour of the native Rust `vl` host with the seed embedded (the embed landed the prior step). `release.yml` now builds all five targets per-OS — ubuntu for both Linux arches (aarch64 via a cross linker), macOS for both Darwin arches, Windows for win-x64 — each `cargo build --features embed-seed`, producing ONE self-contained file per target with no V8/node/binaryen in the shipped artifact. `compiler/cli.ts`, `scripts/build-binary.ts`, and `scripts/smoke-binary.ts` are DELETED. The deno `build-binary.ts` is replaced by `scripts/build-binary.sh` — a thin cargo wrapper (host build by default; `--target <T>`/`--all` to cross-build) single-sourced with the release matrix + Formula. `smoke-binary.ts` retires with `cli.ts`: it asserted binaryen runs *inside* the `deno compile`d binary and checked TS-specific codegen wording (`"Codegen error:"`/`"recursion limit exceeded"`), neither true of the native host (binaryen is a PATH soft-dep, the native emitter phrases errors differently); the native binary's surface is covered by the seed-gated suite + the prior step's embed smoke. `fetch-seed.sh` is made checksum-portable (`sha256sum` **or** `shasum -a 256 -c`) so the macOS/Windows release builds — and any Mac dev — verify the seed too. `deno task compile` now aliases the host build; `deno task smoke` is removed. This leaves **`checker-parity-sweep.ts` as the TS compiler core's sole remaining out-of-core consumer**. Docs: DECISIONS C5 marked RETIRED, deno-deprecation J5 done, README/Formula/cli-design/AGENTS updated. (`.github/workflows/release.yml`, `scripts/build-binary.sh`, `scripts/fetch-seed.sh`, `deno.json`, `Formula/vl.rb`)
- **kill-TS: the TS compiler core is DELETED — the two compilers are now one** — the endgame. With `cli.ts` gone (prior step), the only remaining consumer of the TS front end was `scripts/checker-parity-sweep.ts`, the batch TS-vs-wasm divergence oracle. A final sweep confirmed the native checker is at accept/reject **verdict parity** over the 456-file single-file corpus (375 agree; the 81 structural divergences are 74 cosmetic span-position deltas + 4 where native is *stricter* + 3 where TS emits an extra cascade diagnostic on a file both reject — **zero** verdict disagreements). So the oracle has done its job and is retired, and the entire TS front end is deleted: `compiler/{ast,buildCache,compile,defaultScope,lexer,lint,moduleRewrite,modules,parser,state,symbols,toAST,toWasm,typecheck,wasmBuiltins,wasmType}.ts` + `scripts/checker-parity-sweep.ts` — **~18.3K LOC across 17 files**. The self-hosted `compiler/*.vl` (the wasm seed) is now the SOLE compiler. Only the two dependency-free type leaves `compiler/coreTypes.ts` + `compiler/diagnostics.ts` survive in `compiler/` — the diagnostic/position vocabulary the LSP + playground import (they drive the seed for all checking). The 81 residual span/ergonomic deltas are preserved in `docs/vl-tech-debt.md` as native diagnostic-polish debt (native is the spec now, so "match the TS span" is no longer the goal — fix only where native is genuinely worse). CI cleanup: the dead `VL_CACHE_DIR` build-cache steps (the TS compiler's whole-compile cache, `buildCache.ts`-only, already orphaned) are removed from both jobs; `deno check compiler/*.ts` now covers just the leaves. Verified: `deno check`/`deno lint` clean, full suite 612 passed / 0 failed, LSP + playground bundles build. Docs: ROADMAP "Kill the TS host" + J0 marked DONE, deno-deprecation J0 done, AGENTS updated. (the whole `compiler/*.ts` front end, `scripts/checker-parity-sweep.ts`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`)
- **kill-TS: delete the orphaned `compiler/builtins/` codegen helpers** — follow-up to the core deletion. `compiler/builtins/{lists,maps,strings}.ts` (the TS compiler's lazily-emitted per-element-type collection/string helpers — `lowerListMethodCall`/`lowerMapMethodCall`/`lowerStringMethodCall`) survived the front-end deletion only because the `deno check compiler/*.ts` glob doesn't recurse into the subdir. They had no importers (the LSP `builtins/` mentions were comments) AND were already broken — each imported the deleted `compiler/toAST.ts` (`deno check compiler/builtins/*.ts` → TS2307). Deleted (1337 LOC); the live equivalent is in the self-hosted `compiler/wasmEmit.vl`. `compiler/` now holds only the `.vl` compiler + the `coreTypes.ts`/`diagnostics.ts` type leaves. (DECISIONS B6 pointer updated.)
- **kill-TS: retire the byte-golden tripwire (redundant with the fixpoint)** — `tests/golden/*.wasm` (14 byte-snapshots of `vl build` output), `tests/selfhost/goldens.ts`, `scripts/native-golden-check.ts`, and the `ci-native` "Native golden byte-tripwire" step are deleted. A whole-module byte snapshot only pins two properties, both already covered more strongly upstream: **determinism** — the native fixpoint (`native-fixpoint.sh`, stage3 == stage4 byte-identical) proves byte-exact self-reproduction on the 660 KB compiler, a far larger surface than 14 tiny programs; and **correctness** — the functional corpus (`cases_wasm` + the `selfhost_native_*` suites) compiles AND runs the programs and checks behavior, where a byte-snapshot only flags that bytes *changed*, never whether the change was good. (This mirrors real compilers: self-hosting toolchains verify determinism by build-twice-and-compare / bootstrap stage comparison — rustc, Go, GCC, reproducible-builds — not committed binary goldens; where they snapshot codegen at all it's *textual + pattern-based* — LLVM FileCheck, Rust `codegen`/`assembly` tests, Go `asmcheck` — asserting specific properties, not whole-module byte-identity.) Net: the goldens added friction (every intentional codegen improvement required an `UPDATE_GOLDENS` re-pin) for a low-signal change-detector. `SELFHOST_DENO_RUN` (the old deno-side emit-golden gate, whose tests were already deleted) is now fully gone. This was also the last byte-pin among the TS-era redundant test harnesses (the TS-vs-VL parity suites — `checker-parity-sweep`, `selfhost_emit_*`, `cases_test`, `cli_*` — were already removed across the kill-TS PRs). (`tests/golden/`, `tests/selfhost/`, `scripts/native-golden-check.ts`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`)

- **compile-time perf: the declaration-fill done-set is O(1), not a scan** — `fillDone` walked `cFillDoneIx` (a list of the arena entries already filled) once per type declaration while the list grew once per type declaration, i.e. O(#typedecls²) across pass 0b. It is now a FLAG COLUMN indexed BY the arena index (`cFillDoneAt`), which is available because `addTy` appends without interning, so a placeholder's index is dense and unique. Measured on N `type S = { a: i32 }` declarations with process startup subtracted per compiler and the `vl check` pump collector pinned to `null` (`VL_PUMP_GC=null`) so GC scheduling cannot alias the curve: analysis time goes 29/76/235 ms → 18/35/73 ms at N = 4000/8000/16000, i.e. delta ratios 2.6 and 3.1 (a linear term plus a quadratic one) collapse to 1.9 and 2.1 (pure linear). **The KEY is unchanged** — arena index, never name and never declaration statement — which is what keeps a redeclared type name filling its one shared placeholder exactly once and keeps the fill order-independent. Three structures were built and timed against each other — the flag column, a name-keyed `Map`, and an arena-index-keyed `Map` — plus a statement-keyed flag column; at N = 16000 all four are indistinguishable (72–74 ms median of 21 interleaved reps, mins 75–76), so nothing was traded for the exact key. The statement-keyed one is the one that would have been chosen for looking natural, and it is UNSOUND: two statements fill the one shared placeholder twice and merge the two bodies, so a field neither declaration wrote type-checks and the emitter then dies on it. No corpus file declared a type name twice, so all 1962 emitted modules were byte-identical under that wrong key; `tests/cases/types/redeclared-type-fills-one-placeholder-once.vl` is the file that catches it. The compiler's own 67 type declarations cannot exercise this (`vl check compiler/entry.vl` is unchanged), which is why the stress input is constructed. (`compiler/typecheck.vl`)

## Parser (Track G — complete)

- **A `>>` closing two nested generics banked its second `>`, and the next `|` / `&` / `[]` spent that credit on the ARGUMENT — so `Box<Box<i32>> | i32` was one type, not two arms** — the native lexer fuses `>>`/`>>>` into a single SHR/USHR token (the compiler's own sources are generics-free, so the shift reading is unambiguous there), which means one token has to close TWO generic levels: `expectTypeGt` consumes it and BANKS the extra `>`s as a credit the enclosing close drains. While a credit is outstanding the enclosing argument list is still OPEN and the only token that may follow is its close — but the argument is parsed by `parseTypeName`, whose union loop reads the raw next token, so the OUTER continuation was folded INTO the argument. Three continuation loops read it that way (`|` in `parseTypeName`, `&` in `parseTypeIsect`, `[]` in `parseTypeAtom`); the generic argument list's own `,` loop already consulted the credit, which is why a multi-ARGUMENT application never had this and a NESTED one always did. `atTypeCont` is that same test given one home, and all four sites now read it. **The mis-built types, from the diagnostics that printed them**: `Box<Box<i32>> | i32` → `Box<Box<i32>|i32>`, `Box<Box<i32>>[]` → `Box<Box<i32>[]>`, `Box<Box<i32>> & W` → `Box<{v:i32,w:i32}>` (absorbed, then simplified), `Box<Box<Box<i32>>> | i32` → `Box<Box<Box<i32>|i32>>` (the `|` lands at the INNERMOST level — the USHR banks two, so the credit is spent one rung deeper). **THE UNION-ARM POSITION WAS SILENT AND THE OTHER ELEVEN WERE LOUD, which is what made it look like two defects**: with both arms folded into one type there is no union at all, so the value took a plain-struct rep and `if u is Box<Box<i32>>` reached `monoStaticIsResult` and **CONST-FOLDED TO FALSE** for a value that IS a member of that arm — `vl check` rc 0, no diagnostic, wrong branch, confirmed as `i32.const 0` / `if` in the disassembly with no `{tag, value}` box anywhere in the module; and the assignment side accepted `const u: Box<Box<i32>> | i32 = { v: 5 }`, an `i32` where `Box<i32>` is demanded, because the FOLDED argument's union makes `v: 5` well typed. Every other position — array, intersection, nullable niche, param, return, struct field, generic argument — was a loud type error against the mis-built type. **Genericity was never the axis; the fused close token is.** Depth-1 `Box<i32> | i32` banks nothing and was always correct, and the named (`type Outer = { v: Inner }`) and inline (`{v: {v: i32}} | i32`) spellings of the same depth-2 shape were already rejecting the same literal — that non-union control (`const u: Box<Box<i32>> = { v: 5 }`, correctly rejected, no trailing `|` for the credit to leak into) is what discriminated. **The credit, not the token, is the discriminator**: `Box<Box<i32> | i32>` closed with a plain `>` banks nothing, so its `|` still extends the argument — two spellings, two trees, from the same continuation token, and that control is pinned. Measured over a 12-cell continuation grid (positions × continuations): **11 of 12 wrong before, 0 after** (the 12th, `type Id<T> = T`, is an unrelated identity-alias gap and is unmoved, though it now names its type correctly). Blast radius: **every corpus file that can bank a `>` credit is BYTE-IDENTICAL, 11/11** (the 15 `.vl` files under `tests/cases/` containing `>>`, less the 4 added here) — and no other `.vl` source in the tree can reach the changed predicate at all, since a credit requires a consumed SHR/USHR inside a generic argument list and the only `>>` in `std/`/`bench/` is an expression shift. Full suite **4203/4203, 0 failed, 7 ignored**; with the guard made inert, exactly the predicted **6 tests redden** (the 3 `>>`-carrying fixtures × the corpus-oracle and native-align suites) and nothing else moves. **A SECOND, INDEPENDENT ROOT is left standing and is now pinned rather than hidden behind this one**: a union variant carrying a STRUCT-TYPED field has no emitted read, so the depth-2 struct arm construct (✓), its `is` discrimination (✓) and a SCALAR sibling field read (✓) all work while `u.v` itself reports `emitProgram: field access receiver is not a struct`. Fixing the parser is what moves the generic spelling out of the silent column and into that loud one beside the named and inline spellings — the inert leg leaves that fixture green, which is the measurement that the two roots are separate. (`tests/cases/generics/nested-application-close-continuation.vl`, `nested-application-union-arm.vl`, `nested-application-union-arm-literal-reject.vl`, `tests/cases/unions/error-struct-typed-variant-field-read.vl`)
- **G Hand-written parser** — replaced antlr4 with hand-written TS lexer + recursive-descent/Pratt parser emitting the typed AST directly; antlr4/Gradle/generated dirs gone. → `DECISIONS.md`
- **G AST source spans** — `NodeSpans` + `spanOf` publicly exposed via `toAST`/`compile`; unblocks D4/D6/D7.
- **G Comment-carrying tokens** — comments retained with spans (flat `comments` list + per-token `leading`/`trailing` trivia) without entering the grammar stream; unblocks AST-driven formatter.
- **G8 Soft-newline statement boundaries** — newlines are never force-required; statements abut on one line. Fixed `return <expr>` followed by a same-line statement (`return 1 print(9)`): the trailing dead statement was mistaken for the block's implicit return value and spuriously checked against an annotated return type (`expected i32, got null`) — now `return 1` carries the value and the trailing statement is left for the next position (flagged by the unreachable-code lint). → `DECISIONS.md` (G8)

## Self-hosting & modules (Track H)

- **H0 Module system phase 1** — relative-path named `import { a, b as c } from "./util"` + `export` modifier on `function`/`let`/`const`/`type`; whole-program resolver walks import graph, detects cycles, type-checks across modules, merges into ONE wasm module with per-module name mangling. `compileProgram`/`checkProgram`. (#96; → `DECISIONS.md`, `docs/modules-design.md`)
- **H-reexport — `export { a, b as c } from "./mod"` (re-export with a STABLE bare ABI name)** — a module can now re-surface another module's exports under its own public names. The merge mangles every module's symbols to `name$mN` (so two modules can both export `foo`), but a library wasm — the compiler itself — needs *some* exports to stay bare and stable for its host (`compileSrc`, not `compileSrc$m7`). A re-export is the mechanism: it records BOTH an import (pull the symbol + the dependency edge, so the host fetches the module) AND a public-name alias; the emitter then adds an export-section entry mapping the **bare** public name to the merged (mangled) target function's index, so `export { compute, helper as helped } from "./dep"` yields callable bare `compute`/`helped` exports (the `as` alias honored). The re-exported names are also bound locally (the merge pulls them in), so the re-exporting module may use them too. Touches the parser (skip `export { … } from` like an import — no AST node), the module subsystem (`modScan` records the re-export, `modCompile` resolves each public name → its `$mN` target), the emitter's export section (the bare alias entries), and the host's two module-fetch gates (`vl build`/`run` in `main.rs`, `vl check`/`--fix` via cli.vl's `cliLineIsImport`) so a re-export arms the fetch loop. Native fixpoint byte-identical; this is the keystone enabling the compiler's own source to migrate from `cat`-concatenation to real `import`/`export` modules while keeping its host ABI stable. (`tests/cases/modules/reexport/`)
- **H-self-modules — the compiler builds from REAL `import`/`export` modules (no more `cat`/sed/rename)** — the self-host build flipped from concatenating the nine source files into one blob (stripping their `import` lines, sed-renaming the lexer's `Tok`/`Diag`/`advance` to dodge the flat-namespace clash) to a genuine module graph: `vl build compiler/entry.vl` resolves `entry → driver → {lexer, ast, parser, typecheck, wasmEmit, lint, format} + cli`. `compiler/entry.vl` re-exports the full host ABI (191 names — the embedding surface, the corpus harness, the LSP query families) under bare, stable names (H-reexport). The driver moved into `compiler/` (so its relative imports resolve) and became a real module importing the pipeline. Every cross-module reference is now an explicit `export`/`import` (targeted, not export-everything); two backward edges that the flat namespace had hidden were broken by relocating their definitions — the value-union NAME classifier (`isValueUnionName` + `splitUnionAtoms`/`unionMemberCount`/`valueAtomKind`) moved wasmEmit → typecheck (it is type-classification the checker needs too), and the re-export alias tables (`reExpName`/`reExpTarget`) moved driver → wasmEmit (the consumer). The formatter learned to preserve a re-export (`emitImports` recovers `export { … } from` verbatim, like an `import`). `refresh-compiler.sh`/`native-fixpoint.sh`/`lint-self.sh` lost their sed/cat/rename glue — they just build/check the entry. The lexer `Tok`/`Diag` clash simply evaporates (each module has its own namespace). The seed re-mints to the module-built bytes; the native fixpoint (`vl build entry` ⇒ stage3 == stage4) holds, the deno corpus (533) and native sweep (PASS=400) pass, and the bare ABI is verified complete. (`compiler/entry.vl`, `compiler/driver.vl`, the nine module files, `scripts/{refresh-compiler,native-fixpoint,lint-self}.sh`)
- **H-split-bignum — first cohesive carve-out of the 26K-line emitter** — now that real module boundaries exist (H-self-modules), `wasmEmit.vl` can shed self-contained clusters. The first: the arbitrary-precision integer math + numeric-literal helpers (`big*` — add/sub/cmp/shift/divmod over little-endian base-2^16 limbs — plus `mulAdd64`, `parseI32`/`parseI64Pair`, `numLexFitsI32`/`numLexIs2Pow31`) move to a new leaf `compiler/emit_bignum.vl`, with the one `gDivRemNonzero` side-channel global moving alongside them (the emitter imports it back). A genuine LEAF — it calls nothing in the emitter and touches no emitter state — so there is no import cycle; the emitter (and the few other modules that used these) import what they need. Behavior-identical: the seed re-mints to the new function ordering, the native fixpoint holds, the deno corpus (533) and native sweep (PASS=400) pass. The pattern (`emit_*.vl` cohesive leaves) is set for further carve-outs. (`compiler/emit_bignum.vl`, `compiler/wasmEmit.vl`)
- **H-split-emitter-vocab — carve the emitter's pure vocabulary + queries (131 functions) into two modules** — the big follow-on to H-split-bignum: every remaining PURE helper (state-free, calls only other pure helpers) leaves `wasmEmit.vl` for two new leaf modules, split along a verified-acyclic seam. `compiler/emit_base.vl` (85 fns) is the VOCABULARY — type-NAME analysis (`name*`/`ty*`/`ann*`/`ret*`/`nul*`), monomorphization name logic (`mono*`/`gae*`), and low-level string/byte/opcode helpers (which are mutually recursive with the type-name code, so they share one module); `compiler/emit_query.vl` (46 fns) is the QUERIES — binding/value-kind predicates (`param*`/`let*`/`is*`/`union*`) and control-flow / valued-return / arr-new scans (`if*`/`block*`/`expr*`/`nli*`), depending only on `emit_base`. The call graph was checked to confirm no `base → query` edge (so the layering `emit_bignum → emit_base → emit_query → wasmEmit` is a DAG, and the module system's cycle rejection is satisfied). Behavior-identical: the seed re-mints to the new ordering, native fixpoint holds, deno corpus (533) and native sweep (PASS=400) pass. **`wasmEmit.vl`: 25698 → 22836 lines** (≈26K → 22.8K across the two split PRs). (`compiler/emit_base.vl`, `compiler/emit_query.vl`, `compiler/wasmEmit.vl`)
- **H-split-emitter-state+bytes — factor the emitter's shared state, then its byte-emission layer** — the stateful core finally yields. First `compiler/emit_state.vl` collects the emitter's 191 shared MUTABLE globals (the output buffer `W`, the WasmGC type-index globals `cloStructIdx`/`aTypeIdx`/`sHeapIdx`/…, the collection tables `localIsRef`/`fnNames`/…, scratch bases, `fnUses*` flags) + the `Emitter` type into a declarations-only LEAF — the emitter and its sub-modules import these and mutate them in place (cross-module global writes work). That move is BYTE-IDENTICAL (globals emit in the same order regardless of file). With state externalized, the byte-emission layer becomes a clean DAG layer: `compiler/emit_bytes.vl` (68 fns) holds the streaming writers (`wU8`/`wULEB`/`wSLEB`/`wName`/`wSection`) and the `fb*` instruction/type encoders (`fbValtype`, `fbStructNew`, `fbCallRef`, …) plus `emitFail` and the map-type-index helpers — its downward call-closure (computed with string/comment-stripped edges, so `emitFail("emitProgram: …")` error text doesn't masquerade as a call) reaches NO lowering function, so `emit_state → emit_bytes → wasmEmit` is acyclic. Behavior-identical: native fixpoint holds, deno corpus (533) and native sweep (PASS=400) pass. **`wasmEmit.vl`: 22836 → 20953 lines** — ≈26K → ≈21K across the split arc, the emitter now layered `{emit_bignum, emit_base, emit_query} → emit_state → emit_bytes → wasmEmit` (the lowering). (`compiler/emit_state.vl`, `compiler/emit_bytes.vl`, `compiler/wasmEmit.vl`)
- **H-split-typecheck-lsp — carve the LSP query layer out of the type-checker** — applying the emitter-split pattern to the second-biggest file. The checker's read-only LSP QUERY layer (63 functions — symbol occurrences / go-to-def / find-refs / hover `sym*`, redundant-type reports `redun*`, inlay hints `inlay*`) moves to `compiler/check_query.vl`, and the LSP STATE it reads (38 symbol-occurrence / redun / inlay table globals) to a declarations-only leaf `compiler/check_state.vl`. The seam is verified acyclic via a TRANSITIVE reachability check: the 78-function LSP closure minus everything reachable from the checker core leaves exactly the read-only queries (the boundary functions the checker also calls — `symResolve`, `symOccHasTok`, `tyToStr` — correctly stay). The checker's `Ty`/`Checker` type system and the two `Checker`-typed globals (`T`, `cUserTypes`) STAY in typecheck (moving them would drag the whole type-algebra across the boundary); `check_query` imports them. So `check_state → typecheck → check_query` is a DAG. Behavior-identical: native fixpoint holds, deno corpus (533) and native sweep (PASS=400) pass. **`typecheck.vl`: 8100 → 7496 lines.** (`compiler/check_state.vl`, `compiler/check_query.vl`, `compiler/typecheck.vl`)
- **H-split-format — split the pure helpers out of `format.vl`** — 30 state-free formatting helpers (whitespace/indent/line-splitting, comment + chain/precedence predicates, `normalizeProgram`/`joinLines`/…) move to a new leaf `compiler/fmt_util.vl`. Their call-closure touches no `format.vl` module-state global (only the parser arena `P` + AST node variants from `./ast`), so it is a clean leaf the formatter imports; no other module imported the moved helpers, and the host ABI (`formatSrc`/`fmtLen`/`fmtByteAt` via the driver) is untouched. Behavior-identical: native fixpoint holds, deno corpus (533) and native sweep (PASS=400) pass. **`format.vl`: 1954 → 1553 lines.** (`compiler/fmt_util.vl`, `compiler/format.vl`)
- **H-split-cli — split the pure helpers out of `cli.vl`** — 21 state-free CLI helpers (ANSI colouring, detab / visual-width, line splitting, severity rank/validation, `--exclude` glob matching, import-line detection) move to a new leaf `compiler/cli_util.vl`. State-free (no `cli.vl` module global — they call only `ast`'s `i32ToStr` + a few driver helpers), a clean leaf the CLI imports; the host ABI (`cliArgReset`/`cliNext`/… via `entry.vl`) is untouched. Behavior-identical: native fixpoint holds, deno corpus (533) and native sweep (PASS=400) pass. **`cli.vl`: 1195 → 957 lines.** (`compiler/cli_util.vl`, `compiler/cli.vl`)
- **H-M1 Distribute via `deno compile`** — same as C5.
- **H1 Parser self-hostable** — same as Track G above.
- **H2 gap-1 Push non-nullable struct array** — `Tok[]` works; backing slot nullable-widened to `(ref null $T)`; reads `ref.as_non_null` to surface non-null type. Unblocks AST-node lists.
- **H2 gap-2 Module-level mutable `let` through a function** — scalar top-level bindings lower to shared wasm `global` (`global.get`/`global.set`). (`tests/cases/globals/`)
- **H2 gap-3 String escapes** — `\n`/`\t`/`\xXX`/`\uXXXX`/… decoded in lexer.
- **H2 gap-4 `toString` prototype-chain leak fix** — scope lookup uses `Object.hasOwn` everywhere (closes `constructor`/`valueOf`/etc. hazards).
- **H2 gap-5 `fromCodePoint(code): string` builtin** — code-point-named; replaces JS-ism `fromCharCode`.
- **H2 gap-6 Ref/string module-globals through a function** — extends gap-2's scalar-only support to ref/string cells.
- **H2 gap-7 One-char string literal `.length`** — string literals soften to nominal `string`; no longer mis-types as a char.
- **H2 char literals + `toString`/stringify** — both landed as part of H2 gap work.
- **H3-gap3 `checkProgram` in value position** — resolved by #89 (void/statement-position value drop); a discarded value is dropped in statement position, so `checkProgram(...)` as a bare statement (or `let r = ...`) no longer hits "Expected numeric type" in codegen. (detail: `docs/selfhost-gaps.md` §3)
- **H4.2 Value-level bitwise/shift operators** — shipped in #99 (see B2 above).
- **H4.3 Unsigned right-shift** (`>>>`) — resolved by #99 (`>>>` is now a native operator); `ulebToArr` in `wasmEmit.vl` uses `v >>> 7` for all i32 values including those with bit 31 set. (detail: `docs/selfhost-gaps.md` §H4.3)
- **H4.4 Signed `%` sign fix** — resolved via H4.2 (#99): `& 0x7f` / `& 0xff` bitwise masks replace the arithmetic correction branches; naturally unsigned, no special-casing needed. (detail: `docs/selfhost-gaps.md` §H4.4)
- **H-pipeline VL-in-VL front end end-to-end** — `lexer.vl → parser.vl → typecheck.vl` chain driving source text through a wired pipeline; proves the front end self-hosts. (`tests/selfhost_pipeline_test.ts` + `tests/selfhost/pipeline_harness.vl`)
- **H-emitProgram structs** — `emitProgram` parses + emits WasmGC struct types, `struct.new`, and `struct.get`; construct + field-read proven by real `WebAssembly.instantiate`. (#137)
- **H-emitProgram arrays** — `emitProgram` parses + emits WasmGC array ops: literal (`array.new_fixed`), index read/set, `.length`; 8 new live-instantiation cases. (#145)
- **H-emitProgram strings** — `emitProgram` lowers string literals + `.length`/`s[i]` to the array-i32 code-point representation (a VL string is CURRENTLY an `array i32` of code points, reusing the arrays slice's `(array (mut i32))` machinery); string-typed locals/params/returns carry the array ref valtype; 7 live-instantiation cases. Concat (`+`), value-equality (`==`/`!=`), and `.slice` land in G6; the UTF-8 `array i8` storage migration (B7) is deferred.
- **H-emitProgram growable arrays / push** — `emitProgram` now lowers `i32[]` to the growable `{ backing, len, cap }` wrapper struct (mirroring `toWasm.ts`'s list rep): `[…]` literals build it, `.push(x)` grows the backing in place (2× / floor 4) when full via `array.new_default` + `array.copy` + `struct.set` then writes + bumps `len`, `.length` reads the `len` field, and `a[i]`/`a[i]=v` go through the backing. `string` stays the raw fixed `(array (mut i32))` rep (immutable, no push). 6 new live-instantiation cases (grow past initial capacity, `while`-loop build-then-sum, `[]`+push+`.length`, push→set→read, list across a function boundary).
- **H-exports Host-callable wasm exports** — entry-module `export function`s become host-callable wasm exports via a thin scalar no-env wrapper; non-entry exports remain DCE-able. (#141; → `DECISIONS.md`)
- **H-emitProgram unions + is** — `emitProgram` now lowers discriminated `type N = A | B | …` struct unions with `is`-narrowing (G1, the bootstrap keystone), mirroring `toWasm.ts`'s boxed tagged-struct rep (`docs/unions.md`): a union value is a `{ tag: i32, value: anyref }` box wrapping the variant payload struct; construction is `struct.new $box (i32.const tagOf(V)) (struct.new $V …)` with global tags assigned by sorted variant structural signature; `n is V` is the box-tag compare (`struct.get $box 0` then `i32.eq`); a narrowed field read `n.f` (inside `if n is V`) recovers + `ref.cast`s the payload then `struct.get`s the field. The type section lifts the one-struct cap (multiple variant struct heap types + the union box). `compiler/parser.vl`/`compiler/ast.vl` gained the union-alias decl + `x is T` guard (the self-host parser parsed neither before). 7 new live-instantiation cases (construct+narrow+read, false-`is` other branch, 2-variant dispatch, 3-variant dispatch, multi-field variant, union local→helper, union local discriminated in place). Deferred: arrays-of-unions (`Node[]`, the G7-ref follow-up), unions mixing scalars + structs, and `!is`/negated guards.
- **H-emitProgram ref-element lists / arrays-of-structs-and-unions (G7-ref)** — `emitProgram` now lowers growable lists whose ELEMENT is a reference — `Tok[]` (a struct ref) and `Node[]` (the union box ref), the self-host parser's `P.nodes: Node[]` arena + token stream shapes. The `{ backing, len, cap }` wrapper is reused, but the backing array slot is `(ref null $elem)` (`0x5e 0x63 <elem> 0x01` — nullable-WIDENED so `array.new_default` defaults spare slots to null; verified against `toWasm.ts`'s `arraySlotWasmType`/`arrayReadCast`); an index READ `array.get`s the slot then `ref.as_non_null` (`0xd4`) recovers the non-null element (reads in `[0,len)` are always populated). `.push`/index-set reuse the i32-list grow/store sequence over the ref list/backing type indices + a ref scratch triple. For a union-element list `ns[i]` yields the union box, usable with `is`-narrowing (`let n0 = ns[i]; if n0 is A { n0.av }`). One ref-element list per program (struct OR union element), alongside the existing i32 list. 6 new live-instantiation cases (struct push+index-a-field+length, `while`-loop build-then-sum, `T[]` literal, struct index-set, union push+index+`is`-narrow, a `Node[]`-shaped mini arena narrowing each element). No `parser.vl`/`ast.vl` change needed (`S[]`/`N[]` already parse to a `TypeRef` with the `[]` suffix).
- **H-emitProgram logical `&&`/`||`/`!` + value-typed `if` (G4)** — `emitProgram` now lowers the short-circuit logical operators, introducing the first VALUE-TYPED `if` (the existing statement `if` stays VOID, blocktype `0x40`). `&&`/`||` are EXPRESSIONS that yield an i32, so they emit an `if` with an i32 RESULT-TYPE blocktype (`0x04 0x7f`) where both arms leave one i32: `a && b` ≡ `if (a) { b } else { 0 }` (then = `b`, `else 0x05`, else = `i32.const 0`, `end 0x0b`); `a || b` ≡ `if (a) { 1 } else { b }` — mirroring `toWasm.ts`'s `m.if(left, right, i32.const 0)` / `m.if(left, i32.const 1, right)` so the RHS is genuinely SKIPPED when short-circuited. `!a` (logical not) is `i32.eqz` (`0x45`) — a `boolean` rides in an i32, so eqz maps 0→1 / non-zero→0, exactly `a == 0`. 8 new live-instantiation cases (`&&` + `||` truth tables, `!a`, `&&`/`||` short-circuit OBSERVED via a global a skipped helper would have written — confirmed untouched, then written when run, on one shared instance, a char-class predicate `(x > 0) && (x < 10)` driving a branch, and a combined `!(a && b) || c`). The value-typed `if` is reusable for any future value-position `if`. No `parser.vl`/`ast.vl` change needed (`&&`/`||` already parse via the precedence table, `!` via `parseUnary`).
- **H-emitProgram boolean params/locals/returns + BoolLit/CharLit (G3)** — `emitProgram` now accepts `boolean` as a valid scalar wherever i32 is (boolean param in `checkParams`, boolean local in `checkLocalI32`, boolean return via `emitExpr`), treating it as the SAME i32 wasm valtype (`0x7f`) since a `boolean` already rides in an i32 (0/1) — no separate boolean valtype is introduced (`vtKindOfType`/`vtKindOfParam` already fall through to i32). `BoolLit` lowers to `i32.const 1` (true) / `i32.const 0` (false) from the already-decoded `boolVal`; `CharLit` lowers to `i32.const <code point>`, decoding `charText` the SAME way string literals decode (`decodeStr` strips the quotes + resolves escapes, yielding exactly one code point). Comparisons/branches on booleans already worked via i32. This unblocks the ~40+ self-host predicates that return `boolean`. 7 new live-instantiation cases (boolean param returned true→1/false→0, `if true`/`if false` BoolLit conditions, a function returning `boolean` (a comparison) used in a branch, a boolean local driving an `if`, `'a'`→97, and a char local + arithmetic `'A' + 1`→66). f64 stays out of scope. No `parser.vl`/`ast.vl` change needed (`boolean`, `true`/`false`, and char literals already parse).
- **H-emitProgram struct field write + module globals (G2)** — `emitProgram` now lowers struct field WRITES (`s.field = v` → `struct.set $0 <fieldidx>`, `0xfb 0x05`, struct-ref-then-value operand order, reusing the `emitPush` field-store shape) and module-level mutable GLOBALS: top-level `let`/`const` lower to a wasm global section (id 6, `<valtype> 0x01 <constexpr-init> 0x0b`) collected by `collectFns` in declaration order, with bare-identifier reads/writes routed to `global.get`/`global.set` (`0x23`/`0x24`) when the name resolves to a global rather than a local. Globals init via constant expressions only — i32 (`i32.const`), string (`array.new_fixed`), struct (`struct.new`), and `i32[]` (`struct.new` of `array.new_fixed`) literals — so no start function is needed (the `__program__` start-fn fallback for non-constexpr initializers is deferred). A bare top-level call statement (helper invoked for its global side effect) now emits the call + `drop`. 9 new live-instantiation cases (field write-then-read, both fields, read-modify-write, field mutated in a `while` loop, i32 global read+written, a counter bumped by a helper across calls, two globals one seeded non-zero, a struct global field-mutated, an array global indexed + `.length`).
- **H-emitProgram string `+`/`==`/slice (G6)** — `emitProgram` now lowers the three string operators the self-host sources lean on (diagnostics build messages with `+`, the lexer keyword tables compare with `==`, lexeme extraction uses `gSrc.slice(start, end)`). A string is the SAME `(array (mut i32))` of code points, so all three lower INLINE (no helper functions, unlike `toWasm.ts`'s `__string_eq__`/`__string_slice__`): `a + b` allocates a new array of `len(a)+len(b)` (`array.new_default`, `0xfb 0x07`) and `array.copy`s (`0xfb 0x11`) a into `[0,lenA)` and b into `[lenA, lenA+lenB)`; `a == b`/`a != b` are ELEMENT-WISE VALUE-equality (a value-typed `block`/`loop`: `false` on a length mismatch, else a per-code-point compare loop, all-equal → 1; `!=` adds `i32.eqz`) — a correctness FIX, since the prior i32 `==` path would have emitted `i32.eq` = WRONG ref-identity for strings; `s.slice(start, end)` clamps each bound JS-style (negative-from-end then `[0,len]`, via `select` `0x1b`) and `array.copy`s the half-open range into a fresh array. Only STRING-typed operands take these paths (via the existing `exprString` tracking) — i32/other-type `+`/`==`/`!=` are unchanged. A shared per-function scratch frame (3 string refs + 4 i32 temps, reserved after the push triples when `fnHasStrOp`) stashes operands so a NESTED string op completes before any scratch write; `exprString` now also recognizes string concat + `.slice` as string-producing (so `let s = a + b` / `let s = x.slice(…)` get the string ref valtype). 14 new live-instantiation cases (concat `.length` + index checks, concat of two locals, `==` same/different-content/different-length, value-equality of two BUILT-UP strings (not ref identity), `!=`, `.slice` compared with `==` + length/index + out-of-range clamp, keyword-table `if word == "let"` dispatch hit + miss, concat built in a `while` loop). `.indexOf`/`.includes`/`.charCodeAt`/`fromCodePoint` and the UTF-8 `array i8` storage migration (B7) stay deferred. No `parser.vl`/`ast.vl` change needed (`+`/`==`/`!=` parse via the precedence table, `.slice(…)` as a member-call).
- **H-emitProgram string-keyed maps via parallel lists (G8)** — `emitProgram` now lowers `{[string]: i32}` maps — the shape the self-host checker (`typecheck.vl`) uses for its scope chain (`Map()`, `m[k] = v`, `m.has(k)`, `m[k] ?? -1`). DELIBERATELY the SIMPLEST correct rep, NOT `toWasm.ts`'s open-addressing hash map: a map is two PARALLEL growable lists + LINEAR SCAN — a 2-field struct `{ keys: (ref $keysList), vals: (ref $i32List) }` reusing the G6/G7 `{backing,len,cap}` wrapper twice (a string-ref keys list over `(array (mut (ref null $strArr)))`, and the existing i32 vals list), with NO hashing/probing/new wasm primitives. `Map()` builds two empty lists boxed into the struct; `m[k]=v` scans `keys` via element-wise string `==` (the just-merged G6 compare) and overwrites `vals[i]` on a hit else `keys.push(k); vals.push(v)`; `m[k] ?? d` is a FUSED scan-then-`vals[i]`-or-`d`; `m.has(k)`/`k in m` scans → 1/0. Absent-key semantics match the checker's usage (it reads every scope map through `?? -1`, guarded by `.has`). A per-function 9-slot map scratch frame (map/key/cur refs, scan + char cursors, found index, two grow-backing refs) is reserved after the string-op frame when a function touches a map; scans are O(n) but the checker's scopes are small so linear is correct and fast. `compiler/parser.vl` gained the `{ [string]: T }` index-signature TYPE annotation (a synthetic `"{[K]:V}"` `TypeRef` name, mirroring how `i32[]` carries its suffix) and the `??` operator (precedence table); `ast.vl` needed no new node (`??` is a plain `BinExpr`, `Map()`/`m[k]`/`m.has` already parse). 6 new live-instantiation cases (set+get, two keys, overwrite-in-place, missing-key `??` default, `.has` present/absent, a scope-chain bind-then-lookup pattern). Iteration/`.keys()`/`.values()`/`delete`/`.pop` are deferred (the checker uses none). With G8 the full annotated self-host front end is now emitProgram-compilable.
- **H-emitProgram maps → real open-addressing hash map (G8)** — `emitProgram`'s map lowering is now a REAL ordered open-addressing hash map (FNV-1a + linear probing + load-factor-1/2 resize), mirroring the host's `compiler/builtins/maps.ts` and SUPERSEDING the parallel-lists/linear-scan placeholder above (which stays in history as a fallback). The map struct grows to 5 fields `{ keys, vals, index, count, size }`: `keys`/`vals` remain the insertion-ordered entry lists (so order is preserved and the G6/G7 grow machinery is reused for append), `index` is a raw i32 array of hash slots → entry-position + 1 (0 = empty, initial cap 8), and `count`/`size` track appended/live entries. `Map()` allocates the lists + an 8-slot zero index; every op HASHES the key (FNV-1a over its code points → non-negative i32, `emitMapHashOf`) into `index` and LINEAR-PROBES (wrapping, `emitMapProbe`) to a free-or-matching slot, comparing keys with the G6 element-wise `==` — no O(n) scan. `m[k]=v` overwrites `vals[entry-1]` on a hit else appends an ordered entry, links `index[slot]=count+1`, bumps count/size, and RESIZES (`emitMapResize`) when `size*2 ≥ cap`: a fresh `2×` index is rebuilt from the ordered entries (hash + inner-probe each, keys/vals untouched). `m[k] ?? d` / `m.has(k)` read the probe result. A per-function 12-slot scratch frame (map/key/cur refs, probe slot/cap/entry, two grow backings, char cursor, hash accumulator, resize index backing + inner slot) replaces the old 9-slot frame. 2 new resize-proving cases (ten keys forcing TWO rehashes with every key looked up; resize interleaved with overwrites + a missing-key probe), atop the 6 original G8 behavior tests — all through real `WebAssembly.instantiate`. DELETE/tombstones (the host's `live` array + compaction) are DEFERRED: the checker's op set has no delete, and the struct shape stays forward-compatible for adding a `live` array later. No `parser.vl`/`ast.vl` change.
- **H-emitProgram front-end attempt: `/`/`%`, implicit trailing return, multiline type/union parse (120 green)** — the slices that let the real `ast.vl`/`parser.vl` source flow through the pipeline. `binOpcode` maps `/` → `i32.div_s` (signed) and `%` → `i32.rem_s` (the two integer ops `ast.vl`'s `i32ToStr` needs). A function whose LAST statement is a bare expression (no `return` keyword) now returns that value — the idiom every `ast.vl` constructor uses (`addNode(n)` / `out` as the final line). The self-host parser's `parseTypeDecl` now skips NEWLINE tokens inside a `{ … }` struct-field list and around `A | B | C` union variants (a `skipNewlines()` helper), so a multiline `type` written with a trailing comma parses (every `ast.vl` `type` is multiline). The unsupported-operator guard test repointed to `a ?? 2` outside a map get (still fails loudly). (`tests/selfhost_emit_program_test.ts`)
- **H-emitProgram multiple struct types + string fields (arena keystone, 132 green)** — `emitProgram`'s single-struct path generalized into a flat multi-struct table (`sNames`/`sFieldNames`/`sFieldStart`/`sFieldCount`/`sFieldTypes`/`sHeapIdx`, mirroring the union-variant table): each `type X = { … }` interns its own WasmGC struct heap type, and object-literal construction / field reads / field writes resolve the struct index (by literal field-set / annotation / param / local / return / global). A per-field wasm type is recorded so a STRING struct field lowers to a non-null `(ref $aTypeIdx)` and round-trips; the type section is wrapped in one WasmGC rec group (`0x4e`) so a struct can forward-reference the raw array type that follows it (V8 rejects an out-of-group forward type-index). `exprString` recognizes a string-typed struct-field read so the local infers the string ref type. String fields extend to UNION VARIANTS too (`uFieldTypes`), unblocking `ast.vl`'s Node variants that carry string payloads (`NumLit.numText`, …). (`tests/selfhost_emit_program_test.ts`)
- **H-emitProgram array struct/variant fields + `.push` (139 green)** — a struct or union-variant field whose type is an ARRAY now lowers to a ref to the matching growable-list wrapper: `i32[]` → `(ref $lTypeIdx)`, `T[]`/`N[]` → `(ref $rlTypeIdx)`, reusing the existing list machinery via the generic `struct.new`/`struct.get`/`struct.set` paths (`fieldTypeCode` classifies a field's `TypeRef`, `pushFieldStorage` emits the storagetype, `memFieldTypeCode` + `Member` branches type a field read as a list so `.length`/indexing/assignment work). A narrowed union-variant array-field read recovers the list ref through the box — the `Node`-variant shape (`Call.callArgs: Node[]`, `Block.blkStmts: i32[]`). `.push` onto a struct-field array runs the same inline grow/append. (`tests/selfhost_emit_program_test.ts`)
- **H-emitProgram `string[]` struct/variant fields + `.push` — arena fully expressible (145 green)** — a `string[]` struct or union-variant field is now a string-ref list (the same `{ backing, len, cap }` wrapper the map's keys list uses, over a `(ref null $aTypeIdx)` string backing — no new heap type; shares `mkArrIdx`/`mkListIdx`): `fieldTypeCode` classifies it as a distinct code, `collectA` forces the string-ref-list + string-backing types when a `string[]` annotation appears (instead of rejecting), construction builds a string-list literal (incl. empty `[]` via a threaded `pendingListKind`), reads recover a non-null string element + the wrapper `len`, and field writes / a `string[]` LOCAL store the list ref. `.push` onto a `string[]` field (`b.names.push("x")`) runs the inline grow/append over a dedicated string-push scratch quad, with the push-kind detector now a 3-way i32/ref/str split so an i32 push can't mis-claim a string-list receiver. With this the self-host arena's full field vocabulary is emittable. (`tests/selfhost_emit_program_test.ts`)
- **H-emitProgram struct-typed params in mutually-recursive groups (151 green)** — closes the keystone gap: passing a struct VALUE into a forward-referenced / mutually-recursive callee whose parameter is a struct type miscompiled when two structs shared a field-name set — a struct-literal call argument was constructed from a field-name guess (the first matching struct) instead of the callee parameter's DECLARED struct type, so the module failed to validate (the `parseExpr(p)`/`parseStmt(p)` recursive-descent shape, where every parser-state struct looks alike). A `pendingStructIdx` hint (mirroring `pendingListKind`) is threaded from `emitCall` — for each struct-typed parameter position it sets the callee's struct index (via `vtKindOfParam`/`paramStructIdxOf` over the callee `FuncDecl`), and `emitObj` consults it to OVERRIDE the field-name match, consuming it so nested literals fall back to inference. The functype param type-indices were already per-param-correct; only the call-site literal construction was wrong. (`tests/selfhost_emit_program_test.ts`)
- **H-fixpoint self-hosting fixpoint — M_self emits the goldens byte-for-byte** — closes the loop from VALIDITY (#241: the emitter self-compiles to an instantiable module) to CORRECTNESS. Drives the genuine lexer→parser→`emitProgram` pipeline over the emitter's OWN source plus a tiny exported entry point to produce `M_self` (the module the self-host emitter emits from itself), then INSTANTIATES `M_self` and runs it AS an emitter over the 14 byte-exact golden sources, asserting each output is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the host-pinned `tests/golden/*.wasm` — i.e. `f_self(P) == f_host(P)` for 14 real programs. The self-emitted module has no `print` import and `emitProgram` lowers only functions + module globals, so it is driven through `export function`s (`runEmit`/`rbyteLen`/`rbyteAt`) and its WasmGC byte buffer read back one byte at a time. The golden source set is single-sourced into `tests/selfhost/goldens.ts` so the byte-exact pin and the fixpoint drive the IDENTICAL set. No `compiler/*.vl` change needed — the emitter was already a correct fixed point. (`tests/selfhost_emit_fixpoint_test.ts`, #242)
- **H-fixpoint full-source fixpoint (gcc stage2==stage3) — M_self re-emits the WHOLE compiler byte-identically** — the bootstrap capstone, on the single largest real input there is: the compiler itself. `M_self` (the self-emitted emitter, ~1.2 MB) re-emits the entire compiler source `X` (lexer+ast+parser+wasmEmit, ~402 K chars → 132157 wasm bytes) BYTE-FOR-BYTE identically to the host-compiled emitter (`self_bytes(X) == host_bytes(X)`, both instantiate), exercising the large-magnitude LEB128 / section-size encodings the toy goldens never reach. GATED behind `SELFHOST_FULL_FIXPOINT=1` (two ~50–64 s host compiles of ~0.8 M / ~1.2 M-char sources embedding `X` as `array.new_fixed` runs); the always-run goldens fixpoint guards the emitter every commit. (`tests/selfhost_emit_fullfixpoint_test.ts`, #242)
- **H-M2 `scripts/native-fixpoint.sh` — the auditable bootstrap proof, committed** — the stage3 == stage4 native fixpoint as a reproducible ~6 s script (seed compiles the compiler; the result compiles + runs a program; the result re-compiles the compiler; byte-compare) with zero TS/deno/V8 past the seed — even the compiler-source assembly is `sed`/`cat`. The source-in/bytes-out DRIVER is single-sourced into `scripts/vl-compiler-driver.vl` (read by the stage-0 seed builder AND cat'd by the fixpoint script), so the seed and a self-rebuilt compiler expose the identical surface by construction — verified by a byte-identical seed rebuild after the extraction. This is the script the "drop TS / binary seed" end-state pins its audit on. (`scripts/native-fixpoint.sh`, `scripts/vl-compiler-driver.vl`, `scripts/build-compiler-wasm.ts`)
- **H2 `fromCodePoints(xs: i32[]): string` builtin — the native fixpoint closes: stage3 == stage4 under wasmtime, self-rebuild 6m29s → 2.8s (138×)** — bulk string construction from a code-point list in ONE copy, end-to-end in BOTH compilers (host: `__codes_to_string__` helper in `toWasm.ts` + `defaultScope` typing; self-host: `typecheck.vl` builtin + `wasmEmit.vl` lowering over the string-op scratch frame — the argument must be a NAMED `i32[]` binding, re-read per use). The native `vl` tool's source feed now pushes code points into an `i32[]` (amortized) and converts once — eliminating the per-char concat churn that the toolchain audit triangulated as THE bottleneck (per-element `array.copy` host libcalls under wasmtime): bench `vl check` 13.8s → **0.11s**, the 561KB compiler self-rebuild 6m29s → **2.8s**, and the previously-infeasible NATIVE fixpoint (stage3 re-compiles the compiler source → stage4) completes in 2.8s and holds **byte-for-byte — the gcc-style self-hosting fixed point with zero TS/deno/V8 anywhere in the loop** (even the source concat is `sed`/`cat`). (`compiler/toWasm.ts`, `compiler/defaultScope.ts`, `compiler/typecheck.vl`, `compiler/wasmEmit.vl`, `scripts/build-compiler-wasm.ts`, `tests/cases/strings/from-code-points.vl`)
- **H-M2 the native `vl` tool — compile and run VL with zero TS/deno/node (post-seed)** — a single deno-style binary (`scripts/vl-host`, Rust + wasmtime 45) with `vl build` / `vl check` / `vl run` subcommands. The Rust side is a THIN OS adapter (argv, file I/O, stdout, engine embedding) — ALL compiler logic lives in `build/vl-compiler.wasm`, the five-module VL-written compiler compiled to wasm by `scripts/build-compiler-wasm.ts` (stage 0: the deno seed, kept permanently as the from-scratch bootstrap path and the fixpoint cross-check; after seeding, `vl build` over the compiler's own source rebuilds the compiler with itself). Future subcommands (fmt, test, …) follow the same shape: brains in the wasm, adapter stays an I/O shim. Two real findings along the way: (1) the source-feed driver's per-code-point string append was QUADRATIC copy churn (a VL string is a real WasmGC array, not a V8 rope) — exhausted wasmtime's GC heap on a compiler-sized input; the driver now accumulates bounded 4096-char chunks. (2) wasmtime 27's DRC GC heap is a FIXED 512 KiB mmap (no growth, no config knob) — bumped to wasmtime 45 (growable GC heaps; its own `wasmtime::Error` replaces anyhow). (`scripts/vl-host/`, `scripts/build-compiler-wasm.ts`)
- **H-emit-portable print imports use STANDALONE functypes — self-hosted output runs on a strict WasmGC host (wasmtime), not just V8** — the self-host emitter wrapped EVERY functype in the module's single WasmGC rec group, so a `print` import's functype carried nominal rec-group identity. V8 accepted that, but a strict-GC host (wasmtime) refuses to match a rec-group-member functype against a host-provided function — so self-hosted-emitted print modules failed to instantiate under wasmtime (`incompatible import type for imports::__print_i32__`, with types that look identical). The fix emits the two print functypes (`(i32)->()`, `()->()`) as STANDALONE rectypes AFTER the rec group instead of inside it: the type section's rectype count goes `1 → 1 + nPrintTypes`, the rec-group's own count drops by the same, and the functype bytes + their type INDICES are unchanged — so the import-section references, the print-FREE goldens, and the full-source fixpoint (the compiler is print-free, `nPrintTypes == 0`) all stay byte-identical. Verified end-to-end (a self-hosted-emitted program runs under wasmtime), with a decode-and-assert test pinning the invariant: every imported functype index lands past the rec group's members. (`compiler/wasmEmit.vl`, `tests/selfhost_corpus_run_test.ts`)
- **H-emit-full-compiler the emitter emits the WHOLE compiler — full-source fixpoint now covers the typechecker (stage2==stage3 on all 5 modules)** — the bootstrap capstone extended from the front end to the COMPLETE compiler: `M_self` re-emits `lexer + ast + parser + typecheck + wasmEmit` byte-for-byte identically to the host emitter, and the result instantiates. Together with the self-typecheck rung (VL typechecks VL), the entire VL compiler now self-hosts through the VL pipeline — it both typechecks and emits its own five-module source. Four gaps closed to emit `typecheck.vl`: (1) a bare-MAP struct field (`Checker.userTypes`) — hoisted to a module-level map global (the emitter handles a map global but not a map struct field; one checker instance, so equivalent); (2) field WRITES on a narrowed union variant / variant-typed param (`if rt is TyMap { rt.mKey = k0 }`, the in-place map-hole filling) — a `struct.set` through the box cast, mirroring the read path; (3) a shared `memberFieldCode` classifier so `exprString`/field reads resolve a field's type through ALL three receiver shapes (narrowed local, variant-typed param `checkXNode(n: Variant)`, plain struct), not just the first two; (4) — a real, latent encoding BUG — valtype heap-type indices were written as UNSIGNED LEB but a `(ref ht)` heap type is SIGNED (s33), so any reference to a type index ≥ 64 mis-decoded (index 64 → `0x40` → `-64`, "unknown heap type"). Identical for indices < 64 (every prior golden / the 4-module fixpoint stayed byte-for-byte), so it only surfaced now that the `Checker` struct references a ref-list wrapper past index 64. Fixed across the valtype emitters (`fbValtype`/`fbValtypeNullable`/`pushFieldStorage`/the rec-group field defs) — instruction immediates (`struct.new`/`array.get` $t, u32 LEB) are untouched. (`compiler/typecheck.vl`, `compiler/wasmEmit.vl`, `tests/selfhost_emit_fullfixpoint_test.ts`)
- **H-self-typecheck VL typechecks VL — the whole compiler, zero diagnostics (Phase 2 capstone)** — the type-checker companion to the full-source emitter fixpoint: the self-hosted `lexer.vl → parser.vl → typecheck.vl` pipeline, running as wasm, typechecks the compiler's ENTIRE own source (all five modules, including `typecheck.vl` itself) with ZERO diagnostics — closing the Phase-1 substrate's coverage track on the largest real input. Surfaced and fixed the one real gap blocking it: `typecheck.vl` typed a CHAR literal `'x'` as `string`, but a char literal is its i32 CODE POINT (composes with arithmetic / `==` / string indexing like a bare int literal — `chars/literals.vl`), so every `c == '0'` / `s[i] == 'x'` idiom in the lexer/parser was a false `cannot compare i32 with string` — 231 spurious diagnostics on the compiler's own source, all cleared by the one-line type fix. GATED behind `SELFHOST_FULL_FIXPOINT=1` (one heavy host compile + a wasm re-typecheck of the ~0.9 M-char five-module pipeline), the same lane as the emitter fixpoint. (`compiler/typecheck.vl`, `tests/selfhost_self_typecheck_test.ts`)
- **H-check self-hosted `vl check` substrate — type-check GATES emit (Phase 1)** — wires the self-host TYPECHECKER (`typecheck.vl`) into the back-end path: `lexer → parser → checkProgram → (gate) → emitProgram`, all in one VL-compiled module. A clean program type-checks then emits a valid, instantiable module; an ill-typed one is REJECTED by `checkProgram` and never reaches `emitProgram`. Pure wiring — the self-host emitter takes only the parser arena and re-derives all types itself (`collectS`/`collectU`/`buildFnMap` + its own narrowing stack), so `checkProgram` is a DIAGNOSTICS GATE with no data handoff to emit; the five modules concatenate with ZERO symbol collisions beyond the usual lexer renames. `checkProgram`'s return rides in expression position (`i32ToStr(checkProgram(root))`, the PR#5 codegen gap), then `T.diags` drives the gate. 7 cases: 4 must-pass (check clean → emit → instantiate → run: `main`/`fib(10)`/locals/call-chain) + 3 must-reject (undeclared identifier, wrong arg count, `i32 = "hi"` assign mismatch — each blocked before emit). Proven on the subset `typecheck.vl` covers today (primitives, arithmetic/logical/compare ops, calls, member access, `let`/`func`/`if`/`return`/`block`, structural assignability, scope/shadowing); growing it to the full emit vocabulary (structs/unions/arrays/strings/maps/`is`/loops → eventually the compiler's own source) is the Phase 2 coverage-closure track. (`tests/selfhost_check_emit_test.ts`)
- **H-check Phase 2.1: STRUCTS in `typecheck.vl`** — the first coverage-closure step grows the self-host checker to type-check declared struct types. A new `userTypes: {[string]: i32}` registry on the `Checker` maps a `type Name = { … }` to its arena `TyObj`, filled by a Program **pass-0** (before function hoisting, so a struct name resolves in param/return/`let` annotations and in later structs' fields — reading `TypeDecl`/`FieldDef`/`TypeRef` INLINE, the mono-visitor constraint); `nameToTy` consults it after the primitives. An `ObjLit` (`{ x: e, … }`) now types as a structural `TyObj` of its field value types, which the existing `assignable`/`objFieldType` machinery matches to a declared struct by STRUCTURE (field order-independent). Struct-typed params/returns/locals, object-literal construction (annotation- / return-typed), member reads (`p.x`), and field WRITES (`p.x = v`, via the existing `=` path over a `Member` LHS) now type-check; field-type mismatches, missing/unknown fields, and unknown type names are rejected. Wired through the check→emit gate: 4 new cases — 2 must-pass (the `g_struct` coverage program → 45; structs + module globals + field write `g_globals` → 42, both emit + instantiate + run) + 2 must-reject (`{x:"hi"}` to `{x:i32}`; read of an absent field — gate-blocked). Forward references to a LATER-declared struct report "unknown type" (deferred — unused by the immediate set). (`compiler/typecheck.vl`, `tests/selfhost_check_emit_test.ts`)
- **H-check Phase 2.2: UNIONS + `is`-narrowing in `typecheck.vl`** — the bootstrap-keystone coverage step (both the self-host AST `Node` and the checker's own `Ty` are discriminated unions). A `type N = A | B` (`UnionDecl`, variant NAMES) registers in the same Program **pass-0** as structs — each variant name resolves (its struct registered earlier in source order) into a `TyUnion` of member type-arena indices. `x is T` (`IsExpr`) always types as `bool` and checks `T` names a known type; the actual **flow narrowing** happens in `if x is T { … }`: when the condition is an `is` over a simple IDENT, a pushed scope shadows `x` with the variant type `T` for the THEN branch only (reading the `IsExpr` + `Ident` INLINE, mono-visitor), so a narrowed field read (`if n is A { n.av }`) type-checks while an un-narrowed union member access stays an error. Union CONSTRUCTION needs no new code — an object literal assignable to ANY variant already satisfies the union-target branch of `assignable`. Wired through the gate: 4 new cases — 2 must-pass (`g_union` → 7: narrow-and-read + `{av:x}` construction typed by a `Node` return; `g_multiunion` → 30: two coexisting unions, each narrowed) + 2 must-reject (`is A` then read B's field → "no field"; a union naming an undeclared variant → "unknown type"). Negative narrowing (the ELSE branch / `!(x is T)`) and narrowing a non-ident operand are deferred. (`compiler/typecheck.vl`, `tests/selfhost_check_emit_test.ts`)
- **H-check Phase 2.3: ARRAYS in `typecheck.vl`** — grows the self-host checker to type-check growable arrays. A new `TyArray { aElem }` arena variant; `nameToTy` resolves a `T[]` annotation by stripping the parser's literal `[]` suffix and wrapping the element type (nests for `T[][]`); `assignable`/`tyToStr` gain array cases (an empty `[]` has element `-1`, assignable to any array — its concrete element pinned by the binding annotation). `checkNode` gains: array literals (`[e, …]` → `T[]` of the first element's type, rest checked assignable; empty `[]` → hole), index `a[i]` (i32 index, yields the element; string index → i32 code point), `.length` on arrays/strings → i32, and the `xs.push(e)` builtin method (receiver-is-array special case in the call path, element-type-checked — reads the Member callee inline, mono-visitor). Wired through the gate: 4 new cases — 2 must-pass (`i32[]` push/index-write/index-read/`.length` → 142; `Tok[]` push object literals + index a field → 32, both emit + instantiate + run) + 2 must-reject (`.push` of the wrong element type; indexing a non-array). (`compiler/typecheck.vl`, `tests/selfhost_check_emit_test.ts`)
